## I Arden’s Lord / 阿登之主

===Original===
It had been a great house once, with farms and fields, money and jewels—with tenants and squires and men-at-arms. The head of the house had ridden out three days’ journey to meet King Henry at the boundary of his estate, and the King had ridden back with him to lie in the tall State bed in the castle guest-chamber. The heir of the house had led his following against Cromwell; younger sons of the house had fought in foreign lands, to the honour of England and the gilding and regilding with the perishable gold of glory of the old Arden name. There had been Ardens in Saxon times, and there were Ardens still—but few and impoverished. The lands were gone, and the squires and men-at-arms; the castle itself was roofless, and its unglazed windows stared blankly across the fields of strangers, that stretched right up to the foot of its grey, weatherworn walls. And of the male Ardens there were now known two only—an old man and a child.

The old man was Lord Arden, the head of the house, and he lived lonely in a little house built of the fallen stones that Time and Cromwell’s round-shot had cast from the castle walls. The child was Edred Arden, and he lived in a house in a clean, windswept town on a cliff.

It was a bright-faced house with bow-windows and a green balcony that looked out over the sparkling sea. It had three neat white steps and a brass knocker, pale and smooth with constant rubbing. It was a pretty house, and it would have been a pleasant house but for one thing—the lodgers. For I cannot conceal from you any longer that Edred Arden lived with his aunt, and that his aunt let lodgings. Letting lodgings is one of the most unpleasant of all possible ways of earning your living, and I advise you to try every other honest way of earning *your* living before you take to that.

===Chinese===
这曾经是一个显赫的门第，有田庄牧场，有金银珠宝——有佃户乡绅，有披甲的家兵。当年的家主曾骑马赶三天路程，到领地边界迎接亨利王，国王也果然随他回府，在城堡客房那张高大的国宾床上安寝。这个家的继承人曾率领族中子弟与克伦威尔作战；几家幼子曾在异国疆场上厮杀，为英格兰增光，为古老的阿登家声一遍又一遍镀上那易逝的荣光之金。撒克逊时代便有阿登家的人，如今也还有——只是人丁寥落，家境贫寒。田产没了，乡绅和家兵也没了；城堡本身没了屋顶，没镶玻璃的窗洞茫然地眺望着陌生人的田地，那些田地一直伸展到它饱经风霜的灰色墙根下。而阿登家的男丁，如今世人所知的只剩两个——一位老人，一个孩子。

那位老人是阿登勋爵（Lord Arden），一家之主，独自住在小屋里，屋子用岁月和克伦威尔的圆炮弹从城墙上震落的石头砌成。那个孩子是埃德雷德·阿登（Edred Arden），住在悬崖上一座干净而风大的小镇里的一所房子中。

那是一所面容明朗的房子，有着凸窗和绿色阳台，俯瞰波光粼粼的大海。门前是三级整洁的白台阶，门上装着黄铜门环，被经年累月的摩挲磨得暗淡光滑。这是一所漂亮的房子，若不是一件事，本该也是一所舒心的房子——房客。因为我再也无法向你隐瞒：埃德雷德·阿登同姑姑住在一起，而他姑姑是出租房间的。出租房间是天下一切谋生之道里最不痛快的一种，我劝你把其余所有正当的谋生方法都试遍了，再去干*你*要干的那一行。

===Original===
Because people who go to the seaside and take lodgings seem, somehow, much harder to please than the people who go to hotels. They want ever so much more waiting on; they want so many meals, and at such odd times. They ring the bell almost all day long. They bring in sand from the shore in every fold of their clothes, and it shakes out of them on to the carpets and the sofa cushions, and everything in the house. They hang long streamers of wet seaweed against the pretty roses of the new wallpapers, and their washhand basins are always full of sea anemones and shells. Also, they are noisy; their boots seem to be always on the stairs, no matter how bad a headache you may have; and when you give them their bill they always think it is too much, no matter how little it may be. So do not let lodgings if you can help it.

Miss Arden could not help it. It happened like this.

Edred and his sister were at school. (Did I tell you that he had a sister? Well, he had, and her name was Elfrida.) Miss Arden lived near the school, so that she could see the children often. She was getting her clothes ready for her wedding, and the gentleman who was going to marry her was coming home from South America, where he had made a fortune. The children’s father was coming home from South America, too, with the fortune that he had made, for he and Miss Arden’s sweetheart were partners. The children and their aunt talked whenever they met of the glorious time that was coming, and how, when father and Uncle Jim—they called him Uncle Jim already—came home, they were all going to live in the country and be happy ever after.

===Chinese===
因为到海边来租房的人，不知怎么的，似乎比住旅馆的人难伺候得多。他们要人服侍的地方多得不得了；他们要吃那么多顿饭，还专挑古怪的钟点。他们差不多整天按铃。他们把海滩上的沙子藏进衣服的每一道褶缝里带进屋来，抖落在地毯上、沙发垫上和屋子里的每样东西上。他们把一长条一长条湿漉漉的海带搭在新墙纸好看的玫瑰花纹上，他们的洗脸盆里永远盛满了海葵和贝壳。还有，他们吵闹；不管你头疼得多厉害，他们的靴子好像总在楼梯上踏个不停；等你把账单递给他们，无论数目多么小，他们总嫌太贵。所以只要躲得开，千万别出租房间。

阿登小姐（Miss Arden）却躲不开。事情是这样的。

埃德雷德和他姐姐本来在上学。（我告诉过你他有个姐姐吗？好吧，他有，她叫埃尔弗丽达［Elfrida］。）阿登小姐住在学校附近，好常常看见孩子们。她正在置办嫁衣，要娶她的那位先生正从南美回来，他在那边发了财。孩子们的爸爸也要从南美回来，带着他赚下的家业，因为他和阿登小姐的心上人是合伙人。孩子们和姑姑每逢见面就谈论那即将到来的好日子，说等爸爸和吉姆叔叔（Uncle Jim）——他们已经管他叫吉姆叔叔了——回来，大家就都搬到乡下住，从此快快活活地过日子。

===Original===
And then the news came that father and Uncle Jim had been captured by brigands, and all the money was lost, too, and there was nothing left but the house on the cliff. So Miss Arden took the children from the expensive school in London, and they all went to live in the cliff house, and as there was no money to live on, and no other way of making money to live on except letting lodgings, Miss Arden let them, like the brave lady she was, and did it well. And then came the news that father and Uncle Jim were dead, and for a time the light of life went out in Cliff House.

This was two years ago; but the children had never got used to the lodgers. They hated them. At first they had tried to be friendly with the lodgers’ children, but they soon found that the lodgers’ children considered Edred and Elfrida very much beneath them, and looked down on them accordingly. And very often the lodgers’ children were the sort of children on whom anybody might have looked down, if it were right and kind to look down on anyone. And when Master Reginald Potts, of Peckham, puts his tongue out at you on the parade and says, right before everybody, “Lodgings! Yah!” it is hard to feel quite the same to him as you did before.

When there were lodgers—and there nearly always were, for the house was comfortable, and people who had been once came again—the children and their aunt had to live in the very top and the very bottom of the house—in the attics and the basement, in fact.

===Chinese===
接着消息传来：爸爸和吉姆叔叔被强盗掳去了，钱也全丢了，只剩下悬崖上那所房子。于是阿登小姐把孩子们从伦敦收费昂贵的学校里领了出来，一家人搬进悬崖屋（Cliff House）去住。既然没有生活费，又除出租房间之外别无挣钱糊口的路子，阿登小姐便拿出她那样勇敢女人的气概，把房间租了出去，而且经营得很好。再后来消息传来：爸爸和吉姆叔叔死了。有一阵子，悬崖屋里生命的光熄灭了。

这是两年前的事了；可孩子们始终没能习惯房客。他们恨房客。起初他们还想同房客的孩子们交朋友，但很快便发现，房客的孩子们觉得埃德雷德和埃尔弗丽达比他们低了一大截，于是照此加以轻视。而那些房客的孩子，往往正是那种——倘若轻视任何人算得上正当和厚道的话——谁都尽可以轻视的孩子。何况当佩卡姆的雷金纳德·波茨（Reginald Potts）少爷在海滨散步道上当着众人的面向你伸舌头，大声说「出租房间！呸！」的时候，你就很难再像从前那样看待他了。

有房客的时候——而几乎总是有房客的，因为房子舒适，来住过的人还会再来——孩子们和姑姑只好住在房子的最顶上和最底下，也就是阁楼和地下室。

===Original===
When there were no lodgers they used all the rooms in turn, to keep them aired. But the children liked the big basement parlour room best, because there all the furniture had belonged to dead-and-gone Ardens, and all the pictures on the walls were of Ardens dead and gone. The rooms that the lodgers had were furnished with a new sort of furniture that had no stories belonging to it such as belonged to the old polished oak tables and bureaux that were in the basement parlour.

Edred and Elfrida went to school every day and learned reading, writing, arithmetic, geography, history, spelling, and useful knowledge, all of which they hated quite impartially, which means they hated the whole lot—one thing as much as another.

The only part of lessons they liked was the homework, when, if Aunt Edith had time to help them, geography became like adventures, history like storybooks, and even arithmetic suddenly seemed to mean something.

===Chinese===
没有房客的时候，他们便轮流使用所有的房间，好让它们透透气。但孩子们最喜欢地下室那间大客厅，因为那里的家具全是从前故去的阿登家的人传下来的，墙上的画像画的也全是故去的阿登家的人。房客住的房间里摆的是新式家具，不像地下室客厅里那些磨得发亮的老橡木桌子和旧写字柜，件件都有自己的来历。

埃德雷德和埃尔弗丽达每天上学，学阅读、写字、算术、地理、历史、拼写，还有实用知识，所有这些他们都一视同仁地痛恨，意思是说他们恨的是全套——恨这个不比恨那个少。

功课里他们唯一喜欢的是做家庭作业的时候——倘若伊迪丝姑姑（Aunt Edith）有空帮忙，地理就变得像冒险，历史像故事书，连算术也忽然显得有了意思。

===Original===
“I wish you could teach us always,” said Edred, very inky, and interested for the first time in the exports of China; “it does seem so silly trying to learn things that are only words in books.”

“I wish I could,” said Aunt Edith, “but I can’t do twenty-nine thousand and seventeen things all at once, and—” A bell jangled. “That’s the seventh time since tea.” She got up and went into the kitchen. “There’s the bell again, my poor Eliza. Never mind; answer the bell, but don’t answer *them*, whatever they say. It doesn’t do a bit of good, and it sometimes prevents their giving you half-crowns when they leave.”

“I do love it when they go,” said Elfrida.

“Yes,” said her aunt. “A cab top-heavy with luggage, the horse’s nose turned stationward, it’s a heavenly sight—when the bill is paid and—But, then, I’m just as glad to see the luggage coming. Chickens! when my ship comes home we’ll go and live on a desert island where there aren’t any cabs, and we won’t have any lodgers in our cave.”

===Chinese===
「真希望你能一直教我们。」埃德雷德说，他手上沾满墨水，头一回对中国出口商品有了兴趣。「要学的东西净是书本上的字句，实在显得太傻了。」

「我也希望我能啊。」伊迪丝姑姑说，「可我没法子同时做两万九千零一十七件事，而且——」铃铛丁零零响了。「这是喝过下午茶以来的第七次。」她起身走进厨房。「铃又响了，我可怜的伊莱扎（Eliza）。别在意；去应铃，可别去应*他们*，随他们说什么都别回嘴。回嘴一点好处也没有，有时还会害得他们临走时少给你半克朗。」

「他们走的时候我可爱看了。」埃尔弗丽达说。

「是啊。」姑姑说。「行李堆得头重脚轻的马车，马鼻子掉转朝向车站，那真是天堂般的景象——账单付清了，而且——不过，行李运进来的时候我照样高兴。小鸡们！等我的船回家了，我们就去住荒岛，岛上没有马车，我们的山洞里也不收任何房客。」

===Original===
“When I grow up,” said Edred, “I shall go across the sea and look for your ship and bring it home. I shall take a steam-tug and steer it myself.”

“Then I shall be captain,” said Elfrida.

“No, I shall be captain.”

“You can’t if you steer.”

===Chinese===
「等我长大了，」埃德雷德说，「我就渡海去找你那条船，把它带回家来。我要开一条蒸汽拖船，亲自掌舵。」

「那我就当船长。」埃尔弗丽达说。

「不行，我当船长。」

「你掌舵就当不成船长。」

===Original===
“Yes, I can!”

“No, you can’t!”

“Yes, I can!”

“Well, do, then!” said Elfrida; “and while you’re doing it—I *know* you can’t—I shall dig in the garden and find a goldmine, and Aunt Edith will be rolling in money when you come back, and she won’t want your silly old ship.”

===Chinese===
「我当得成！」

「你当不成！」

「我当得成！」

「好啊，那你就当吧！」埃尔弗丽达说；「等你掌舵的时候——我*知道*你当不成——我就在园子里挖出金矿来，等你回来，伊迪丝姑姑已经躺在钱堆里打滚，才不稀罕你那条蠢船呢。」

===Original===
“Spelling next,” said Aunt Edith. “How do you spell ‘disagreeable’?”

“Which of us?” asked Edred acutely.

“Both,” said Aunt Edith, trying to look very severe.

===Chinese===
「下面听写。」伊迪丝姑姑说。「『讨厌的』这个词怎么拼？」

「问我们哪一个？」埃德雷德机敏地问。

「两个都问。」伊迪丝姑姑说，努力装出非常严厉的样子。

===Original===
When you are a child you always dream of your ship coming home—of having a hundred pounds, or a thousand, or a million pounds to spend as you like. My favourite dream, I remember, was a thousand pounds and an express understanding that I was not to spend it on anything useful. And when you have dreamed of your million pounds, or your thousand, or your hundred, you spend happy hour on hour in deciding what presents you will buy for each of the people you are fond of, and in picturing their surprise and delight at your beautiful presents and your wonderful generosity. I think very few of us spend our dream fortunes entirely on ourselves. Of course, we buy ourselves a motor-bicycle straight away, and footballs and bats—and dolls with real hair, and real china tea-sets, and large boxes of mixed chocolates, and *Treasure Island*, and all the books that Mrs. Ewing ever wrote, but when we have done that we begin to buy things for other people. It is a beautiful dream, but too often, by the time it comes true—up to a hundred pounds or a thousand—we forget what we used to mean to do with our money, and spend it all in stocks and shares, and eligible building sites, and fat cigars and fur coats. If I were young again I would sit down and write a list of all the kind things I meant to do when my ship came home, and if my ship ever did come home I would read that list, and—But the parlour bell is ringing for the eighth time, and the front-door bell is ringing too, and the first-floor is ringing also, and so is the second-floor, and Eliza is trying to answer four bells at once—always a most difficult thing to do.

===Chinese===
你是个孩子的时候，总梦想着你的船回家——梦想有一百镑，或一千镑，或一百万镑，随你心意花销。我记得我自己最心爱的梦，是一千镑，外加一个明明白白的约定：不许花在任何有用的东西上。等你梦想好了你的一百万镑、一千镑或一百镑，你就一小时又一小时地快活盘算着，要给你喜欢的每一个人买什么礼物，想象他们收到你漂亮的礼物、见识了你了不起的慷慨时又惊又喜的模样。我想我们中间很少有人把梦里的财产全花在自己身上。当然，我们会立刻给自己买一辆摩托自行车，买足球和球棒——买长着真头发的娃娃、真的瓷茶具、大盒大盒的什锦巧克力，还有《金银岛》，还有尤因太太（Mrs. Ewing）写的全部的书；可是买完这些，我们就开始给别人买东西了。这是个美丽的梦，可等到它成真的时候——成真到一百镑或一千镑的地步——我们往往已经忘了当初打算拿这些钱做什么，把它全花在股票债券上、体面的宅基上、粗雪茄和皮大衣上了。要是我能再年轻一回，我一定坐下来，把我的船回家时打算做的一切好事列成一张单子；要是我的船真回了家，我就把单子拿出来照着办，可是——客厅的铃第八次响了，前门的铃也在响，二楼在响，三楼也在响，伊莱扎正想一个人同时应四处的铃——这向来是顶顶难办的事。

===Original===
The front-door bell was rung by the postman; he brought three letters. The first was a bill for mending the lid of the cistern, on which Edred had recently lighted a fire, fortified by an impression that wood could not burn if there were water on the other side—a totally false impression, as the charred cistern lid proved. The second was an inquiry whether Miss Arden would take a clergyman in at half the usual price, because he had a very large family which had all just had measles. And the third was *the* letter, which is really the seed, and beginning, and backbone, and rhyme, and reason of this story.

Edred had got the letters from the postman, and he stood and waited while Aunt Edith read them. He collected postmarks, and had not been able to make out by the thick half-light of the hall gas whether any of these were valuable.

The third letter had a very odd effect on Aunt Edith. She read it once, and rubbed her hand across her eyes. Then she got up and stood under the chandelier, which wanted new burners badly, and so burned with a very unlighting light, and read it again. Then she read it a third time, and then she said, “Oh!”

===Chinese===
前门的铃是邮差按响的；他带来三封信。第一封是修理水箱盖的账单——埃德雷德前不久在水箱盖上生过火，他坚信背面积着水的木头烧不起来——这想法完全错误，烧焦的水箱盖便是明证。第二封是来问阿登小姐肯不肯半价收一位牧师住宿，因为他有一大家子人，而且全家刚出过麻疹。第三封则是*那封*信——它实在是这个故事的种子、开端、脊梁、韵脚和缘由。

信是埃德雷德从邮差手里接的，他站在一旁等伊迪丝姑姑读完。他收集邮戳，刚才在门厅煤气灯昏沉的微光里，没能看清这几枚邮戳值不值钱。

第三封信在伊迪丝姑姑身上产生了十分古怪的效果。她读了一遍，用手抹了抹眼睛。随后她站起身，走到枝形吊灯底下——那灯早该换新灯头了，于是发出一种照不亮什么的灯光——又读了一遍。然后她读了第三遍，然后她说：「哦！」

===Original===
“What is it, auntie?” Elfrida asked anxiously; “is it the taxes?” It had been the taxes once, and Elfrida had never forgotten. (If you don’t understand what this means ask your poorest relations, who are also likely to be your nicest, and if they don’t know, ask the washerwoman.)

“No; it’s not the taxes, darling,” said Aunt Edith; “on the contrary.”

I don’t know what the contrary (or opposite) of taxes is, any more than the children did—but I am sure it is something quite nice—and so were they.

“Oh, auntie, I *am* so glad,” they both said, and said it several times before they asked again, “What is it?”

“I think—I’m not quite sure—but I think it’s a ship come home—oh, just a quite tiny little bit of a ship—a toy boat—hardly more than that. But I must go up to London tomorrow the first thing, and see if it really is a ship, and, if so, what sort of ship it is. Mrs. Blake shall come in, and you’ll be good as gold, children, won’t you?”

===Chinese===
「是什么呀，姑姑？」埃尔弗丽达着急地问；「是税单吗？」有一回来的就是税单，埃尔弗丽达一直没忘记。（你要是不懂这话的意思，就去问你那些最穷的亲戚——他们多半也是你最好的亲戚——要是他们也不知道，就去问洗衣妇。）

「不是；不是税单，亲爱的。」伊迪丝姑姑说；「恰恰相反。」

税单的反面（或者说对立面）是什么，我同孩子们一样不知道——但我确信那准是顶好的东西——他们也这么想。

「哦，姑姑，我们*真*高兴。」姐弟俩都说，而且说了好几遍才又问：「到底是什么呀？」

「我想——我不太有把握——不过我想，是一条船回家了——哦，只是顶小顶小的一条船——一只玩具船——比玩具船大不了多少。可我明天一早头一件事就得去伦敦，看看它到底是不是一条船，倘若是，又是条什么样的船。我会请布莱克太太（Mrs. Blake）过来照看，你们会乖得像金子一样，孩子们，好不好？」

===Original===
“Yes—oh, yes,” said the two.

“And not make booby traps for the butcher, or go on the roof in your nightgowns, or play Red Indians in the dustbin, or make apple-pie beds for the lodgers?” Aunt Edith asked, hastily mentioning a few of the little amusements which had lately enlivened the spare time of her nephew and niece.

“No, we really won’t,” said Edred; “and we’ll truly try not to think of anything new and amusing,” he added, with real self-sacrifice.

“I must go by the eight-thirty train. I wish I could think of some way of—of amusing you,” she ended, for she was too kind to say “of keeping you out of mischief for the day,” which was what she really thought. “I’ll bring you something jolly for your birthday, Edred. Wouldn’t you like to spend the day with nice Mrs. Hammond?”

===Chinese===
「好——哦，好。」两人说。

「而且不给屠夫设捉弄人的机关，不穿着睡衣上房顶，不在垃圾桶里玩红印第安人，不给房客铺那种床单对折、叫人钻不进被窝的『苹果派床』？」伊迪丝姑姑一口气点出几样近来为她侄子侄女的闲暇时光增过色的小小娱乐。

「不干了，真不干了。」埃德雷德说；「我们还真心实意地尽量不去想任何新鲜好玩的事。」他补了一句，颇有一点自我牺牲精神。

「我得坐八点半的火车走。我真想得出个什么法子——好让你们高兴高兴。」她这样收尾，因为她心地太好，不肯把真心话「让你们一整天别惹祸」说出口，虽然她心里想的正是这个。「我会给你带件痛快的生日礼物回来，埃德雷德。你们不愿意到和气的哈蒙德太太（Mrs. Hammond）家去度过这一天吗？」

===Original===
“Oh, *no*,” said Edred; and added, on the inspiration of the moment, “Why mayn’t we have a picnic—just Elf and me—on the downs, to keep my birthday? It doesn’t matter it being the day before, does it? You said we were too little last summer, and we should this, and now it is this and I have grown two inches and Elf’s grown three, so we’re five inches taller than when you said we weren’t big enough.”

“Now you see how useful arithmetic is,” said the aunt. “Very well, you shall. Only wear your old clothes, and always keep in sight of the road. Yes; you can have a whole holiday. And now to bed. Oh, there’s that bell again! Poor, dear Eliza.”

===Chinese===
「哦，*不*想去。」埃德雷德说；跟着灵机一动，又补上：「为什么不让我和埃尔夫两个人——去丘原上野餐，给我过生日呢？日子提前一天没关系吧？去年夏天你说我们太小，说今年就行了；现在就是今年了，我长高了两英寸，埃尔夫长高了三英寸，比你说我们不够大的时候，一共高出五英寸了。」

「这下你瞧见算术多么有用了吧。」姑姑说。「很好，准了。只是要穿旧衣裳，而且永远别走到看不见大路的地方。好啦；你们可以痛痛快快放一整天假。现在睡觉去。哦，铃又响了！可怜的、亲爱的伊莱扎。」

===Original===
A Clapham cub, belonging to one of the lodgers, happened to be going up to bed just as Edred and Elfrida came through the baize door that shut off the basement from the rest of the house. He put his tongue out through the banisters at the children of the house and said, “Little slaveys.” The cub thought he could get up the stairs before the two got round the end of the banisters, but he had not counted on the long arm of Elfrida, whose hand shot through the banisters and caught the cub’s leg and held on to it till Edred had time to get round. The two boys struggled up the stairs together and then rolled together from top to bottom, where they were picked up and disentangled by their relations. Except for this little incident, going to bed was uneventful.

===Chinese===
一个克拉珀姆来的小崽子——是某位房客家的——恰好在姐弟俩推开那扇把地下室同房子其余部分隔开的粗呢门时上楼睡觉。他隔着栏杆朝这家的孩子们伸出舌头，说了声「小打杂的」。那崽子自以为能赶在两人绕过栏杆之前冲上楼去，却没料到埃尔弗丽达的长胳膊——她的手从栏杆空隙里探出去，一把抓住崽子的腿，死死攥住，直到埃德雷德绕了过来。两个男孩一路扭打着上了楼梯，又抱在一起从楼梯顶滚到楼梯脚，才被各自的家人扶起来解开。除了这桩小小的意外，上床睡觉的事平平常常，无事可记。

===Original===
Next morning Aunt Edith went off by the eight-thirty train. The children’s school satchels were filled, not with books, but with buns; instead of exercise-books there were sandwiches; and in the place of inky pencil-boxes were two magnificent boxes of peppermint creams which had cost a whole shilling each, and had been recklessly bought by Aunt Edith in the agitation of the parting hour when they saw her off at the station.

They went slowly up the redbrick-paved sidewalk that always looks as though it had just been washed, and when they got to the top of the hill they stopped and looked at each other.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，伊迪丝姑姑坐八点半的火车走了。孩子们的书包里装的不是课本，而是小圆面包；练习本的位置上放的是三明治；代替沾着墨水的铅笔盒的，是两盒气派非凡的薄荷奶油糖，每盒整整花了一先令——那是伊迪丝姑姑在车站送别时刻的心慌意乱之中，大手笔买下的。

他们慢慢走上那条红砖铺的人行道——那路永远像刚冲洗过似的——走到山顶时，两人停下脚步，面面相觑。

===Original===
“It can’t be wrong,” said Edred.

“She never told us not to,” said Elfrida.

“I’ve noticed,” said Edred, “that when grown-up people say ‘they’ll see about’ anything you want it never happens.”

“I’ve noticed that, too,” said Elfrida. “Auntie always said she’d see about taking us there.”

“Yes, she did.”

===Chinese===
「这总不可能是错的。」埃德雷德说。

「她又没说过不许。」埃尔弗丽达说。

「我注意到，」埃德雷德说，「大人要是说对你想要的什么事『回头再说』，那事就永远不会实现。」

「我也注意到了。」埃尔弗丽达说。「姑姑总说带我们去那儿的事『回头再说』。」

「是啊，她总这么说。」

===Original===
“We won’t be mean and sneaky about it,” Edred insisted, though no one had suggested that he *would* be mean and sneaky. “We’ll tell auntie directly she gets back.”

“Of course,” said Elfrida, rather relieved, for she had not felt at all sure that Edred meant to do this.

“After all,” said Edred, “it’s *our* castle. We *ought* to go and see the cradle of our race. That’s what it calls it in *Cliffgate and Its Environs*. I say, let’s call it a pilgrimage. The satchels will do for packs, and we can get halfpenny walking-sticks with that penny of yours. We can put peas in our shoes, if you like,” he added generously.

===Chinese===
「我们可不做鬼鬼祟祟的小气事。」埃德雷德坚持道，尽管并没有人暗示他*会*鬼鬼祟祟、小里小气。「姑姑一回来我们就告诉她。」

「当然。」埃尔弗丽达说，心里松了口气，因为她原本压根拿不准埃德雷德是不是真打算这么做。

「说到底，」埃德雷德说，「那是*我们的*城堡。我们*理应*去看看我们家族的摇篮。《克利夫盖特及其周边》（*Cliffgate and Its Environs*）上就是这么叫它的。我说，咱们把这叫做朝圣吧。书包正好当行囊，拿你那个便士还能买半便士一根的徒步手杖。愿意的话，咱们还可以往鞋里搁豌豆。」他大方地补了一句。

===Original===
“We should have to go back for them, and I don’t expect the split kind count, anyhow. And perhaps they’d hurt,” said Elfrida doubtfully. “And I want my penny for—” She stopped, warned by her brother’s frown. “All right, then,” she ended; “you can have it. Only give me half next time you get a penny; that’s only fair.”

“I’m not usually unfair,” said Edred coldly. “Don’t let’s be pilgrims.”

“But I should like to,” said Elfrida.

===Chinese===
「那咱们就得回去取豌豆，再说我看裂开的那种也未必算数。何况可能会硌脚。」埃尔弗丽达将信将疑地说。「我还要拿我的便士去——」她停住了，弟弟的眉头提醒了她。「那好吧，」她最后说；「给你就是。不过下回你再得着便士，得分我一半；这才公平。」

「我平时又不是不讲公平。」埃德雷德冷冷地说。「咱们别当朝圣者了。」

「可我倒是想当。」埃尔弗丽达说。

===Original===
Edred was obstinate. “No,” he said, “we’ll just walk.”

So they just walked, rather dismally.

The town was getting thinner, like the tract of stocking that surrounds a hole; the houses were farther apart and had large gardens. In one of them a maid was singing to herself as she shook out the mats—a thing which, somehow, maids don’t do much in towns.

> “Good luck!” says I to my sweetheart,
> “For I will love you true;
> And all the while we’ve got to part,
> My luck shall go with you.”

===Chinese===
埃德雷德很固执。「不。」他说，「我们就只是走路。」

于是他们就只是走路，走得颇为没精打采。

小镇渐渐变得稀薄了，像破洞四周那一圈渐渐织细的袜纱；房屋彼此离得远了，都有宽敞的园子。其中一家里，一个女仆一边抖拍地垫，一边自顾自唱着歌——这种事，不知怎么，女仆们在城里是不大做的。

> 「祝你好运！」我对心上人说，
> 「我会真心爱你；
> 既然我们不得不分离，
> 我的福运就随你去。」

===Original===
“That’s lucky for us,” said Elfrida amiably.

“We’re not her silly sweetheart,” said Edred.

“No; but we heard her sing it, and he wasn’t here, so he couldn’t. There’s a signpost. I wonder how far we’ve gone? I’m getting awfully tired.”

“You’d better have been pilgrims,” said Edred. “*They* never get tired, however many peas they have in their shoes.”

“I will now,” said Elfrida.

“You can’t,” said Edred; “it’s too late. We’re miles and miles from the stick shop.”

===Chinese===
「这歌对我们倒是吉利的。」埃尔弗丽达和颜悦色地说。

「我们又不是她那傻乎乎的心上人。」埃德雷德说。

「不是；可我们听见她唱了，他不在场，所以听不见。那儿有个路牌。不知我们走了多远？我累得要命。」

「你倒不如当初就当朝圣者。」埃德雷德说。「*他们*是从来不会累的，鞋里有多少颗豌豆都不碍事。」

「我现在就当。」埃尔弗丽达说。

「当不成了。」埃德雷德说；「太晚了。我们离卖手杖的铺子有好几里地呢。」

===Original===
“Very well, I shan’t go on,” said Elfrida. “You got out of bed the wrong side this morning. I’ve tried to soft-answer you as hard as ever I could all the morning, and I’m not going to try any more, so there.”

“Don’t, then,” said Edred bitterly. “Go along home if you like. You’re only a girl.”

“I’d rather be only a girl than what *you* are,” said she.

“And what’s that, I should like to know?”

===Chinese===
「好哇，那我不走了。」埃尔弗丽达说。「你今早是从床的错误一侧爬起来的。一早上我拼了命地跟你和声细气，往后我可不再费这个劲了，哼。」

「不费就不费。」埃德雷德刻薄地说。「乐意的话你自己回家去。你不过是个丫头。」

「我宁可不过是个丫头，也不愿像*你*这样。」她说。

「倒要请教，我是什么样？」

===Original===
Elfrida stopped and shut her eyes tight.

“Don’t, don’t, don’t, don’t!” she said. “I won’t be cross, I won’t be cross, I won’t be cross! Pax. Drop it. Don’t let’s!”

“Don’t let’s what?”

“Quarrel about nothing,” said Elfrida, opening her eyes and walking on very fast. “We’re always doing it. Auntie says it’s a habit. If boys are so much splendider than girls, they ought to be able to stop when they like.”

“Suppose they don’t like?” said he, kicking his boots in the thick, white dust.

===Chinese===
埃尔弗丽达停下脚步，紧紧闭上眼睛。

「别，别，别，别！」她说。「我不生气，我不生气，我不生气！休战。翻篇。咱们别吵了！」

「别什么？」

「别为一点小事吵架。」埃尔弗丽达说着睁开眼睛，飞快地往前走。「我们老是这样。姑姑说这是习惯。男孩子要是比女孩子神气那么多倍，就该想停就停得住才对。」

「要是他们不想停呢？」他说着，把靴子踢进厚厚的白色尘土里。

===Original===
“Well,” said she, “I’ll say I’m sorry first. Will *that* do?”

“I was just going to say it first myself,” said Edred, in aggrieved tones. “Come on,” he added more generously, “here’s the signpost. Let’s see what it says.”

It said, quite plainly and without any nonsense about it, that they had come a mile and three-quarters, adding, most unkindly, that it was eight miles to Arden Castle. But, it said, it was a quarter of a mile to Ardenhurst Station.

===Chinese===
「那么，」她说，「我先说对不起。*这*样行了吧？」

「我本来正要自己先说呢。」埃德雷德委屈地说。「走吧。」他又大方起来，「路牌到了。看看上面写的什么。」

路牌说得清清楚楚，直截了当，毫不含糊：他们已经走了一英里又四分之三；又不怀好意地补充说，到阿登城堡（Arden Castle）还有八英里。不过，它说，到阿登赫斯特车站（Ardenhurst Station）只有四分之一英里。

===Original===
“Let’s go by train,” said Edred grandly.

“No money,” said Elfrida, very forlornly indeed.

“Aha!” said Edred; “now you’ll see. *I’m* not mean about money. I brought my new florin.”

“Oh, Edred,” said the girl, stricken with remorse, “you *are* noble.”

“Pooh!” said the boy, and his ears grew red with mingled triumph and modesty; “that’s nothing. Come on.”

===Chinese===
「咱们坐火车去。」埃德雷德气派地说。

「没钱。」埃尔弗丽达说，凄惶得很。

「啊哈！」埃德雷德说；「这下你瞧吧。*我*在钱上可不小气。我带来了我那枚新的两先令银币。」

「哦，埃德雷德。」姑娘悔愧交加地说，「你*真*高尚。」

「呸！」男孩说，耳朵因得意与羞怯参半而红了；「这算什么。走吧。」

===Original===
So it was from the train that the pilgrims got their first sight of Arden Castle. It stands up boldly on the cliff where it was set to keep off foreign foes and guard the country round about it. But of all its old splendour there is now nothing but the great walls that the grasses and wild flowers grow on, and round towers whose floors and ceilings have fallen away, and roofless chambers where owls build, and brambles and green ferns grow strong and thick.

The children walked to the castle along the cliff path where the skylarks were singing like mad up in the pale sky, and the bean-fields, where the bees were busy, gave out the sweetest scent in the world—a scent that got itself mixed with the scent of the brown seaweed that rises and falls in the wash of the tide on the rocks at the cliff-foot.

“Let’s have dinner here,” said Elfrida, when they reached the top of a little mound from which they could look down on the castle. So they had it.

Two bites of sandwich and one of peppermint cream; that was the rule.

And all the time they were munching they looked down on the castle, and loved it more and more.

===Chinese===
于是，两位「朝圣者」是在火车上头一回望见阿登城堡的。它雄赳赳地耸立在悬崖上——当初建在那儿，是为了抵御外敌、守卫四周乡野。可它旧日的气派如今只剩下：青草野花滋生的巨大城墙，楼板和顶棚均已坍塌的圆塔，猫头鹰筑巢的无顶厅堂，还有长得又壮又密的荆棘和青蕨。

孩子们沿着悬崖小径走向城堡。小径上空，云雀在淡淡的天色里唱得如痴如狂；蜜蜂忙碌的蚕豆田里，散发出世界上最香甜的气息——这气息又同褐色海藻的气味缠在一起，海藻在崖脚礁石间随潮水起伏漂荡。

「咱们就在这儿吃午饭吧。」埃尔弗丽达说，这时他们登上一座小丘的顶上，从那里可以俯瞰城堡。于是他们就在那儿吃了。

三明治咬两口，薄荷奶油糖咬一口；规矩如此。

他们一边大嚼，一边俯望着城堡，越看越爱。

===Original===
“Don’t you wish it was real, and we lived in it?” Elfrida asked, when they had eaten as much as they wanted—not of peppermint creams, of course; but they had finished them.

“It is real, what there is of it.”

“Yes; but I mean if it was a house with chimneys, and fireplaces, and doors with bolts, and glass in the windows.”

“I wonder if we could get in?” said Edred.

“We might climb over,” said Elfrida, looking hopefully at the enormous walls, sixty feet high, in which no gate or gap showed.

“There’s an old man going across that field—no, not that one; the very green field. Let’s ask him.”

===Chinese===
「你不想让它变成真的，好让我们住在里面吗？」埃尔弗丽达问，这时他们想吃多少已经吃了多少——薄荷奶油糖自不待言，早就吃得精光。

「就现存的部分而言，它是真的。」

「是啊；可我的意思是，假如它是一所带烟囱、带壁炉、门上有门闩、窗上镶玻璃的房子。」

「不知咱们进得去进不去？」埃德雷德说。

「咱们可以翻进去。」埃尔弗丽达说着，满怀希望地望着那六十英尺高的巍峨城墙——墙上既没有门，也没有豁口。

「那边田里有个老头儿正走过去——不，不是那块；是那块特别绿的。咱们去问问他。」

===Original===
So they left their satchels lying on the short turf, that was half wild thyme, and went down. But they were not quite quick enough; before they could get to him the old man had come through the field of young corn, clambered over a stile, and vanished between the high hedges of a deep-sunk lane. So over the stile and down into the lane went the children, and caught up with the old man just as he had clicked his garden gate behind him and had turned to go up the bricked path between beds of woodruff, and anemones, and narcissus, and tulips of all colours.

===Chinese===
于是他们把书包撂在矮草皮上——草皮有一半是野百里香——走了下去。可他们的脚步还不够快；没等他们赶到，老头儿已经穿过那片青嫩的麦田，翻过一道栅栏踏级，消失在一条深陷的大路两旁的高树篱之间了。于是孩子们也翻过踏级，下到大路上，总算赶上了老头儿——他刚把自家花园的门在身后咔哒带上，正要转身走上砖铺的小径，小径两旁是一畦畦的车叶草、银莲花、水仙和五颜六色的郁金香。

===Original===
His back was towards them. Now it is very difficult to address a back politely. So you will not be surprised to learn that Edred said, “Hi!” and Elfrida said, “Halloa! I say!”

The old man turned and saw at his gate two small figures dressed in what is known as sailor costume. They saw a very wrinkled old face with snowy hair and mutton-chop whiskers of a silvery whiteness. There were very bright twinkling blue eyes in the sun-browned face, and on the clean-shaven mouth a kind, if tight, smile.

“Well,” said he, “and what do *you* want?”

“We want to know—” said Elfrida.

===Chinese===
他背朝着他们。要对着一个背影客气地搭话，实在很难。所以听到埃德雷德喊了声「喂！」、埃尔弗丽达喊了声「哈罗！我说！」，你不必惊讶。

老头儿转过身来，看见自家门口立着两个小身影，穿的是所谓水手服。孩子们看见的是一张皱纹深深的老脸，一头雪白的头发，两撇羊排似的银白络腮胡。晒成古铜色的脸上，一双蓝眼睛晶亮有神，刮得干干净净的嘴边挂着一丝和善——尽管有些绷着的——笑意。

「唔。」他说，「*你们*要什么？」

「我们想知道——」埃尔弗丽达说。

===Original===
“About the castle,” said Edred. “Can we get in and look at it?”

“I’ve got the keys,” said the old man, and put his hand in at his door and reached them from a nail.

“I s’pose no one *lives* there?” said Elfrida.

“Not now,” said the old man, coming back along the garden path. “Lord Arden, he died a fortnight ago come Tuesday, and the place is shut up till the new lord’s found.”

“I wish *I* was the new lord,” said Edred, as they followed the old man along the lane.

“An’ how old might *you* be?” the old man asked.

===Chinese===
「关于城堡的事。」埃德雷德说。「我们能进去看看吗？」

「钥匙在我这儿。」老头儿说着，把手从门里伸进去，从一枚钉子上取下钥匙。

「我想总没有人在那儿*住*吧？」埃尔弗丽达说。

「如今没有了。」老头儿顺着花园小径走回来，说。「阿登勋爵，他到礼拜二就死满两礼拜啦，宅子封着，等找到新勋爵再说。」

「巴不得*我*就是新勋爵。」埃德雷德说，这时他们跟着老头儿走在那条深路上。

「那你*多大*啦？」老头儿问。

===Original===
“I’m ten nearly. It’s my birthday tomorrow,” said Edred. “How old are you?”

“Getting on for eighty. I’ve seen a deal in my time. If you was the young lord you’d have a chance none of the rest of them ever had—you being the age you are.”

“What sort of chance?”

“Why,” said the old man, “don’t you know the saying? I thought everyone knowed it hereabouts.”

“What saying?”

“I ain’t got the wind for saying and walking too,” said the old man, and stopped; “leastways, not potery.” He drew a deep breath and said—

===Chinese===
「快十岁了。明天就是我的生日。」埃德雷德说。「您多大年纪？」

「快八十喽。我这辈子见过的世面多啦。你要是那位小爵爷，就有一样他们谁都没赶上的造化——赶的正是你这个岁数。」

「什么样的造化？」

「嗨。」老头儿说，「你不知道那句老话吗？我当这一带人人都知道咧。」

「什么老话？」

「我又没那份气力，一边走路一边说话。」老头儿说着停了下来；「起码，念诗不成。」他深深吸了口气，念道——

===Original===
> “When Arden’s lord still lacketh ten
> And may not see his nine again,
> Let Arden stand as Arden may
> On Arden Knoll at death of day.
> If he have skill to say the spell
> He shall find the treasure, and all be well!”

“I *say*!” said both the children. “And where’s Arden Knoll?” Edred asked.

“Up yonder.” He pointed to the mound where they had had lunch.

Elfrida inquired, “What treasure?”

But that question was not answered—then.

===Chinese===
> 「阿登之主尚未满十，
> 九岁年华不可复拾；
> 任凭阿登听天由命，
> 日落时分立在阿登丘顶。
> 他若能念出这句咒语，
> 必得宝藏，万事顺遂！」

「哎呀！」两个孩子一齐说。「阿登丘（Arden Knoll）在哪儿？」埃德雷德问。

「那边上头。」他指了指他们吃午饭的那座小丘。

埃尔弗丽达问：「什么宝藏？」

可那个问题当时没有得到回答。

===Original===
“If I’m to talk I must set me down,” said the old man. “Shall we set down here, or set down inside of the castle?”

Two curiosities struggled, and the stronger won. “In the castle,” said the children.

So it was in the castle, on a pillar fallen from one of the chapel arches, that the old man sat down and waited. When the children had run up and down the grassy enclosure, peeped into the ruined chambers, picked their way along the ruined colonnade, and climbed the steps of the only tower that they could find with steps *to* climb, then they came and sat beside the old man on the grass that was white with daisies, and said, “Now, then!”

===Chinese===
「我要是还要讲话，就得坐下歇歇。」老头儿说。「咱们是在这儿坐，还是到城堡里头坐？」

两样好奇心争了一争，强的那样赢了。「到城堡里头。」孩子们说。

于是，在城堡里，在一根从礼拜堂拱门上跌落的石柱上，老头儿坐下来等着。孩子们在围满青草的场院里跑上跑下，往一间间荒废的厅堂里张望，沿着倾颓的柱廊择路而行，又爬上了唯一一座还找得着台阶*可*爬的塔的台阶，然后才走过来，在老头儿身边缀满白雏菊的草地上坐下，说：「好啦，讲吧！」

===Original===
“Well, then,” said the old man, “you see the Ardens was always great gentry. I’ve heard say there’s always been Ardens here since before William the Conker, whoever *he* was.”

“Ten-sixty-six,” said Edred to himself.

“An’ they had their ups and downs like other folks, great and small. And once, when there was a war or trouble of some sort abroad, there was a lot of money, and jewlery, and silver plate hidden away. That’s what it means by treasure. And the men who hid it got killed—ah, them was unsafe times to be alive in, I tell you—and nobody never knew where the treasure was hid.”

===Chinese===
「那么，」老头儿说，「你们知道，阿登家向来是大户人家。听说打威廉大王那阵儿以前——管他是谁呢——这儿就一直有阿登家的人。」

「一〇六六。」埃德雷德自言自语。

「他们也跟别家一样，大大小小起起落落过。有一回，外头打仗还是闹什么乱子来着，有一大笔钱，还有珠宝、银器，都藏了起来。所谓宝藏，说的就是这个。藏东西的人后来都送了命——啊，那些年月，活着可真不保险，我告诉你——结果谁也不知道宝藏藏在哪儿。」

===Original===
“Did they ever find it?”

“Ain’t I telling you? An’ a wise woman that lived in them old ancient times, they went to her to ask her what to do to find the treasure, and she had a fit directly, what you’d call a historical fit nowadays. She never said nothing worth hearing without she was in a fit, and she made up the saying all in potery whilst she was in her fit, and that was all they could get out of her. And she never would say what the spell was. Only when she was a-dying, Lady Arden, that was then, was very took up with nursing of her, and before she breathed her lastest she told Lady Arden the spell.” He stopped for lack of breath.

===Chinese===
「后来找到过吗？」

「我不是正讲着吗？那老早老早的年代里有个智慧的女人，他们就去请教她，怎么才能找到宝藏，她当场就发了羊痫风——搁如今你们兴许管那叫历史性的发作。她不犯病就说不出一句值得听的话来，那句口诀全是用诗编的，就是她犯病那阵儿编的，他们从她嘴里也就得了这个。可咒语到底是什么，她从来不肯说。只有到她要死的时候，当时的阿登夫人（Lady Arden）正一心一意地看护她，她临咽最末一口气之前，才把咒语说给了阿登夫人。」他气力不济，停了下来。

===Original===
“And what is the spell?” said the children, much more breathless than he.

“Nobody knows,” said he.

“But where is it?”

“Nobody knows. But I’ve ’eard say it’s in a book in the libery in the house yonder. But it ain’t no good, because there’s never been a Lord Arden come to his title without he’s left his ten years far behind him.”

===Chinese===
「那咒语是什么？」孩子们问，喘得比他还厉害。

「没人知道。」他说。

「可它在哪儿？」

「没人知道。不过我听说，它就写在那边宅子书房里的一本书里。可写了也白搭，因为哪一任阿登勋爵承袭爵位的时候，不早就把十岁甩得远远的啦。」

===Original===
Edred had a queerer feeling in his head than you can imagine; his hands got hot and dry, and then cold and damp.

“I suppose,” he said, “you’ve got to be *Lord* Arden? It wouldn’t do if you were just plain John or James or Edred Arden? Because my name’s Arden, and I would like to have a try?”

The old man stooped, caught Edred by the arm, pulled him up, and stood him between his knees.

===Chinese===
埃德雷德脑子里生出一种你想都想不出的古怪感觉；他的手先是发热发干，随后又发凉发潮。

「我琢磨着，」他说，「非得当上*阿登勋爵*才行吧？要是只是个平平常常的约翰、詹姆斯或者埃德雷德·阿登，就不顶事，对吧？因为我正好姓阿登，我很想试一试？」

老头儿俯下身，一把攥住埃德雷德的胳膊，把他拉起来，让他站在自己两膝之间。

===Original===
“Let’s have a look at you, sonny,” he said; and had a look. “Aye,” he said, “you’re an Arden, for sure. To think of me not seeing that. I might have seen your long nose and your chin that sticks out like a spur. I ought to have known it anywhere. But my eyes ain’t what they was. If you *was* Lord Arden—What’s your father’s name—his chrissened name, I mean?”

“Edred, the same as mine. But father’s dead,” said Edred gravely.

“And your grandf’er’s name? It wasn’t George, was it—George William?”

“Yes, it was,” said Edred. “How did you know?”

===Chinese===
「让我瞧瞧你，小子。」他说着，瞧了瞧。「哎。」他说，「你是阿登家的人，错不了。想不到我竟没看出来。你那长鼻子，你那像马刺一样翘出的下巴，我本该看出来的。走到哪儿我都该认得出。可我这双眼睛不比当年喽。你要是阿登勋爵——你父亲叫什么——我是说，他受洗的名字？」

「埃德雷德，跟我同名。不过爸爸已经不在了。」埃德雷德庄重地说。

「那你祖父叫什么？不是乔治吧——乔治·威廉？」

「正是。」埃德雷德说。「您怎么知道的？」

===Original===
The old man let go Edred’s arms and stood up. Then he touched his forehead and said—

“I’ve worked on the land ’ere man and boy, and I’m proud I’ve lived to see another Lord Arden take the place of him as is gone. Lauk-alive, boy, don’t garp like that,” he added sharply. “You’re Lord Arden right enough.”

“I—I can’t be,” gasped Edred.

“Auntie said Lord Arden was a relation of ours—a sort of great-uncle—cousin.”

===Chinese===
老头儿松开埃德雷德的胳膊，站起身来。然后他碰了碰额头，说——

「我打小到老都在这块地上做工，如今又亲眼看见一位新阿登勋爵接了去了的那位的位子，我算露脸喽。老天爷，小子，别那么目瞪口呆地傻瞪着眼。」他厉声补了一句。「你是阿登勋爵，千真万确。」

「我——我不可能是。」埃德雷德倒抽一口气。

「姑姑说过，阿登勋爵是我们的亲戚——算是叔祖——表亲来着。」

===Original===
“That’s it, missy,” the old man nodded. “Lord Arden—chrissen name James—’e was first cousin to Mr. George as was your grandf’er. His son was Mr. Edred, as is your father. The late lord not ’avin’ any sons—nor daughters neither for the matter of that—the title comes to your branch of the family. I’ve heard Snigsworthy, the lawyer’s apprentice from Lewis, tell it over fifty times this last three weeks. You’re Lord Arden, I tell you.”

“If I am,” said Edred, “I shall say the spell and find the treasure.”

“You’ll have to be quick about it,” said Elfrida. “You’ll be over ten the day after tomorrow.”

“So I shall,” said Edred.

===Chinese===
「正是这么回事，姑娘。」老头儿点头说。「阿登勋爵——受洗的名字叫詹姆斯——他是老乔治先生的亲表兄弟，老乔治先生就是你们祖父。他的儿子是埃德雷德先生，也就是你们父亲。先前的勋爵没有儿子——说起来女儿也没有一个——爵位就落到了你们这一房头上。这话这三个礼拜里，我听刘易斯来的律师学徒斯尼格斯沃西（Snigsworthy）讲了总有五十遍。我告诉你，你就是阿登勋爵。」

「我要真是。」埃德雷德说，「我就念咒语，把宝藏找出来。」

「那你可得抓紧。」埃尔弗丽达说。「后天你就满十岁啦。」

「也是。」埃德雷德说。

===Original===
“When you’re Lord Arden,” said the old man very seriously—“I mean, when you grow up to enjoy the title—as, please God, you may—you remember the poor and needy, young master—that’s what you do.”

“If I find the treasure I will,” said Edred.

“You do it whether or no,” said the old man. “I must be getting along home. You’d like to play about a bit, eh? Well, bring me the keys when you’ve done. I can trust you not to hurt your own place, that’s been in the family all these hundreds of years.”

“I should think you could!” said Edred proudly. “Goodbye, and thank you.”

“Goodbye, my lord,” said the old man, and went.

===Chinese===
「等你做了阿登勋爵，」老头儿极其郑重地说——「我是说，等你长大成人享受爵位的时候——求上帝保佑你能有那一天——你要记着穷苦人，小主人——这就是你该做的。」

「我要是找着宝藏，一定记着。」埃德雷德说。

「找不找着都得记着。」老头儿说。「我得家去喽。你们还想在这儿玩玩儿，是吧？行，玩够了把钥匙给我送来。你们不会糟蹋自家的宅子，这个我信得过——它可在这个家里传了这几百年啦。」

「那还用说！」埃德雷德自豪地说。「再见，谢谢您。」

「再见，大人。」老头儿说完便走了。

===Original===
“I say,” said Edred, with the big bunch of keys in his hand—“if I *am* Lord Arden!”

“You are! you are!” said Elfrida. “I am perfectly certain you are. And I suppose I’m Lady Arden. How perfectly ripping! We can shut up those lodging-children now, anyhow. What’s up?”

Edred was frowning and pulling the velvet covering of moss off the big stone on which he had absently sat down.

“Do you think it’s burglarish,” he said slowly, “to go into your own house without leave?”

===Chinese===
「我说。」埃德雷德手里攥着那一大串钥匙——「我要*真是*阿登勋爵呢！」

「你是！你是！」埃尔弗丽达说。「我百分之百肯定你是。那我想我就是阿登夫人了。简直太带劲了！这下无论如何，咱们能把那些房客孩子关在外面了。你怎么啦？」

埃德雷德皱着眉，正把一大块石头上的青苔绒垫一片片扯下来——他方才心不在焉地坐到了那块石头上。

「你说。」他慢吞吞地说，「不经允许走进自家的房子，算不算撬门贼的行径？」

===Original===
“Not if it *is* your own house. Of course not,” said Elfrida.

“But suppose it isn’t? They might put you in prison for it.”

“You could tell the policeman you thought it was yours. I say, Edred, let’s!”

“It’s not vulgar curiosity, like auntie says; it’s the spell I want,” said the boy.

“As if I didn’t know that,” said the girl contemptuously. “But where’s the house?”

===Chinese===
「只要那*真是*你自己的房子，就不算。当然不算。」埃尔弗丽达说。

「可万一不是呢？人家会为这个把你关进牢里的。」

「你可以告诉警察，你以为那是你家的。我说，埃德雷德，走吧！」

「这可不是姑姑说的那种庸俗的好奇心；我要的是咒语。」男孩说。

「说得好像我不知道似的。」姑娘轻蔑地说。「可是宅子在哪儿呢？」

===Original===
She might well ask, for there was no house to be seen—only the great grey walls of the castle, with their fine fringe of flowers and grass showing feathery against the pale blue of the June sky. Here and there, though, there were grey wooden doors set in the grey of the stone.

“It must be one of those,” Edred said. “We’ll try all the keys and all the doors till we find it.”

So they tried all the keys and all the doors. One door led to a loft where apples were stored. Another to a cellar, where brooms and spades and picks leaned against the damp wall, and there were baskets and piles of sacks. A third opened into a tower that seemed to be used as a pigeon-cote. It was the very last door they tried that led into the long garden between two high walls, where already the weeds had grown high among the forget-me-nots and pansies. And at the end of this garden was a narrow house with a red roof, wedged tightly in between two high grey walls that belonged to the castle.

===Chinese===
她问得有理，因为放眼望去根本没有房子——只有城堡巨大的灰色城墙，墙头那一线细密的花草，衬着六月淡蓝的天空，显得毛茸茸的。不过灰色石墙上这里那里嵌着几扇灰色的木门。

「准是那几扇门里的一扇。」埃德雷德说。「咱们把所有的钥匙挨个试遍所有的门，总能找着。」

于是他们把所有的钥匙挨个试遍了所有的门。一扇门通向存放苹果的阁楼。另一扇通向一间地窖，扫帚、铁锹、镐头斜倚在潮湿的墙上，还有篮子和成堆的麻袋。第三扇开进一座塔，看样子是当鸽房用的。直到试到最末一扇门，才走进两道高墙之间那长长的花园——园里的勿忘我和三色堇中间，杂草早已长得老高。花园尽头是一座带红屋顶的窄房子，紧紧夹在两堵属于城堡的高灰墙之间。

===Original===
All the blinds were down; the garden was chill and quiet, and smelt of damp earth and dead leaves.

“Oh, Edred, do you think we ought?” Elfrida said, shivering.

“Yes, I do,” said Edred; “and you’re not being good, whatever you may think. You’re only being frightened.”

Elfrida naturally replied, “I’m not. Come on.”

But it was very slowly, and with a feeling of being on tiptoe and holding their breaths, that they went up to those blinded windows that looked like sightless eyes.

The front door was locked, and none of the keys would fit it.

===Chinese===
百叶窗全都拉着；园子里又冷又静，散发着湿土和枯叶的气味。

「哦，埃德雷德，你说咱们该进去吗？」埃尔弗丽达打着哆嗦说。

「该，我认为该。」埃德雷德说；「还有，你别自以为在表现乖。你那只是害怕。」

埃尔弗丽达自然而然地回敬道：「我才不怕。走。」

可他们走向那些拉严了百叶窗、像失明的眼睛似的窗户时，脚步很慢，心里像踮着脚尖、屏着呼吸。

前门锁着，哪把钥匙也对不上号。

===Original===
“I don’t care,” said Edred. “If I am Lord Arden I’ve got a right to get in, and if I’m not I don’t care about anything, so here goes.”

Elfrida almost screamed, half with horror and half with admiration of his daring, when he climbed up to a little window by means of an elder-tree that grew close to it, tried to open the window, and when he found it fast deliberately pushed his elbow through the glass.

“Thus,” he said rather unsteadily, “the heir of Arden Castle reenters his estates.”

He got the window open and disappeared through it. Elfrida stood clasping and unclasping her hands, and in her mind trying to get rid of the idea of a very large and sudden policeman appearing in the garden door and saying, in that deep voice so much admired in our village constables, “Where’s your brother?”

===Chinese===
「我不管。」埃德雷德说。「我要是阿登勋爵，就有权进去；我要不是，那我也什么都不在乎了，说进就进。」

他攀着窗边一棵长得很近的接骨木爬到一扇小窗前，试着推开窗子，发现推不动后，便不慌不忙地用胳膊肘捅穿了玻璃。埃尔弗丽达差点尖叫出来，一半出于惊骇，一半出于对他这份大胆的佩服。

「就这样。」他的声音有点发颤，「阿登城堡的继承人重登自己的领地。」

他把窗子弄开，从窗口钻了进去。埃尔弗丽达站在原地，手指绞了又松，松了又绞，脑子里拼命想甩掉一个念头：一个高大魁梧的警察蓦地出现在花园门口，用我们村警那种备受赞赏的低沉嗓音问：「你弟弟哪儿去了？」

===Original===
No policeman came, fortunately, and presently a blind went up, a French window opened, and there was Edred beckoning her with the air of a conspirator.

It needed an effort to obey his signal, but she did it. He closed the French window, drew down the blind again, and—

“Oh, don’t let’s,” said Elfrida.

“Nonsense,” said Edred; “there’s nothing to be frightened of. It’s just like our rooms at home.”

===Chinese===
幸而警察没有来。不一会儿，一扇百叶窗升了起来，一扇落地长窗打开了，埃德雷德正站在那儿，带着密谋分子的神情朝她招手。

服从他的暗号需要一点勇气，可她做到了。他关上落地窗，重新放下百叶窗，然后——

「哦，咱们别这样。」埃尔弗丽达说。

「胡说。」埃德雷德说；「没什么好怕的。这跟咱们家里的屋子一个样。」

===Original===
It was. They went all over the house, and it certainly was. Some of the upper rooms were very bare, but all the furniture was of the same kind as Aunt Edith’s, and there were the same kind of pictures. Only the library was different. It was a very large room, and there were no pictures at all. Nothing but books and books and books, bound in yellowy leather. Books from ceiling to floor, shelves of books between the windows and over the mantelpiece—hundreds and thousands of books. Even Edred’s spirits sank. “It’s no go. It will take us years to look in them all,” he said.

===Chinese===
果然一个样。他们走遍了整座房子，千真万确就是一个样。楼上有些房间非常空，但所有的家具都同伊迪丝姑姑家的是一路货，墙上挂的也是同一路的画。只有书房不同。那是很大的一间屋子，墙上连一张画也没有。什么都没有，只有书、书、书，一律皮面发黄。书从天花板排到地板，窗与窗之间、壁炉架上方也都是一架一架的书——成百上千册。连埃德雷德的精神也垮了。「没戏。要把这些书全翻一遍，得花咱们好几年。」他说。

===Original===
“We may as well look at some of them,” said Elfrida, always less daring, but more persevering than her brother. She sat down on the worn carpet and began to read the names on the backs of the books nearest to her. *Burton’s Atomy of Melon Something*, she read, and *Locke on Understanding*, and many other dull and wearying titles. But none of the books seemed at all likely to contain a spell for finding treasure. *Burgess on the Precious Metals* beguiled her for a moment, but she saw at once that there was no room in its closely-printed, brown-spotted pages for anything so interesting as a spell. Time passed by. The sunlight that came through the blinds had quite changed its place on the carpet, and still Elfrida persevered. Edred grew more and more restless.

===Chinese===
「咱们不妨也翻上几本。」埃尔弗丽达说。她一向不如弟弟胆大，却比他有长性。她在磨旧的地毯上坐下，开始念身边最近那些书脊上的书名。她念到伯顿的《甜瓜什么解剖学》，洛克的《论理解》，还有许多别的枯燥乏味的书名。可这些书里，哪一本也不像藏着寻宝咒语的样子。《伯吉斯论贵金属》一时哄得她动了心，但她一眼便看出，那印得密密麻麻、褐斑点点的书页里，容不下咒语这么有趣的东西。时间过去。从百叶窗透进来的阳光在地毯上早已挪了地方，埃尔弗丽达还在坚持。埃德雷德却越来越坐立不安。

===Original===
“It’s no use,” he kept saying, and “Let’s chuck it,” and “I expect that old chap was just kidding us. I don’t feel a bit like I did about it,” and “Do let’s get along home.”

But Elfrida plodded on, though her head and her back both ached. I wish I could say that her perseverance was rewarded. But it wasn’t; and one must keep to facts. As it happened, it was Edred who, aimlessly running his finger along the edge of the bookshelf just for the pleasure of looking at the soft, mouse-coloured dust that clung to the finger at the end of each shelf, suddenly cried out, “What about this?” and pulled out a great white book that had on its cover a shield printed in gold with squares and little spots on it, and a gold pig standing on the top of the shield, and on the back, *The History of the Ardens of Arden*.

===Chinese===
「没用的。」他不住口地说，又说「算了吧」，又说「我看那老头儿不过是哄咱们玩玩。我现在一点儿没那阵儿的感觉了」，又说「求求你，咱们回家吧」。

可埃尔弗丽达埋头苦干，尽管她的头和背都疼了。我很想说，她的坚韧得到了报偿。可惜没有；事实就是事实。事有凑巧，偏偏是埃德雷德——他正百无聊赖地用手指沿书架边缘划过去，只为看看每排书架尽头沾在指头上的那种老鼠灰色的软尘土——忽然喊了一声：「这本怎么样？」随即抽出一大本白书，封面上烫金印着一面盾牌，盾上缀着方格和小圆点，盾顶上立着一头金猪，书脊上写着《阿登的阿登家族史》（*The History of the Ardens of Arden*）。

===Original===
In an instant it was open on the floor between them, and they were turning its pages with quick, anxious hands. But, alas! it was as empty of spells as dull old Burgess himself.

It was only when Edred shut it with a bang and the remark that he had had jolly well enough of it that a paper fluttered out and swept away like a pigeon, settling on the fireless hearth. And it was the spell. There was no doubt of that.

Written in faint ink on a square yellowed sheet of letter-paper that had been folded once, and opened and folded again so often that the fold was worn thin and hardly held its two parts together, the writing was fine and pointed and ladylike. At the top was written: “The Spell Aunt Anne Told Me.—December 24, 1793.”

===Chinese===
一眨眼间，书已摊开在他们中间的地板上，两双急切的手飞快地翻着书页。可是天哪！它和乏味的老伯吉斯一样，书里一个咒语也没有。

直到埃德雷德「砰」地合上书，宣布自己实在受够了，才有一张纸片飘出来，像鸽子一样滑翔出去，落在没有火的炉膛前。而那正是咒语。这一点毫无疑问。

那是一张方形的、发黄的信纸，曾对折过一回，此后又打开又折上过那么多回，折痕已经磨得半透，几乎拢不住两半纸了。纸上用淡淡的墨水写着字，笔迹纤细尖巧，一望而知出自闺秀之手。顶头一行写着：「安妮姑母传授我的咒语。——1793 年 12 月 24 日。」

===Original===
And then came the spell:—

> “Hear, Oh badge of Arden’s house,
> The spell my little age allows;
> Arden speaks it without fear,
> Badge of Arden’s house, draw near,
> Make me brave and kind and wise,
> And show me where the treasure lies.”

===Chinese===
接下来便是咒语：——

> 「听啊，阿登之家的徽记，
> 我小小年纪念动这咒语；
> 阿登家人无所畏惧，
> 阿登之家的徽记，请来到这里，
> 赐我勇敢、善良又明智，
> 指给我宝藏埋藏之地！」

===Original===
“To be said,” the paper went on, “at sun-setting by a Lord Arden between the completion of his ninth and tenth years. But it is all folly and not to be believed.”

“This is it, right enough,” said Edred. “Come on, let’s get out of this.” They turned to go, and as they did so something moved in the corner of the library—something little, and they could not see its shape.

Neither drew free breath again till they were out of the house, and out of the garden, and out of the castle, and on the wide, thymy downs, with the blue sky above, where the skylarks sang, and there was the sweet, fresh scent of the seaweed and the bean-fields.

===Chinese===
纸上接着写：「须由一位年满九岁、未满十岁的阿登勋爵在日落时诵念。然此纯属愚妄，不可信之。」

「就是这个，错不了。」埃德雷德说。「走，咱们出去。」他们转身要走，就在这时，书房角落里有什么东西动了一下——一样小小的东西，他们看不清它的形状。

直到跑出房子、跑出花园、跑出城堡，踏上长满百里香的辽阔丘原，两人头顶蓝天——云雀在那儿歌唱，海藻和蚕豆田的香气清新甜美——姐弟俩才重新畅快地喘过气来。

===Original===
“Oh,” said Elfrida, then, “I am so glad it’s not at midnight you’ve got to say the spell. You’d be too frightened.”

“I shouldn’t,” said Edred, very pale and walking quickly away from the castle. “I should say it just the same if it was midnight.” And he very nearly believed what he said.

Elfrida it was who had picked up the paper that Edred had dropped when that thing moved in the corner. She still held it fast.

“I expect it was only a rat or something,” said Edred, his heart beating nineteen to the dozen, as they say in Kent and elsewhere.

“Oh, yes,” said Elfrida, whose lips were trembling a little; “I’m sure it was only a rat or something.”

===Chinese===
「哦。」埃尔弗丽达这才说，「真庆幸念咒语不用等到半夜。要不你非吓坏了不可。」

「我才不会。」埃德雷德说着，脸色煞白，脚下飞快地远离城堡。「就算是半夜，我也会照样念。」他说这话时，自己都几乎信了。

角落里那东西动起来时，埃德雷德撒手丢了纸，是埃尔弗丽达把它捡起来的。此刻她仍紧紧攥着它。

「我估计不过是只耗子什么的。」埃德雷德说，他的心咚咚跳得飞快——照肯特郡和别处的说法，是「十九赶十二」。

「哦，是啊。」埃尔弗丽达说，她的嘴唇还有些发抖；「我敢肯定，那不过是只耗子什么的。」

===Original===
When they got to the top of Arden Knoll there was no sign of sunset. There was time, therefore, to pull oneself together, to listen to the skylarks, and to smell the bean-flowers, and to wonder how one could have been such a duffer as to be scared by a “rat or something.” Also there were some bits of sandwich and crumbled cake, despised at dinnertime, but now, somehow, tasting quite different. These helped to pass the time till the sun almost seemed to rest on a brown shoulder of the downs, that looked as though it were shrugging itself up to meet the round red ball that the evening mists had made of the sun.

The children had not spoken for several minutes. Their four eyes were fixed on the sun, and as the edge of it seemed to flatten itself against the hill-shoulder Elfrida whispered, “Now!” and gave her brother the paper.

They had read the spell so often, as they sat there in the waning light, that both knew it by heart, so there was no need for Edred to read it. And that was lucky, for in that thick, pink light the faint ink hardly showed at all on the yellowy paper.

===Chinese===
他们登上阿登丘顶的时候，还没有一点日落的迹象。于是有的是时间定下神来，听听云雀，闻闻蚕豆花，再纳闷自己怎么会那样笨，竟被一只「耗子什么的」吓着了。另外还剩些三明治碎块和蛋糕渣，吃午饭时被嫌弃，此刻不知怎么，滋味全然不同了。这些帮助他们挨过了时间，直到太阳几乎靠在丘原一面褐色的肩膀上——那肩膀仿佛正耸起身来，去迎接晚雾把太阳滚成的那只又圆又红的球。

孩子们已经有好几分钟没说话了。四只眼睛盯住了太阳。当太阳的边缘仿佛贴着山肩压扁自己的那一刻，埃尔弗丽达悄声说：「念！」把纸递给弟弟。

坐在渐暗的天光里，他们把那咒语念了不知多少遍，两人都早已背得滚瓜烂熟，埃德雷德根本用不着照着念。这也算运气，因为在那浓稠的粉色光线里，淡淡的墨迹在发黄的纸上几乎显不出来。

===Original===
Edred stood up.

“Now!” said Elfrida, again. “Say it now.” And Edred said, quite out loud and in a pleasant sort of singsong, such as he was accustomed to use at school when reciting the stirring ballads of the late Lord Macaulay, or the moving tale of the boy on the burning deck:—

> “‘Hear, Oh badge of Arden’s house,
> The spell my little age allows;
> Arden speaks it without fear,
> Badge of Arden’s house, draw near,
> Make me brave and kind and wise,
> And show me where the treasure lies.’”

===Chinese===
埃德雷德站起身。

「念啊！」埃尔弗丽达又说。「现在就念。」于是埃德雷德高声念了起来，用的是一种怪好听的吟诵调——就是他在学校里背已故麦考莱勋爵（Lord Macaulay）那些激动人心的歌谣，或背那个着火甲板上的男孩的动人故事时惯用的调子：——

> 「『听啊，阿登之家的徽记，
> 我小小年纪念动这咒语；
> 阿登家人无所畏惧，
> 阿登之家的徽记，请来到这里，
> 赐我勇敢、善良又明智，
> 指给我宝藏埋藏之地。』」

===Original===
He said it slowly and carefully, his sister eagerly listening, ready to correct him if he said a word wrong. But he did not.

“Where the treasure lies,” he ended, and the great silence of the downs seemed to rush in like a wave to fill the space which his voice had filled.

And nothing else happened at all. A flush of pink from the sun-setting spread over the downs, the grass-stems showed up thin and distinct, the skylarks had ceased to sing, but the scent of the bean-flowers and the seaweed was stronger than ever. And nothing happened till Edred cried out, “What’s that?” For close to his foot something moved, not quickly or suddenly so as to startle, but very gently, very quietly, very unmistakably—something that glittered goldenly in the pink, diffused light of the sun-setting.

“Why,” said Elfrida stooping, “why, it’s—”

===Chinese===
他念得又慢又仔细，姐姐热切地听着，准备他念错一个字就纠正。可他一个字也没错。

「指给我宝藏埋藏之地。」他念完了最后一句，丘原上浩大的寂静便像一道浪涌了进来，填满他的声音方才占据的空间。

别的什么也没有发生。日落时的一抹绯红漫过丘原，草茎纤细分明地显现出来，云雀已停止了歌唱，蚕豆花和海藻的香气却比先前更浓。什么也没有发生——直到埃德雷德喊出声来：「那是什么？」原来在他脚边，有什么东西动了动，不快也不猛，不足以吓人一跳，而是非常轻、非常静、又非常确凿无疑——一样东西，在日落弥散的粉色光晕里，闪着金子般的光。

「呀。」埃尔弗丽达俯下身去，「呀，这是——」

## XI Developments / 显影与进展

===Original===
“Come on,” said Edred, “you measure out the hypo and put the four pie-dishes ready. I’ll get the water.”

He got it, with Mrs. Honeysett’s help﻿—two brimming pails full.

“You mustn’t come in for anything, will you, Mrs. Honeysett?” he earnestly urged. “You see, if the door’s open ever so little, all the photographs will be done for.”

“Law, love a duck!” said Mrs. Honeysett, holding her fat waist with her fat hands. “*I* shan’t come in; I ain’t got nothing to come in *for*.”

===Chinese===
「快来。」埃德雷德说，「你把海波（hypo，定影药粉）量出来，把四只馅饼盘摆好。我去打水。」

水是他打来的，靠了哈尼塞特太太（Mrs. Honeysett）帮忙——满满两桶，齐着桶沿。

「不管为了什么，您都千万不能进来，好不好，哈尼塞特太太？」他恳切地叮嘱。「您想，门哪怕只开一条小缝，照片就全完了。」

「哎呀，乖乖！」哈尼塞特太太说着，两只胖手叉住胖腰。「*我*才不进去呢；我本来就没有什么要*进去*找的东西。」

===Original===
“We’ll bolt the door, all the same,” said Edred, when she was gone, “in case she was to think of something.”

He shot the great wooden bolt.

“Now it’ll be quite dark,” he said.

And, of course, it wasn’t. You know the aggravating way rooms have of pretending to be quite dark until you want them to *be* dark﻿—and then﻿—by no means! This room didn’t even pretend to be dark, to begin with. Its shutters had two heart-shaped holes, high up, through which the light showed quite dazzlingly. Edred had to climb up on to the window-seat and stuff up the holes very tight with crushed newspaper, to get which he had to unbolt the door.

===Chinese===
不过等她走了，埃德雷德还是说：「咱们照样把门闩上，防的就是她万一想起什么东西来。」

他推上了那根粗大的木门闩。

「这下可要漆黑了。」他说。

当然，并没有黑。你知道房间有的那股气人劲儿：平时尽自装得漆黑，等你当真要它*黑*下来——嘿，想都别想！这间屋子起先连装都没装黑。它的窗板高处有两个心形的小洞，光从洞里漏进来，晃得人眼花。埃德雷德只好爬上窗座，用揉皱的报纸把小洞塞得严严实实——为了拿报纸，他还得先把门闩拉开。

===Original===
“There,” he said, as he pulled and patted the newspaper till it really and darkly filled the heart-shaped holes, “now it will be quite dark.”

And again it wasn’t! Long, dusty rays of light came through the cracks where the hinges of the shutters were. Newspapers were no good for them. The door had to be unbolted and Mrs. Honeysett found. She was sitting in a little low chair at the back door plucking a white chicken. The sight of the little white feathers floating fluffing about brought wonderful memories to Edred. But he only said﻿—

“I say, you haven’t any old curtains, have you? Thick ones﻿—or thin, if they were red.”

Mrs. Honeysett laid the chicken down among his white feathers and went to a chest of drawers that stood in the kitchen.

===Chinese===
「瞧。」他一边又拽又拍打报纸，直到它结结实实、乌漆墨黑地填满了心形小洞，一边说，「这下可要漆黑了。」

谁知还是没有！一道道蒙尘的长条光，从窗板合页的缝里漏了进来。报纸对付不了它们。只好又拉开门闩，去找哈尼塞特太太。她正坐在后门口一把低矮的小椅子上，拔一只白鸡的毛。小小的白羽毛飘飘扬扬、纷纷洒洒，那景象勾起了埃德雷德奇妙的回忆。可他只说——

「我说，您家里有没有旧窗帘？厚的那种——薄也行，只要是红的。」

哈尼塞特太太把鸡搁在它那身白毛中间，走到厨房里一口五斗柜跟前。

===Original===
“Here you are,” she said, handing out two old red velvet curtains, with which he disappeared. But he was back again quite quickly.

“You haven’t got a hammer, I suppose?” said he.

The dresser-drawer yielded a hammer, and Edred took it away, to return almost at once with﻿—

“I suppose there aren’t any tacks﻿—?”

===Chinese===
「给。」她说着，递出两条旧的红丝绒窗帘，他抱着窗帘走了。可不大工夫又回来了。

「您总该有把锤子吧？」他说。

碗柜的抽屉交出一把锤子，埃德雷德拿走了，几乎立刻又回来——

「想来不会连钉子也一枚不剩——？」

===Original===
“I suppose,” said Mrs. Honeysett, laughing, “there ain’t much sense locking that still-room door on the inside when it ain’t me that keeps all a-popping in, but you that keeps all a-popping out.”

However, she gave him the tacks﻿—rusty ones, in a damp screw of paper.

When he had hammered his fingers a good deal and the tacks a little the tacks consented to hold up the curtain, or the curtain condescended to be held up by the tacks.

===Chinese===
「我说，」哈尼塞特太太笑起来，「从里头锁配膳室（still-room）的门，实在没多大意思——一个劲儿往里钻的又不是我，一个劲儿往外跑的倒是你们。」

不过钉子她还是给了——锈钉子，包在一小卷潮乎乎的纸里。

等他把手指锤了个够、把钉子锤了一小顿之后，钉子总算肯挂住窗帘了——或者不如说，窗帘总算肯屈尊让钉子挂住了。

===Original===
“And now,” said Edred, shutting the door, “it really *is*﻿—”

Dark, he meant. But of course it wasn’t. There was a gap under the door so wide, as Elfrida said, that you could have almost crawled through it. *That* meant another appeal to Mrs. Honeysett for another curtain, and this time Mrs. Honeysett told him to go along with him for a little worrit, and threw a handful of downy soft white feathers at him. But she laughed, too, and gave him the curtain.

At last it really *was* dark, and then they had to unbolt the door again, because Elfrida had forgotten where she had put the matches.

===Chinese===
「这下，」埃德雷德关上门说，「可*真的*要——」

他要说的是：黑了。当然，还是没有黑。门底下那条缝宽得很，照埃尔弗丽达的说法，人差不多能从底下爬过去。*这*就意味着又得去找哈尼塞特太太再讨一条窗帘；这一回哈尼塞特太太骂他是个小磨人精，还抓起一把绒毛似的软白鸡毛朝他扔过去。不过她也笑了，窗帘还是给了他。

好不容易真的*黑*了，他们却只好再把门闩拉开，因为埃尔弗丽达忘了火柴搁在哪儿了。

===Original===
You will readily understand that, after all this preparation, the children were at the last point of impatience, and everything seemed to go slowly. The lamp with the red shade burned up presently, and then the four pie-dishes were filled with water that looked pink in that strange light.

“One good thing,” said Edred, “the hypo has had time to melt.”

And now there was careful snipping, and long ribbons of black paper curled unheeded round the legs of the operators.

“I wish we were born photographers like the man who took Aunt Edith and you on the beach with the donkey,” said Edred nervously, as he began to pass the film in and out of the water in pie-dish Number One.

===Chinese===
你不难理解，折腾完这一大通之后，孩子们的不耐烦已经到了顶点，偏偏样样事情都显得慢吞吞的。红罩灯不多时亮了起来，四只馅饼盘也盛上了水，在那古怪的光里泛着粉红。

「有一样好。」埃德雷德说，「海波有工夫化开了。」

接下来是一番小心翼翼的裁剪，黑色的纸带长长地卷在两个操作者的腿边，谁也顾不上理会。

「但愿咱们生来就是照相师，就像给伊迪丝姑姑和你照那张海滩驴子合影的人一样。」埃德雷德紧张地说，一面开始把胶片在一号馅饼盘的水里提进提出。

===Original===
“Oh, be sure there are no air-bubbles!” said Elfrida; “you might let me do some of it.”

“You shall do the next one,” said Edred, almost holding his breath.

Dear reader, do you recall the agitating moment when you pass the film through the hypo﻿—and hold it up to the light﻿—and nothing happens? Do you remember the painful wonder whether you may have forgotten to set the shutter? Or whether you have got hold of an unexposed film by mistake? Your breath comes with difficulty, your fingers feel awkward, and the film is unnaturally slippery. You dip it into the hypo-bath again, and draw it through and through with the calmness of despair.

“I don’t believe it’s coming out at all,” you say.

===Chinese===
「哦，千万别有气泡！」埃尔弗丽达说；「有些活儿不妨让我干干。」

「下一张让你干。」埃德雷德说，气儿都快屏住了。

亲爱的读者，你可记得那个揪心的时刻：你把胶片从海波里提出来——举到灯前——却什么也没有？你可记得那种煎熬的疑心：是不是忘了上快门？还是错拿了一卷没曝过光的胶片？你呼吸发紧，手指发笨，胶片滑得反常。你又把它浸进海波定影液里，怀着绝望的镇定，一遍又一遍地拉过来拉过去。

「我看根本就显不出来。」你说。

===Original===
And then comes the glorious moment when you hold it up again to the red light and murmur rapturously, “Ah! it is beginning to show!”

If you will kindly remember all the emotions of those exciting moments﻿—on an occasion, let us say, when you had not had your camera very long﻿—then multiply by seven million, add *x*﻿—an unknown quantity of an emotion quite different from anything you have ever felt﻿—and you will have some idea of what Edred and Elfrida felt when the first faint, grey, formless patches began to appear on the film.

But you might multiply till you had used up the multiplication table, and add *x*’s as long as you could afford them, and yet never imagine the rapture with which the two children saw the perfect development of the six little perfect pictures. For they *were* perfect. They were perfect pictures of Arden Castle at a time when it, too, was perfect. No broken arches, no crumbling wall, but every part neat and clear-cut as they had seen it when they went into the past that was three hundred years ago.

===Chinese===
接着，那光辉的时刻到了：你把它再举到红灯前，喜不自胜地喃喃道：「啊！开始显影了！」

倘蒙你好心回想一下那些激动时刻的全部心情——比如说，在你拥有照相机还不很久的时候——先乘上七百万，再加个*x*——一种与你平生任何感受都不同的、未知量的心情——你对埃德雷德和埃尔弗丽达此刻的心情就能有个大概了：胶片上开始浮出最初的浅灰色的、不成形状的斑影。

可是，哪怕你把乘法口诀乘到底，把*x*加到你出得起的地步，也想象不出两个孩子望着六张小小的完美照片完美显影时的那阵狂喜。它们*确实*完美。它们是阿登城堡在它自己也还完好时的完美照片。没有断裂的拱门，没有剥落的墙壁，每一处都齐整鲜明，正如他们走进三百年前的过去时亲眼见过的那样。

===Original===
They were equally fortunate with the second film. It, too, had its six faultless pictures of Arden Castle three hundred years ago. And the last film developed just as finely. Only, just before the moment which was the right moment for taking the film out of the hypo-bath and beginning to wash it, a tiny white feather fell out of Edred’s hair into the dish. It was so tiny that in that dim light he did not notice it. And it did not stick to the film or do any of those things which you might have feared if you had seen the little, white thing flutter down. It may have been the feather’s doing; I don’t know. I just tell you the thing as it happened.

Of course, you know that films have to be pinned up to dry.

Well, the first film was pinned on the right-hand panel of the door and the second film was pinned on the left-hand panel of the door. And when it came to the third, the one that had had the little white feather dropped near it, there was nothing wooden left to pin it to﻿—for the walls were of stone﻿—nothing wooden except the shutters. And it was pinned across these.

===Chinese===
第二卷胶片同样顺利。它也有六张三百年前阿登城堡的无瑕照片。最后一卷显影得也一样漂亮。只是，就在该把胶片从海波定影液里取出来、开始水洗的那一刻之前，一根小小的白羽毛从埃德雷德的头发里飘落，掉进了盘中。它那么小，在那样昏暗的光里他压根儿没有留意。它也没有粘在胶片上，没有闯下任何祸——倘若你瞧见那个白色小东西飘飘悠悠落下去，这些祸事你都是要替它担心的。这也许正是那根羽毛干出来的事；我不知道。我只是把事情照它发生的样子讲给你听。

当然，你知道胶片得用大头针钉起来晾干。

于是，第一卷钉在了门右边的门板上，第二卷钉在门左边的门板上。轮到第三卷——就是那卷旁边落过小小白羽毛的——木头的地方已经没有剩下——因为墙是石头的——除了窗板，再没有木头。它就横钉在了窗板上。

===Original===
“It doesn’t matter,” said Edred, “because we needn’t open the shutters till it’s dry.”

And with that he stuck in four pins at its four corners, and turned to blow out the lamp and unbolt the door. He meant to do this, but the door, as a matter of fact, wasn’t bolted at all, because Edred had forgotten to do it when he came back with the dusters, so he couldn’t have unbolted it anyway.

But he could blow out the red-sided lamp; and he did.

And then the wonderful thing happened. Of course the room ought to have been quite dark. I’m sure enough trouble had been taken to make it so. But it wasn’t. The window, the window where the shutters were﻿—the shutters that the film was pinned on﻿—the film on which the little white feather had fallen﻿—the little white feather that had settled on Edred’s hair when Mrs. Honeysett was plucking that chicken at the back door﻿—that window now showed as a broad oblong of light. And in that broad oblong was a sort of shining, a faint sparkling movement, like the movement of the light on the sheet of a cinematograph before the pictures begin to show.

===Chinese===
「不要紧。」埃德雷德说，「反正晾干之前，咱们用不着开窗板。」

说罢，他在胶片四角钉了四枚大头针，转身去吹灯、拉门闩。他是打算这么办的；可事实上，门压根儿就没有闩上——埃德雷德抱着抹布回来那回，忘了闩——所以横竖他也拉不上什么门闩。

但红灯他是能吹灭的；他也吹灭了。

接着，奇妙的事情发生了。按理，屋里本该一团漆黑。我敢担保，为着这个没少费力气。可偏偏不黑。那扇窗，装着窗板的那扇窗——窗板上钉着胶片——胶片上落过那根小小的白羽毛——那根白羽毛，是哈尼塞特太太在后门口拔鸡的时候，落在埃德雷德头发上的——此刻那扇窗亮成了一大片长方形的光。那片光里有一种闪亮，一阵微微的、星星点点的浮动，像电影开演之前，放映机打在布幕上的光的那种浮动。

===Original===
“Oh!” said Elfrida, catching at Edred’s hand. What she did catch was his hair. She felt her way down his arm, and so caught what she had meant to catch, and held it fast.

“It’s *more* magic,” said Edred ungratefully. “I do wish﻿—”

“Oh, hush!” said Elfrida; “look﻿—oh, look!”

===Chinese===
「哦！」埃尔弗丽达叫了一声，一把去抓埃德雷德的手。她实际抓住的是他的头发。她顺着他的胳膊一路摸下去，这才抓住了本来要抓的东西，紧紧攥住不放。

「*又*是魔法。」埃德雷德不知好歹地说。「我真希望——」

「哦，别作声！」埃尔弗丽达说；「看——哦，你看！」

===Original===
The light﻿—broad, oblong﻿—suddenly changed from mere light to figures, to movement. It was a living picture﻿—rather like a cinematograph, but much more like something else. The something else that it was more like was *life*.

It seemed as though the window had been opened﻿—as though they could see through it into the world of light and sunshine and blue sky﻿—the world where things happen.

There was the castle, and there were people going across the drawbridge﻿—men with sacks on their backs. And a man with a silver chain round his neck and a tall stick in his hand, was standing under the great gateway telling them where to take the sacks. And a cart drove up, with casks, and they were rolled across the drawbridge and under the tall arch of the gate-tower. The men were dressed. Then something blinked, and the scene changed. It was indoors now﻿—a long room with many pictures on one side of it and many windows on the other; a lady, in a large white collar and beautiful long curls, very like Aunt Edith, was laying fine dresses in a chest. A gentleman, also with long hair, and with a good deal of lace about his collar and cuffs, was putting jugs and plates of gold and silver into another chest; and servants kept bringing more golden grand things, and more and more.

===Chinese===
那片光——宽宽的、长方形的——忽然从单纯的光变成了人影，变成了动作。那是一幅活的图画——有点像电影，可更像另外一样东西。它更像的那一样东西，是*生活*。

仿佛窗子打开了——仿佛他们能透过它望进那个光明的、晴朗的、蓝天白云的世界——那个万事万物正在发生的世界。

城堡就在那儿，人们正从吊桥上走过——是背上扛着麻袋的男人们。一个脖子上挂着银链、手里拄着高杖的人，站在大门口，指点他们把麻袋往哪儿送。一辆大车驶来，装着木桶，桶被滚过吊桥，滚进门楼高高的拱门。男人们个个穿着旧时的衣装。接着有什么东西眨了一下，画面变了。这回是在室内——一间长屋，一边墙上挂满画，另一边是一排窗户；一位夫人，戴着大大的白领，垂着好看的长卷发，模样极像伊迪丝姑姑，正把讲究的裙袍往一口箱子里放；一位先生，也是长发，领口和袖口缀着许多花边，正把金银的壶和盘子放进另一口箱子；用人们不断捧来更多金光灿灿的贵重物件，越来越多，越来越多。

===Original===
Edred and Elfrida did not say a word. They couldn’t. What they were looking at was far too thrilling. But in each heart the same words were uttered﻿—

“That’s the treasure!” And each mind held the same thought.

“If it only goes on till the treasure’s hidden, we shall see where they put it, and then we can go and find it.”

I think myself that the white Mouldiwarp was anxious to help a little. I believe it had arranged the whole of this exhibition so that the children might get an idea of the whereabouts of the treasure, and so cease to call on it at all hours of the day and night with the sort of poetry which even a mole must see not to be so *very* good. However this may be, it was a wonderful show. One seemed to see things better somehow like that, through the window that looked into the past, than one did who was really *in* the past taking an active part in what was going on.

===Chinese===
埃德雷德和埃尔弗丽达一句话也没说。他们说不出。眼前的景象太惊心动魄了。可两颗心里喊出的是同一句话——

「那就是宝藏！」两人的脑子里转的也是同一个念头。

「只要它演到宝藏藏好，咱们就能看见他们把它放在哪儿，然后就能去找了。」

依我自己看，白鼹穆迪沃普（Mouldiwarp，「鼹鼠」的古称）是存心要帮点忙了。我相信，这一整场放映全是它安排的，好让孩子们对宝藏的所在有个数，从此别再白天黑夜、随时随地拿着那种连鼹鼠自己也看得出并不*很*高明的诗来召唤它。且不管是不是这样，那都是一场了不起的演出。隔着这扇望进过去的窗子看事情，不知怎么，比当真*身*在往昔、在里头亲历其事的人，看得还要清楚些。

===Original===
There appeared, at any rate, to be no doubt that this really *was* the treasure, and still less that it was a treasure both plentiful and picturesque. Quickly and more quickly the beautiful rich things were being packed into the chests. More and more pale looked the lady; more and more anxious the gentleman.

The lady was taking from her waiting-woman little boxes and bundles with which the woman’s apron was filled, and the chest before which she was kneeling was nearly full when the door at the end of the gallery opened suddenly, and Elfrida and Edred, in the dark in the still-room, were confronted with the spectacle of themselves coming down the long picture-gallery towards that group of chests and treasure, and hurried human people. They saw themselves in blue silk and lace and black velvet, and they saw on their own faces fear and love, and the wonder what was to happen next. They saw themselves embraced by the grownups, who were quite plainly father and mother﻿—they saw themselves speak, and the grownups reply.

“I’d give all my pocket-money for a year to hear what they’re saying,” Edred told himself.

===Chinese===
无论如何，这确实*就是*宝藏，这一点看来毫无疑问；至于它又丰盛又好看，就更没有疑问了。华美的贵重物件被越来越快地装进箱子。夫人的脸色越来越白；先生的神色越来越急。

夫人正从贴身女仆手里接过一只只小盒、一包包小物件，那女人的围裙里塞满了这些；她跪着装的那口箱子眼看要满了。就在这时，长廊尽头的门忽然打开，黑黢黢的配膳室里的埃尔弗丽达和埃德雷德，冷不防看见了他们自己，正沿着长长的画廊，朝那几口箱子、那批宝藏和那群慌乱的人走来。他们看见自己穿着蓝绸衣裳、蕾丝和黑天鹅绒，看见自己脸上有恐惧、有爱，还有对接下来会发生什么的怔忡。他们看见自己被大人们搂进怀里——那两位分明就是爸爸和妈妈——他们看见自己开口说话，看见大人们回答。

「把一年的零花钱全搭上，我也想听听他们在说什么。」埃德雷德心里说。

===Original===
“That daddy’s just like *my* daddy,” Elfrida was telling herself; “and just like the daddy in the Tower that was so like my own daddy.”

Then the children in the picture kneeled down, and the daddy in the picture laid his hands on their heads, and the children out of the picture bent their own heads there in the dark still-room, for they knew what was happening in the picture. Elfrida even half held out her arms; but it was no good.

Again the scene changed. A chest was being carried by four men, who strained and staggered under its weight. They were carrying it along a vaulted passage by ropes that passed under the chest and over their shoulders. Every now and then they set it down and stretched, and wiped their faces. And the picture kept on changing so that the children seemed to be going with the men down a flight of stairs into a spacious hall full of men, all talking, and very busy with armour and big boots, and then across the courtyard, full of more men, very busy too, polishing axes and things that looked like spears, cleaning muskets and fitting new flints to pistols and sharpening swords on a big grindstone. Edred would have loved to stay and watch them do these things, but they and their work were gone quite quickly, and the chest and the men who carried it were going under an archway. Here one of the men wanted to rest again, but the others said it was not worth while﻿—they were almost there. It was quite plain that they said this, though no sound could be heard.

===Chinese===
「那位爸爸真像*我的*爸爸。」埃尔弗丽达心里念叨；「也像伦敦塔里的那位爸爸——他本来就那么像我自己的爸爸。」

画面里的孩子们跪了下来，画面里的爸爸把手按在他们头上；画面外的孩子们在黑黢黢的配膳室里也低下了自己的头，因为他们知道画面里正在发生的是什么。埃尔弗丽达甚至把胳膊伸出去了一半；可是没有用。

画面又变了。一口箱子由四个男人抬着，压得他们弯腰打晃。他们抬着它走过一条拱顶通道，绳子从箱底穿过去，搭在他们肩上。走一程，他们就撂下箱子直直腰，抹抹脸。画面不停地变换，孩子们仿佛跟着那些人下了一段台阶，进了一间宽敞的大厅，厅里满是男人，人人都在说话，忙着收拾盔甲和大靴子；然后穿过院子，院里又是更多的人，也在忙，擦斧头和长矛模样的家伙，擦拭火枪，给手枪换上新燧石，在大磨石上磨剑。埃德雷德巴不得留下来看他们干这些活儿，可那些人和活儿很快就过去了，那口箱子和抬箱人钻进了一道拱门。有个男人想再歇一歇，其余的人说犯不着——快到了。虽然一点声音也听不见，他们说这话的模样却一清二楚。

===Original===
“Now we shall *really* know,” said Edred to himself. Elfrida squeezed his hand. That was just what she was thinking, too.

The men stopped at a door, knocked, knocked again, and yet once more. And, curiously enough, the children in the still-room could hear the sound of the knocking quite plainly, though they had heard nothing else.

The men looked at each other across the chest that they had set down. Then one man set his shoulder to the door. There was a scrunching sound and the picture disappeared﻿—went out; and there were the shutters with the film pinned across them, and behind them the door, open, and Mrs. Honeysett telling them that dinner﻿—which was roast rabbit and a boiled hand of pork﻿—would be cold if they didn’t make haste and come along.

===Chinese===
「这下咱们*可真*要知道了。」埃德雷德心里说。埃尔弗丽达捏了捏他的手。她心里想的也正是这句。

那些人在一扇门前停住，敲了敲，又敲，再敲了一遍。说也奇怪，配膳室里的孩子们竟把敲门声听得清清楚楚，虽然别的什么声音他们都没听见。

抬箱人隔着撂下的箱子面面相觑。随后一个男人用肩膀向门撞去。只听嘎吱一声碎响，画面消失了——熄灭了；眼前是钉着胶片的窗板，窗板后面的门开着，哈尼塞特太太正告诉他们：午饭——烤兔肉，还有一只煮猪肘——再不赶紧去，可就要凉了。

===Original===
“Oh, Mrs. Honeysett,” said Elfrida, with deep feeling, “you are too bad﻿—you really are!”

“I hope I’ve not spoiled the photos,” said Mrs. Honeysett; “but I did knock three times, and you was that quiet I was afraid something had happened to you﻿—poisoned yourselves without thinking, or something of that.”

“It’s too bad,” said Edred bitterly; “it’s much too bad. I don’t want any dinner; I don’t want anything. Everything’s spoiled.”

===Chinese===
「哦，哈尼塞特太太。」埃尔弗丽达满心感触地说，「您真是太坏了——真是的！」

「我该没糟蹋了照片吧。」哈尼塞特太太说；「我可是敲了三回门，你们静得那个样子，我还当你们出了什么事——不留神把自己毒着了，或是别的什么。」

「太糟了。」埃德雷德酸楚地说；「糟透了。我不要吃什么午饭；我什么都不要。全完了。」

===Original===
“Perhaps,” said Mrs. Honeysett patiently, “I might ha’ gone on knocking longer, only I thought the door was bolted﻿—you did so keep on a-bolting of it at the beginning, didn’t you? So I just got hold of the handle to try, and it come open in my hand. Come along, lovey; don’t bear malice now. I didn’t go for to do it. An’ I’ll get you some more of whatever it is that’s spoiled, and you can take some more photos tomorrow.”

“You might have known we were all right,” said Edred, still furious; but both thought it only fair to say, “It wasn’t the *photographs* that were spoiled”﻿—and they said it at the same moment.

“Then what was it?” said Mrs. Honeysett. “And do come along, for goodness’ sake, and eat your dinner while it’s hot.”

===Chinese===
「兴许，」哈尼塞特太太耐心地说，「我本可以多敲一阵子，只是我当门是闩上了的——你们开头不就是一个劲儿地闩门来着吗？我就握住门把儿试了试，它一下子就开在我手里啦。来吧，小亲亲；可别记恨喽。我不是存心的。不管糟蹋掉的是什么，我再给你们弄些来，明天你们再多照些就是。」

「您本该知道我们没事的。」埃德雷德说，还在气头上；可两人都觉得说句公道话才对：「糟蹋掉的不是*照片*」——而且两人是同时说的。

「那是什么呀？」哈尼塞特太太说。「千万来吧，看在老天爷份上，趁热把午饭吃了。」

===Original===
“It was﻿—it was a different sort of picture,” said Elfrida, with a gulp, “and it *was* a pity.”

“Never mind, love,” said Mrs. Honeysett, who was as kind as a grandmother, and I can’t say more than that; “there’s a lovely surprise coming by and by for good little gells and boys, and the rabbit’ll be stone cold if you don’t make haste﻿—leastways, it would have been if I hadn’t thought to pop it in the oven when I came to call you, knowing full well what your hands would be like after all that messing about with poison in dishes; and if I was your aunt I’d forbid it downright. And now come along and wash your hands, and don’t let’s have any more nonsense about it. Do you hear?”

I daresay you notice that Mrs. Honeysett was quite cross at the end of this speech and quite coaxing and kind at the beginning. She had just talked herself into being cross. It’s quite easy. I daresay you have often done it.

===Chinese===
「那是——那是另外的一种画。」埃尔弗丽达哽了一下说，「那*确实*可惜。」

「别往心里去，亲爱的。」哈尼塞特太太说——她慈祥得像个老祖母，再高的夸法我想不出了；「待会儿有顶好的惊喜，等着乖小姑娘乖小伙子呢，你们再不赶紧，兔肉可要冰凉了——起码本来会凉的，幸亏我来叫你们的时候，想着把它塞进了烤炉；我还不清楚吗，你们在盘子里摆弄完那些毒药，手上还能是个什么样子；要我是你们姑姑，干脆一句话禁止你们再摆弄。现在来洗净手，这事就别再磨牙了。听见没有？」

想来你注意到了：这番话开头，哈尼塞特太太又是哄又是疼，说到末尾却相当恼了。她是说着说着，把自己说恼了的。这很容易。我敢说你也常常这么干。

===Original===
It was a silent dinner﻿—the first silent meal since the children had come to Arden Castle. You can judge of Edred’s feelings when I tell you that he felt as though the rabbit would choke him, and refused a second helping of gooseberry pie with heartfelt sincerity. Elfrida did not eat so much as usual either. It really was a bitter disappointment. To have been so near seeing where the treasure was, and then﻿—just because they hadn’t happened to bolt the door that last time﻿—all was in vain. Mrs. Honeysett thought they were sulking about a silly trifle, and nearly said so when Edred refused the pie.

It was at the end of dinner that Elfrida, as she got down from her chair, saw Mrs. Honeysett’s face, and saw how different it looked from the kind face that she usually wore. She went over to her very slowly, and very quickly threw her arms round her and kissed her.

“I’m sorry we’ve been so piggy,” she said. “It’s not your fault that you’re not clever enough to know about pictures and things, is it?”

===Chinese===
那是一顿无声的午饭——自从孩子们来到阿登城堡，这还是头一顿没有话的饭。我告诉你，埃德雷德觉得那兔肉简直要噎死他，而且出自肺腑地谢绝了第二份醋栗馅饼，你就可以掂量出他的心情了。埃尔弗丽达也吃得比平常少。这实在是一次痛心的失望。眼看就要看到宝藏藏在哪儿了，结果——只因为最后那回偏偏没闩门——一切都白费了。哈尼塞特太太只当他们为点傻乎乎的小事闹别扭，埃德雷德拒绝馅饼的时候，她差点把这话说了出来。

就是在这顿饭的末尾，埃尔弗丽达从椅子上下来时，看见了哈尼塞特太太的脸，看出它同她平日戴着的那张和气的脸多么不一样。她慢吞吞地朝她走过去，却一下子张开双臂搂住她，吻了她。

「对不起，我们刚才太猪脾气了。」她说。「您不够聪明、不懂什么照片不照片的，那又不怪您，对吧？」

===Original===
If Mrs. Honeysett hadn’t been a perfect dear, this apology would have been worse than none. But she *was* a perfect dear, so she laughed and hugged Elfrida, and somehow Edred got caught into the hug and the laugh, and the three were friends again. The sky was blue and the sun began to shine.

And then the two children went down to old Beale’s.

There were roses in his garden now, and white English flags and lupins and tall foxgloves bordering the little brick path. Old Beale was sitting “on a brown Windsor chair,” as Edred said, in the sun by his front door. Over his head was a jackdaw in a wicker cage, and Elfrida did not approve of this till she saw the cage door was open, and that the jackdaw was sitting in the cage because he liked it, and not because he must. She had been in prison in the Tower, you remember, and people who have been in prison never like to see live things in cages. There was a tabby and white cat of squarish shape sitting on the wooden threshold. (Why are cats who live in country cottages almost always tabby and white and squarish?) The feathery tail of a brown spaniel flogged the flags lazily in the patch of shade made by the water-butt. It was a picture of rural peace, and old Beale was asleep in the middle of it. I am glad to tell you that Lord Arden and his sister were polite enough to wait till he awoke of his own accord, instead of shouting “hi!” or rattling the smooth brown iron latch of the gate, as some children would have done.

===Chinese===
倘若哈尼塞特太太不是个顶顶可亲的人，这句道歉不如不道。可她偏偏*就是*，于是她笑着搂住埃尔弗丽达，不知怎么，埃德雷德也被卷进了那个拥抱和那阵笑声，三个人重归于好。天空瓦蓝，太阳也露出了脸。

随后，两个孩子下老比尔（Beale）家去了。

如今他的园子里开了玫瑰，还有白色的英国鸢尾、羽扇豆和高高的毛地黄，沿着砖砌小径镶成一道边。老比尔，照埃德雷德的说法，「坐在一把棕色温莎椅上」，在自家前门口晒太阳。他头顶上方，一只寒鸦关在柳条笼里；埃尔弗丽达起初不以为然，直到看见笼门是敞开的，寒鸦待在笼里是它乐意，不是非待不可，才没了话说。她在伦敦塔蹲过监狱，你是记得的；蹲过监狱的人，见不得活物关在笼子里。木门槛上蹲着一只四四方方的虎斑白猫。（为什么住乡下村舍的猫，几乎总归是虎斑加白、四四方方？）一只棕色西班牙猎犬那羽毛似的尾巴，在雨水桶投下的一小片荫凉里，懒懒地拍打着石板。这是一幅乡野太平安乐图，老比尔就酣睡在图的正当间。我很高兴地告诉你：阿登勋爵和他姐姐懂礼得很，一直等到他老人家自家醒来，而没有像某些孩子那样扯开嗓子喊「喂！」，或者把大门上那杆光滑的褐色铁门闩拨得哗啦啦响。

===Original===
They just sat down on the dry, grassy bank, opposite his gate, and looked at the blue and white butterflies and the flowers and the green potato-tops through the green-grey garden palings.

And while they sat there Elfrida had an idea﻿—so sudden and so good that it made her jump. But she said nothing, and Edred said﻿—

“Pinch the place hard, and if it’s still there you’ll kill it perhaps”﻿—for he thought she had jumped because she had been bitten by an ant.

When they had finished looking at the butterflies and the red roses and the green-growing things, they looked long and steadily at old Beale, and, of course, he awoke, as people always do if you look at them long enough and hard enough. And he got up, rather shaking, and put his hand to his forehead, and said﻿—

===Chinese===
他们就在他家大门对面干燥的草坡上坐下，隔着绿灰两色的花园栅栏，看蓝蝴蝶白蝴蝶，看花，看绿油油的土豆秧。

坐着坐着，埃尔弗丽达忽然来了个主意——来得又突然又妙，惊得她跳了一下。可她什么也没说，倒是埃德雷德开了口——

「使劲掐那地方，要是它还在，兴许能把它掐死。」——他只当她是被蚂蚁咬了一口才跳起来的。

看罢了蝴蝶、红玫瑰和绿生生的草木，他们便久久地、直直地望着老比尔；他自然就醒了——人被人望得够久够狠，总是要醒的。他站起身，身子微微发颤，把手举到额前，说——

===Original===
“My lord﻿—”

“How are you?” said Elfrida. “We haven’t found the treasure yet.”

“But ye will, ye will,” said old Beale. “Come into the house now; or will ye come round along to the arbour and have a drink of milk?”

“We’d as soon stay here,” said Edred﻿—they had come through the gate now, and Edred was patting the brown spaniel, while Elfrida stroked the squarish cat. “Mrs. Honeysett said you knew all the stories.”

===Chinese===
「大人——」

「您好啊？」埃尔弗丽达说。「我们还没找着宝藏呢。」

「会的，你们会找着的。」老比尔说。「现在进屋来吧；还是绕到凉亭那边，喝口牛奶去？」

「我们就在这儿待着也一样好。」埃德雷德说——这会儿他们已经进了大门，埃德雷德拍着那条棕色的西班牙猎犬，埃尔弗丽达抚着那只四四方方的猫。「哈尼塞特太太说，您什么故事都知道。」


===Original===
“Ah,” said old Beale, “a fine girl, Mrs. Honeysett. Her father had Sellinge Farm, where the fairies churn the butter for the bride so long as there’s no cross words. They don’t ever get too much to do, them fairies.” He chuckled, sighed, and said﻿—

“I know a power of tales. And I know, always I do, which it is that people want. What you’re after’s the story of the East House. Isn’t it now? Is the old man a-failing of his wits, or isn’t he?”

“We want to know,” said Edred, companionably sharing the flagstone with the feather-tailed spaniel, “the story about why that part of the house in the castle is shut up and all cobwebby and dusty and rusty and musty, and whether there’s any reason why it shouldn’t be all cleaned up and made nice again, if we find the treasure so that we’ve got enough money to pay for new curtains and carpets and things?”
