“It’s a sad tale, that,” said old Beale, “a tale for old folks﻿—or middle-aged folks, let’s say﻿—not for children. You’d never understand it if I was to tell it you, likely as not.”

===Chinese===
「啊，」老比尔说，「哈尼塞特太太，从前是个好姑娘。她父亲原有塞林格农场（Sellinge Farm），那地方只要新娘子不说拌嘴的话，仙人们就替她搅黄油。那些仙人啊，从来也不见有多少活儿干。」他咯咯笑了一声，叹了口气，说——

「我肚子里故事多得是。我还总能知道人家要听的是哪一个，回回都知道。你们打听的是东屋（East House）的故事。是不是？你们说，我这老头子的脑筋还使得使不得？」

「我们想知道，」埃德雷德说——他正同那条羽毛尾巴的西班牙猎犬亲亲热热地合占着一块石板——「城堡里那一片屋子为什么封了起来，蛛网尘土、锈的霉的闹成一片；还有，等我们找着了宝藏，钱够添置新窗帘新地毯什么的了，有没有什么缘故，不能把它统统打扫干净、重新收拾漂亮？」

「那是个伤心的故事。」老比尔说，「是讲给老年人听的——或者中年人吧——不是讲给孩子的。我要是讲给你们听，多半你们也听不懂。」

===Original===
“We *like* grown-up stories,” said Elfrida, with dignity; and Edred added﻿—

“We can understand *anything* that grownups understand if it’s told us properly. I understand all about the laws of gravitation, and why the sun doesn’t go round the earth but does the opposite; I understood *that* directly Aunt Edith explained it, and about fixed stars, and the spectroscope, and microbes, and the Equator not being real, and﻿—and heaps of things.”

“Ah,” said old Beale admiringly, “you’ll be a-busting with book-larnin’ afore you come to your twenty-one, I lay. I only hope the half of it’s true and they’re not deceiving of you, a trusting innocent. I never did hold myself with that about the sun not moving. Why, you can see it a-doin’ of it with your own naked eyes any day of the week.”

“*You* wouldn’t deceive anyone,” said Elfrida gently. “*Do* tell us the story.”

===Chinese===
「我们*就爱*听大人的故事。」埃尔弗丽达端庄地说；埃德雷德又补上一句——

「只要讲得得法，大人懂的*任何*东西我们都懂。万有引力定律我全明白，还有太阳为什么不绕着地球转、倒是反着来；伊迪丝姑姑一讲，我*当时*就懂了，还有恒星、分光镜、微生物、赤道不是真的一条线，还有——还有好一大堆东西。」

「啊，」老比尔听得佩服，「不到二十一岁，你就要让书本学问撑破肚皮喽，我敢保。我只盼这里头有一半是真的，他们没有哄你这么个实心眼的孩子。太阳不动这种话，我从来就不信那一套。嗨，随便哪一天，你睁着自己那双肉眼，都瞧得见它正走动呢。」

「*您*可不会哄人。」埃尔弗丽达轻声说。「*一定*要讲给我们听呀。」

===Original===
So old Beale began, and he began like this﻿—

“It was a long time ago﻿—before my time even, it was, but not so long afore, ’cause I can recomember my father talking about it. He was coachman at the castle when it all happened, so, of course, he knew everything there was to know, my mother having been the housekeeper and gone through it all with the family. There was a Miss Elfrida then, same as there is now, only she was older’n what you are, missy. And the gentlemen lads from far and near they come a-courting her, for she was a fine girl﻿—a real beauty﻿—with hair as black as a coal and eyes like the sea when it’s beating up for a storm, before the white horses comes along. So I’ve heard my father say﻿—not that I ever see her myself. And she kept her pretty head in the air and wouldn’t turn it this way or that for e’er a one of them all. And the old lord he loved her too dear to press her against her wish and will, and her so young. So she stayed single and watched the sea.”

“What did she do that for?” Edred asked.

===Chinese===
于是老比尔讲了起来，他是这么开头的——

「那是好久以前的事啦——早在我出生以前，可也不算太早，因为我记得我父亲说起过。事情闹出来的时候，他在城堡里当马车夫，所以里里外外的事他自然样样知道，我母亲又是那儿的管家娘子，跟着主家从头到尾经历了这一切。那时候也有一位埃尔弗丽达小姐，跟如今一样，只是她比你年岁大些，姑娘。远远近近的公子哥儿们都来向她求亲，因为她是个好姑娘——真正的美人儿——头发黑得像煤，眼睛像起了风暴的海，还没翻出白浪花时候的那种。这都是我听我父亲说的——我自己可没见过她。她那颗俏脑袋老是扬得高高的，任凭谁来了也不肯偏一偏。老勋爵疼她疼得厉害，不舍得违背她的心愿逼她，何况她还那么年轻。她就这么守着单身，守着看海。」

「她看海做什么？」埃德雷德问。

===Original===
“To see if her sweetheart’s ship wasn’t a-coming home. For she’d got a sweetheart right enough, she had, unbeknown to all. It was her cousin Dick﻿—a ne’er-do-weel, if ever there was one﻿—and it turned out afterwards she’d broken the sixpence with him and swore to be ever true, and he’d gone overseas to find a fortune. And so she watched the sea every day regular, and every day regular he didn’t come. But every day another young chap used to come a-riding﻿—a fine young gentleman and well-to-do, but he was the same kidney as Master Dick, only he’d got a fine fortune, so his wild oats never got a chance to grow strong like Dick’s.”

“Poor Dick!” said Elfrida.

“Not so fast, missy,” said the old man. “Well, her father and mother, they said, ‘Have him that’s here and loves you, dear,’ as the saying is﻿—a Frewin he was, and his christened name Arnold. And she says ‘No.’ But they keeps on saying ‘Yes,’ and he keeps on saying ‘Do!’ So they wears her down, telling her Dick was drowned dead for sure, and I don’t know what all. And at last she says, ‘Very well, then, I’ll marry you﻿—if you can stand to marry a girl that’s got all her heart in the sea along of a dead young chap as she was promised to.’ And the wedding was set for Christmas. Miss Elfrida, she slep’ in the room in the East House that looks out towards Arden Knoll, and the servants in the attics, and the old people in the other part of the house.

===Chinese===
「看她的心上人的船是不是回家来了。她确确实实有个心上人，瞒过了所有人。是她的表哥迪克（Dick）——天底下头一号不成器的浪荡子——后来大家才知道，她同迪克分过一枚掰成两半的六便士，起过誓永不变心，迪克漂洋过海找发财路子去了。于是她天天准点儿看海，天天准点儿他没回来。倒是天天另有一位年轻爷们儿骑马上门——一表人才的阔少爷，可跟迪克少爷是一路货色，只是他有大笔家私，那点荒唐劲儿没机会像迪克的那样疯长罢了。」

「可怜的迪克！」埃尔弗丽达说。

「先别忙着可怜，姑娘。」老头儿说。「后来呀，她爹她娘就说：『嫁眼前这个疼你的人吧，亲爱的』——俗话说得好——他姓弗鲁因（Frewin），受洗的名字叫阿诺德。她说『不』。可他们只管说『嫁吧』，他也只管说『嫁吧』。他们磨啊磨，说迪克准是淹死了，还说三道四了一堆。末了她说：『好吧，我嫁你——只要你受得了娶一个姑娘，她那颗心整个儿在海里，陪着一个同她有过婚约的死了的年轻人。』婚期定在圣诞节。埃尔弗丽达小姐呢，睡在东屋那间朝阿登丘的屋子里，用人们睡阁楼，老两位睡房子另一头。」

===Original===
“And that night, when all was asleep, I think she heard a tap, tap at her window, and at first she’d think it was the ivy﻿—but no. So presently she’d take heart to go to the window, and there was a face outside that had climbed up by the ivy, and it was her own true love that they’d told her was drowned.”

“How splendid!” said Edred.

“How dreadful for Mr. Frewin,” said Elfrida.

===Chinese===
「那天夜里，等大家都睡熟了，我琢磨她是听见了笃、笃的敲窗声，起先她只当是常春藤——可不是。后来她壮起胆子走到窗前，外头贴着一张脸，是顺着常春藤爬上来的，正是他们告诉她已经淹死了的、她自家的真爱。」

「多带劲啊！」埃德雷德说。

「弗鲁因先生多可怜啊。」埃尔弗丽达说。

===Original===
“That’s what she thought, miss, and she couldn’t face it. So she puts on her riding-coat and she gets out of window and down the ivy with him, and off to London. And in the morning, when the bells began to ring for her wedding, and the bridegroom came, there wasn’t no bride for him. She left a letter to say she was very sorry, but it had to be. So then they shut up the East House.”

“So that’s the story,” said Elfrida.

“Half of it, miss,” said old Beale, and he took out a black clay pipe and a screw of tobacco, and very slowly and carefully filled the pipe and lighted it, before he went on, “They shut up the East House, where she’d been used to sleep; but it was kep’ swep’ and dusted, and the old folks was brokenhearted, for never a word come from Miss Elfrida. An’ if I know anything of the feelings of a parent, they kept on saying to each other, ‘She might ha’ trusted us. She might ’a’ known we’d never ’a’ denied her nothing.’ And then one night there was a knock at the door, and there was Miss Elfrida that was﻿—Mrs. Dick now﻿—with her baby in her arms. Mr. Dick was dead, sudden in a accident, and she’d come home to her father and mother. They couldn’t make enough of the poor young thing and her baby. She had her old rooms and there she lived, and she was getting a bit happier and worshipping of her baby and the old people worshipping it and her too. And then one night someone comes up the ivy, same as Master Dick did, and takes away﻿—not her﻿—but the baby.”

===Chinese===
「她想的也正是这个，小姐，她受不住。于是她穿上骑装，翻出窗户，跟他顺着常春藤溜下去，一逃逃到了伦敦。第二天早上，成亲的钟敲响了，新郎官来了，新娘却没了影儿。她留下一封信，说她非常抱愧，可事情只能这样。打那以后，东屋就封了。」

「原来这就是那个故事。」埃尔弗丽达说。

「一半儿，小姐。」老比尔说着，掏出一支黑泥烟斗和一小包烟丝，慢条斯理、仔仔细细地装满烟斗、点着了，才接着讲下去，「他们封了东屋，那是她睡惯的地方；可屋子一直有人扫、有人掸，老两位伤透了心，因为埃尔弗丽达小姐一星半点音信也没有。要说我懂得一点做爹娘的心思，他们准是一遍遍互相念叨：『她本可以信得过我们。她该知道，我们从来没有舍不得过她什么。』后来有一天夜里，门上有人敲，门外站着从前的埃尔弗丽达小姐——如今是迪克太太了——怀里抱着她的娃娃。迪克先生死了，一场横祸，说去就去了，她回爹娘家来了。老两口疼那苦命的年轻人和她的娃娃，怎么疼也疼不够。她还住回原来的屋子，就在那儿过活，人也一点点快活起来，把娃娃当心尖子，老两位也把娃娃和她当心尖子。可后来有一天夜里，有人顺着常春藤爬上来，就跟迪克少爷当年一样，抱走了——不是她——是娃娃。」

===Original===
“How dreadful!” breathed Elfrida. “Did they get it back?”

“Never. And never a word was ever found out about who took it, or why, or where they took it to. Only a week or two after Mr. Frewin was killed in the hunting-field, and as they picked him up he said, ‘Elfrida; tell Elfrida﻿—’ and he was trying to say what they was to tell her, when he died. Some folks hold as ’twas him stole the baby, to be even with her for jilting of him, or else to pretend to find it and get her to marry him out of gratitude. But no one’ll ever know. And the baby’s mother, she wore away bit by bit, to a shadow, and then she died, and after that the East House was shut up for good and all, to fall into rot and ruin like it is now. Don’t you cry, missie. I know’d you wouldn’t like the story, but you would have it; but don’t you cry. It’s all long ago, and she and her baby and her young husband’s all been happy together in heaven this long time now, I lay.”

“I *do* like the story,” said Elfrida, gulping, “but it *is* sad, isn’t it?”

===Chinese===
「多可怕呀！」埃尔弗丽达屏着气说。「找回来了吗？」

「再没找回来。是谁抱走的，为什么，抱到哪儿去了，也再没打听出一个字。只过了个把礼拜，弗鲁因先生在猎场上送了命，人们扶起他的时候，他说：『埃尔弗丽达；告诉埃尔弗丽达——』他想交代他们该告诉她什么，话没说完就断了气。有人说娃娃就是他偷的，为着她悔婚甩了他，要跟她算这笔账；要不就是想装作寻见了娃娃，好让她感恩嫁他。可谁也不会知道了。娃娃的娘呢，一天天消损下去，瘦成一道影子，后来也死了。打那以后，东屋就永远封死了，任它朽烂坍塌，成了如今这副模样。别哭哇，姑娘。我知道你不会爱听这故事，可你偏要听；别哭啦。这都是老早以前的事了，她呀、她的娃娃呀、她那年轻的丈夫呀，这好些年，天上早就团团圆圆的了，我敢保。」

「我*是*爱听这故事。」埃尔弗丽达吸着鼻子说，「可它*实在*伤心，是不是？」

===Original===
“Thank you for telling it,” Edred said; “but I don’t think it’s any good, really, being unhappy about things that are so long ago, and all over and done with.”

“I wish we could go back into the past and find the baby for her,” Elfrida whispered﻿—and Edred whispered back﻿—

“It’s the treasure we’ve got to find. Excuse our whispering, Mr. Beale. Thank you for the story﻿—oh, and I wanted to ask you who owns the land now﻿—all the land about here, I mean, that used to belong to us Ardens?”

“That Jackson chap,” said old Beale, “him that made a fortune in the soap boiling. The Tallow King, they call him. But he’s got too rich for the house he’s got. He’s bought a bigger place in Yorkshire, that used to belong to the Duke of Sanderstead, and the Arden lands are to be sold next year, so I’m told.”

===Chinese===
「谢谢您讲给我们听。」埃德雷德说；「不过我觉得，为那么久以前、早完了结了的事难过，实在没什么用处。」

「但愿我们能回到过去，替她把娃娃找回来。」埃尔弗丽达悄声说——埃德雷德也悄声回答——

「咱们要找的是宝藏。比尔先生，恕我们咬耳朵。谢谢您的故事——哦，我还想请问您，如今这片地是谁的——我是说，这一带从前属于我们阿登家的那些地。」

「还不是杰克逊（Jackson）那家伙。」老比尔说，「熬肥皂熬出份家业的那个。人家管他叫牛脂大王。只是他的钱多得跟手头的宅子不相称了。他在约克郡又买了处更大的宅子，从前是桑德斯特德公爵（Duke of Sanderstead）家的，听说阿登家的地，明年就要拍卖了。」

===Original===
“Oh,” said Edred, clasping his hands, “if we could only find the treasure, and buy back the land! We haven’t forgotten what we said the first time: if we found the treasure we’d make all the cottages comfortable, and new thatch everywhere.”

“That’s a good lad,” said old Beale, “you make haste and find the treasure. And if you don’t find it, never fret; there’s ways of helping other folks without finding of treasure, so there is. You come and see old Beale again, my lord, and I shouldn’t wonder but what I’d have a white rabbit for you next time you come along this way.”

“He *is* an old dear,” said Elfrida, as they went home, “and I do think the films will be dry by the time we get back; but perhaps we’d better not print them till tomorrow morning.”

===Chinese===
「哦。」埃德雷德紧握着双手说，「要是咱们能找着宝藏，把地买回来就好了！咱们没忘头一回说过的话：找着了宝藏，就把家家户户的村舍都弄舒坦，处处换上新茅草顶。」

「好小子。」老比尔说，「那就快快去找宝藏吧。找不着也别愁；不靠宝藏，帮衬别人的法子也多着呢，真的。大人，您再来瞧瞧老比尔，下回您打这条道上来，我这儿要真给您备下一只白兔，我也不奇怪。」

「他*真是个*老宝贝。」回家的路上，埃尔弗丽达说，「我也真觉得，等咱们到家，胶片该晾干了；不过也许还是等到明天早上再印相的好。」

===Original===
“There’s plenty of light today,” said Edred, and Elfrida said﻿—

“I say?”

“Well?”

“Did you notice the kind of clothes we wore in those pictures﻿—where they were stowing away the treasure?”

===Chinese===
「今天光线足得很。」埃德雷德说。埃尔弗丽达说——

「我说？」

「嗯？」

「你注意到那些画面里，咱们穿的是哪种衣裳了吗——他们收藏宝的那会儿？」

===Original===
“*Oh!*” groaned Edred, recalled to a sense of his wrongs. “If only Mrs. Honeysett hadn’t opened the door just when she did, we should know exactly where the treasure was. It was the West Tower they took it to, wasn’t it?”

“I’m not sure,” said Elfrida, “but﻿—”

“And if it had gone on we *should* have been sure﻿—we should have seen them come away again.”

“Yes,” said Elfrida, and again she remarked, “I say?”

===Chinese===
「*哦！*」埃德雷德哼了一声，重新想起自己满腹的委屈。「要是哈尼塞特太太偏偏不那时候开门，咱们就能确切知道宝藏的所在了。他们是把它搬进西塔去的，对不对？」

「我说不准。」埃尔弗丽达说，「不过——」

「要是画面再演下去，咱们*就*有把握了——咱们就能看见他们从那儿出来了。」

「是啊。」埃尔弗丽达说，跟着又来了一句，「我说？」

===Original===
Edred again said, “Well﻿—?”

“Well—suppose we looked in the chests we should be sure to find clothes like *those*, and then we should be back there﻿—living in those times, and we could *see* the treasure put away, and then we really *should* know.”

“A1, first class, ripping!” was Edred’s enthusiastic rejoinder. “Come on﻿—I’ll race you to the gate.”

He did race her, and won by about thirty white Mouldiwarp’s lengths.

===Chinese===
埃德雷德又应了声：「嗯——？」

「嗯——要是咱们去翻那些箱子，准能找到*那样的*衣裳，那咱们就又回到那儿了——生活在那些年月里，就能*亲眼*看着宝藏收好藏好，那咱们可就*真的*知道了。」

「顶呱呱，头等一流，妙极了！」埃德雷德热烈地响应。「走——我跟你赛跑到大门。」

他果真跟她赛了一场，以大约三十个白鼹穆迪沃普身长的优势赢了。

===Original===
There had been no quarrel now for quite a long time﻿—if you count as time the days spent in the Gunpowder Plot adventure﻿—so the attic was easily found, and once more the children stood among the chests, with the dusty roof, and the dusty sunbeams, and the clittering pigeon feet, and the soft pigeon noises overhead.

“Come on,” cried Elfrida joyously. “I shall know the dress directly I see it. Mine was blue silk with sloping shoulders, and yours was black velvet and a Vandyke collar.”

Together they flung back the lid of a chest they had not yet opened. It held clothes far richer than any they had seen yet. The doublets and cloaks and bodices were stiff with gold embroidery and jewels. But there was no blue silk dress with sloping shoulders and no black velvet suit and Vandyke collar.

===Chinese===
他们已经好长时间没吵过架了——要是你把「火药阴谋」那场冒险里的日子也算作时间的话——所以阁楼一找就着，孩子们又一次站在那些箱子中间，头顶上是积灰的屋顶、蒙尘的日光、嗒嗒作响的鸽爪，还有鸽子咕咕的柔声。

「来呀。」埃尔弗丽达欢欢喜喜地喊。「我一见那条裙子就认得。我的是溜肩的蓝绸裙，你的是黑天鹅绒套装配范戴克领（Vandyke collar）。」

两人合力掀开一口他们还没开过的箱盖。里头的衣裳，比他们此前见过的任何衣裳都华贵得多。紧身上衣、斗篷和紧身胸衣，金绣和珠宝缀得又硬又挺。可就是没有溜肩的蓝绸裙，也没有黑天鹅绒套装和范戴克领。

===Original===
“Oh, never mind,” said Edred, bundling the splendid clothes back by double armfuls. “Help me to smooth these down so that the lid will shut properly, and we’ll try the next chest.”

But the lid would not shut at all till Elfrida had taken all the things out and folded them properly, and then it shut quite easily.

Then they went on to the next chest.

===Chinese===
「哦，没关系。」埃德雷德说，双臂一抱一抱地把华服往回塞。「帮我压平这些，好让箱盖盖严实，咱们再试下一口。」

可箱盖怎么也合不上，直到埃尔弗丽达把东西统统搬出来，一件一件叠齐整了，它才轻轻巧巧地合上了。

然后他们接着去开下一口箱子。

===Original===
“I have a magic inside feeling that they’re in *this* one,” said Elfrida gaily. And so they may have been. The children never knew﻿—for the next chest was *locked*, and the utmost efforts of four small arms failed to move the lid a hair’s breadth.

“Oh, bother!” said Edred; “we’ll try the next.”

But the next was locked, too﻿—and the next, and the one after that, and the one beyond, and﻿—Well, the fact is, they were *all* locked.

===Chinese===
「我心里有一种魔法般的感觉，衣裳就在*这口*箱子里。」埃尔弗丽达快活地说。兴许真在里头。孩子们永远无从知道了——因为下一口箱子是*锁着的*，四条小胳膊使出浑身解数，也没能让箱盖挪动一根头发丝的宽度。

「哦，讨厌！」埃德雷德说；「咱们试下一口。」

可下一口也是锁着的——再下一口，再再下一口，再再再下一口，都锁着——唉，实话实说，它们*全*锁着。

===Original===
The children looked at each other in something quite like despair.

“I feel,” said the boy, “like a baffled burglar.”

“I feel,” said the girl, “as if I was just going to understand something. Oh, wait a minute; it’s coming. I think,” she added very slowly﻿—“I think it means if we go anywhere we’ve got to go wherever it was they wore those glorious stiff gold clothes. That’s what the chest’s open for; that’s what the others are locked for. See?”

===Chinese===
两个孩子面面相觑，神情已经近乎绝望。

「我觉得，」男孩说，「自己像个撬不开锁的小贼。」

「我觉得，」女孩说，「自己眼看就要明白点什么了。哦，等一等；来了。我想，」她非常慢地补上一句——「我想，这是说，咱们不管去哪儿，都得去他们穿那种金光挺括的华服的地方和年月。那口箱子为这个才敞着；其余的为这个才锁着。明白吗？」

===Original===
“Then let’s put them on and go,” said Edred.

“I don’t think I want any more Tower of Londons,” said Elfrida doubtfully.

“I don’t mind what it is,” said Edred. “I’ve found out one thing. We always come safe out of it, whatever it is. And besides,” he added, remembering many talks with his good friend, Sir Walter Raleigh, “an English gentleman must be afraid of nothing save God and his conscience.”

“All right,” said Elfrida, laying hands on the chest-lid that hid the golden splendour. “You might help,” she said.

===Chinese===
「那就快穿上出发。」埃德雷德说。

「我可不想再来一回伦敦塔了。」埃尔弗丽达将信将疑地说。

「去哪儿我都不在乎。」埃德雷德说。「我弄明白了一件事。不管遇上什么，咱们总能平安脱身。再说，」他想着同他的好朋友沃尔特·雷利爵士（Sir Walter Raleigh）多次的交谈，又补了一句，「英国绅士除了上帝和自己的良心，什么都不应当怕。」

「好吧。」埃尔弗丽达说着，把双手按上那遮着满箱金辉的箱盖。「你也搭把手。」她说。

===Original===
But Edred couldn’t. He laid hands on the chest, of course, and he pulled and Elfrida pulled, but the chest-lid was as fast now as any of the others.

“Done in the eye!” said Edred. It was a very vulgar expression, and I can’t think where he picked it up.

> “ ‘He that will not when he may,
>  He shall not when he would﻿—a,’”

===Chinese===
可埃德雷德帮不上。他自然也把手按了上去，他拉，埃尔弗丽达也拉，可这箱盖如今同别的任何一口一样，纹丝不动。

「上了大当啦！」埃德雷德说。这话非常粗俗，我想不出他是从哪儿学来的。

> 「『他能有时不肯干，
>  他想干时已枉然——啊，』」

===Original===
said Elfrida﻿—and I do know where she learned that. It was from an old song Mrs. Honeysett used to sing when she blackleaded the stoves.

“I suppose we must chuck it for today,” said Edred, when he had quite hurt his fingers by trying all the chests once more, and had found that every single one was shut tight as wax. “Come on﻿—we’ll print the photographs.”

But the films were not dry enough. They never are when you just expect them to be; so they locked the still-room door on the outside, and hung the key on a nail high up in the kitchen chimney. Mrs. Honeysett was not in the kitchen at that moment, but she came hurrying in the next.

===Chinese===
埃尔弗丽达念道——这个，我倒知道她是从哪儿学来的。是哈尼塞特太太给炉子刷黑铅的时候，常唱的一支老歌。

「我看今天只好作罢。」埃德雷德说。这时他又把所有的箱子挨个拉了一遍，指头都拉疼了，发现每一口都封得跟蜡一样严实。「走——咱们印照片去。」

可胶片干得还不够透。胶片这东西，你越是指望它干，它越是不干；于是他们从外面锁上配膳室的门，把钥匙挂上厨房烟囱高处的一枚钉子。那一刻哈尼塞特太太不在厨房，可紧接着就急忙忙地进来了。

===Original===
“Here you are, my lambs,” she said cheerily, “and just in time for the surprise.”

“Oh, I’d forgotten the surprise. That makes two of it, doesn’t it?” said Elfrida. “Do tell us what it is. We need a nice surprise to make up for everything, if you only knew.”

“Ah,” said Mrs. Honeysett, “you mean because of me opening that there door. Well, there *is* two surprises. One’s roast chicken. For *supper*,” she added impressively.

===Chinese===
「来啦，我的小羊羔们。」她欢欢喜喜地说，「来得正是时候，赶上惊喜啦。」

「哦，我把惊喜给忘了。这下好事成双了，是不是？」埃尔弗丽达说。「快告诉我们是什么吧。您要是知道就好啦，我们正需要个好惊喜，把这一切都补回来。」

「啊。」哈尼塞特太太说，「你们是说为我开那扇门的事。好吧，惊喜*倒真有*两个。一个是烤鸡。*晚饭吃的*。」她郑重其事地补了一句。

===Original===
“Then I know the other,” said Edred. “Aunt Edith’s coming.”

And she was﻿—indeed, at that very moment, as they looked through the window, they saw her blue dress coming over the hill, and joyously tore out to meet her.

It was after the roast chicken, when it was nearly dark and almost bedtime, that Aunt Edith said, suddenly﻿—

===Chinese===
「那另一个我知道了。」埃德雷德说。「伊迪丝姑姑要来了。」

她果然要来——真的，正在那一刻，他们望出窗外，看见她的蓝裙子正翻过山冈，便欢天喜地地冲出去迎接她。

吃过烤鸡之后，天色将黑、快到睡觉的时候，伊迪丝姑姑忽然说——

===Original===
“Children, there’s something I wanted to tell you. I’ve hesitated about it a good deal, but I think we oughtn’t to have any secrets from each other.”

Edred and Elfrida exchanged guilty glances.

“Not *real* secrets, of course,” said Edred, hastily; “but you don’t mind our having magic secrets, do you?”

“Of course not,” said Aunt Edith, smiling; “and what I’m going to tell you is rather like magic﻿—if it’s true. I don’t know yet whether it’s true or not.”

===Chinese===
「孩子们，有件事我一直想告诉你们。我犹豫了好半天，可我想，咱们彼此之间不该有秘密。」

埃德雷德和埃尔弗丽达心虚地交换了一个眼色。

「当然不是*真的*秘密。」埃德雷德急忙说；「不过我们有几个魔法秘密，您不介意吧？」

「当然不介意。」伊迪丝姑姑微笑着说；「我要告诉你们的事，就有点像魔法——倘若它是真的。我还不知道它到底是不是真的。」

===Original===
Here Aunt Edith put an arm round each of the children as they sat on the broad window-seat, and swallowed something in her throat and sniffed.

“Oh, it’s *not* bad news, is it?” Elfrida cried. “Oh, darling auntie, don’t be miserable, and don’t say that they’ve found out that Arden isn’t ours, or that Edred isn’t really Lord Arden, or something.”

“Would you mind so very much,” said Aunt Edith gently, “if you weren’t Lord Arden, Edred? Because﻿—”

===Chinese===
这时姐弟俩正坐在宽敞的窗座上，伊迪丝姑姑一手一个搂住他们，喉咙里咽下了什么，抽了抽鼻子。

「哦，*不是*坏消息吧，是不是？」埃尔弗丽达喊道。「哦，亲爱的姑姑，别难过，可别说人家查出阿登家不是咱们的，或是埃德雷德其实不是阿登勋爵，或是别的什么。」

「假如你不是阿登勋爵，你会不会非常非常在意呢，埃德雷德？」伊迪丝姑姑温柔地说，「因为——」

## XII Films and Clouds / 胶片与云

===Original===
The films were quite dry by bedtime, when, after a delightful evening with no magic in it at all but the magic of undisturbed jolliness, Edred slipped away, unpinned them and hid them in Elfrida’s corner drawer, which he rightly judged to be a cleaner resting-place for them than his own was likely to be. So there the precious films lay between Elfrida’s best lace collar and the handkerchief-case with three fat buttercups embroidered on it that Aunt Edith had given her at Christmas. And Edred went back to the parlour for one last game of Proverbs before bed. As he took up his cards he thought how strange it was that he, who had been imprisoned in the Tower and had talked with Sir Walter Raleigh, should be sitting there quietly playing Proverbs with his aunt and his sister, just like any other little boy.

“Aha!” said Edred to himself, “I am living a double life, that’s what I’m doing.”

He had seen the expression in a book and the idea charmed him.

===Chinese===
到上床的时候，胶片已经干透了。那个晚上过得实在愉快，内中毫无魔法，只有不受搅扰的欢乐自具的魔法；就在这时分，埃德雷德悄悄溜了出去，起下胶片上的大头针，把它们藏进埃尔弗丽达墙角的抽屉里——他没估错，那地方确实比他自己的抽屉干净，正合给胶片安身。于是这几张宝贵的胶片便躺在埃尔弗丽达最好的花边衣领和那只手绢袋之间——袋上绣着三朵肥嘟嘟的毛茛花，是伊迪丝姑姑圣诞节送她的。埃德雷德则回到客厅，睡前再玩最后一局「谚语」（Proverbs）牌。他拾起牌，心里想这有多奇怪：他这个人，蹲过伦敦塔的牢，同沃尔特·雷利爵士（Sir Walter Raleigh）交谈过，此刻竟安安静静坐在这儿，跟姑姑和姐姐玩「谚语」牌，活脱脱就是个寻常的小男孩。

「啊哈！」埃德雷德心里说，「我过的是双重生活，我干的就是这个。」

这说法是他从一本书上看来的，这念头叫他着迷。

===Original===
“How pleased Edred looks with himself!” said Aunt Edith; “I’m sure he’s got a whole proverb, or nearly, in his hand already.”

“You’ll be looking pleased presently,” he said; “you always win.”

And win she did, for Edred’s thoughts were wandering off after the idea how pleased Aunt Edith would look when he and Elfrida should come to her, take her by the hand, and lead her to the hiding-place of the treasure, and then say, “Behold the treasure of our house! Now we can rebuild the castle and mend the broken thatch on the cottages, and I can go to Eton and Oxford, and you can have a diamond tiara, and Elfrida can have a pony to ride, and so can I.”

Elfrida’s thoughts were not unlike his﻿—so Aunt Edith won the game of Proverbs.

===Chinese===
「埃德雷德一副自鸣得意的样子！」伊迪丝姑姑说；「我敢说，他手里已经攥着一整句谚语了，起码也差不离。」

「待会儿得意的就是您啦。」他说；「回回都是您赢。」

她也果然赢了，因为埃德雷德的心思早已溜到别处去了，正一路想着：等他和埃尔弗丽达走到姑姑跟前，拉起她的手，把她领到宝藏藏放的地方，然后说：「看哪，我们家的宝藏！如今咱们可以重建城堡，补好茅屋上破了的草顶，我可以进伊顿和牛津，您可以有一顶钻石头冠，埃尔弗丽达可以有一匹小马骑，我也一样」——那时候伊迪丝姑姑脸上该多么得意呢。

埃尔弗丽达想的心事同他的大同小异——于是这局「谚语」牌又是伊迪丝姑姑赢了。

===Original===
“You have been very good children, Mrs. Honeysett tells me,” said Aunt Edith, putting the cards together.

“Not so extra,” said Edred; “I mean it’s easy to be good when everything’s so jolly.”

“We have quarrelled once or twice, you know,” said Elfrida virtuously.

“Yes, we have,” said Edred firmly.

They needn’t, they felt, have confessed this﻿—and that made them feel that they were good now, if never before.

“Well, don’t quarrel any more. I shall be coming over for good quite soon, then we’ll have glorious times. Perhaps we’ll find the treasure. You’ve heard about the treasure?”

===Chinese===
「哈尼塞特太太（Mrs. Honeysett）告诉我，你们一直是顶乖的孩子。」伊迪丝姑姑说着，把牌收拢到一处。

「也没多乖。」埃德雷德说；「我是说，样样事都这么痛快的时候，乖是很容易的。」

「不过我们也吵过一两回架，您知道。」埃尔弗丽达一脸贤惠地说。

「是的，我们吵过。」埃德雷德斩钉截铁地说。

他们自觉这层本来大可不必招认——而正因招认了，他们便觉得如今自己算是乖的了，哪怕从前从来不曾乖过。

「好了，别再吵架了。我不久就要长住过来了，那时咱们要过快活日子了。说不定咱们还能找到宝藏呢。宝藏的事你们听说过吧？」

===Original===
“I should jolly well think we had,” Edred couldn’t help saying. And Elfrida added﻿—

“And looked for it, too﻿—but we haven’t found it. Did *you* ever look for it?”

“No,” said Aunt Edith, “but I always wanted to. My grandfather used to look for it when he was a little boy.”

“Was your grandfather Lord Arden?” Edred asked.

“No; he was the grandson of the Lord Arden who fought for King James the Third, as they called him﻿—the Pretender, you know﻿—when he was quite a boy. And they let him off because of his being so young. And then he mortgaged all the Arden lands to keep the Young Pretender﻿—Prince Charlie, you know, in the ballads. He got money to send to him, and of course Prince Charlie was going to pay it back when he was king. Only he never *was* king,” she sighed.

“And is that why the Tallow King got all the Arden land?”

“Yes, dear﻿—that’s why English people prefer Tallow kings to Stuart kings. And old Lord Arden mortgaged everything. That means he borrowed money, and if he didn’t pay back the money by a certain time he agreed to let them take the land instead. And he couldn’t pay; so they took the land﻿—all except a bit in the village and Arden Knoll﻿—that was fixed so that he couldn’t part from it.”

===Chinese===
「那还用说！」埃德雷德忍不住说。埃尔弗丽达又补上一句——

「还找过呢——可没找着。*您*找过它吗？」

「没有，」伊迪丝姑姑说，「可我一直想找来着。我祖父小时候就常找它。」

「您祖父是阿登勋爵吗？」埃德雷德问。

「不是；他是那位阿登勋爵的孙子。那位勋爵还是个十足的孩子时，就替『詹姆斯三世』效过力——人家那么叫他，其实就是『老王位觊觎者』，你知道的——因为他年纪那么小，人家便饶过了他。后来他把阿登家的田产统统抵押出去，供养『小王位觊觎者』——就是歌谣里的查理王子。他张罗了钱给他送去，当然啦，查理王子原是要等做了国王就还的。只可惜他*从来*没当上国王。」她叹了口气。

「所以牛脂大王才把阿登家的地全弄到手的吗？」

「是的，亲爱的——所以英国人才宁要牛脂大王，不要斯图亚特王啊。老阿登勋爵把一切都抵押了出去。抵押的意思是：他借了钱，要是到约定的期限还不上，就答应让人家拿地抵数。他还不上；于是人家把地拿走了——只剩下村里一小块和阿登丘——那是定死了归他、不许他脱手的。」

===Original===
“When we get the treasure we’ll buy back the land again,” said Edred. “The Tallow King’s going to sell it. He’s got so tallowy that Arden land isn’t good enough for him. Old Beale told us. And, I say, Auntie, we’ll rebuild the castle, too, won’t we, and mend the holes in the thatch﻿—where the rain comes in﻿—in people’s cottages, I mean.”

“Have you been much into people’s cottages?” Aunt Edith asked anxiously﻿—with the strange fear of infection which seems a part of a grownup’s nature.

“Everyone in the village, I think,” said Elfrida cheerfully. “Old Beale told us we ought to﻿—in case we found the treasure﻿—so as to know what to do. The people are such dears. I believe they like us because we’re Ardens. Or is it because Edred’s a lord?”

“We *must* find the treasure,” said Edred, looking as he always did when he was very much in earnest, so like his lost father that Aunt Edith could hardly bear it﻿—“so as to be able to look after our people properly.”

“And to kick out the Tallow King,” said Elfrida.

“But you won’t be discontented if you *don’t* find it,” said Aunt Edith. “It’s only a sort of game really. No one *I* ever knew ever found a treasure. And think what we’ve found already! Arden Castle instead of Sea View Terrace﻿—and the lodgers. Good night, chicks.”

===Chinese===
「等咱们得到宝藏，就把地再买回来。」埃德雷德说。「牛脂大王要卖地了。他熬得浑身牛脂气，阿登家的地已经配不上他啦。老比尔告诉我们的。还有，我说姑姑，咱们还要把城堡重建起来，对吧，还要补茅屋顶上的窟窿——就是漏雨的那些——我是说，补人家茅屋上的。」

「你们常到人家的茅屋里去吗？」伊迪丝姑姑不安地问——带着那种莫名其妙的怕沾染什么的恐惧，这恐惧仿佛是大人天性的一部分。

「我看村里家家都去过了。」埃尔弗丽达快快活活地说。「老比尔说我们应当去——万一咱们找到了宝藏——好知道该做些什么。那些人真是可亲可爱。我相信他们喜欢咱们，因为咱们是阿登家的人。要不，是因为埃德雷德是个勋爵？」

「咱们*一定*要找到宝藏。」埃德雷德说。他每逢动了真格的，脸上就是这副神情，像他那位失去的父亲像得伊迪丝姑姑几乎看不下去——「好让咱们能好好地照管咱们的百姓。」

「还要把牛脂大王一脚踢出去。」埃尔弗丽达说。

「可要是*找不着*，你们也不许不痛快。」伊迪丝姑姑说。「这说到底不过是场游戏。我认得的人里，从来没有人真找到过宝藏。再想想咱们已经得着了什么！有了阿登城堡，替代了海景排屋——和房客们。晚安，小鸡们。」

===Original===
She was gone before they were up in the morning, and the morning’s first business was the printing of the photographs.

They printed them in the kitchen, because Mrs. Honeysett was turning out the parlour, and besides the kitchen window was wide and sunny, and the old table, scoured again and again till the grain of the wood stood up in ridges, was a nice, big, clear place to stand toning dishes on. They printed on matt paper, because it seemed somehow less common, and more like a picture than the shiny kind. The printing took the whole morning, and they had only one frame. And when they had done there were eighteen brown prints of the castle from all sorts of points of the compass﻿—north and south and﻿—but I explained all this to you before. When the prints were dried﻿—which, as you know, is best done by sticking them up on the windows﻿—it became necessary to find a place to put them in. One could not gloat over them forever, though for quite a long time it seemed better to look at them again and again, and to say, “That’s how it ought to be﻿—that’s the way we’ll have it,” than to do anything else.

===Chinese===
第二天早上他们还没起床，她已经走了；而早晨的头一桩大事，就是印照片。

他们在厨房里印，因为哈尼塞特太太正在彻底打扫客厅，再说厨房的窗子又宽敞又向阳；那张老桌子刷了一遍又一遍，刷得木纹都隆起成一道道棱，正是一块又大又清爽的好地方，好摆那些调色盘。他们印在无光相纸上，因为不知怎么，无光的似乎不那么俗气，比亮光的那种更像画。印相占去了整整一个上午，他们只有一副印相框。印完之后，一共得了十八张棕褐色的城堡相片，四面八方各种方位拍的——北边、南边，还有——不过这些我从前都给你解释过了。相片晾干之后——你知道，晾干它们最好的法子是把它们贴在窗上——就得找个地方安置它们了。人总不能没完没了地对着它们饱看不厌，虽说有那么好一阵子，翻来覆去地看它们，一面说「就该是这个样子——咱们就要弄成这样」，似乎比干什么别的都强。

===Original===
Elfrida and Edred took the prints into the parlour, which was now neat as a new pin, and smelt almost *too* much of beeswax and turpentine, spread them on the polished oval dining-table and gloated over them.

“You can see every little bit exactly right,” said Elfrida. “They’re a little tiny bit muzzy. I expect our distance wasn’t right or something, but that only makes them look more like real pictures, and us having printed them on paper that’s too big makes it more pictury too. And anyone who knew about how buildings are built would know how to set it up. It would be like putting the bricks back into the box from the pattern inside the lid.”

Here Mrs. Honeysett called from the kitchen, “You done with all this litter?” and both children shouted “Yes!” and went on looking at the pictures. It was well that the shout was from both. If only one had done it there might have been what Mrs. Honeysett called “words” about the matter later; for next moment both said, “The films!” and rushed to the kitchen﻿—just in time to see the kitchen fire enlivened by that peculiar crackling flare which fire and films alone can produce. Mrs. Honeysett had thrown the films on the fire with the other “litter,” and it was no one’s fault but the children’s, as Mrs. Honeysett pointed out.

===Chinese===
埃尔弗丽达和埃德雷德把相片搬进客厅——这会儿客厅拾掇得崭新锃亮，一股蜂蜡和松节油的气味，浓得几乎*过分*了——摊在擦得发亮的椭圆形餐桌上，饱看个不够。

「每一小块都看得清清楚楚、原原本本。」埃尔弗丽达说。「就是有一丁点儿模糊。我估计是咱们的距离没量准还是怎么的，可这反倒叫它们更像真正的画了，再说咱们把像印在了太大的纸上，也添了几分画意。懂房屋盖造的人一看就知道该怎么把它搭起来。就像照盒盖内侧印着的图样，把积木一块块装回盒里去。」

这时哈尼塞特太太在厨房里喊：「这些破烂儿你们使完了没有？」两个孩子齐声喊「使完了！」便接着看相片。幸亏是齐声喊的。倘若只有一个应了，回头这事就要出哈尼塞特太太所谓的「口角」了；因为紧接着两人同时喊出：「胶片！」冲进厨房——正赶上看见厨房的火兴旺起来，腾起那种特别的噼啪的亮焰，那是火和胶片凑在一处才有的光景。哈尼塞特太太已经把胶片连同别的「破烂儿」一块儿扔进了火里，而正如她指出的，这要怪也只能怪孩子们自己。

===Original===
“I ask you if you done with it all, an’ you says ‘Yes’﻿—only yourselves to thank,” she repeated again and again amid their lamentations, and they had to own that she was right.

“We must take extra special care of the prints, that’s all,” said Edred, and the *History of the Ardens* was chosen as a hiding-place both safe and appropriate.

“It doesn’t matter so much about the films,” said Elfrida, “because we could never have shown them to anyone. If we find the treasure we’ll arrange for Auntie to find these prints﻿—leave the History about or something﻿—and she’ll think they’re photographs of painted pictures. So *that’ll* be all right.”

===Chinese===
「我问你们使完没有，你们说『使完了』——怪不得别人。」在两人的悲叹声中她把这话念叨了一遍又一遍，两人也只好承认她说得对。

「咱们只消对这些相片格外加意小心就是了。」埃德雷德说。于是《阿登家史》被选作了藏匿之所，既稳妥又再合适不过。

「胶片倒不很要紧。」埃尔弗丽达说，「反正咱们本来也没法拿给谁看。要是咱们找到了宝藏，咱们就安排让姑姑发现这些相片——把《家史》摊在哪儿之类的——她会当它们是画下来的画的照片。这样*就*没事了。」

===Original===
As they arranged the prints between the leaves of the History Elfrida’s eye was caught by the words “moat” and “water-supply,” and she read on and turned the page.

“Don’t stop to read,” said Edred, but she waved him away.

“I say, listen,” she said, turning back; and she read﻿—

===Chinese===
两人把相片夹进《阿登家史》的书页间时，埃尔弗丽达的目光被「护城河」和「供水」两个词勾住了，她一路读下去，又翻过一页。

「别停下来看书。」埃德雷德说，可她挥手叫他走开。

「我说，你听着。」她说着又翻回来；她念道——

===Original===
“ ‘In ancient times Arden Castle was surrounded by a moat. The original architects of the venerable pile, with that ingenuity whose fruits the thinking world so much admires in the lasting monuments of their labours, diverted from its subterraneous course a stream which rose through the chalk in the hills of the vicinity, and is said to debouch into the sea about fifty yards below high-water mark. The engineering works necessary for this triumph of mind over matter endured till 1647, when the castle was besieged by the troops of that monster in human form Oliver Cromwell. To facilitate his attack on the castle the officer in command gave orders that the stream should be diverted once more into its original channel. This order was accordingly executed by his myrmidons, and the moat was left dry, this assisting materially the treacherous designs of the detestable regicides. It is rumoured that the stream, despite the lapse of centuries, still maintains its subterranean course; but the present author, on visiting, during the autumn of 1821, the residence of the present Earl of Arden, and by his permission, most courteously granted, exploring the site thoroughly, was unable to find any trace of its existence. The rural denizens of the district denied any knowledge of such a stream, but they are sunk in ignorance and superstition, and have no admiration for the works of philosophy or the awe-inspiring beauties of Nature.’ ”

===Chinese===
「『古时候，阿登城堡四周环着一条护城河。这座令人肃然起敬的宏伟建筑的原始设计者们，怀着那种巧思——其硕果，思想界至今仍在他们传世的工程纪念物中备极叹赏——把一道溪流从它的地下河道引开；此溪出自邻近山间的白垩地层，据说在满潮线以下约五十码处注入大海。此一心智战胜物质之伟业所必需的工程设施，一直存续到 1647 年；那一年，城堡遭到那个披人皮的怪物奥利弗·克伦威尔的部队围困。为便于攻城，指挥官下令把溪流重新引回原河道。这道命令由他的爪牙们如法执行，护城河从此干涸，大有助于那伙可憎的弑君者的奸谋。风闻此溪虽历经数百年，至今仍循地下河道而行；然本作者于 1821 年秋造访当今阿登伯爵府邸，承伯爵慨然俯允，将该址彻底查勘一过，竟未能觅得此溪存在之任何痕迹。该地之乡野居民矢口否认知有此溪；然彼辈沉溺于愚昧与迷信，于哲思之作品暨大自然之庄严壮美，俱无景仰之心。』」

===Original===
“What a dull chap he is!” said Edred. “But, I say, when was it printed﻿—1822?﻿ ﻿… I believe I know why the rural What’s-his-names wouldn’t let on about the stream. Don’t you see, it’s the stream that runs through the smugglers’ cave? and they were smuggling then for all they were worth.”

“That’s clever of you,” said Elfrida.

“Well, I bet we find traces of its existence, when we’ve found the treasure. Come on; let’s try the chests again. We’ll put on the first things we find, and chance it, this time. There’s nothing to stop us. We haven’t quarrelled or anything.”

===Chinese===
「这人写得可真乏味！」埃德雷德说。「不过，我说，这书是什么时候印的来着——1822 年？……我明白那些乡下的叫不上名儿的家伙为什么不肯说溪流的事了。你还不明白吗，从走私贩子山洞里流过的就是这条溪呀！那时候他们正拼着命地走私呢。」

「你这一下真聪明。」埃尔弗丽达说。

「等着吧，等咱们找到了宝藏，我打赌准能找到这溪流还在的痕迹。走；再去试试那些箱子。这回咱们抓着什么衣裳就穿什么，碰碰运气。没有什么拦着咱们。咱们也没吵架什么的。」

===Original===
They had not quarrelled, but there was something to stop them, all the same. And that something was the fact that they could not find The Door. It simply was not there.

“And we haven’t quarrelled or anything,” said Elfrida, despairing when they had searched the East House again and again, and found no door that would consent to lead them to the wonderful attic where the chests stood in their two wonderful rows. She sat down on the top step of the attic stairs, quite regardless of the dust that lay there thick.

“It’s all up﻿—I can see that,” said Edred. “We’ve muffed it somehow. I wonder whether we oughtn’t to have taken those photographs.”

===Chinese===
他们是没吵架，可到底还是有东西拦着他们。那东西就是：他们找不到那扇门。它干脆就是不在那儿。

「咱们也没吵架什么的呀。」埃尔弗丽达绝望地说。他们把东屋找了一遍又一遍，也没找到一扇肯领他们去那奇妙阁楼的门——阁楼里，那些箱子排成奇妙的两行。她在阁楼楼梯最上面一级坐下来，全然不顾那上面积得厚厚的灰尘。

「全完了——我看得出来。」埃德雷德说。「不知怎么的，咱们把这事搞砸了。我不知道，咱们当初是不是压根儿就不该拍那些照片。」

===Original===
“Do you think perhaps﻿ ﻿… *could* we have dreamed it all?”

“*No*,” said Edred, “there *are* the prints﻿—at least, I suppose they’re there. We’ll go down and see.”

Miserably doubting, they went down and saw that the photographs were where they had put them, in between the pages of the *History of Arden*.

“I don’t see *what* we can do. Do you?” said Edred forlornly. It was a miserable ending to the happenings that had succeeded each other in such a lively procession ever since they had been at Arden. It seemed as though a door had been shut in their faces, and “Not any more,” written in very plain letters across the chapter of their adventures.

===Chinese===
「你想，会不会……咱们兴许是把这一切全*梦*出来的？」

「*不*，」埃德雷德说，「相片*就*在那儿——起码，我琢磨着在。咱们下去瞧瞧。」

两人满腹疑虑地下了楼，看见那些照片果然还在他们放的地方，夹在《阿登家史》的书页中间。

「我看不出咱们*还能*有什么办法。你呢？」埃德雷德凄凄惶惶地说。自从来到阿登，一桩桩趣事本是排着轻快的队伍接连而来的，如今却落了这么个惨淡的收场。仿佛有一扇门当着他们的面砰地关上，他们那部历险记的这一章上，用再明白不过的字迹写着：「到此为止。」

===Original===
“I wish we could find the witch again,” said Elfrida; “but she said she couldn’t come into these times more than once.”

“I wonder why,” said Edred, kicking his boots miserably against the leg of the table on which he sat. “That Dicky chap must have been here pretty often, to have an address at New Cross. I say, suppose we wrote to him. It would be something to do.”

So they wrote. At least Elfrida did, and they both signed it. This was the letter:﻿—

===Chinese===
「真希望咱们能再找到那位女巫。」埃尔弗丽达说；「可她说过，她只能到这些年代来一回。」

「这是为什么呢。」埃德雷德说，一面坐在桌上，可怜巴巴地拿靴子一下下磕着桌腿。「那个小迪克准是常到这儿来，在新十字（New Cross）还落了个通信地址呢。我说，咱们干脆给他写信吧。也好算件事做。」

于是他们写了。至少是埃尔弗丽达执的笔，两人一起署的名。信是这样写的：——

===Original===
> Dear Cousin Richard﻿—You remember meeting us at the Gunpowder Plot. If you are at these modern times again we should like to know you and to know how you get into the future. Perhaps we could get into the past the same way, because the way we used to get we can’t any more.
> Perhaps you could come here next time instead of New Cross.
> Your affectionate friends at a distance,
> (Miss) Elfrida Arden
> (Lord) Edred Arden.
> P.S.﻿—I don’t know how lords sign letters because I have not been it long, but you’ll know who it is.
> P.S.S.﻿—Remember old Parrot-nose.

===Chinese===
> 亲爱的理查德表哥（Cousin Richard）——你可记得在火药阴谋那回见过我们。要是你又到了这些现代年头，我们想同你结识，也想知道你是怎样进入未来的。也许咱们能走同一条道进入过去，因为咱们从前走的那个道，如今走不成了。
> 也许下回你能上我们这儿来，别在新十字了。
> 你们远方的亲爱的朋友
> （小姐）埃尔弗丽达·阿登
> （勋爵）埃德雷德·阿登。
> 又及——我不知道勋爵在信上怎么署名，因为我当勋爵的日子还不长，不过你知道这是谁。
> 再及——记得老鹦鹉鼻子。

===Original===
They walked down to the post with this, and as they went they remembered how they had gone to the “George” with old Lady Arden’s letter in Boney’s time; and Edred remarked, listlessly, that it would be rather fun to find the smugglers’ cave. So when they had bought a stamp and licked it and put it on the letter they went up on the cliff and looked among the furze-bushes for the entrance to the smugglers’ cave. But they did not find it. Nothing makes you hotter than looking for things that you can’t find﻿—and there is no hotter place to look for things than a furze forest on the downs on a sunny summer afternoon. The children were glad to sit down on a clean, smooth, grassy space and look out at the faint blue line of the sea.

===Chinese===
两人揣着这封信一路走到邮局去；走着走着，他们想起在波尼（Boney，当年英国人对拿破仑的谑称）那阵子，他们曾带着老阿登夫人的信上「乔治」客栈去；埃德雷德有气无力地说，去找找走私贩子的山洞倒也挺好玩。于是买了邮票、舔湿、贴上信封之后，他们爬上悬崖，在荆豆丛里寻摸走私贩子山洞的洞口。可是没有找到。天下再没有比找一件找不着的东西更叫人浑身发热的了——而要找东西，再没有比晴朗夏日午后丘原上的荆豆丛更热的地方了。两个孩子乐得在一块干净平整的草地上坐下，望着远方那一线淡淡的海色。

===Original===
They had not really enjoyed looking for the smugglers’ cave. Vain regrets were busy in each breast. Edred gave voice to them when he said﻿—

“Oh, if only we had put those gold clothes on when we had the chance!”

And Elfrida echoed the useless heartfelt wail with, “Oh, if we only had!”

===Chinese===
找山洞这件事，他们其实并没有尝到多少乐趣。徒然的悔恨在两人心头忙个不停。埃德雷德把它说了出来——

「唉，当初有机会的时候，咱们要是把那些金衣裳穿上就好了！」

埃尔弗丽达也应和着这发自衷肠而无济于事的哀叹：「唉，要是穿了多好！」

===Original===
And then they sat in silence and looked at the sea for quite a long time.

Now, if you sit perfectly silent for a long time and look at the sea, or the sky, or the running water of a river, something happens to you﻿—a sort of magic. Not the violent magic that makes the kind of adventures that I have been telling you about, but a kind of gentle but very strong *inside* magic, that makes things clear, and shows you what things are important, and what are not. You try it next time you are in a very bad temper, or when you think someone has been very unjust to you, or when you are very disappointed and hurt about anything.

===Chinese===
然后他们默默坐了很久，望着大海。

要知道，倘若你安安静静坐上好久，望着大海，或者天空，或者河里的流水，你身上就会起一种变化——一种魔法。不是那种闹得惊天动地、造出我一路讲给你听的这类冒险的魔法，而是一种温和却非常有力的*内心*魔法，它让事情变得清楚，让你看清哪些事要紧，哪些不要紧。下回你大发脾气的时候，或是觉得有人待你极不公道的时候，或是为什么事非常失望、非常伤心的时候，不妨试一试。

===Original===
The magic worked in Edred and Elfrida till Edred said﻿—

“After all, we’ve got the castle;” and Elfrida said﻿—

“And we have had some ripping times.”

And then they looked at the sea in more silence, during which Hope came and whispered to Elfrida, who instantly said﻿—

“The Mouldiwarp! Perhaps it’s not all over. It told us to find the door. And we did find the door. Perhaps it would tell us something new if we called it now﻿—and if it came.”

“And if it came,” said Edred.

===Chinese===
这魔法在埃德雷德和埃尔弗丽达身上起了作用，直到埃德雷德说——

「不管怎么说，咱们还有城堡；」埃尔弗丽达则说——

「咱们也过过些痛快的日子。」

接着他们越发沉默地望着海，就在这沉默里，希望来对埃尔弗丽达附耳低语，她立刻说——

「白鼹穆迪沃普（Mouldiwarp）！说不定还没有全完。它叫咱们找那扇门。咱们也真找到了门。要是咱们现在召唤它——要是它肯来——说不定它会告诉咱们点新鲜事儿。」

「要是它肯来。」埃德雷德说。

===Original===
“Don’t talk﻿—make poetry,” said Elfrida. But that was one of the things that Edred never could do. Trying to make poetry was, to him, like trying to remember a name you have never heard, or to multiply a number that you’ve forgotten by another number that you don’t recollect.

But Elfrida, that youthful poet, frowned and bit her lips and twisted her hands, and reached out in her mind to words that she just couldn’t quite think of, till the words grew tame and flew within reach, and she caught them and caged them behind the bars of rhyme. This was her poem﻿—

===Chinese===
「别说话——做诗。」埃尔弗丽达说。可这是埃德雷德永远办不到的事。对他来说，试着做诗，就像试着记起一个你从没听过的名字，或是用一个你忘掉的数去乘另一个你想不起来的数。

可埃尔弗丽达，这位少年诗人，皱起眉头，咬着嘴唇，绞着双手，在心里向那些怎么也想不大起来的字句伸出手去，直到字句渐渐驯服，飞进够得着的范围，她把它们捉住，关进韵脚的栅栏里。她的诗是这样的——

===Original===
> “Dear Mouldiwarp, do come if you can,
 

 And tell us if there is any plan
 

 That you can tell us of for us two
 

 To get into the past like we used to do.
 

 Dear Mouldiwarp, we don’t want to worry
 

 You﻿—but we *are* in a frightful hurry.”

===Chinese===
> 「亲爱的穆迪沃普，能来就请来，
> 告诉我们你可有什么好安排——
> 有什么法子让你我这一双，
> 像从前一样回到旧时光。
> 亲爱的穆迪沃普，我们不想搅扰
> 你——可我们*真是*急得受不了。」

===Original===
“So you be always,” said the white Mouldiwarp, suddenly appearing between them on the yellowy dry grass. “Well, well! Youth’s the season for silliness. What’s to do now? I be turble tired of all this. I wish I’d only got to give ye the treasure and go my ways. You don’t give a poor Mouldiwarp a minute’s rest. You do terrify me same’s flies, you do.”

“Is there any other way,” said Elfrida, “to get back into the past? We can’t find the door now.”

“Course you can’t,” said the mole. “That’s a chance gone, and gone forever.

> “ ‘He that will not when he may,
 

 He shall not when he would-a.’

Well, tell me where you want to go, and I’ll make you a backways-working white clock.”

===Chinese===
「你们就老是这么急。」白鼹穆迪沃普说着，忽然在两人中间发黄的干草上现了形。「嗳，嗳！年轻嘛，正是犯傻的季节。这会儿又出了什么事？俺是烦透这一切咧。俺倒巴不得只消把宝藏交给你们，俺就好走俺自己的路了。你们不给可怜的穆迪沃普一分钟的安生。你们闹得俺心里发毛，跟苍蝇似的，真是的。」

「还有没有别的法子，」埃尔弗丽达说，「回到过去？咱们如今找不到那扇门了。」

「你们当然找不着。」鼹鼠说。「机会错过去一遭，就永远错过去了。

> 「『肯时你不肯，想肯不能肯。』」

好啦，说说你们要上哪儿去，俺给你们造一座倒着走的白钟。」

===Original===
“Anywhere you like,” said Edred incautiously.

“Tch, tch!” said the mole, rubbing its nose with vexation. “There’s another chance gone, and gone forever. You be terrible spending with your chances, you be. Now, answer sharp as weasel’s nose. Be there anyone in the past you’d like to see?

> “ ‘If you don’t know,
 

 Then you don’t go.’

And that’s poetry as good as yours any day of the week.”

===Chinese===
「随便哪儿都行。」埃德雷德冒冒失失地说。

「啧，啧！」鼹鼠说着，懊恼地揉了揉鼻子。「又一个机会没了，永远没了。你们花起机会来可真大手大脚，真是的。听好，答话要尖快，快得像黄鼠狼的鼻子。过去里头可有什么人，是你们想见的？

> 「『心里没个数，脚下没条路。』」

这诗随便是哪一天，都跟你们的诗一样好。」

===Original===
“Cousin Richard,” said Elfrida and Edred together. This was the only name they could think of.

“Bide ye still, my dears,” said the Mouldiwarp, “and I’ll make you a white road right to where he is.”

So they sat still, all but their tongues.

===Chinese===
「理查德表哥。」埃尔弗丽达和埃德雷德异口同声地说。他们想得起来的，只有这个名字。

「乖乖待着别动，亲爱的们。」穆迪沃普说，「俺给你们造一条白路，一直通到他跟前。」

于是他们坐定不动——只有舌头不肯闲着。

===Original===
“Is he in the past?” said Elfrida; “because if he *is*, it wasn’t much good our writing to him.”

“You hold your little tongues,” said the Mouldiwarp, “and keep your little mouths shut, and your little eyes open, and wish well to the white magic. There never was a magic yet,” the mole went on, “that was the worse for being well-wished.”

“May I say something,” said Elfrida, “without its stopping the magic?”

“Put your white handkerchief over your face and talk through it, and then you may.”

===Chinese===
「他在过去吗？」埃尔弗丽达问；「因为要是他*真*在过去，咱们那封信就白写了。」

「管住你们的小舌头。」穆迪沃普说，「小嘴巴闭紧，小眼睛睁圆，一心盼着白魔法好。从来还没有哪一种魔法，」鼹鼠接着说，「是因为被人好心祝过反倒变坏的。」

「我说一句话行不行，」埃尔弗丽达问，「不会把魔法搅散吧？」

「拿你的白手绢蒙住脸，隔着它说话，就可以。」

===Original===
By a most fortunate and unusual chance, Elfrida’s handkerchief *was* white: it was, in fact, still folded in the sixteen blameless squares into which the laundress had ironed it. She threw it over her face as she lay back on the turf and spoke through it.

“I’d like to see the nurse witch again,” she said.

“Instead of Cousin Richard?”

“No: as well as.”

“That’s right,” said the magic mole. “You shouldn’t *change* your wishes; but there’s no rule against enlarging them﻿—on the contrary. Now look!”

Elfrida whisked away the handkerchief and looked.

===Chinese===
多亏一个又凑巧又难得的机缘，埃尔弗丽达的手绢*恰好*是白的：它实际上还规规矩矩折着十六个清清白白的方块，正是洗衣妇熨出来的那副样子。她仰躺在草皮上，把手绢蒙在脸上，隔着它说话。

「我想再见见那位当保姆的女巫。」她说。

「不要理查德表哥了？」

「不是——是除了他，还要见她。」

「这就对了。」魔法鼹鼠说。「愿望是*不该换*的；可也没有哪条规矩不许你把愿望扩大——倒恰恰相反。瞧吧！」

埃尔弗丽达一下子掀掉手绢，望过去。

===Original===
Have you ever noticed the way the bath water runs away when you pull up the bath tap? Have you ever seen bottles filled through a funnel?

The white Mouldiwarp reached up its hands﻿—its front feet I ought perhaps to say﻿—towards the deep-blue sky, where white clouds herded together like giant sheep.

And it spoke. At least, it did not speak, but it sang. Yet I don’t know that you could call it singing either. It was more like the first notes that a violin yields to the bow wielded by the hand of a master musician. And the white clouds stooped to answer it. Round and round in the blue sky they circled, drawing together and swirling down, as the bath water draws and swirls when you pull up the knob labelled “Waste”﻿—round and round till they showed like a vast white funnel whose neck hung, a great ring, above the group on the dry grass of the downs. It stooped and stooped. The ring fitted down over them, they were in a white tower, narrow at its base where that base touched the grass, but widening to the blue sky overhead.

===Chinese===
你可曾留意过，拔开浴缸的塞子时，浴缸里的水是怎样旋着退下去的？你可曾见过用漏斗灌瓶子？

白鼹穆迪沃普向上伸出双手——或者我也许该说，它的前爪——伸向深蓝的天空，天上，白云正聚成群，像一群巨羊。

于是它开口了。说开口，其实它并没有说话，而是唱。可要说唱歌，我又不知道你是否能管那叫唱。那更像小提琴在一位演奏大师手中的弓下吐出的最初几个音符。白云便俯身应答。它们在蓝天里一圈一圈地转，聚拢来，打着旋降下来，就像你拔起标着「排水」字样的旋钮时，浴缸里的水打着旋退下去——一圈又一圈，直到现出一座巨大的白色漏斗，漏斗的颈口像一只大环，悬在丘原干草上这一小群人的头顶。它越垂越低。那环恰恰罩落下来罩住他们，他们便置身在一座白塔里，塔底碰着草的地方收得窄窄的，往上却越来越宽，一直宽到头顶的蓝天。

===Original===
“Take hands,” cried the Mouldiwarp. “Always hold hands when there is magic about.”

The children clasped hands.

“Both hands,” said the Mouldiwarp; and each child reached out a hand, that was caught and held. Round and round, incredibly swifter and swifter, went the cloud funnel, and the voice of the mole at their feet sounded faint and far away.

“Up!” it cried, “up! Shall the very clouds dance for your delight, and you alone refrain and tread not a measure?”

===Chinese===
「拉起手来。」穆迪沃普喊道。「四下里有魔法的时候，永远要手拉着手。」

孩子们拉紧了手。

「两只手都要拉。」穆迪沃普说；于是每个孩子各伸出一只手，那只手被抓住、握紧。云的漏斗一圈一圈转着，快得叫人不能相信地越来越快，脚下鼹鼠的声音听着微弱又遥远。

「起来！」它喊道，「起来！连云都肯为你们跳舞取乐，难道偏你们两个按兵不动，一步也不踏吗？」

===Original===
The children leaped up﻿—and through the cloud came something that was certainly music, though it was so vague and far away that the sharpest music-master you ever had could not have made out the tune. But the rhythm of it was there, an insistent beat, beat, beat﻿—and a beat that made your feet long to keep time to it. And through the rhythm presently the tune pierced, as the sound of the pipes pierces the sound of the drums when you see the Church Brigade boys go by when you are on your holiday by the sea near their white-tented, happy camps. And that time the children’s feet could not resist. They danced steps that they had not known they knew. And they knew, for the first time, the delight of real dancing: none of your waltzes, or even minuets, but the dancing that means youth and gaiety, and being out for a holiday, and determined to enjoy everything to the last breath.

===Chinese===
孩子们跳起身来——云中透出一种东西，那确确实实是音乐，尽管它那么飘忽、那么遥远，连你遇过的顶顶厉害的乐理教师也辨不出那曲调。可节奏是在那里的，一阵执拗的拍子，一下，一下，又一下——拍得你两脚直痒，恨不得跟着打点。不多时，曲调穿过节奏迸了出来，就像你在海边度假、赶上教会少年队的孩子们从他们白帐篷的快乐营地旁走过时，笛声穿过鼓声透出来那样。这一回，孩子们的脚再也抗拒不住了。他们跳出了自己原本不知道自己会的舞步。他们头一回体会到真正跳舞的快乐：才不是你们那种华尔兹，连小步舞也不是，而是那种意味着青春与欢畅、意味着出门过节、打定主意直到最后一口气也要把样样都享受个痛快的舞蹈。

===Original===
And as they danced the white cloud funnel came down and closed about them, so that they danced, as it were, in a wrapping of white cotton-wool too soft for them even to feel it. And there was a sweet scent in the air. They did not know in that cloudy, soft whiteness, what flower bore that scent, but they knew that it smelt of the spring, and of fields and hedges far away from the ugliness of towns. The cloud thinned as the scent thickened, and green lights showed through it.

The green lights grew, the cloud funnel lifted. And Edred and Elfrida, still dancing, found themselves but two in a ring of some thirty children, dancing on a carpet of green turf between walls of green branches. And every child wore a wreath of white May-blossoms on its head. And that was the magic scene that had come to them through the white cloud of the white Mouldiwarp’s magic.

===Chinese===
他们跳着舞，白云的漏斗便落下来，合拢在他们四周，于是他们仿佛裹在一层白棉花里跳舞，那棉花软得他们连觉都觉不出来。空气里有一股甜香。在那云也似、软绵绵的白茫茫里，他们认不出这香气是哪种花散的，可他们知道它闻起来是春天的气息，是远离丑陋城镇的田野和树篱的气息。香气渐浓，云色渐薄，绿光从云里透了进来。

绿光越来越亮，云的漏斗升了上去。埃德雷德和埃尔弗丽达兀自跳着舞，发现自己不过是三十来个孩子围成的一圈里的两个，在一块绿草皮织成的地毯上跳，四壁是绿枝编成的墙。每个孩子头上都戴着一顶白山楂花编的花环。这便是白鼹穆迪沃普的魔法借白云给他们送来的魔法景象。

===Original===
“What is it? Why are we dancing?” Edred incautiously asked of the little girl whose hand﻿—and not Elfrida’s﻿—he found that his left hand was holding. The child laughed﻿—just laughed, she did not answer. It was Elfrida who had his right hand, and her own right hand was clasped in that of a boy dressed in green.

“Oh!” she said, with a note of glad recognition. “It’s you! I’m so glad! What is it? Why are we dancing?”

“It’s May-day,” said Cousin Richard, “and the King is coming to look on at the revels.”

“What king?” she asked.

“Who but King Harry?” he said. “King Harry and his new Queen, that but of late was the Lady Anna Boleyn.”

“I say, Dick,” said Edred across his sister, “I am jolly glad to see you again. We﻿—”

“Not now,” said Dick earnestly; “not a word now. It is not safe. And besides﻿—here comes the King!”

===Chinese===
「这是怎么回事？咱们干吗跳舞呢？」埃德雷德又冒冒失失地问起话来——问他左手拉着的那只手的小主人，那不是埃尔弗丽达的手。那孩子笑了——只是笑，并不答话。埃德雷德的右手才是埃尔弗丽达拉着的，而她自己的右手，被一个穿绿衣裳的男孩握着。

「哎呀！」她欢喜地、认出来了似的说。「是你呀！我真高兴！这是怎么回事？咱们干吗跳舞呢？」

「今天是五朔节。」理查德表哥说，「国王要来看歌舞游戏。」

「哪位国王？」她问。

「除了哈里王还能是谁？」他说。「哈里王和他的新王后——那位不久之前还是安娜·博林小姐（Lady Anna Boleyn）的贵人。」

「我说，迪克。」埃德雷德隔着姐姐喊道，「又见到你我真太高兴了。我们——」

「现在不行。」迪克急切地说；「现在一个字也别说。不安全。再说——国王来啦！」

## VIII Guy Fawkes / 盖伊·福克斯

===Original===
Three days, because there had been a quarrel. But days pass quickly when the sun shines, and it is holiday-time, and you have a big ruined castle to explore and examine—a castle that is your own, or your brother's.

"After all," said Elfrida sensibly, "we might quite likely find the treasure ourselves, without any magic Mouldiwarpiness at all. We'll look thoroughly. We won't leave a stone unturned."

"We shall have to leave a good many stones unturned," said Edred, looking at the great grey mass of the keep that towered tall and frowning above them.

"Well, you know what I mean," said Elfrida. "Come on!" and they went.

===Chinese===
三天，因为吵了一场架。可日子过得快：太阳照着，又是假期，手边还有一座废弃的大城堡可探可察——一座属于你自己的、或者说属于你哥哥的城堡。

「说到底，」埃尔弗丽达明事理地说，「咱们满可以自己找到宝藏，全然用不着什么穆迪沃普（Mouldiwarp）式的魔法。咱们细细地找。一块石头也不放过。」

「那咱们可有得石头没法翻呢。」埃德雷德说，望着主楼那灰沉沉的一大团，又高又吓人地耸在他们头顶上。

「呃，你懂我的意思嘛。」埃尔弗丽达说。「走！」他们便走了。

===Original===
They climbed the steep, worn stairs that wound round and round in the darkness—stairs littered with dead leaves and mould and dropped feathers, and the dry, deserted nests of owls and jackdaws; stairs that ended suddenly in daylight and a steep last step, and the top of a broad ivy-grown wall from which you could look down, down, down; past the holes in the walls where the big beams used to be, past the old fireplaces still black with the smoke of fires long since burnt out, past the doors and windows of rooms whose floors fell away long ago; down, down, to where ferns and grass and brambles grew green at the very bottom of the tower.

Then there were arched doors that led to colonnades with strong little pillars and narrow windows, wonderful little unexpected chambers and corners—the best place in the whole wide world for serious and energetic hide-and-seek.

===Chinese===
他们爬上那些陡峭的、磨旧了的楼梯，楼梯在黑暗里一圈一圈盘旋——梯级上积着枯叶、霉斑和掉落的羽毛，还有猫头鹰和寒鸦弃下的干鸟窝；楼梯忽然到了头，眼前一亮，末一级又陡，接着是一道爬满常春藤的宽墙头，站在上面可以往下望，望下去，一直望下去；墙上是当年搁大梁的孔洞，烟熏火燎早已熄灭多年的老壁炉还是黑的，那些屋子门啊窗啊都还在，屋里的地板却早就塌没了；望下去，望下去，一直望到塔底，那最底下，蕨草、青草和荆棘长得绿汪汪的。

再往里是些拱门，通向带小柱子的柱廊和窄窗，还有一间间妙不可言、出其不意的小屋和角落——全世界全天下顶顶适合郑郑重重、热火朝天玩捉迷藏的地方。

===Original===
"How glorious," said Elfrida, as they rested, scarlet and panting, after a thrilling game of "I spy,"—"if all these broken bits were mended, so that you couldn't see where the new bits were stuck on! And if it could all be exactly like it was when it was brand-new."

"There wasn't the house when it was brand-new—the house like it is now, I mean," said Edred. "I don't suppose there was any attic with chests in when the castle was new."

"There couldn't be, not with *all* the chests," said Elfrida; "of course not, because some of the clothes in the chest weren't made till long after the castle was built. I believe grownups can tell what a broken thing was like when it was new. I know they can with bones—mastodons and things. And they made out what Hercules was like out of one foot of him that they found, I believe," she added hazily.

===Chinese===
「多壮丽啊，」埃尔弗丽达说。一场叫人心跳的「我发现」游戏过后，两人歇下来，脸红红的，直喘气——「要是这些破的碎的都补好，补得看不出新料子接在哪儿！要是它能完完全全恢复崭新时候的样儿。」

「它崭新的时候还没有那座宅子呢——我是说，如今这样的宅子。」埃德雷德说。「城堡新的时候，我想还没有哪间装着箱子的阁楼呢。」

「不可能有——不可能连*全部*箱子都有，」埃尔弗丽达说；「当然不可能，因为有的箱子里的衣裳，是城堡造好很久以后才做出来的。我相信大人能看出一件破东西新的时候是什么模样。骨头他们准看得出来——乳齿象什么的。我还相信，他们凭找到的赫拉克勒斯的一只脚，就推想出他整个人长什么样。」她含含糊糊地补了一句。

===Original===
"I've got an idea," said Edred, "if we could get back to where the castle was all perfect like a model and draw pictures of every part. Then when we found the treasure we should know exactly what to build it up like, shouldn't we?"

"Yes," said Elfrida very gently. "We certainly should. But then we should have to know how to draw first, shouldn't we?"

"Of course we should," Edred agreed, "but that wouldn't take long if we really tried. I never do try at school. I don't like it. But it's jolly easy. I know that. Burslem mi. always takes the drawing prize, and you know what a duffer *he* is. We might begin to learn now, don't you think?"

Elfrida sat down on a fallen stone in the middle of the castle yard, and looked at the intricate wonderful arches and pillars, the crenulated battlements of the towers, the splendid stoutness of the walls, and she sighed.

"Yes," she said, "let's begin now—"

===Chinese===
「我有个主意，」埃德雷德说，「要是咱们能回到城堡完好得像模型的那会儿，把每个部分都画下来。那样等咱们找到宝藏，就一清二楚该把它重建成什么样子了，对不对？」

「是啊，」埃尔弗丽达轻轻地说。「咱们当然就知道了。可那样咱们就得先学会画画儿，对不对？」

「当然得会。」埃德雷德附和，「不过真下功夫的话，费不了多少工夫。我在学校从来不下功夫。我不喜欢。可画画儿容易得很。这我知道。小伯斯勒姆（Burslem mi.）回回拿绘画奖，*他*有多笨你是知道的。咱们现在就可以开始学，你说呢？」

埃尔弗丽达在城堡院子当中一块倒下的石头上坐下，望着那些精巧奇妙的拱门和柱子、塔上锯齿状的雉堞、墙壁堂堂的厚实，叹了口气。

「是啊，」她说，「咱们现在就开始——」

===Original===
"And you'll have to lend me one of your pencils," said he, "because I broke mine all to bits trying to get the parlour door open the day you'd got the key in your pocket. Quite a long one it was. You'll have to lend me a long one, Elf. I can't draw with those little endy-bits that get inside your hand and prick you with the other end."

"I don't mind," said she, "so long as you don't put it in your mouth."

So they got large sheets of writing-paper, and brown calf-bound books for the paper to lie flat on, and they started to draw Arden Castle. And as Elfrida tried to draw everything she knew was there, as well as everything she could see, her drawing soon became almost entirely covered with black-lead.

===Chinese===
「那你还得借我一支铅笔，」他说，「因为那天钥匙在你兜里，我想撬开客厅的门，把我的铅笔折得粉碎。那还是支挺长的呢。你得借我支长的，埃尔夫（Elf）。那些小铅笔头我使不来，攥在手心里，另一头还直扎人。」

「我不在乎，」她说，「只要你别往嘴里放。」

于是他们找了大幅的书写纸，又搬来棕色小牛皮面的簿子垫平纸页，动手画起阿登城堡来。埃尔弗丽达又想画她知道在那儿的每一样，又想画她看得见的每一样，这么一来，她那张画很快几乎全叫黑铅道儿盖满了。

===Original===
They had no indiarubber, and if you drew anything wrong it had to stay drawn. When you first begin to draw, you draw a good many things wrong, don't you? I assure you that nobody would have known that the black and grey muddle on Elfrida's paper was meant to be a picture of a castle. Edred's was much more easily recognised, even before he printed "Arden Castle" under it in large, uneven letters. He never once raised his eyes from his paper, and just drew what he thought the front of the castle looked like from the outside. Also he sucked his pencil earnestly—Elfrida's pencil, I mean—and this made the lines of his drawing very black.

"There!" he said at last, "it's ever so much liker than yours."

"Yes," said Elfrida, "but there's more *in* mine."

"It doesn't matter how much there is in a picture if you can't tell what it's meant for," said Edred, with some truth. "Now, in mine you can see the towers, and the big gate, and the windows, and the twiddly in-and-outness on top."

===Chinese===
他们没有橡皮，画错了什么，错处就得留在纸上。人初学画画儿的时候，总要画错好多东西，是不是？我向你担保，谁也认不出埃尔弗丽达纸上那摊黑灰相杂的乱糟糟，原是想画一座城堡。埃德雷德那张就好认多了，何况他还在底下用歪歪扭扭的大写字母印上了「阿登城堡」。他眼睛压根儿没离开过纸，只管照他心里想的、城堡正面的外样子画。他还起劲地嘬铅笔——我是说，嘬埃尔弗丽达的铅笔——这么着，他画上的线条就非常黑。

「瞧！」他末了说，「比你的像多啦。」

「是啊，」埃尔弗丽达说，「可我这张里头*东西*多。」

「一幅画里东西再多，认不出画的是什么也白搭。」埃德雷德说，这话有几分道理。「瞧我这张，塔瞧得见，大门瞧得见，窗户瞧得见，顶上那些绕来绕去、凹进凸出的玩意儿也瞧得见。」

===Original===
"Yes," said Elfrida, "but … well, let's do something else. I don't believe we should either of us learn to draw well enough to rebuild Arden by; not before we've found the treasure, I mean. Perhaps we might meet a real artist, like the one we saw drawing the castle yesterday—in the past I mean—and get him to draw it for us, and bring the picture back with us, and—"

"Oh," cried Edred, jumping up and dropping his masterpiece, and the calf-bound volume and the pencil. "*I* know. The Brownie!"

"The Brownie?"

"Yes—take it with us. Then we could photograph the castle all perfect."

"But we can't take it with us."

"Can't we?" said Edred; "that's all *you* know. Now I'll tell you something. That first time—a bit of plaster was in my shoe when we changed, and it was in my shoe when we got there, and I took it out when we were learning about ‘dog's delight.’ And I flipped it out of the window. And when we got back, and I'd changed and everything, there was that bit of plaster in my own shoe. If we can take plaster we can take photographs—cameras, I mean." This close and intelligent reasoning commanded Elfrida's respect, and she wished she had thought of it herself. But then she had not had any plaster in her shoe. So she said—

===Chinese===
「是啊，」埃尔弗丽达说，「不过……唉，咱们还是干点别的吧。我不信咱们俩谁能把画学得够照着重建阿登；我是说，在找到宝藏之前学不成。说不定咱们能碰上一位真正的画家，就像昨天咱们瞧见画城堡的那位——我是说，在过去瞧见的——请他替咱们画下来，把画带回来，然后——」

「哦！」埃德雷德喊道，一蹦而起，他的大作、小牛皮簿子和铅笔都撒了手。「*我*知道了。布朗尼（Brownie）！」

「布朗尼？」

「对——带着它一块儿去。那咱们就能把完完整整的城堡照下来了。」

「可咱们带不过去呀。」

「带不过去？」埃德雷德说；「那是*你*不明白罢了。听我告诉你件事。头一回——咱们换装的时候，我鞋里有一小片墙皮；到了那边，它还在我鞋里；学『狗儿的乐事』那阵子，我把它掏出来，弹出了窗外。等咱们回来，我换好了装，一切都弄妥了，那片墙皮又在我自己鞋里了。墙皮带得过去，照片就带得过去——我是说，相机带得过去。」这番缜密而高明的推理赢得了埃尔弗丽达的敬意，她但愿这是自己想出来的。不过话说回来，她鞋里又没进来过墙皮。于是她说——

===Original===
"You're getting quite clever, aren't you?"

"Aha," said Edred, "you'd like to have thought of that yourself, wouldn't you? I can be clever sometimes, same as you can."

It is very annoying to have our thoughts read. Elfrida said swiftly, "Not often you can't," and then stopped short. For a moment the children stood looking at each other with a very peculiar expression. Then a sigh of relief broke from each.

"Fielded!" said Edred.

"Just in time!" said Elfrida. "It wasn't a quarrel; nobody could say it was a quarrel. Come on, let's go and look at the cottages, like the witch told us to."

===Chinese===
「你变得挺聪明了，是不是？」

「啊哈，」埃德雷德说，「这你自己也巴不得想出来呢，是不是？我有时候也能聪明一把，跟你一样。」

叫人看透心事是很恼人的。埃尔弗丽达脱口便道：「你也聪明不了几回。」说完猛地刹住。两个孩子面面相觑，脸上的神情说不出的古怪。随后两人不约而同舒了一口气。

「接住啦！」埃德雷德说。

「接得正及时！」埃尔弗丽达说。「那不算吵架；谁也不能说那算吵架。走，咱们看茅屋去，照巫婆吩咐的。」

===Original===
They went. They made a tour of inspection that day and the next and the next. And they saw a great many things that a grown-up inspector would never have seen. Poor people are very friendly and kind to you when you are a child. They will let you come into their houses and talk to you and show you things in a way that they would never condescend to do with your grown-up relations. This is, of course, if you are a really nice child, and treat them in a respectful and friendly way. Edred and Elfrida very soon knew more about the insides of the cottages round Arden than any grownup could have learned in a year. They knew what wages the master of the house got, what there was for dinner, and what, oftener, there wasn't, how many children were still living, and how many had failed to live. They knew exactly where the rain came through the rotten thatch in bad weather, and where the boards didn't fit and so let the draughts in, and how some of the doors wouldn't shut, some wouldn't open, and how the bedroom windows were, as often as not, not made to open at all.

===Chinese===
他们去了。那天、第二天、第三天，他们巡视了一遍。他们看见了许多大人巡视员永远看不见的东西。你是个孩子的时候，穷苦人待你非常和气亲热。他们会让你进他们的家，同你说话，把东西指给你看，那份热络，他们对你的大人是决不肯屈尊的。当然啦，这得你真是个讨人喜欢的孩子，待他们又尊敬又亲热才成。没过多久，埃德雷德和埃尔弗丽达对阿登一带茅屋内里的了解，就胜过任何一个大人一年学得会的了。谁家的当家人挣多少工钱，饭桌上有什么——更常有的是什么都没有——几个孩子还活着，几个没能活下来，他们全知道。坏天气里雨水从哪块烂茅草顶漏进来，哪几块地板不严实、漏风，哪几扇门关不上，哪几扇又打不开，卧房的窗子十有八九压根儿就没做成能开的——他们都一清二楚。

===Original===
And when they weren't visiting the cottages or exploring the castle they found a joyous way of passing the time in the reading aloud of the history of Arden. They took it in turns to read aloud. Elfrida looked carefully for some mention of Sir Edward Talbot and his pretending to be the Chevalier St. George. There was none, but a Sir Edward Talbot had been accused, with the Lord Arden of the time, of plotting against His Most Christian Majesty King James I.

"I wonder if he was like my Edward Talbot?" said Elfrida. "I would like to see him again. I wish I'd told him about us having been born so many years after he died. But it would have been difficult to explain, wouldn't it? Let's look in Green's *History Book* and see what they looked like when it was His Most Christian Majesty King James the First."

Perhaps it was this which decided the children, when the three days were over, to put on the clothes which most resembled the ones in the pictures of James I's time in Green's *History*.

===Chinese===
不去走访茅屋、不去探察城堡的时候，他们还找到了一件快活的消遣：轮流高声朗读《阿登家史》。埃尔弗丽达细心地找有没有提起爱德华·塔尔博特爵士（Sir Edward Talbot）和他假扮圣乔治骑士（the Chevalier St. George）的地方。一处也没有，倒是有位爱德华·塔尔博特爵士，曾同当时的阿登勋爵一道，被指控阴谋加害最基督教的国王陛下詹姆斯一世（James I）。

「我纳闷他像不像我那位爱德华·塔尔博特？」埃尔弗丽达说。「我想再见见他。真希望我告诉过他，咱们生在他死后那么多年。可那不容易解释明白，对不对？咱们翻翻格林的《历史书》（Green's *History*），瞧瞧最基督教的国王詹姆斯一世当朝时人们都是什么穿戴。」

也许正是这个缘故，三天期满时，孩子们才挑了最像格林《历史》里詹姆斯一世时代画中人穿戴的衣裳穿上了。

===Original===
Edred had full breeches, puffed out like balloons, and a steeple-crowned hat, and a sort of tunic of crimson velvet, and a big starched ruff round his little neck more uncomfortable even than your Eton collar is after you've been wearing flannels for days and days. And Elfrida had long, tight stays with a large, flat-shaped piece of wood down the front, and very full, long skirts over a very abrupt hoop.

When the three days were over the door of the attic, which, as usual after a quarrel, had been quite invisible and impossible to find, had become as plain as the nose on the face of the plainest person you know, and the children had walked in, and looked in the chests till they found what they wanted.

And now they put on ruffs and all the rest of it to the accompaniment, or, as it always seemed, with the help, of soft pigeon noises.

===Chinese===
埃德雷德穿一条鼓鼓囊囊、吹得像气球似的蓬蓬短裤，戴一顶尖顶高帽，套一件深红天鹅绒的紧身短褂，小脖子上围一圈浆硬的大皱领，比你好些好多天一直穿法兰绒之后再戴伊顿领还要难受。埃尔弗丽达呢，穿一件又长又紧的紧身褡，前胸嵌着一块扁扁的大木片，裙撑突兀地一撑，外头罩着又长又宽的大裙子。

三天一到，那扇阁楼门——吵架之后照例踪影全无、怎么也找不着的那扇——变得明明白白，就像你认得的其貌最不扬的那人脸上的鼻子一样明白，孩子们走了进去，在箱子里翻找，直到翻出想要的东西。

这时他们便套上皱领和其余的一切，耳边陪着——或者说，依仗着——鸽子咕咕的轻响。

===Original===
While they were dressing Elfrida held the Brownie camera tightly, in one hand or the other. This made dressing rather slow and difficult, but the children had agreed that if it were not done the Brownie would be, as Edred put it, "liable to vanish," as everything else belonging to their own time always did—except their clothes. I can't explain to you just now how it was that their clothes *didn't* vanish. It would take too long. But it was all part of the magic of white feathers which are, as you know, the clothes of white pigeons.

===Chinese===
换装的当儿，埃尔弗丽达一只手换一只手，紧紧攥着布朗尼相机。这么着穿衣裳就慢了、费事了，可两个孩子说定了：不这样办，布朗尼就会——照埃德雷德的说法——「保不齐没了影儿」，同一切属于他们自己时代的东西一样——除了他们的衣裳。他们的衣裳*为什么*不会没了影儿，这我眼下没法跟你解释。太费工夫。可这全归在白羽毛的魔法里头——白羽毛，如你所知，正是白鸽的衣裳。

===Original===
And now a very odd thing happened. As Edred put on his second shoe—which was the last touch to their united toilets—the walls seemed to tremble and shake and go crooked, like a house of cards at the very instant before it topples down. The floor slanted to that degree that standing on it was so difficult as to be at last impossible. The rafters all seemed to get crooked and mixed, like a box of matches when you spill them on the floor. The tiled roof that showed blue daylight through seemed to spin like a top, and you could not tell at all which way up you were. All this happened with dreadful suddenness, but almost as soon as it had begun it stopped with a jerk like that of a clockwork engine that has gone wrong. And the attic was gone—and the chests, and the blue-chinked tiles of the roof, and the walls and the rafters. And the room had shrunk to less than half its old size. And it was higher, and it was not an attic any more, but a round room with narrow windows, and just such a fireplace, with a stone hood, as the ones the children had seen when they looked down from the tops of the towers. You must have often heard of events that take people's breath away. This sudden change did really take away the breaths of Edred and Elfrida, so that for a few moments they could only stare at each other "like Guy Fawkes masks," as Elfrida later said.

===Chinese===
就在这时，一件顶顶古怪的事发生了。埃德雷德穿上第二只鞋——那是两人梳妆的最后一道工序——墙壁仿佛颤起来、晃起来、歪起来，像一座纸牌搭的房子正要倒塌的那一瞬。地板斜到了那个地步，站上去先是难，末了干脆站不住。椽子仿佛全拧了、全乱套了，像一盒火柴撒了一地。透进蓝白天光的瓦顶仿佛陀螺似的打转，你压根儿分不清哪头朝上。这一切来得可怕地突然，可几乎刚一开始，它就像出了毛病的发条火车似的猛一抽，停住了。阁楼没有了——箱子没有了，屋顶那些漏蓝天的瓦缝没有了，墙壁和椽子也没有了。屋子缩得不足原先一半大。它还变高了，不再是阁楼，而是一间带窄窗的圆屋子，壁炉带着石头炉罩，同孩子们从塔顶望下去时见过的那些一模一样。你一定常听说叫人屏住呼吸的事。这突如其来的变化当真夺走了埃德雷德和埃尔弗丽达的呼吸，害得他们有好一阵只能干瞪着眼你瞧我、我瞧你，「活像两张盖伊·福克斯（Guy Fawkes）面具」——埃尔弗丽达后来说。

===Original===
"*I* see," said Edred, when breath enough for speech had returned to him. "This is the place where the attic was after the tower fell to pieces."

"But there isn't any attic really," said Elfrida. "You know we can't find it if we quarrelled, and Mrs. Honeysett doesn't ever find it. It isn't anywhere."

"Yes, it is," said Edred. "We couldn't find it if it wasn't."

===Chinese===
「*我*明白了，」等气儿缓得够说话了，埃德雷德说。「这是塔坍塌之后，阁楼所在的那个地方。」

「可其实压根儿没有什么阁楼，」埃尔弗丽达说。「你知道的，咱们一吵架就找不着它，哈尼塞特太太又从来没有找着过。它不在任何地方。」

「在的。」埃德雷德说。「它要是不在，咱们也就找不着了。」

===Original===
"Well," said Elfrida gloomily, "I only hope we *may* find it, that's all. I suppose we may as well go out. It's no use sticking in this horrid little room." Her hand was on the door, but even as she fumbled with the latch, which was of iron and of a shape to which she was wholly unaccustomed, something else happened, even more disconcerting than the turnover-change in which the attic and the chests had disappeared. It is very difficult to describe. Perhaps you happen to dislike travelling in trains with your back to the engine? If you do dislike it, you dislike it very much indeed. It makes your head ache, and gives you a queer feeling at the back of your neck, and makes you turn so pale that the grown-up people with whom you are travelling will ask you what is the matter, and sometimes heartlessly insist that the buns you had at the junction, or the chocolate creams pressed into your hand at the parting hour by Uncle Fred or Aunt Imogen, are the cause of your sufferings. The worst feeling of all is that terrible sensation, as though your heart and lungs and the front part of your waistcoat were being drawn slowly but surely through your backbone, and taken a very long way off.

===Chinese===
「唉，」埃尔弗丽达蔫蔫地说，「我只盼咱们*当真*找得着它，就这么着。我想咱们还是出去吧。赖在这讨厌的小屋里没益处。」她的手已经搭上门，可就在她摸那铁闩——那形状她全然不惯——的当儿，又有一桩事发生了，比阁楼和箱子消失的那场翻个儿的变化还更叫人发慌。这很难描摹。也许你碰巧不喜欢坐火车背朝机车？你要是不喜欢，那可真是不喜欢得很。它叫你头痛，叫你后脖颈起一种怪怪的感觉，叫你脸白得同行的大人要问你哪儿不对劲，有时还狠心地一口咬定，你难受的根子，要么是换车时吃的圆面包，要么是临开车的钟点弗雷德叔叔或伊莫金姑妈塞进你手心的奶油夹心巧克力。最难受的是那股可怕的感觉，仿佛你的心、你的肺、你马甲的前襟，正被慢慢地、却是稳稳地顺着你的脊椎抽出去，带到很远很远的地方。

===Original===
The sensations which now held Edred and Elfrida were exactly like those which—if you don't like travelling backwards—you know only too well—and the sensations were so acute that both children shut their eyes. The whirling feeling, and the withdrawing-waistcoat feeling, and the headache, and the back-of-the-neck feeling stopped as suddenly as they had begun, and the two children opened their eyes in a room which Edred at least had never seen before. To Elfrida it seemed strange yet familiar. The shape of the room, the position of doors and windows, the mantelpiece with its curious carvings—these she knew. And some of the furniture, too. Yet the room seemed bare—barer than it should have been. But why should it look bare—barer than it should have been—unless she knew how much less bare it once was? Unless, in fact, she had seen it before?

===Chinese===
此刻攫住埃德雷德和埃尔弗丽达的，正是那些你——倘若你不喜欢倒着坐——熟悉得不能再熟悉的感觉；那感觉来势凶猛，两个孩子都闭上了眼睛。天旋地转的感觉、马甲被抽走的感觉、头痛、后脖颈的那股劲儿，来得突然去得也突然，两个孩子睁开眼，已是在一间屋子里——至少埃德雷德从没见过。在埃尔弗丽达看来，它陌生，却又眼熟。屋子的形状，门窗的位置，雕着古怪花纹的壁炉架——这些她都认得。有几件家具也认得。可屋子显得空——空得过了头。可它若不是本来装着多得多的东西，她怎么会觉得它空得过了头呢？说到底，除非她从前见过它？

===Original===
"Oh, I know," she cried, standing in her stiff skirts and heavy shoes in the middle of the room. "I know. This is Lord Arden's town house. This is where I was with Cousin Betty. Only there aren't such nice chairs and things, and it was full of people then."

Edred remained silent, his mouth half open and his eyes half shut in a sort of trance of astonishment. This was very different from the last adventure in which he had taken part. For then he had only gone to the house in Arden Castle as it was in Boney's time, and he had gone to it by the simple means of walking down a staircase with which he was already familiar. But now he had been transported in a most violent and unpleasing manner, not only from his own times to times much earlier, but also from Arden Castle, which he knew, to Arden House, which he did not know. So he was silent, and when he did speak it was with discontent verging on disgust.

===Chinese===
「哦，我知道了，」她站在屋子正中，僵挺的裙子、沉重的鞋里，喊道。「我知道了。这是阿登勋爵城里的宅子。我同贝蒂表姐（Cousin Betty）来过这儿。只是如今没有那时候那些好椅子好摆设，那时候还满屋子都是人。」

埃德雷德不作声，嘴半张着，眼半闭着，惊得出了神。这同他上一回的历险大不一样。上一回，他不过是走进了拿破仑时代的阿登城堡里的那座宅子，走的是一道他本来就熟的楼梯，再简单不过。可这一回，他是被一种极其猛烈、极不痛快的方式搬运来的：不光从自己的时代搬到了早得多的时候，还从他所熟识的阿登城堡，搬到了他不认识的阿登宅邸（Arden House）。所以他闷声不响，等到开口，那不满已近乎嫌恶。

===Original===
"I don't like it," he began. "Let's go back. I don't like it. And we didn't take the photograph. And I don't like it. And my clothes are horrid. I feel something between a balloon and a Bluecoat boy. And you've no idea how silly you look—like Mrs. Noah out of the Ark, only tubby. And I don't know who we're supposed to be. And I don't suppose this is Arden House. And if it is, you don't know *when*. Suppose it's Inquisition times, and they put us on the stake? Let's go back; I don't like it," he ended.

"Now you just listen," said Elfrida, knitting her brows under the queer cap she wore. "I know inside me what I mean, but *you* won't unless you jolly well attend."

"Fire ahead."

"Well, then, even if it was Inquisition times it would be all right—for us."

===Chinese===
「我不喜欢这儿，」他开腔了。「咱们回去吧。我不喜欢这儿。再说照片也没照成。还有我不喜欢这儿。我这身衣裳也糟糕透顶。我觉得自己活像一只气球和一个蓝袍学堂学生的中间物。你也想不到你自己瞧着多蠢——像方舟里那位挪亚太太，只是更圆墩墩。再说咱们也不知道人家把咱们当成谁。我看这儿也未必是阿登宅邸。就算是，你也不知道*是什么年头*。万一赶上宗教裁判所的时代，人家把咱们绑上火刑柱怎么办？回去吧；我不喜欢这儿。」他末了说。

「你给我好好听着。」埃尔弗丽达说，在那顶怪帽子底下皱起眉。「我心里明白我的意思，可*你*不明白，除非你老老实实集中精神听。」

「放马过来。」

「那么，就算真是宗教裁判所的时代，也不要紧——对咱们不要紧。」

===Original===
"How do you know?"

"I don't know how I know, but I know I *do* know," said Elfrida firmly. "You see, *I've* been here before. It's not real, you see."

"It *is*," said Edred, kicking the leg of the table.

"Yes, of course … but … look here! You remember the water-shoot at Earl's Court, and you were so frightened."

"I wasn't."

"Yes, you were; and I didn't half like it myself. I wished we hadn't, rather. And when it started, and we knew we'd *got* to go on with it. Oh, horrible! And when it was over we wanted to go again, and we did, and it's been so jolly to remember. This is like that. See?"

===Chinese===
「你怎么知道？」

「我不知道我是怎么知道的，可我知道我*确实*知道。」埃尔弗丽达斩钉截铁地说。「你瞧，*我*从前到过这儿。这不是真的，你明白吗。」

「是真的。」埃德雷德说，一脚踢在桌子腿上。

「是，当然是真的……可是……你听着！你总记得伯爵宫（Earl's Court）的水滑梯吧，你吓得那样儿。」

「我才没有。」

「你有；我自己也怕得够呛。我倒宁愿咱们压根儿没去。等它一开动，咱们明白*非*坐到底不可。哦，可怕死了！可一坐完，咱们又想再坐，也真坐了，如今回想起来多痛快。这就跟那一样。明白吗？」

===Original===
"I don't," said Edred, "understand a single word you say. This isn't a bit like the water-shoot or anything. Now, is it?"

Elfrida frowned. Afterwards she was glad that she had done no more than frown. It is dangerous, as you know, to quarrel in a boat, but far more dangerous to quarrel in a century that is not your own. She frowned and opened her mouth. And just as her mouth opened the door of the room followed its example, and a short, dark, cross-looking woman in a brown skirt and strange cap came hurrying in.

"So it's here you've hidden yourselves!" she cried. "And I looking high and low to change your dress."

"What for?" said Edred, for it was his arm which she had quite ungently caught.

"For what?" she said, as she dragged him out of the room. "Why, to attend my lord your father and your lady mother at the masque at Whitehall. Had you forgot already? And thou so desirous to attend them in thy new white velvet broidered with the orange-tawny, and thy lady mother's diamond buckles, and the silken cloak, and the shoe-roses, and the cobweb-lawn starched ruff, and the little sword and all."

===Chinese===
「我不，」埃德雷德说，「懂你的一个字。这跟水滑梯也好、别的也好，一点不像。你说像吗？」

埃尔弗丽达皱起了眉。事后她庆幸自己只皱了皱眉。你知道的，在船上吵架危险，可在不属于你的世纪里吵架，还要危险得多。她皱着眉，张开了嘴。她的嘴刚张开，屋门也依样画葫芦地开了，一个矮小、黝黑、一脸愠色的女人，穿棕裙，戴怪帽，急匆匆闯了进来。

「原来你们俩躲在这儿！」她嚷道。「害我里里外外高高低低找了个遍，要给你们换装呢。」

「换什么装？」埃德雷德说，因为她一把攥住的正是他的胳膊，攥得可不温柔。

「换什么装？」她一面把他往屋外拖一面说。「嗨，去白厅（Whitehall）的假面剧上，侍奉你父亲大人和你母亲夫人呀。这就忘啦？你还那么巴巴地盼着，穿上那身绣橘褐色花样的雪白新丝绒去见他们，戴上你母亲夫人的钻石鞋扣，还有丝斗篷、鞋上的蔷薇花结、蛛网细麻浆的皱领，外加小宝剑，一整套呢。」

===Original===
The woman had dragged Edred out of the room and by the stairs by this time. Elfrida, following, decided that her speech was the harshest part of her.

"If she was really horrid," thought the girl, "she wouldn't try to cheer him up with velvet and swords and diamond buckles.

"Can't *I* go?" she said aloud.

The woman turned and slapped her—not hard, but smartly. "I told thee how it would be if thou wouldst not hold that dunning tongue. No; thou can't go. Little ladies stay at home and sew their samplers. Thou'll go to Court soon enough, I warrant."

===Chinese===
这时候那女人已把埃德雷德拖出屋子，拖到楼梯口。埃尔弗丽达跟在后头，断定她的嗓门是她浑身上下最凶的部位。

「她要是真心眼坏，」姑娘想，「就不会拿丝绒、宝剑和钻石鞋扣哄他高兴了。」

「*我*不能去吗？」她出了声说。

那女人回身掴了她一记——不重，却脆生。「我早告诉过你，你要是不肯收住那张讨债似的嘴，会是什么光景。不能；你去不成。小小的淑女待在家里，绣她的刺绣样本。你进宫廷的日子，往后多着呢，我打包票。」

===Original===
So Elfrida sat and watched while Edred was partially washed—the soap got in his eyes just as it gets in yours nowadays—and dressed in the beautiful white page's dress, white velvet, diamond buckles, little sword, and all.

"You are splendid," she said. "Oh, I do wish I was a boy!" she added, for perhaps the two thousand and thirty-second time in her short life.

"It's not that thou'll be wishing when *thy* time comes to go to Court," said the woman. "There, my little lord, give thy old nurse a kiss and stand very cautious and perfect, not to soil thy fine feathers. And when thou hearest thy mother's robes on the stairs go out and make thy bow like thy tutor taught thee."

===Chinese===
于是埃尔弗丽达坐着旁观，看埃德雷德被草草洗了一回——肥皂进了他眼睛，就跟如今肥皂进你眼睛一个样——又看他穿上那身漂亮的白色侍童装：白丝绒、钻石鞋扣、小宝剑，一应俱全。

「你神气极了。」她说。「哦，我真巴不得自己是个男孩！」她又添一句，这在她短短一生里，恐怕已是第二千零三十二回了。

「等到*你*进宫廷的日子，你巴望的可就不是这个了。」那女人说。「来，我的小爵爷，亲亲你的老奶妈，站得小心些、周正些，别蹭脏了你的漂亮羽毛。听见你母亲的裙子在楼梯上一响，就出去行礼，照你老师教你的那样。」

===Original===
It was not Edred's tutor who had taught him to bow. But when a rustling of silks sounded on the stairs he was able to go out and make a very creditable obeisance to the stately magnificence that swept down towards him. Elfrida thought it best to curtsey beside her brother. Aunt Edith had taught them to dance the minuet, and somehow the bow and curtsey which belong to that dance seemed the right thing now. And the lady on the stairs smiled, well pleased. She was a wonderfully dressed lady. Her bodice was of yellow satin, richly embroidered; her petticoat of gold tissue with stripes; her robe of red velvet, lined with yellow muslin with stripes of pure gold. She had a point lace apron and a collar of white satin under a delicately worked ruff. And she was a blaze of beautiful jewels.

"Thou'rt a fine page, indeed, my dear son," said the lady. "Stand aside and take my train as I pass. And thou, dear daughter, so soon as thou'rt of an age for it, thou shalt have a train and a page to carry it for thee."

She swept on, and the children followed. Lord Arden was in the hall, hardly less splendid than his wife, and they all went off in a coach that was very grand, if rather clumsy. Its shape reminded Elfrida of the coach which the fairy-godmother made for Cinderella out of the pumpkin, and she herself, as she peeped through the crowd of liveried servants to see it start, felt as much like Cinderella as anyone need wish to feel, and perhaps a little more. But she consoled herself by encouraging a secret feeling she had that something was bound to happen; and sure enough something did. And that is what I am going to tell you about. I own that I should like to tell you also what happened to Edred, but his part of the adventure was not really an adventure at all—though it was a thing that he will never forget as long as he remembers any magic happenings.

===Chinese===
教埃德雷德行礼的，其实并不是他的老师。可楼梯上一响起丝绸的窸窣声，他还是走出去，朝那朝他款款而下的雍容华贵，行了一个颇拿得出手的礼。埃尔弗丽达觉得最好在哥哥身旁行个屈膝礼。伊迪丝姑姑教过他们跳小步舞，而小步舞里配套的鞠躬与屈膝，此刻不知怎么正对路。楼梯上的夫人微微一笑，满心欢喜。她是位穿戴得美妙绝伦的夫人。她的紧身衣是黄缎的，绣得富丽；衬裙是织金的，带着条纹；长袍是红天鹅绒的，里子是黄纱，间着纯金的条纹。她系一条针绣花边的围裙，细工细作的皱领底下衬着白缎领片。而且她浑身珠宝，灼灼生辉。

「你真是个俊俏的侍童，我亲爱的儿子。」夫人说。「站到旁边去，我走过时替我牵住裙裾。还有你，亲爱的女儿，等你到了年纪，也会有裙裾，也会有个小侍童替你捧着。」

她款款前行，孩子们跟在后面。阿登勋爵在大厅里候着，气派几乎不输他的夫人，随后全家登上一辆马车出发。那马车很堂皇，只是嫌笨重了些。它的模样叫埃尔弗丽达想起仙女教母用南瓜给灰姑娘变的那一辆；她自己从一群穿号衣的仆人中间张望马车启程时，那份做灰姑娘的感觉，比谁想尝的都足，兴许还多上几分。可她自有宽心的法子：撺掇心底那点秘而不宣的预感——横竖准有事情要发生；果然就发生了。那正是我这就讲给你听的。我承认，我也想把埃德雷德的遭遇讲给你听，可他那一段历险实在算不得历险——虽说那件事他一辈子忘不了，只要他还记得任何一桩魔法奇遇。

===Original===
"We went to the King's house," he told Elfrida later. "Whitehall is the name. I should like to call my house Whitehall—if it wasn't called Arden Castle, you know. And there were thousands of servants, I should think, all much finer than you could dream of, and lords and ladies, and lots of things to eat, and bear-baiting and cockfighting in the garden."

"Cruel!" said Elfrida. "I hope you didn't look."

"A little I did," said Edred. "Boys have to be brave to bear sights of blood and horror, you know, in case of them growing up to be soldiers. But I liked the masque best. The Queen acted in it. There wasn't any talking, you know, only dressing up and dancing. It was something like the pantomime, but not so sparkly.
