And there was a sea with waves that moved all silvery, and panelled scenes, and dolphins and fishy things, and a great shell that opened, and the Queen and the ladies came out and danced, and I had a lot to eat, such rummy things, and then I fell asleep, and when I woke up the King himself was looking at me and saying I had a bonny face. Bonny means pretty. You'd think a King would know better, wouldn't you?"

This was all that Edred could find to tell. I could have told more, but one can't tell everything, and there is Elfrida's adventure to be told about.

===Chinese===
「我们去了国王的宅子，」他后来告诉埃尔弗丽达。「名叫白厅。我倒愿意管我自己的宅子叫白厅——要不是它已经叫了阿登城堡的话，你知道。里面的仆人我瞧着得有上千个，个个穿得比你梦得见的还讲究，还有伯爵啊、夫人啊，好些吃食，花园里还斗熊、斗鸡。」

「残忍！」埃尔弗丽达说。「但愿你没看。」

「看了一小会儿。」埃德雷德说。「男孩子得硬起心肠看血腥吓人的场面，你知道，万一将来长大要当兵呢。不过我最喜欢假面剧。王后还亲自登台演了呢。里头没有道白，你知道，光是装扮和跳舞。有点像哑剧滑稽戏，只是没那么闪亮。有一片海，浪一晃一晃全是银色的，还有一格一格的画板布景，海豚和各色鱼儿，一只张开的大贝壳，王后和夫人们从里头出来跳舞。我吃了好些东西，尽是些古怪玩意儿，后来我睡着了，醒来时国王他老人家正瞧着我，说我有张『俊』脸蛋。『俊』就是漂亮。你总当国王的谈吐该更讲究些，对不对？」

埃德雷德能搜肠刮肚说出来的就这么多。我本可以多讲些，可一个人不能什么都讲，何况埃尔弗丽达的历险还没讲呢。

===Original===
When the coach had disappeared in the mist and the mud—for the weather was anything but summer weather—Elfrida went upstairs again to the room where she had left the old nurse. She did not know where else to go.

"Sit thee down," said the nurse, "and sew on thy sampler."

There was the sampler, very fine indeed, in a large polished wood frame.

"I wish I needn't," said Elfrida, looking anxiously at the fine silks.

"Tut, tut," said the nurse, "how'll thee grow to be a lady if thou doesn't mind thy needle?"

"I'd much rather talk to you," said Elfrida coaxingly.

===Chinese===
马车消失在雾气和泥泞里之后——那天气压根儿算不得夏天——埃尔弗丽达又上楼回到她撇下老奶妈的那间屋。她不知道此外还能上哪儿去。

「你坐下，」奶妈说，「绣你的刺绣样本去。」

刺绣样本就在那儿，绣工着实精美，绷在一只磨得光亮的大木框里。

「我巴不得不绣呢。」埃尔弗丽达说着，忧心忡忡地望望那些精细的丝线。

「啧啧，」奶妈说，「你不把针线当回事，往后怎么长成位淑女？」

「我宁可同您说话。」埃尔弗丽达哄着说。

===Original===
"Thou canst chatter as well as sew," the nurse said, "as well I know to my cost. Would that thy needle flew so fast as thy tongue! Sit thee down, and if the little tree be done by dinnertime thou shalt have leave to see thy Cousin Richard."

"I suppose," thought Elfrida, taking up the needle, "that I am fond of my Cousin Richard."

The sewing was difficult, and hurt her eyes, but she persevered. Presently someone called the nurse, and Elfrida was left alone. Then she stopped persevering. "Whatever *is* the good," she asked herself, "of working at a sampler that you haven't time to finish, and that would be worn out, anyhow, years and years before you were born? The Elfrida who's doing that sampler is the same age as me, and born the same day," she reflected. And then she wondered what was the date, and what was the year. She was still wondering, and sticking the needle idly in and out of one hole, without letting it take the silk with it, when there was a sort of clatter on the stairs, the door burst open, and in came a jolly boy of about her own age.

"Thy task done?" he cried. "Mine too. Old Parrot-nose kept me hard at it, but I thought of thee, and for once I did all his biddings. So now we are free. Come, play ball in the garden." This, Elfrida concluded, must be Cousin Dick, and she decided at once that she *was* fond of him.

===Chinese===
「你手上绣得，嘴上也说得，」奶妈说，「这个我有数，吃过亏的。但愿你的针有你舌头一半快！坐下吧，要是到开饭时把那棵小树绣完了，就准你去见你的理查德表哥（Cousin Richard）。」

「我大概，」埃尔弗丽达拿起针，心里想，「是喜欢我这位理查德表哥的。」

针线活难，还磨眼睛，可她咬牙撑着。不一会儿有人唤走奶妈，埃尔弗丽达独自留下了。她随即不撑了。「这到底图个什么，」她问自己，「绣一幅你没工夫绣完的样本？何况不等生下你，它多少多少年前就早磨坏了？如今绣这样本的埃尔弗丽达同我一样大，同我同一天生。」她这样寻思。接着她又纳闷，今天是几号，今年是哪一年。她还纳闷着，一边把针懒洋洋地往同一个针眼里戳进戳出，不叫它带丝线，忽然楼梯上一阵噔噔响，门砰地开了，闯进来一个快快活活的男孩，年纪同她相仿。

「你的功课做完了？」他嚷道。「我的也完了。老鹦鹉鼻子押着我埋头苦干，可我想着你要来，这一回他吩咐的我全照办了。这下咱们自由啦。来，上花园玩球去。」这位，埃尔弗丽达断定，准是迪克表哥，而且她当场认定，自己*确实*喜欢他。

===Original===
There was a big and beautiful garden behind the house. The children played ball there, and they ran in the box alleys, and played hide-and-seek among the cut trees and stone seats, and statues and fountains.

Old Parrot-nose, who was Cousin Richard's tutor, and was dressed in black, and looked as though he had been eating lemons and vinegar, sat on a seat and watched them, or walked up and down the flagged terrace with his thumb in a dull-looking book.

When they stopped their game to rest on a stone step, leaning against a stone seat, old Parrot-nose walked very softly up behind the seat, and stood there where they could not see him and listened. Listening is very dishonourable, as we all know, but in those days tutors did not always think it necessary to behave honourably to their pupils.

I always have thought, and I always shall think, that it was the eavesdropping of that tiresome old tutor, Mr. Parados—or Parrot-nose—which caused all the mischief. But Elfrida has always believed, and always will believe, that the disaster was caused by her knowing too much history. That is why she is so careful to make sure that no misfortune shall ever happen on *that* account, any way. That is one of the reasons why she never takes a history prize at school. "You never know," she says. And, in fact, when it comes to a question in an historical examination, she never *does* know.

===Chinese===
宅子后面有一座又大又美的花园。孩子们在园里玩球，在黄杨树篱夹成的巷子里奔跑，在修剪齐整的树木、石座、雕像和喷泉之间捉迷藏。

老鹦鹉鼻子是理查德表哥的老师，穿一身黑，那副神情活像刚吞了柠檬就着醋。他坐在一张座位上看管他们，或是大拇指掐着一本灰扑扑的书，在石板铺的台地上踱来踱去。

孩子们停下游戏，靠着一领石座在一级石阶上歇脚，老鹦鹉鼻子蹑手蹑脚绕到座位后头，站在他们瞧不见的地方听。偷听顶不光彩，这咱们都知道；可在那个年头，做老师的并不总认为对学生也须讲信义。

我一向这么想，往后也永远这么想：闯下这全部祸端的，正是那个讨嫌的老教师帕拉多斯先生（Mr. Parados）——也就是鹦鹉鼻子——的偷听。可埃尔弗丽达一向相信、往后也永远相信，这场灾祸是她历史知道得太多招来的。所以她才那样小心翼翼，务求再不因*那种*缘故摊上什么不幸。这也是她在学校从不拿历史奖的原因之一。「谁说得准呢。」她说。而事实是，一到历史考试的问题跟前，她就真的什么都不知道了。

===Original===
This was how it happened. Elfrida, now that she was no longer running about in the garden, remembered the question that she had been asking herself over the embroidery frame, and it now seemed sensible to ask the question of someone who could answer it. So she said—

"I say, Cousin Richard, what day is it?"

Elfrida understood him to say that it was the fifth of November.

"Is it really?" she said. "Then it's Guy Fawkes day. Do you have fireworks?" And in pure lightness of heart began to hum—

> "Please to remember
> The Fifth of November
> The gunpowder treason and plot.
> I see no reason
> Why gunpowder treason
> Should ever be forgot."

===Chinese===
事情是这样发生的。埃尔弗丽达这会儿不在花园里跑了，便又想起她在绣花框上自问的那个问题；如今拿它去问一个答得上的人，似乎正当其时。于是她说——

「我说，理查德表哥，今天是什么日子？」

埃尔弗丽达听他说，是十一月五日。

「真是吗？」她说。「那今天就是盖伊·福克斯日呀。你们放不放烟花？」她一时兴起，满心轻快，便哼起来——

> 「请记住呀，
> 十一月五日，
> 火药叛国的阴谋。
> 我看不出有什么理由，
> 火药叛国的阴谋
> 竟会被忘个干净。」

===Original===
"’Tis not a merry song, cousin," said Cousin Richard, "nor a safe one. ’Tis best not to sing of treason."

"But it didn't come off, you know, and he's always burnt in the end," said Elfrida.

"Are there more verses?" Cousin Dick asked.

"No."

"I wonder what treason the ballad deals with?" said the boy.

===Chinese===
「这不是支快活的歌，表妹，」理查德表哥说，「也不是支稳当的歌。顶好别唱叛国的事。」

「可它后来没成啊，你知道，而且到头来他年年都要被烧掉呢。」埃尔弗丽达说。

「还有别的段落没有？」迪克表哥问。

「没有。」

「我纳闷这歌谣说的是哪桩叛国？」男孩说。

===Original===
"Don't you know?" It was then that Elfrida made the mistake of showing off her historical knowledge. "*I* know. And I know some of the names of the conspirators, too, and who they wanted to kill, and everything."

"Tell me," said Cousin Richard idly.

"The King hadn't been fair to the Catholics, you know," said Elfrida, full of importance, "so a lot of them decided to kill him and the Houses of Parliament. They made a plot—there were a whole lot of them in it. They said Lord Arden was, but he wasn't, and some of them were to pretend to be hunting, and to seize the Princess Elizabeth and proclaim her Queen, and the rest were to blow the Houses of Parliament up when the King went to open them."

"I never heard this tale from my tutor," said Cousin Richard laughing. "Proceed, cousin."

"Well, Mr. Piercy took a house next the Parliament House, and they dug a secret passage to the vaults under the Parliament Houses; and they put three dozen casks of gunpowder there and covered them with faggots. And they would have been all blown up, only Mr. Tresham wrote to his relation, Lord Monteagle, that they were going to blow up the King and—"

===Chinese===
「你不知道吗？」就在这时，埃尔弗丽达犯下了卖弄历史知识的错处。「*我*知道。我还知道几个同谋者的名字，知道他们要害谁，什么都知道。」

「讲来听听。」理查德表哥懒洋洋地说。

「国王待天主教徒不公道，你知道，」埃尔弗丽达煞有介事地说，「于是他们中间一批人决定把他杀掉，连议会两院一锅端。他们订下一条计——掺和进去的人可不少。人家还说阿登勋爵也在内呢，其实他不在。有的人打算装作打猎，趁机捉住伊丽莎白公主，立她做女王；其余的人呢，等国王去主持议会开幕，就把议会大厦炸上天。」

「这样的事我从不曾听我老师讲过。」理查德表哥笑道。「往下讲，表妹。」

「是这样：皮尔西先生（Mr. Piercy）在议会大厦旁边赁了座宅子，他们挖了条暗道，一直通到议会大厦底下的地窖；他们在那儿搁了三打桶火药，上头盖满柴捆。本来他们全都要给炸上天的，谁知特雷沙姆先生（Mr. Tresham）给他亲戚蒙特伊格尔勋爵（Lord Monteagle）写了封信，说他们要把国王炸死，还有——」

===Original===
"What King?" said Cousin Richard.

"King James the First," said Elfrida. "Why—what—" for Cousin Richard had sprung to his feet, and old Parrot-nose had Elfrida by the wrist.

He sat down on the seat and drew her gently till she stood in front of him—gently, but it was like the hand of iron in the velvet glove (of which, no doubt, you have often read).

"Now, Mistress Arden," he said softly, "tell me over again this romance that you tell your cousin."

Elfrida told it.

"And where did you hear this pretty story?" he asked.

===Chinese===
「什么国王？」理查德表哥说。

「詹姆斯一世国王。」埃尔弗丽达说。「怎么——什么——」原来理查德表哥已一跃而起，老鹦鹉鼻子已攥住了埃尔弗丽达的手腕。

他在座位上坐下，把她轻轻拽到跟前，直到她站在他面前——轻轻的，可那就像天鹅绒手套里的一只铁手（这你无疑在书里常读到）。

「好了，阿登小姐，」他轻声说，「把你讲给你表哥听的这段传奇，再给我原原本本讲一遍。」

埃尔弗丽达讲了。

「这么好听的故事，你是打哪儿听来的？」他问。

===Original===
"Where are we now?" gasped Elfrida, who was beginning to understand.

"Here—in the garden—where else?" said Cousin Richard, who seemed to understand nothing of the matter.

"Here—in my custody," said the tutor, who thought he understood everything. "Now tell me all—every name, every particular—or it will be the worse for thee and thy father."

"Come, sir," said Cousin Richard, "you frighten my cousin. It is but a tale she told. She is always merry, and full of many inventions."

"It is a tale she shall tell again before those of higher power than I," said the tutor, in a thoroughly disagreeable way, and his hand tightened on Elfrida's wrist.

"But—but—it's *history*," cried Elfrida, in despair. "It's in all the books."

===Chinese===
「咱们这会儿是在哪儿？」埃尔弗丽达喘着气问，她开始明白了。

「在这儿——在花园里——还能在哪儿？」理查德表哥说，他对这事似乎一窍不通。

「在这儿——在我的看管之下。」家庭教师说，他自以为什么都通了。「全招出来——每个名字、每个细节——不然的话，于你、于你父亲都不好看。」

「得了，先生，」理查德表哥说，「您吓着我表妹了。那不过是她讲的一个故事。她一向快活，满肚子稀奇点子。」

「这故事她还得再讲一遍，讲给比我有权势的人听。」家庭教师说，那样子讨厌透顶，攥着埃尔弗丽达手腕的手也收紧了。

「可是——可是——那是*历史*啊！」埃尔弗丽达绝望地喊。「所有的书里都写着呢。」

===Original===
"Which books?" he asked keenly.

"I don't know—all of them," she sullenly answered; sullenly, because she now really did understand just the sort of adventure in which her unusual knowledge of history, and, to do her justice, her almost equally unusual desire to show off, had landed her.

"Now," said the hateful tutor, for such Elfrida felt him to be, "tell me the names of the conspirators."

"It *can't* do any harm," Elfrida told herself. "This is James the First's time, and I'm in it. But it's three hundred years ago all the same, and it all *has* happened, and it can't make any difference what I say, so I'd better tell all the names I know."

===Chinese===
「哪些书？」他尖利地问。

「不知道——所有的书。」她绷着脸答道；绷着脸，是因为她这会儿真的明白了，她那不同寻常的历史知识——还有，说句公道话，她那几乎同样不同寻常的卖弄欲——把她陷进的是怎样一种历险。

「好了，」那可恨的教师说——埃尔弗丽达已经觉得他可恨了——「说出同谋者的名字。」

「不可能有什么害处，」埃尔弗丽达对自己说。「这是詹姆斯一世的时代，我在这里头。可它同时又是三百年前，一切*都*已经发生过，我说什么都改不了什么，所以我顶好把知道的名字都说出来。」

===Original===
The hateful tutor shook her.

"Yes, all right," she said; and to herself she added, "It's only a sort of dream; I may as well tell." Yet when she opened her mouth to tell all the names she could remember of the conspirators of the poor old Gunpowder Plot that didn't come off, all those years ago, she found herself not telling those names at all. Instead, she found herself saying—

"I'm not going to tell. I don't care what you do to me. I'm sorry I said anything about it. It's all nonsense—I mean, it's only history, and you ought to be ashamed of yourself, listening behind doors—I mean, *out* of doors behind stone seats, when people are talking nonsense to their own cousins."

===Chinese===
那可恨的教师摇撼她。

「是，好吧。」她说；心里又添了一句，「这不过是一场梦罢了；说不说都一样。」可等到她张开嘴，要把那桩多年以前、终究没能得逞的可怜的老火药阴谋的同谋者名字、凡记得的一古脑儿说出来时，她发现自己说的压根不是那些名字。她说出来的是——

「我不说。随你们把我怎么样。我后悔提起了这件事。那全是胡说八道——我是说，那不过是历史，你该为自己害臊，躲在门后偷听——我是说，在*门*外头、石头座位后头偷听，听人家跟自己的表哥讲胡话。」

===Original===
Elfrida does not remember very exactly what happened after this. She was furiously angry, and when you are furiously angry things get mixed and tangled up in a sort of dreadful red mist. She only remembers that the tutor was very horrid, and twisted her wrists to make her tell, and she screamed and tried to kick him; that Cousin Richard, who did not scream, did, on the other hand, succeed in kicking the tutor; that she was dragged indoors and shut up in a room without a window, so that it was quite dark.

"If only I'd got Edred here," she said to herself, with tears of rage and mortification, "I'd try to make some poetry and get the Mouldiwarp to come and fetch us away. But it's no use till he comes home."

===Chinese===
这以后的事，埃尔弗丽达记不很清了。她气疯了；人气疯的时候，种种事情会在一股可怕的红雾里搅作一团、缠作一团。她只记得那教师凶得吓人，拧她的手腕逼她招，她尖叫着、踢他；理查德表哥没有尖叫，倒是当真把那教师踢着了；随后她被拖进屋里，锁进一间没有窗的屋子，黑黢黢的。

「要是埃德雷德这会儿在跟前，」她又是气、又是臊，噙着泪自言自语，「我就试着作几句诗，请穆迪沃普来把咱们接走。可他不回家，说什么也白搭。」

===Original===
When he did come home—after the bear-baiting and the cockfighting and the banquet and the masque—Lord and Lady Arden came with him, of course. And they found their house occupied by an armed guard, and in the dark little room a pale child exhausted with weeping, who assured them again and again that it was all nonsense, it was only history, and she hadn't meant to tell—indeed she hadn't.

"Thou'st done no harm," said Lord Arden; "a pack of silly tales. Tomorrow I'll see my Lord Salisbury and prick this silly bubble. Go thou to bed, sweetheart," he said to his wife, "and let the little maid lie with thee—she is all a-tremble with tears and terrors. Tomorrow, my Lord Secretary shall teach these popinjays their place, and Arden House shall be empty of them, and we shall laugh at this fine piece of work that a solemn marplot has made out of a name or two and a young child's fancies. By tomorrow night all will be well, and we shall lie down in peace."

===Chinese===
等他真回了家——斗熊、斗鸡、宴席、假面剧都过去了——阿登勋爵和阿登夫人自然也一同回来。他们发现自己家里进驻了一队持械卫士，那间黑黑的小屋里，一个哭得没了力气的孩子面色苍白，正一遍又一遍地向他们保证：那全是胡说，那不过是历史，她本不是有意要说的——实在不是。

「你没有招灾惹祸，」阿登勋爵说，「一堆蠢话罢了。明天我去见索尔兹伯里大老爷（my Lord Salisbury），把这个蠢泡泡戳破。你去睡吧，心肝，」他对妻子说，「让小丫头挨着你躺——她又哭又吓，浑身直哆嗦。明天，秘书大老爷自会教这帮多嘴鹦鹉认清自己的斤两，阿登宅邸里便再没有他们，这场精彩文章——一个煞有介事的成事不足败事有余的家伙，凭一两个名字、一个小孩子的痴念做出来的文章——咱们只管笑话。到明儿夜里，一切都会安好，咱们安安稳稳睡下。」

===Original===
But when tomorrow night came it had, as all nights have, the day's work behind it. Lord Arden and his lady and the little children lay, not in Arden House in Soho, not in Arden Castle on the downs by the sea, but in the Tower of London, charged with high treason and awaiting their trial.

For my Lord Salisbury had gone to those vaults under the Houses of Parliament, and had found that bold soldier of fortune, Guy Fawkes, with his dark eyes, his dark lantern, and his dark intent; and the names of those in the conspiracy had been given up, and King James was saved, and the Parliaments—but the Catholic gentlemen whom he had deceived, and who had turned against him and his deceits, were face to face with the rack and the scaffold.

And I can't explain it at all—because, of course, Elfrida knew as well as I do that it all happened three hundred years ago—or, if you prefer to put it that way, that it had never happened, and that anyway, it was Mr. Tresham's letter to Lord Monteagle, and not Elfrida's singing of that silly rhyme, that had brought the Ardens and all these other gentlemen to the Tower and to the shadow of death. And yet she felt that it was *she* who had betrayed them. She felt also that if she had betrayed a base plot, she ought to be glad, and she was not glad. She felt—and called herself—a sneak. She had taken advantage of having been born so much later than all these people, and of having been rather good at history to give away the lives of all these nobles and gentlemen. That they were traitors to King and Parliament made no manner of difference. It was she, as she felt but too bitterly, who was the traitor. And in the thick-walled room in the Tower, where the name of Raleigh was still fresh in its carving, Elfrida lay awake, long after Lady Arden and Edred were sleeping peacefully, and hated herself, calling herself a Traitor, a Coward, and an Utter Duffer.

===Chinese===
可等到明儿夜里真的来了，它同所有的夜一样，身后已经驮着白天干下的事。阿登勋爵和他的夫人以及两个小家伙躺下的地方，不是索霍的阿登宅邸，不是海边丘原上的阿登城堡，而是伦敦塔，头上顶着叛国大罪的罪名，等候开审。

原来索尔兹伯里大老爷已经去过议会大厦底下的那些地窖，找到了那个胆大包天的亡命武人盖伊·福克斯，连同他那双黑眼睛、他那盏遮光灯、他那肚里黑漆漆的图谋；同谋者的名字都一一供了出来，国王詹姆斯得救了，议会也得救了——只是那些受他欺瞒、又起而反抗他和他的欺诈的天主教绅士们，如今面对面站着的是拉肢刑架和断头台。

这一节我全然解释不来——因为埃尔弗丽达自然同我一样明白，这一切都发生在三百年前；或者，你爱那样说也成：它压根儿不曾发生；而且无论如何，把阿登一家和所有这些绅士送进伦敦塔、送到死亡阴影跟前的，是特雷沙姆先生写给蒙特伊格尔勋爵的信，不是埃尔弗丽达哼的那支傻歌谣。可她还是觉得，出卖了他们的正是*她*。她还觉得，倘若她出卖的是一桩卑鄙的阴谋，她本该高兴才是，可她高兴不起来。她觉得——并且骂自己是个——告密鬼。她仗着自己生得比这许多人晚那么许多年，仗着自己历史学得挺不赖，就把所有这些贵族绅士的性命白白断送了。他们对国王和议会不忠，这一点儿也改变不了什么。恰恰是她——她痛切地感到——才是那个叛徒。在伦敦塔那间厚墙的屋子里，壁上刻着的「雷利」名字还清晰如新，埃尔弗丽达久久醒着；阿登夫人和埃德雷德早已睡得安稳，她却恨自己，骂自己是叛徒、懦夫、十成十的大笨蛋。

## III In Boney’s Times / 在波尼的年代

===Original===
Edred crept back to his stool, and took his corner of the marble-backed book of Dr. Watts with fingers that trembled. If you are inclined to despise him, consider that it was his first real adventure. Even in ordinary life, and in the time he naturally lived in, nothing particularly thrilling had ever happened to happen to him until he became Lord Arden and explored Arden Castle. And now he and Elfrida had not only discovered a disused house and a wonderful garret with chests in it, but had been clothed by mysterious pigeon noises in clothes belonging to another age. But, you will say, pigeon noises can’t clothe you in anything, whatever it belongs to. Well, that was just what Edred told himself at the time. And yet it was certain that they did. This sort of thing it was that made the whole business so mysterious. Further, he and his sister had managed somehow to go back a hundred years. He knew this quite well, though he had no evidence but that one sheet of newspaper. He felt it, as they say, in his bones. I don’t know how it was, perhaps the air felt a hundred years younger. Shepherds and country people can tell the hour of night by the feel of the air. So perhaps very sensitive people can tell the century by much the same means. These, of course, would be the people to whom adventures in times past or present would be likely to happen. We must always consider what is likely, especially when we are reading stories about unusual things.

===Chinese===
埃德雷德蹑手蹑脚溜回自己的凳子，伸出打颤的手指，捧起华兹博士（Dr. Watts）那本大理石纹封面的大书，占住自己的一角。你若是要小看他，且想想这可是他头一遭真正的奇遇。就算在平平常常的日子里，在他本来生活的那个时代，直到他当上阿登勋爵、探过阿登城堡之前，也从没赶上过什么真正叫人心跳的事。可如今，他和埃尔弗丽达不但发现了一座久无人住的宅子和一间满是箱子的奇妙阁楼，还叫神秘的鸽子咕咕声给换上了一身另一个时代的衣裳。不过你准会说，鸽子咕咕声可没法给人换什么衣裳，不管那衣裳属于哪个时代。可不是嘛，埃德雷德当时也正是这么对自己说的。然而千真万确，它就是换上了。正是这一类事情，让整桩事变得如此神秘。还不止呢，他和他姐姐还不知怎么就回到了一百年前。这一点他清楚得很，虽然除了那一页报纸，他拿不出任何凭据。他是——像人们说的那样——打骨头缝里觉出来的。我说不上是怎么回事，兴许是那空气闻着比一百岁还要年轻些。牧羊人和乡下人凭空气的感觉能说出夜里的时辰。那么极敏感的人，兴许也大可以用差不多的法子说出眼下是哪个世纪。这样的人，自然也正是过去的或如今的奇遇最可能落到头上的人。咱们凡事都得掂量掂量合不合常理，读不寻常的故事时尤其如此。

===Original===
“I say,” Edred whispered presently, “we’ve got back to 1807. That paper says so.”

“I know,” Elfrida whispered. So she must have had more of that like-shepherds-telling-the-time-of-night feeling than even her brother.

“I wish I could remember what was happening in history in 1807,” said Elfrida, “but we never get past Edward IV. We always have to go back to the Saxons because of the new girls.”

“But we’re not in history. We’re at Arden,” Edred said.

“We *are* in history. It’ll be awful not even knowing who’s king,” said Elfrida; and then the stiff old lady looked up over very large spectacles with thick silver rims, and said—

“Silence!”

===Chinese===
「我说，」过了一会儿，埃德雷德悄声道，「咱们回到 1807 年了。那报纸上就这么写的。」

「我知道。」埃尔弗丽达悄声道。可见她那股子牧羊人夜里凭感觉报时似的直觉，比她弟弟还要灵些。

「但愿我记得起来 1807 年历史上正出着什么事，」埃尔弗丽达说，「可咱们的历史课从来学不到爱德华四世往后。老是因为新来的女生，又得回头从撒克逊人学起。」

「可咱们这会儿不是在历史书里，是在阿登啊。」埃德雷德说。

「咱们*就是*在历史里。连国王是谁都不知道，那可糟透了。」埃尔弗丽达说。这时那位神情刻板的老太太从厚厚的银边大眼镜上方抬起眼来，说道——

「肃静！」

===Original===
Presently she laid down the *Times* and got ink and paper—no envelopes—and began to write. She was finishing a letter, the large sheet was almost covered on one side. When she had covered it quite, she turned it round and began to write across it. She used a white goose-quill pen. The inkstand was of china, with gold scrolls and cupids and wreaths of roses painted on it. On one side was the inkwell, on the other a thing like a china pepper-pot, and in front a tray for the pens and sealing-wax to lie in. Both children now knew their unpleasant poem by heart; so they watched the old lady, who was grandmother to the children she supposed them to be. When she had finished writing she sprinkled some dust out of the pepper-pot over the letter to dry the ink. There was no blotting-paper to be seen. Then she folded the sheet, and sealed it with a silver seal from the pen-tray, and wrote the address on the outside. Then—

===Chinese===
不多时她放下《泰晤士报》，取来墨水和纸——没有信封——动笔写起来。她是在给一封信收尾，大张信纸的一面已经快写满。等这一面写得满满当当，她把纸转了个方向，在原先的字行上横着又写起来。她用的是一支白鹅毛笔。墨水台是瓷的，上面画着金色卷涡花纹、小爱神和玫瑰花环。一头是墨水池，另一头是个像瓷胡椒瓶的物件，前头还有个浅盘，搁鹅毛笔和火漆用。两个孩子这时已把那首讨厌的诗背得烂熟，便只管望着那位老太太——她只当这两个孩子是自己的孙儿孙女。她写完了，从那胡椒瓶模样的物件里往信纸上撒了些细砂，好吸干墨迹。哪儿也不见吸墨纸的影子。然后她把信纸折起来，用浅盘里的银印章打了火漆，在外头写上地址。然后——

===Original===
“Have you got your task?” she asked.

“Here it is,” said Elfrida, holding up the book.

“No impudence, miss!” said the grandmother sternly. “You very well know that I mean, have you got it by rote yet? And you know, too, that you should say ‘ma’am’ whenever you address me.”

“Yes, ma’am,” said Elfrida; and this was taken to mean that she knew her task.

“Then come and say it. No, no; you know better than that. Feet in the first position, hands behind you, heads straight, and do not fidget with your feet.”

So then first Elfrida and then Edred recited the melancholy verses.

===Chinese===
「功课带来了吗？」她问。

「在这儿呢。」埃尔弗丽达说着，举起那本书。

「不许顶嘴，小姐！」祖母厉声说。「你心里明白得很，我问的是你们背熟了没有。你也该知道，同我说话就得称『夫人』。」

「是，夫人。」埃尔弗丽达说。这句就被当作她功课已经会了的意思。

「那就过来背。不，不；你知道不该那样站。脚摆第一位置，手放在背后，头摆正，脚不许扭来扭去。」

于是先是埃尔弗丽达、后是埃德雷德，把那几节忧郁的诗背了一遍。

===Original===
“Now,” said the old lady, “you may go and play in the garden.”

“Mayn’t we take your letter to the post?” Elfrida asked.

“Yes; but you are not to stay in the ‘George’ bar, mind, not even if Mrs. Skinner should invite you. Just hand her the letter and come out. Shut the door softly, and do not shuffle with your feet.”

“Yes, ma’am,” said Elfrida; and on that they got out.

“They’ll find us out—bound to,” said Edred; “we don’t know a single thing about anything. I don’t know where the ‘George’ is, or where to get a stamp, or anything.”

===Chinese===
「好了，」老太太说，「你们可以到花园里玩去了。」

「我们不可以替您把信送去寄吗？」埃尔弗丽达问。

「可以；可你们不许在『乔治』的酒柜边逗留，记住了，哪怕斯金纳太太（Mrs. Skinner）请你们也不行。把信交到她手里就出来。关门要轻，走路不许拖着脚蹭地。」

「是，夫人。」埃尔弗丽达说。说完他们就出来了。

「他们准会看出破绽——一定会的。」埃德雷德说，「咱们什么事都不知道。『乔治』在哪儿我不知道，邮票上哪儿弄也不知道，什么都不知道。」

===Original===
“We must find someone we can trust, and tell them the truth,” said Elfrida.

“There isn’t anyone,” said Edred, “that I’d trust. You can’t trust the sort of people who stick this sort of baby flummery round a chap’s neck.” He crumpled his starched frill with hot, angry fingers.

“Mine prickles all round, too,” Elfrida reminded him, “and it’s lower, and you get bigger as you go down, so it prickles more of me than yours does you.”

“Let’s go back to the attic and try and get back into our own time. I expect we just got in to the wrong door, don’t you? Let’s go now.”

“Oh, no,” said Elfrida. “How dreadfully dull! Why, we shall see all sorts of things, and be top in history for the rest of our lives. Let’s go through with it.”

===Chinese===
「咱们得找个信得过的人，把实情告诉她。」埃尔弗丽达说。

「没有一个我信得过的。」埃德雷德说，「把这种娃娃气的花边玩意儿往人家小伙子脖子上围的人，你就没法信任。」他用气得发热的手指，揉搓着胸前上过浆的褶边。

「我这儿也是一圈都扎人。」埃尔弗丽达提醒他，「而且我这圈位置更低，人往下身越宽，扎着我的地方就比扎着你的多。」

「咱们回阁楼去吧，想法子回到咱们自己的时代。我看咱们不过是走错了门，你说是不是？这就去吧。」

「哦，不。」埃尔弗丽达说，「那多没劲啊！咱们能见到各式各样的新鲜事，往后一辈子历史课都考第一呢。咱们把这趟走到底吧。」

===Original===
“Do you remember which door it was—the attic, I mean?” Edred suddenly asked. “Was it the third on the left?”

“I don’t know. But we can easily find it when we want it.”

“I’d like to know *now*,” said Edred obstinately. “You never know when you *are* going to want things. Mrs. Honeysett says you ought always to be able to lay your hand on anything you want the moment you *do* want it. I should like to be quite certain about being able to lay our hands on our own clothes. Suppose someone goes and tidies them up. You know what people are.”

“All right,” said Elfrida, “we’ll go and tidy them up ourselves. It won’t take a minute.”

===Chinese===
「你还记得是哪扇门吗——我是说阁楼那扇？」埃德雷德忽然问，「是左边第三扇吗？」

「不知道。可咱们要用的时候，一找就能找到。」

「我*现在*就想知道。」埃德雷德倔头倔脑地说，「你永远说不准什么时候*偏偏*就要用什么。哈尼塞特太太（Mrs. Honeysett）就常说，凡是东西，一等你*真*要用，就得马上伸手摸得着才好。咱们自己的衣裳能不能伸手就摸着，我想弄个十拿九稳。万一叫谁去收拾了怎么办。人是怎么样你也知道。」

「好吧，」埃尔弗丽达说，「咱们自己去把它们收拾妥帖。费不了一分钟。」

===Original===
It would certainly not have taken five—if things had been as the children expected. They raced up the stairs to the corridor where the prints were.

“It’s not the first door, I’m certain,” said Edred, so they opened the second. But it was not that either. So then they tried all the doors in turn, even opening, at last, the first one of all. And it was not that, even. *It was not any of them.*

“We’ve come to the wrong corridor,” said the boy.

“It’s the only one,” said the girl. And it was. For though they hunted all over the house, upstairs and downstairs, and tried every door, the door of the attic they could not find again. And what is more, when they came to count up, there were fifty-seven doors without it.

===Chinese===
要是事情真如两个孩子料想的那样，肯定连五分钟也用不了。他们一路跑上楼梯，奔到挂版画的那条走廊。

「准不是头一扇门。」埃德雷德说。于是他们推开第二扇。可也不是。接着他们挨个儿把所有的门都试了一遍，到末了连头一扇也打开了。连那扇也不是。*哪一扇都不是。*

「咱们走错走廊了。」男孩说。

「全宅子就这一条。」女孩说。也确实只有这一条。因为尽管他们楼上楼下翻遍了整座宅子，挨个儿试了每一扇门，那扇阁楼的门却再也找不着了。还不止呢，待他们数了数，不算那扇门，一共五十七扇。

===Original===
“Fifty-five, fifty-six, fifty-seven,” said Elfrida, and ended in a sob—“the door’s gone! We shall have to stay here for ever and ever. Oh, I want auntie—I do, I do!”

She sat down abruptly on a small green mat in front of the last door, which happened to be that of the kitchen.

Edred says he did not cry too. And if what he says is true, Elfrida’s crying must have been louder than was usual with her; for the kitchen door opened, and the two children were caught up in two fat arms and hurried into a pleasant kitchen, where bright brass and copper pots hung on the walls, and between a large fire and a large meat screen a leg of mutton turned round and round with nobody to help it.

===Chinese===
「五十五、五十六、五十七。」埃尔弗丽达数到末了，抽噎起来——「门没了！咱们只好永远永远待在这儿了。哦，我要姑姑——我真的要，真的要！」

她一屁股坐在末了一扇门前的一小块绿色地垫上，那扇门不偏不倚，正是厨房的门。

据埃德雷德自己说，他可没哭。他这话若是真的，那埃尔弗丽达这回哭得准比平时响；因为厨房的门开了，两个孩子被两条胖胳膊一捞，急急忙忙抱进了一间舒舒服服的厨房。厨房墙上挂着亮闪闪的黄铜锅和紫铜锅，一大炉火同一面大挡肉屏之间，一条羊腿自顾自滴溜溜转个不停，用不着谁搭手。

===Original===
“Hold your noise,” said the owner of the fat arms, who now proved to be a very stout woman in a chocolate-coloured print gown sprigged with blue roses. She had a large linen apron and a cap with flappy frills, and between the frills just such another good, kind, jolly face as Mrs. Honeysett’s own. “Here, stop your mouths,” she said, “or your granny’ll be after you—to say nothing of Boney. Stop your crying, do, and see what cookie’s got for you.”

She opened a tin canister and picked out two lumps of brown stuff that looked like sand—about the size and shape of prunes they were.

“What’s that?” Edred asked.

“Drabbit me,” said the cook, “what a child it is! Not know sugar when he sees it! Well, well, Master Edred, what next, I should like to know?”

===Chinese===
「别嚷嚷了。」那双胖胳膊的主人说。原来是个特胖特胖的女人，穿一条巧克力色的印花布裙，上头印着蓝玫瑰花枝。她系一条大亚麻围裙，戴一顶耷拉着花边的帽子，花边当中露出的那张脸，又和善又乐呵，跟哈尼塞特太太那张脸活像一个模子。「来，闭上嘴，」她说，「不然你们奶奶要来收拾你们了——这还没算波尼（Boney，当年英国人对拿破仑的谑称）呢。别哭了，乖，瞧瞧老厨娘给你们拿了什么。」

她打开一只铁罐，拣出两块褐色的、看着像沙子的东西——大小形状都跟西梅干差不多。

「那是什么？」埃德雷德问。

「我的老天，」厨娘说，「这孩子真是的！见了糖都不认得！哎，哎，埃德雷德少爷，接下来还要认不得什么，我倒想听听？」

===Original===
The children took the lumps and sucked them. They were of sugar, sure enough, but the sugar had a strong, coarse taste behind its sweetness, and if the children had really not been quite extra polite and kind they would have followed the promptings of Nature and—But, of course, they knew that this would be both disgusting and ungrateful. So they got the sugar down somehow, while cook beamed at them with a wide, kind smile between her cap-frills, and two hands, as big as little beefsteak puddings, on her hips.

“Now, no more crybabying,” she said; “run along and play.”

“We’ve got to take granny’s letter to post,” said Edred, “and we don’t—”

“Cook,” said Elfrida, on a sudden impulse, “can you keep a secret?”

===Chinese===
两个孩子接过糖块，含在嘴里嘬。确确实实是糖，可那甜味后头压着一股又冲又粗的味道，倘若两个孩子不是格外有礼、格外和气，早顺着天性行事，把它吐——当然，他们明白那样做又恶心又忘恩负义。于是他们好歹把糖咽了下去，厨娘呢，帽花边当中绽开一脸宽厚和善的笑，两只像小号牛肉布丁一般大的手叉在腰上，笑眯眯望着他们。

「好了，别再哭鼻子了。」她说，「去吧，玩儿去。」

「我们还得把奶奶的信送去寄呢。」埃德雷德说，「可我们不知道——」

「厨娘，」埃尔弗丽达一时冲动，开口道，「您能守住秘密吗？」

===Original===
“Can’t I?” said the cook. “Haven’t I kept the secret of how furmety’s made, and Bakewell pies and all? There’s no furmety to hold a candle to mine in this country, as well you know.”

“We don’t know *anything*,” said Elfrida; “that’s just it. And we daren’t let granny know how much we don’t know. Something’s happened to us, so that we can’t remember anything that happened more than an hour ago.”

“Bless me,” said the cook, “don’t you remember old cookie giving you the baked apple-dumplings when you were sent to bed without your suppers a week come Thursday?”

“No,” said Elfrida; “but I’m sure you did. Only what are we to do?”

===Chinese===
「那还用问？」厨娘说，「牛奶麦糊（furmety）是怎么做的，还有贝克韦尔馅饼（Bakewell pies）那些个，我不是都守得严严实实？方圆这一带，谁的牛奶麦糊也傍不上我的边儿，你们又不是不知道。」

「我们*什么都*不知道。」埃尔弗丽达说，「毛病正出在这儿。我们不敢让奶奶知道我们有多不知道。我们出了点事，一个钟头以前的事，一概记不起来了。」

「哎哟我的天，」厨娘说，「上上个礼拜四你们没吃晚饭就被打发上床，老厨娘给你们烤苹果布丁吃，也不记得了？」

「不记得了。」埃尔弗丽达说，「可我信您准给过。只是我们该怎么办呢？」

===Original===
“You’re not deceiving poor cookie, are you now, like you did about the French soldiers being hid in the windmill, upsetting all the village like you did?”

“No; it’s true—it’s dreadfully true. You’ll have to help us. We don’t remember *anything*, either of us.”

The cook sat down heavily in a polished armchair with a patchwork cushion.

“She’s overlooked you. There’s not a doubt about it. You’re bewitched. Oh, my pretty little dears, that ever I should see the day—”

The cook’s fat, jolly face twisted and puckered in a way with which each child was familiar in the face of the other.

===Chinese===
「你们这会儿不是又在糊弄可怜的老厨娘吧，啊？就像那回说法国兵藏在风车里，闹得全村翻天覆地那样？」

「不是；是真的——真得可怕。您一定得帮帮我们。我们俩，*什么都*记不得。」

厨娘一屁股坐进一把上了亮漆的扶手椅，椅上垫着块百衲布坐垫。

「她给你们使了邪眼，这错不了。你们是中了邪了。哦，我的小亲亲们，想不到竟叫我活到这一天——」

厨娘那张胖乎乎乐呵呵的脸扭了皱了，那副模样，姐弟俩彼此在对方脸上见得可熟了。

===Original===
“Don’t cry,” they said both together; and Elfrida added, “Who’s overlooked what?”

“Old Betty Lovell has—that I’ll be bound! She’s bewitched you both, sure as eggs is eggs. I knew there’d be some sort of a to-do when my lord had her put in the stocks for stealing sticks in the wood. We’ve got to get her to take it off, my dears, that’s what we’ve got to do, for sure; without you could find a white Mouldiwarp, and that’s not likely.”

“A white Mouldiwarp?” said both the children, and again they spoke together like a chorus and looked at each other like conspirators.

“You know the rhyme—oh! but if you’ve forgotten everything you’ve forgotten that too.”

“Say it, won’t you?” said Edred.

===Chinese===
「别哭。」两人齐声说。埃尔弗丽达又问：「谁给谁使了邪眼？」

「老贝蒂·洛弗尔（Betty Lovell）呗——我敢打包票！她给你们俩都下了咒，就像鸡蛋总是鸡蛋一样千真万确。老爷为她在林子里偷拾柴枝那回事罚她上了足枷，那会儿我就知道要生出一场是非。咱们得叫她把咒解了，我的心肝们，咱们就得这么办，一定的；除非你们能寻着一只白穆迪沃普（Mouldiwarp），可那指望不大。」

「白穆迪沃普？」姐弟俩同声说。又是齐声得像唱班，又是对望得像同谋。

「你们知道那首歌谣——哦！可要是你们什么都忘了，那个自然也忘了。」

「您念一遍，好不好？」埃德雷德说。

===Original===
“Let’s see, how do it go?—

> “White Mouldiwarp a spell can make,
> White Mouldiwarp a spell can break;
> When all be well, let Mouldiwarp be,
> When all goes ill, then turn to he.”

===Chinese===
「让我想想，怎么念来着？——

> 「白穆迪沃普能把咒儿施，
> 白穆迪沃普能把咒儿解；
> 万事都顺遂，由它自去；
> 祸事一临头，便去求它。」

===Original===
“Well, all’s not gone ill yet,” said Elfrida, wriggling her neck in its prickly muslin tucker. “Let’s go and see the witch.”

“You’d best take her something—a screw of sugar she’d like, and a pinch of tea.”

“Why, she’d not say ‘Thank you’ for it,” said Edred, looking at the tiny packets.

“I expect you’ve forgotten,” said cook gently, “that tea’s ten shillings a pound and sugar’s gone up to three-and-six since the war.”

===Chinese===
「喏，眼下还没出祸事呢。」埃尔弗丽达说，在扎人的细麻布领布里扭了扭脖子。「咱们这就去看巫婆吧。」

「你们顶好带点东西去——一小纸包糖，她准喜欢，再捏一撮茶叶。」

「嗐，这么一丁点儿，她可不会为它道什么『谢』。」埃德雷德瞧着那两个小纸包说。

「我看你们是忘了，」厨娘温温和和地说，「如今茶叶十先令一磅，打起仗以来，糖又涨到三先令六便士啦。」

===Original===
“What war?”

“The French war. You haven’t forgotten we’re at war with Boney and the French, and the bonfire we had up at the church when the news came of the drubbing we gave them at Trafalgar, and poor dear Lord Nelson and all? And your grandfather reading out about it to them from the ‘George’ balcony, and all the people waiting to cheer, and him not able to get it out for choking pride and because of Lord Nelson—God bless him!—and the people couldn’t get their cheers out neither, for the same cause, and everyone blowing their noses and shaking each other’s hands like as if it was a mad funeral?”

“How splendid!” said Elfrida. “But we don’t remember it.”

===Chinese===
「打的什么仗？」

「跟法国人打的那场仗呗。咱们这会儿正跟波尼和法国人开战呢，这个也忘了？特拉法尔加（Trafalgar）一战咱们把他们揍了个稀里哗啦，还有可怜的、敬爱的纳尔逊勋爵（Lord Nelson）——这一桩桩也忘了？消息传来，教堂那头点起了大篝火，也忘了？还有你们爷爷站在『乔治』的阳台上给大家念这消息，底下的人全等着喝彩，他老人家呢，一肚子自豪哽在喉咙口，又加上纳尔逊勋爵——上帝保佑他！——一个字也念不出来；老百姓也为了同样的缘故，喝彩喝不出口；人人擤着鼻涕、握着彼此的手，倒像一场发了疯的葬礼——这也忘了？」

「多带劲啊！」埃尔弗丽达说，「可我们不记得了。」

===Original===
“Nor you don’t remember how you killed all the white butterflies last year because you said they were Frenchies in their white coats? And the birching you got, for cruelty to dumb animals, his lordship said. You howled for an hour together after it, so you did.”

“I’m glad we’ve forgotten *that*, anyhow,” said Edred.

“Gracious!” said the cook. “Half after eleven, and my eggs not so much as broke for my pudding. Off you go with your letter. Don’t you tell anyone else about you forgetting. And then you come home along by Dering’s Spinney—and go see old Betty. Speak pretty to her and give her the tea and sugar, and keep your feet crossed under your chair if she asks you to sit down. And I’ll give you an old knife-blade apiece to put in your pockets; she can’t do nothing if you’ve got steel on you. And get her to take it off—the ill-wishing, I mean. And don’t let her know you’ve got steel; they don’t like to think you’ve been beforehand with them.”

===Chinese===
「那去年你们把白蝴蝶全弄死了，说它们是穿白外套的法国佬，这也不记得了？为这个挨的那顿藤条——虐待不会说话的畜生，老爷是这么说的——也记得了？挨完了你们连着嚎了一个钟头，可不是嘛。」

「不管怎么说，*那件事*忘了倒好。」埃德雷德说。

「老天爷！」厨娘说，「十一点半了，我做布丁的鸡蛋还没磕开呢。快带着信去吧。你们记性不好的事，谁也别再告诉。回来的时候打德林小树林（Dering’s Spinney）那边走——去看看老贝蒂。跟她说话嘴要甜，把茶叶和糖给她，她要是请你们坐，就把两只脚在椅子底下交叉起来。我再给你们一人一片旧刀片，搁在口袋里；身上带着钢，她就使不了坏。还得叫她把咒解了——我说的是那个邪咒。还有，别让她知道你们带着钢；她们那种人，不乐意想到你们先下了一手防着她们。」

===Original===
So the children went down across the fields to the “George,” and the bean-flowers smelt as sweet, and the skylarks sang as clearly, and the sun and the sky were just as golden and blue as they had been last week. And last week was really a hundred years on in the future. And yet it was last week too—from where they were. Time is a very confusing thing, as the children remarked to each other more than once.

===Chinese===
于是两个孩子穿田过野往「乔治」去。豆花开得一样香，云雀唱得一样脆，太阳和天空同上礼拜一样金灿灿、蓝莹莹。而上礼拜，实实在在是往今后一百年去的上礼拜。可从他们此刻站的地方说，它又确确实实就是上礼拜。时间这东西真是搅人，两个孩子彼此不止一次这么议论。

===Original===
They found the “George” halfway up Arden village, a stately, great house shaped like an E, with many windows and a great porch with a balcony over it. They gave their letter to a lady in a round cap who sat sewing in a pleasant room where there were many bottles and kegs, and rows of bright pewter ale-pots, and little fat mugs to measure other things with, and pewter plates on a brown dresser. There were greyhounds, too, all sprawling, legs and shoulders and tails entangled together like a bunch of dead eels, before the widest hearth the children had ever seen. They hurried away the moment they had given the letter. A coach, top-heavy with luggage, had drawn up in front of the porch, and as they went out they saw the ostlers leading away the six smoking horses. Edred felt that he *must* see the stables, so they followed, and the stables were as big as the house, and there were horses going in and horses going out, and hay and straw, and ostlers with buckets and ostlers with harness, and stalls and loose-boxes beyond counting, and bustle and hurry beyond words.

===Chinese===
「乔治」客栈在阿登村的半坡上，是座气派的大宅，E 字形，开着一排排窗户，正面一座大门廊，门廊上头带阳台。他们把信交给一位戴圆帽的太太，她正坐在一间敞亮屋子里做针线；屋里摆着许多瓶瓶罐罐和小桶，一排排亮堂堂的白镴（pewter）麦酒壶，还有量别样东西用的小胖杯，棕色餐具柜上码着白镴盘子。屋里还有灵缇犬，四仰八叉瘫作一堆，腿啊肩啊尾巴啊缠作一团，活像一捆死鳝鱼，就瘫在孩子们平生见过的最宽的大炉膛前。信一交完他们就赶紧出来了。一辆行李堆得头重脚轻的马车刚在门廊前停稳，出门时他们正瞧见马夫们把六匹冒着热气的马牵走。埃德雷德觉得马厩他*非*看不可，两人便跟了去。马厩同宅子一样大，马进马出，干草稻草，提桶的马夫、携挽具的马夫，马栏和单间马厩数也数不清，那忙乱热闹，说不尽道不完。

===Original===
“How ever many horses have you got?” said Elfrida, addressing a man who had not joined in the kindly chorus of “Hulloa, little ’uns!” that greeted the children. So she judged him to be a newcomer. As he was.

“Two-and-fifty,” said the man.

“What for?” Elfrida asked.

“Why, for the coaches, and the post-shays, and the King’s messengers, for sure,” the man answered. “How else’d us all get about the country, and get to hear the newses, if it wasn’t for the stable the ‘George’ keeps?”

===Chinese===
「你们到底养了多少匹马呀？」埃尔弗丽达冲一个男人问道。孩子们进来时，大家伙儿都和和气气齐声招呼「哈罗，小家伙们！」，独独他没搭腔。她便断定他是个新来的。他也确实是。

「五十二匹。」那人说。

「干什么用？」埃尔弗丽达问。

「还能为什么，拉公共马车、拉邮用轻车、送王室信使呗。」那人答道，「要不是『乔治』养着这马厩，咱们大伙儿怎么在乡下来来去去，怎么听得到各样新闻？」

===Original===
And then the children remembered that this was the time before railways and telegrams and telephones.

It is always difficult to remember exactly where one is when one happens to get into a century that is not one’s own.

Edred would have liked to stay all day watching the busyness of everyone and the beautifulness of the horses, but Elfrida dragged him away.

They had to find the witch, she reminded him; and in a dreadful tumbledown cottage, with big holes in its roof of rotten thatch, they did find her.

===Chinese===
这时两个孩子才想起来，这是铁路、电报和电话之前的时代。

人一个不小心闯进了不属于自己的世纪，总归很难确切记得自己身在何处。

埃德雷德本想待上一整天，看众人忙忙碌碌、看马匹漂漂亮亮，可埃尔弗丽达把他拽走了。

还有巫婆要找呢，她提醒他。在一座破得吓人的塌草屋里——烂草顶上豁着大洞——他们果真找到了她。

===Original===
She was exactly like the pictures of witches in story books, only she had not a broomstick or a high-pointed hat. She had instead a dirty cap that had once been white, and a rusty gown that had once been black, and a streaky shawl that might once, perhaps, have been scarlet. But nobody could be sure of that now. There was a black cat sitting on a very dirty wooden settle, and the old woman herself sat on a rickety three-legged stool, her wrinkled face bent over a speckled hen which she was nursing in her lap and holding gently in her yellow, wrinkled hands.

===Chinese===
她同故事书里的巫婆画像一模一样，只是没有扫帚，也没有尖顶高帽。她有的是一顶脏帽子——从前是白的；一条锈色的裙子——从前是黑的；一条斑驳的披肩——兴许，从前是红的。可如今谁也说不准了。一只黑猫蹲在一张脏兮兮的木靠椅上，老太婆自己坐在一张摇摇晃晃的三脚凳上，满是皱纹的脸低垂着，对着膝上一只花斑母鸡，用一双黄皱黄皱的手轻轻捧着，像在呵护病人。

===Original===
As soon as Edred caught sight of her through the crooked doorway, he stopped. “I’m not going in,” he said, “what’s the good? We know jolly well she *hasn’t* bewitched us. And if we go cheeking her she *may*, and then we shall be in a nice hole.”

“There’s the tea and sugar,” said Elfrida.

“You just give it her and come away. I’ll wait for you by the stile.”

So Elfrida went into the cottage alone, and said “Good morning” in rather a frightened way.

===Chinese===
埃德雷德打那歪歪扭扭的门口一望见她，就站住了。「我不进去。」他说，「进去有什么用？咱们明明白白知道她*并没*给咱们下咒。可咱们要是去招惹她，她说不定*就*下了，到时候咱们可就掉进坑里爬不出来了。」

「这不是有茶叶和糖嘛。」埃尔弗丽达说。

「你干脆替我把东西给她，就出来。我在梯磴边上等你。」

于是埃尔弗丽达独自进了草屋，怯生生地道了声「早上好」。

===Original===
“I’ve brought you some tea and sugar,” she said, and stood waiting for the “Thank you,” without which it would not be polite to say “Good morning” and to go away.

The “Thank you” never came. Instead, the witch stopped stroking the hen, and said—

“What for? I’ve not done you no ’arm.”

“No,” said Elfrida. “I’m sure you wouldn’t.”

===Chinese===
「我给您带了些茶叶和糖。」她说，站着等那句「谢谢你」——少了这一声，道完「早上好」转身就走，便不算礼貌。

那声「谢谢你」始终没有来。巫婆反倒停了停抚母鸡的手，说道——

「这是干什么？我又没害过你们什么。」

「是没害过。」埃尔弗丽达说，「我信您也不会害人。」

===Original===
“Then what have you brought it for?”

“For—oh, just for you,” said Elfrida. “I thought you’d like it. It’s just a—a love-gift, you know.”

This was Aunt Edith’s way of calling a present that didn’t come just because it was your birthday or Christmas, or you had had a tooth out.

“A love-gift?” said the old woman slowly. “After all this long time?”

===Chinese===
「那你带来做什么？」

「为——哦，就为您呀。」埃尔弗丽达说，「我想您会喜欢的。这不过是件——一件爱的礼物，您知道。」

一件礼物，不是因为你过生日、过圣诞才送的，也不是因为你拔了颗牙才送的，伊迪丝姑姑就管它叫「爱的礼物」。

「爱的礼物？」老太婆慢悠悠地说，「隔了这么许多年？」

===Original===
Elfrida did not understand. How should she? It’s almost impossible for even the most grown up and clever of us to know how women used to be treated—and not so very long ago either—if they were once suspected of being witches. It generally began by the old woman’s being cleverer than her neighbours, having more wit to find out what was the matter with sick people, and more still to cure them. Then her extra cleverness would help her to foretell storms and gales and frosts, and to find water by the divining rod—a very mysterious business. And when once you can find out where water is by just carrying a forked hazel twig between your hands and walking across a meadow, you can most likely find out a good many other things that your stupid neighbours would never dream of. And in those long-ago days—which really aren’t so very long ago—your being so much cleverer than your neighbours would be quite enough. You would soon be known as the “wise woman”—and from “wise woman” to witch was a very short step indeed.

===Chinese===
埃尔弗丽达不懂。她又怎么可能懂呢？一个女人，一旦被人疑心是巫婆，从前——其实也没多久以前——会受什么样的对待，这连我们当中最老成、最聪明的人也几乎无从知晓。事情多半是这样开头的：老太婆比左邻右舍聪明些，瞧出病人害的是什么病的机灵多些，治好病的本事更多些。接着，这份过人的聪明又帮她预知风暴、大风和霜冻，还帮她用占卜杖（divining rod）找水——一桩顶顶神秘的营生。而一个人既然能两手夹着一根杈开的榛树枝走过草地就找出水在哪儿，多半也查得出许多别的、你的蠢邻居们做梦也想不到的事情。在那些久远的年月里——其实也真没那么久远——你比邻居聪明这么多，就尽够了。很快你就会被叫作「智慧的女人」——而从「智慧的女人」到巫婆，实实在在只差一小步。

===Original===
“Yes; is your fowl ill?”

“’Twill mend,” said the old woman—“’twill mend. The healing of my hands has gone into it.” She rose, set the hen on the hearth, where it fluttered, squawked, and settled among grey ashes, very much annoying the black cat, and laid her hands suddenly on Elfrida’s shoulders.

“And now the healing of my hands is for you,” she said. “You have brought me a love-gift. Never a gift have I had these fifty years but was a gift of fear or a payment for help—to buy me to take off a spell or put a spell on. But you have brought me a love-gift, and I tell you you shall have your heart’s desire. You shall have love around and about you all your life long. That which is lost shall be found. That which came not shall come again. In this world’s goods you shall be blessed, and blessed in the goods of the heart also. I know—I see—and for you I see everything good and fair. Your future shall be clean and sweet as your kind heart.”

===Chinese===
「是呀。您的母鸡病了吗？」

「会好的。」老太婆说——「会好的。我这双手的治愈力已经进了它的身子。」她站起身，把母鸡放到炉膛边。母鸡扑腾了一阵，咯咯叫着，在灰白的炉灰里卧定了，惹得黑猫大为光火。她却突然把双手搭上埃尔弗丽达的肩头。

「如今我这双手的治愈力就归你了。」她说，「你给我带来了一件爱的礼物。这五十年来，我收的礼物不是出于惧怕，便是为了酬谢——买我去解一个咒，或是下一个咒。可你带来的是爱的礼物，我告诉你，你必得偿心愿。你一生一世，前前后后都有爱环绕。失落的必将寻回。未到的必将再来。世上的财物，你必蒙福；心里的财富，你也必蒙福。我知道——我看见——为你，我看见的一切又好又美。你的将来，必像你善良的心一样干净甜美。」

===Original===
She took her hands away. Elfrida, very much impressed by these flattering remarks which she felt she did not deserve, stood still, not knowing what to say or do; she rather wanted to cry.

“I only brought it because cook told me,” she said.

“Cook didn’t give you the kind heart that makes you want to cry for me now,” said the witch.

The old woman sank down in a crouching heap, and her voice changed to one of singsong.

===Chinese===
她把双手收了回去。这一番奉承话叫埃尔弗丽达深受震动，自觉当不起，呆呆站着，不知说什么、做什么才好；她倒有点想哭。

「我只是厨娘叫我带来，我才带来的。」她说。

「厨娘可给不了你这样一颗好心，叫你这会儿想为我哭。」巫婆说。

老太婆蜷成一团蹲坐下去，嗓音也换成了吟唱似的调子。

===Original===
“I know,” she said—“I know many things. All alone the livelong day and the death-long night, I have learned to see. As cats see through the dark, I see through the days that have been and shall be. I know that you are not here, that you are not now. You will return whence you came, and this time that is not yours shall bear no trace of you. And my blessing shall be with you in your own time and your own place, because you brought a love-gift to the poor old wise woman of Arden.”

“Is there anything I can do for you?” Elfrida asked, very sorry indeed, for the old woman’s voice was very pitiful.

“Kiss me,” said the old woman—“kiss me with your little child’s mouth, that has come back a hundred years to do it.”

Elfrida did not wish to kiss the wrinkled, grey face, but her heart wished her to be kind, and she obeyed her heart.

===Chinese===
「我知道。」她说——「我知道许多事。长日里孤零零，长夜里死一般，我一个人学会了看。像猫看穿黑暗那样，我看穿已经有过和将要有的日子。我知道你不在此地，你不在此时。你将从来的地方回去，这段不属于你的时光，不会留下你一丝痕迹。我的祝福将伴随你在你自己的时代、你自己的地方，因为你给阿登这位可怜的、智慧的老女人带来了一件爱的礼物。」

「有什么我能为您做的吗？」埃尔弗丽达问，心里实在难过，因为老太婆的声音实在凄惨。

「吻我。」老太婆说——「用你那张小娃娃的嘴吻我，那张嘴为一吻回来了一百年。」

埃尔弗丽达并不想吻那张皱巴巴的灰脸，可她的心要她善良，她便听从了她的心。

===Original===
“Ah!” said the wise woman, “now I see. Oh, never have I had such a vision. None of them all has ever been like this. I see great globes of light like the sun in the streets of the city, where now are only little oil-lamps and guttering lanterns. I see iron roads, with fiery dragons drawing the coaches, and rich and poor riding up and down on them. Men shall speak in England and their voice be heard in France—more, the voices of men dead shall be kept alive in boxes and speak at the will of those who still live. The handlooms shall cease in the cottages, and the weavers shall work in palaces with a thousand windows lighted as bright as day. The sun shall stoop to make men’s portraits more like than any painter can make them. There shall be ships that shall run under the seas like conger-eels, and ships that shall ride over the clouds like great birds. And bread that is now a shilling and ninepence shall be fivepence, and the corn and the beef shall come from overseas to feed us. And every child shall be taught who can learn, and—”

===Chinese===
「啊！」智慧的女人说，「这下我看见了。哦，我从没有过这样的异象。历来所有的异象，没有一个比得上这个。我看见城中街上一个个光球，亮得像太阳，而如今那儿只有一盏盏小油灯和油将尽、直打晃的风灯。我看见铁铸的大路，喷火的龙拉着车厢奔驰，穷人和富人都乘坐着来来往往。人将在英格兰开口，声在法兰西入耳——还不止，死者的嗓音将收在匣子里长活，听凭还活着的人的意旨开口。农舍里的手织机将归于沉寂，织工将进了千窗灯火、亮如白昼的宫殿里做工。太阳将俯下身来替人画像，比哪个画家画得都像。将有船像海鳗一样在海底窜行，也有船像大鸟一样驾着云飞。如今卖一先令九便士的面包，到那时只卖五便士；粮食和牛肉将从海外运来养活我们。凡学得进的孩子，个个都将有书读，而且——」

===Original===
“Peace, prater,” cried a stern voice in the doorway. Elfrida turned. There stood the grandfather, Lord Arden, very straight and tall and grey, leaning on his gold-headed cane, and beside him Edred, looking very small and found-out.

The old witch did not seem to see them; her eyes, that rolled and blinked, saw nothing. But she must have heard, for—

“Loss to Arden,” she said; “loss and woe to Arden. The hangings of your house shall be given to the spider, and the mice shall eat your carved furnishings. Your gold shall be less and less, and your house go down and down till there is not a field that is yours about your house.”

===Chinese===
「住口，嚼舌的。」门口一声严厉的断喝。埃尔弗丽达回过头去。祖父阿登勋爵站在那儿，身板笔挺，高大，须发灰白，拄着他的金头手杖；他旁边站着埃德雷德，看上去小小的，一副被当场拿住的样儿。

老巫婆似乎看不见他们；她的眼珠转啊眨啊，什么也没看见。可她一定听见了，因为——

「阿登要遭损失。」她说，「损失和灾殃要落到阿登头上。你家的帷幔将归了蜘蛛，老鼠要啃你的雕花家具。你的金子将越来越少，你的门第将越跌越低，直到你宅子四周再没有一亩田是你家的。」

===Original===
Lord Arden shrugged his shoulders.

“Likely tales,” he said, “to frighten babes with. Tell me rather, if you would have me believe, what shall hap tomorrow.”

“Tomorrow,” said the wise woman, “the French shall land in Lymchurch Bay.”

Lord Arden laughed.

===Chinese===
阿登勋爵耸了耸肩。

「编派得倒像真的。」他说，「拿这个吓唬吃奶娃娃罢咧。你要我信，倒不如告诉我，明天要出什么事。」

「明天，」智慧的女人说，「法国人要在利姆彻奇湾（Lymchurch Bay）登陆。」

阿登勋爵笑了。

===Original===
“And I give you a sign—three signs,” said the woman faintly; for it is tiring work seeing into the future, even when you are enlightened with a kiss from someone who has been there. “You shall see the white Mouldiwarp, that is the badge of Arden, on your threshold as you enter.”

“That shall be one sign,” said the old man mockingly.

“And the second,” she said, “shall be again the badge of your house, in your own chair in your own parlour.”

“That seems likely,” said Lord Arden, sneering.

===Chinese===
「我再给你一个征兆——三个征兆。」那女人有气无力地说；因为窥看未来是桩累人的活计，哪怕窥看的人刚得了一位去过那边的人的一吻、开了灵窍。「你进门的时候，将在门槛上看见白穆迪沃普，那是阿登家的徽记。」

「这算头一个征兆。」老头儿讥诮地说。

「第二个，」她说，「还是你家的徽记，在你自家的客厅、你自家的椅子上。」

「这听着倒像会有的。」阿登勋爵冷笑道。

===Original===
“And the third,” said she, “shall be the badge of your house in the arms of this child.”

She turned her back, and picked the hen out of the ashes.

Lord Arden led Edred and Elfrida away, one in each hand, and as he went he was very severe on disobedient children who went straying after wicked witches, and they could not defend themselves without blaming the cook, which, of course, they would not do.

===Chinese===
「第三个，」她说，「是你家的徽记，在这孩子怀里。」

她转过背去，把母鸡从炉灰里捡了出来。

阿登勋爵一手牵一个，把埃德雷德和埃尔弗丽达带走了。一路上他把不听话、乱跑去追邪巫婆的孩子狠狠数落了一顿。他们没法自辩，一辩就得怪到厨娘头上，那他们当然是万万不肯的。

===Original===
“Bread and water for dinner,” he said, “to teach you better ways.”

“Oh, grandfather,” said Elfrida, catching at his hand, “don’t be so unkind! Just think about when *you* were little. I’m sure you liked looking at witches, didn’t you, now?”

Lord Arden stared angrily at her, and then he chuckled. “It’s a bold girl, so it is,” he said. “I own I remember well seeing a witch ducked no further off than Newchurch, and playing truant from my tutor to see it, too.”

===Chinese===
「午饭吃面包喝白水。」他说，「好教你们学个乖。」

「哦，爷爷。」埃尔弗丽达一把抓住他的手，说，「别这么狠心嘛！想想*您*小时候。我敢说您也爱看巫婆的，是不是，啊？」

阿登勋爵怒冲冲地瞪了她一眼，随即又呵呵笑了。「好个胆大的丫头，真是的。」他说，「我承认，我记得清清楚楚，就在不远的纽彻奇（Newchurch），我看过巫婆受沉水刑，为看那个，我还逃了家庭教师的课呢。」

===Original===
“There now, you see,” said Elfrida coaxingly, “we don’t mean to be naughty; we’re just like what you were. You won’t make it bread and water, will you? Especially if bread’s so dear.”

Lord Arden chuckled again.

“Why, the little white mouse has found a tongue, and never was I spoken to so bold since the days I wore petticoats myself,” he said. “Well, well; we’ll say no more about it this time.”

And Edred, who had privately considered that Elfrida was behaving like an utter idiot, thought better of it.

===Chinese===
「您瞧瞧，这不结了。」埃尔弗丽达哄着说，「我们不是存心淘气；我们就跟您从前一个样。您总不至于真让吃面包喝白水吧，会不会？何况面包又这么贵。」

阿登勋爵又呵呵笑了。

「嗬，小白耗子也长出舌头来了，」他说，「打我自己还穿小罩裙那阵子起，还没人敢跟我这么说话呢。好好好，这一回就算了，不提了。」

埃德雷德原本私下觉得埃尔弗丽达这会儿蠢得没救了，此刻也回心转意了。

===Original===
So they turned across the summer fields to Arden Castle. There seemed to be more of the castle than when the children had first seen it, and it was tidier, much. And on the doorstep sat a white mole.

“There now!” said Elfrida. The mole vanished like a streak of white paint that is rubbed out.

“Pooh!” said Lord Arden. “There’s plenty white moles in the world.”

But when he saw the white mole sitting up in his own carved armchair in the parlour, he owned that it was very unusual.

===Chinese===
于是他们穿过夏日的田野，转向阿登城堡。城堡瞧着比孩子们头回见时多出了好些气象，也齐整多了，齐整得多。台阶上蹲着一只白鼹鼠。

「您瞧！」埃尔弗丽达说。那鼹鼠像一道被抹掉的白漆，倏地不见了。

「哼！」阿登勋爵说，「世上白鼹鼠多得是。」

可等他看见那只白鼹鼠端坐在客厅里他自己那把雕花扶手椅上，他也不得不承认，这事非同寻常。

===Original===
Elfrida stooped and held out her arms. She was extremely glad to see the mole. Because ever since she and her brother had come into this strange time she had felt that it would be the greatest possible comfort to have the mole at hand—the mole, who understood everything, to keep and advise; and, above all, to get them safely back into the century they belonged to.

And the Mouldiwarp made a little run and a little jump, and Elfrida caught it and held it against her waist with both her hands.

“Stay with me,” whispered Elfrida to the mole.

“By George!” said Lord Arden to the universe.

“So now you see,” said Edred to Lord Arden.

===Chinese===
埃尔弗丽达蹲下身，张开双臂。见到鼹鼠，她高兴到了极点。因为打从她和弟弟进到这个陌生的时代起，她就一直觉得，身边有这只鼹鼠才是天大的安慰——这只什么都懂的鼹鼠，护着他们、给他们出主意；顶要紧的是，平平安安把他们送回属于他们的那个世纪。

穆迪沃普小跑两步，轻轻一跳，埃尔弗丽达接住它，用双手把它贴身搂在腰间。

「陪着我。」埃尔弗丽达对鼹鼠悄声说。

「我的老天！」阿登勋爵冲着天地万物说。

「这下您明白了吧。」埃德雷德对阿登勋爵说。

## XIII May-Blossom and Pearls / 五月花与珍珠

===Original===
The King came slowly on a great black horse, riding between the green trees. He himself wore white and green like the May-bushes, and so did the gracious lady who rode beside him on a white horse, whose long tail almost swept the ground and whose long mane fluttered in the breeze like a tattered banner.
The lady had a fine face﻿—proud and smiling﻿—and as her brave eyes met the King’s even the children could see that, for the time at least, she and the King were all the world to each other. They saw that in the brief moment when, in the whirl of the ringed dance, their eyes were turned the way by which the King came with his Queen.
“I wish I didn’t know so much history,” gasped Elfrida through the quick music. “It’s dreadful to know that her head﻿—” She broke off in obedience to an imperative twitch of Richard’s hand on hers.
“Don’t!” he said. “I have *not* to think. And I’ve heard that history’s all lies. Perhaps they’ll always be happy like they are now. The only way to enjoy the past is not to think of the future﻿—the past’s future, I mean﻿—and I’ve got something else to say to you presently,” he added rather sternly.

===Chinese===
国王骑着一匹高大的黑马，缓缓地穿行在绿树之间。他本人一身白绿两色的衣裳，同五月的花树正相衬；在他身侧骑马相伴的那位雍容的夫人也是一样的装束。她骑一匹白马，长长的尾巴几乎扫着地，长长的鬃毛在微风里飘动，像一面破碎的旌旗。

那位夫人有一张姣好的脸——又骄傲，又含着笑意——当她那双无畏的眼睛迎上国王的眼睛时，连孩子们都看得出，至少在此时此刻，她和国王便是彼此的整个世界。他们看出这一层，是在环舞转到急处、他们的眼睛恰好转向国王携王后而来的那条路上去的一刹那。

「但愿我没读过那么多历史。」埃尔弗丽达在急促的乐声里喘着气说。「知道她的头将要——实在太可怕了。」理查德的手在她的手上不容分说地一抽，她便把话打住了。

「别说了！」他说。「我*不许*自己去想。况且我听说过，历史全是谎话。也许他们会永远像现在这样快活呢。享受过去的唯一法子，就是不去想将来——我是说，过去的将来——待会儿我还有别的话要跟你说。」他补了一句，口气相当严厉。

===Original===
The ring broke up into an elaborate figure. The children found themselves fingering the coloured ribbons that hung from the Maypole that was the centre of their dance, twining, intertwining, handing on the streamers to other small, competent fingers. In and out, in and out﻿—a most complicated dance. It was pleasant to find that one’s feet knew it, though one’s brain could not have foreseen, any more than it could have remembered, how the figures went. There were two rings round the Maypole﻿—the inner ring, where Edred and Elfrida were, of noble children in very fine clothes, and the outer ring, of village children in clothes less fine but quite as pretty. Music from a band of musicians on a raised platform decked with May-boughs and swinging cowslip balls inspired the dancers. The King and Queen had reined up their horses and watched the play, well pleased.
And suddenly the dance ended and the children, formed into line, were saluting the royal onlookers.
“A fair dance and footed right featly,” said the King in a great, jolly voice. “Now get you wind, my merry men all, and give us a song for the honour of the May Queen and of my dear lady here.”

===Chinese===
圆圈散开，变出一个繁复的队形。孩子们发觉自己的手指正捻着从五月柱（Maypole）上垂下来的彩带——那柱子便是这场舞的中心——彩带缠了又缠、绕了又绕，再传到别的一双双灵巧的小手里。进进出出，进进出出——好一场繁复精巧的舞。叫人愉快的是，自己的两只脚居然认得它，虽说自己的脑袋既预料不到、也记不住这些队形是怎么走的。五月柱外围着两圈人——内圈是埃德雷德和埃尔弗丽达所在的贵族孩子，衣裳极讲究；外圈是村里的孩子，衣裳不那么讲究，却一样漂亮。一座高台上坐着乐师奏乐，台上扎着五月树枝，悬着摇摇荡荡的黄花九轮草花球，乐声鼓舞着跳舞的人。国王和王后已经勒住了马，看得满心欢喜。

舞忽然就完了，孩子们排成一列，正向观舞的王室贵人行礼。

「好一场舞，脚步又踏得这般利落齐整！」国王用洪亮欢快的嗓音说。「如今都喘口气吧，我快活的臣民们，再给我们唱一支歌，敬一敬五月王后，也敬一敬我这位亲爱的夫人。」

===Original===
There was whispering and discussion. Then Richard Arden stepped out in front of the group of green-clad noble children.
“With a willing heart, my liege,” he said, “but first a song of the King’s good Majesty.”
And with that all the children began to sing﻿—

> “The hunt is up, the hunt is up,
 

 And it is well-nigh day,
 

 And Harry our King is gone a-hunting
 

 To bring his deer to bay.”

===Chinese===
一阵窃窃私语，一阵商量。随后理查德·阿登从穿绿衣裳的贵族孩子那一群里跨步上前。

「心甘情愿，陛下。」他说，「不过先唱一支歌，敬颂王上的仁德圣明。」

说着，所有的孩子便齐声唱起来——

> 「出猎啦，出猎啦，
> 天色已近破晓；
> 咱们的哈利王打猎去了，
> 要把他的鹿儿逼得无处可逃。」

===Original===
It is a rousing tune, and it was only afterwards that Edred and Elfrida were surprised to find that they knew it quite well.
But even while they were singing Elfrida was turning over in her mind the old question, Could anything they did have any effect on the past? It seemed impossible that it should not be so. If one could get a word alone with that happy, stately lady on the white horse, if one could warn her, could help somehow! The thought of the bare scaffold and the black block came to Elfrida so strongly that she almost thought she saw them darkling among the swayed, sun-dappled leaves of the greenwood.
Somebody was pulling at her green skirt. An old woman in a cap that fitted tightly and hid all her hair﻿—an old woman who was saying, “Go to her! go!” and pushing her forward. Someone else put a big bunch of wild flowers into her hand, and this person also pushed her forward. And forward she had to go, quite alone, the nosegay in her hand, across the open space of greensward under the eyes of several hundreds of people, all in their best clothes and all watching her.

===Chinese===
那是一支令人振奋的曲子，而直到事后，埃德雷德和埃尔弗丽达才惊讶地发觉，这曲子他们竟熟得很。

可还在唱歌的当儿，埃尔弗丽达心里已经翻来覆去地想着那个老问题：他们所做的事，究竟能不能对过去有一点影响？要说不能，似乎不可能。要是能同白马上的那位快活庄严的夫人单独说上一句话，要是能警告她，能帮上一点什么忙，该多好！光秃秃的断头台和黑黢黢的垫头木，这念头朝埃尔弗丽达涌来，来势那么猛，她几乎当真看见它们黑沉沉地隐现在绿林里摇曳的、洒满日光斑点的树叶中间。

有人在扯她的绿裙子。一个老婆婆，戴着顶箍得紧紧、把头发遮得一点不剩的帽子——一个嘴里说着「到她跟前去！去呀！」、把她往前推的老婆婆。另一个人把一大束野花塞进她手里，这人也在把她往前推。于是她只好往前走了，孤零零一个人，手里捧着花束，穿过那片开阔的草地，从几百个人眼皮底下走过去——人人穿着过节的好衣裳，人人都望着她。

===Original===
She went on till she came to the spot where the King and Queen were, and then she paused and dropped two curtsies, one to each of them. Then, quite without meaning to do it, she found herself saying﻿—

> “May-day! May-day!
 

 This is the happy play day!
 

 All the woods with flowers are gay,
 

 Lords and ladies, come and play!
 

 Lords and ladies, rich and poor,
 

 Come to the wild woods’ open door!
 

 Hinds and yeomen, Queen and King,
 

 Come do honour to the Spring!
 

 And join us in our merrymaking.”

And when she had said that she made two more nice little curtsies and handed up the flowers to the Queen.
“If we had known your Majesties’ purpose,” said a tall, narrow-faced man in a long gown, “your Majesties had had another than this rustic welcome.”
“Our purpose,” said the King, “was to surprise you. The Earl of Arden, you say, is hence?”
“His son and daughter are here to do homage to your Highness,” said the gowned man, and then Elfrida saw that Edred was beside her.
“Hither, lad,” said the King, and reaching down a hand caught Edred’s. “Your foot on mine,” said his Majesty. “So!” and he swung Edred up on to the saddle in front of him. Elfrida drew nearer to the white horse as the Queen beckoned her, and the Queen stooped low over her saddle to ask her name. Now was the moment that Elfrida had wished for, now was the chance, if ever, to warn the Queen.

===Chinese===
她一路走到国王和王后所在的地方，站定下来，行了两个屈膝礼，给他们一人一个。接着，她全然不假思索地发觉自己开口念道——

> 「五月节！五月节！
> 这是快活的耍日子！
> 满林子花儿开得欢畅，
> 贵人仕女，来玩一场！
> 贵人仕女，无论贫富，
> 都到野树林敞开的门户！
> 雇工自耕农，王后国王，
> 都来向春天致敬颂扬！
> 来同我们一起共赴欢畅。」

念完了，她又端端正正行了两个漂亮的小屈膝礼，把那束花举上去献给了王后。

「倘若臣等早知两位陛下的来意，」一个瘦长脸、穿长袍的男人说，「两位陛下所受的迎迓，便不至于这般乡野粗陋了。」

「我们此来的用意，」国王说，「正是要出其不意。你是说，阿登伯爵不在此地？」

「他的儿女在此恭迎殿下大驾。」长袍男人说。这时埃尔弗丽达才发觉埃德雷德已经到了她身边。

「到这儿来，小子。」国王说着，探下一只手，攥住了埃德雷德的手。「把你的脚踩上我的脚。」王上说。「起！」他一送胳膊，把埃德雷德甩上了身前的马鞍。王后向埃尔弗丽达招手，她便向那匹白马凑近去；王后在鞍上深深俯下身来，问她的名字。埃尔弗丽达盼望的正是这一刻；要警告王后，倘若有机会，机会就在眼前了。

===Original===
“Elfrida Arden’s my name,” she said. “Your Majesty, may I say something?”
“Say on,” said the Queen, raising fine eyebrows, but smiling too.
“I should like to come quite close and whisper,” said Elfrida stoutly.
“Thou’rt a bold lass,” said the Queen, but she stooped still lower.

===Chinese===
「我叫埃尔弗丽达·阿登。」她说。「陛下，我能说句话吗？」

「说吧。」王后说，秀气的眉毛一扬，却仍旧含着笑。

「我想凑得很近，悄悄地说。」埃尔弗丽达壮起胆子说。

「你倒是个大胆的姑娘。」王后说，可她把身子俯得更低了。

===Original===
“I want to warn you,” said Elfrida, quickly whispering, “and *don’t* not pay attention because I’m only a little girl. I *know*. You may think I don’t know, but I do. I want to warn you﻿—”
“Already once, this morning I have been warned,” said the Queen. “What croaking voices for May-day!”
“Who warned you, your Majesty?”
“An old hag who came to my chamber in spite of my maids, said she had a May charm to keep my looks and my lord’s love.”
“What was the charm?” Elfrida asked eagerly, forgetting to say “Majesty” again.
“It was quite simple,” said the Queen. “I was to keep my looks and my love so long as I *never dropped a kerchief*. But if I dropped a kerchief I should lose more than my looks and my love; she said I should lose my head,”﻿—the Queen laughed low﻿—“within certain days from the dropping of that kerchief﻿—this head you see here;” she laughed again.

===Chinese===
「我要警告您。」埃尔弗丽达急急地悄声说，「*千万*别因为我只是个小姑娘就不当回事。我*知道*。您也许以为我不知道，可我知道。我要警告您——」

「今天早晨，我已经受过一回警告了。」王后说。「五月节的日子，倒尽听见些乌鸦叫！」

「谁警告您的，陛下？」

「一个老妖婆，硬闯过我这儿侍女们的拦阻进了我的卧房，说她有一道五月节的法咒，能保住我的容颜和我夫君的恩爱。」

「那法咒是什么样的？」埃尔弗丽达急切地问，又忘了称「陛下」。

「简单得很。」王后说。「只要我*从不掉落一方手绢*，我的容颜和爱情便都保得住。可我若掉了一方手绢，失掉的就不止是容颜和爱情了；她说我会掉了脑袋，」王后低低地笑了，「从那方手绢落地之日起，不出几天——就是你现在瞧见的这一颗；」她又笑了。

===Original===
“Don’t, oh, don’t!” said Elfrida. “Nineteen days, that’s the warning﻿—I do hope it’ll do some good. I do like you, dear Queen. You are so strong and splendid. I would like to be like you when I grow up.”
The Queen’s fine face looked troubled.
“Please Heaven, thou’lt be better than I,” she said, stooping lower still from her horse; Elfrida standing on tiptoe, she kissed her.
“Oh, do be careful,” said Elfrida. “Your darling head!” and the Queen kissed her again.

===Chinese===
「别说，哦，别说啦！」埃尔弗丽达说。「十九天——这就是那警告——我真心盼望它能有点用处。我真心喜欢您，亲爱的王后。您这样坚强，这样光彩照人。我长大了，但愿能像您一样。」

王后那张姣好的脸罩上了愁云。

「上天保佑，愿你将来比我更好。」她说着，在马上把身子俯得更低；埃尔弗丽达踮起脚尖，她吻了吻她。

「哦，千万当心。」埃尔弗丽达说。「您那可爱的脑袋！」王后又吻了她一回。

===Original===
Then a noise rather like bagpipes rose shrill and sudden, and all drew back, making room for the rustic maids and swains to tread the country dance. Other instruments joined in, and suddenly the King cried, “A merry tune that calls to the feet. Come, my sweeting, shall we tread a measure with the rest?” So down they came from their horses, King and Queen, and led the country dance, laughing and gay as any country lad and lass.
Elfrida could have cried. It seemed such a pity that everybody should not always be good and happy, as everybody looked today.
The King had sprung from his horse with Edred in his arms, and now he and his sister drew back towards Cousin Richard.
“How pretty it all is!” said Edred. “I should like to stay here forever.”

===Chinese===
随后，一阵颇像风笛的声音尖利地骤然扬起，人群都往后退，腾出地方来让乡下的姑娘小伙子们踏乡村舞。别的乐器也加入进来，国王忽然喊道：「这曲子欢快，叫人脚底发痒。来吧，我的心肝儿，咱们也去同大伙儿踏一轮舞步如何？」于是国王和王后下了马，领头跳起乡村舞来，欢欢喜喜，跟随便哪一对乡下青年男女相比都不逊色。

埃尔弗丽达简直要哭出来。人不能永远都这样善良快活——像今天人人脸上的模样——这似乎太可惜了。

国王抱着埃德雷德翻身下了马，这时他和姐姐便退到理查德表哥身边去。

「这一切多美呀！」埃德雷德说。「我真想永远待在这儿。」

===Original===
“If I were you,” said Richard, very disagreeably indeed, “I would not stay here an hour.”
“Why? Is it dangerous? Will they cut our heads off?”
“Not that I know of,” said Cousin Richard, still thoroughly disagreeable. “I wasn’t thinking about your heads. There are more important things than your heads in the world, I should think.”
“Not so very much more,” said Elfrida meekly﻿—“to us, I mean. And what are you so cross about?”

===Chinese===
「我要是你们，」理查德说，口气实在很不客气，「在这儿一个钟头也不待。」

「为什么？这儿危险吗？他们会砍咱们的头吗？」

「据我所知不会。」理查德表哥说，还是那副十足不客气的腔调。「我想的又不是你们的脑袋。这个世界上比你们的脑袋要紧的东西，我看有的是。」

「要紧也要紧不了多少。」埃尔弗丽达温顺地说——「我是说，对咱们来说。你这到底是在生什么气呀？」

===Original===
“I should have thought,” Richard was beginning, when the old woman who told Elfrida to go forward with the nosegay of ceremony sidled up to them.
“Into the woods, my children,” she whispered quickly﻿—“into the woods. In a moment the Queen will burst into tears, and the King will have scant kindness for those whose warnings have set his Queen to weeping.”
They backed into the bushes, and the green leaves closed behind the four.

===Chinese===
「我原以为——」理查德刚开口，那个先前叫埃尔弗丽达捧着仪礼花束上前的老婆婆侧着身子凑到他们跟前。

「到林子里去，孩子们。」她急急地悄声说——「到林子里去。转眼王后就要放声哭了，谁的警告惹得王后落泪，国王对谁可就不会客气了。」

他们退进了灌木丛，绿叶在他们四人身后合拢了。

===Original===
“Quick!” said the witch; “this way.” They followed her through the wood under oaks and yew-trees, pressing through hazels and chestnuts to a path.
“Now run!” she said, and herself led the way nimbly enough for one of her great age. Their run brought them to a thinning of the wood﻿—then out of it﻿—on to the downs, whence they could see Arden Castle and its moat, and the sea.
“Now,” the old woman said, “mark well the spot where the moat stream rises. It is there that the smugglers’ cave was, when Betty Lovell foretold the landing of the French.”
“Why,” said Edred and Elfrida, “you’re the witch again! You’re Betty Lovell!”

===Chinese===
「快！」巫婆说；「这边走。」他们跟着她穿过树林，走在橡树和紫杉底下，拨开榛树和栗树，来到一条小径上。

「跑！」她说，自己带头跑了起来，以她那样的高龄，脚步够灵便的了。这一跑，他们跑到树木渐稀的地方——接着跑出了树林——上了丘原，从那儿望得见阿登城堡和它的护城河，还有大海。

「记住了，」老婆婆说，「好好记住护城河水涌出来的那个地方。当年贝蒂·洛弗尔预言法国人登陆的时候，走私贩子的山洞就在那里。」

「哎呀，」埃德雷德和埃尔弗丽达说，「又是你这巫婆！你就是贝蒂·洛弗尔！」

===Original===
“Who else?” said the old woman. “Now, call on the Mouldiwarp and hasten back to your own time. For the King will raise the country against the child who has made his sweeting to shed tears. And she will tell him, she keeps nothing from him, and﻿ ﻿… yet﻿—”
“She won’t tell him about the kerchief?”
“She will, and when she drops it on that other May-day at Greenwich he will remember. Come, call your Mouldiwarp and haste away.”
“But we’ve only just come,” said Edred, “and what’s Elfrida been up to?”

===Chinese===
「不然还能是谁？」老婆婆说。「快，唤穆迪沃普来，赶紧回你们自己的时代去。国王为了惹哭他心肝儿的孩子，是要发动全国上下来搜捕的。她会告诉他的，她什么事都不瞒他，还有……不过——」

「她不会把手绢的事告诉他吧？」

「她会的。等她在格林尼治的那个五月节把手绢掉落时，他就会记起来的。来吧，唤你们的穆迪沃普，快快走吧。」

「可咱们才刚来呀。」埃德雷德说，「埃尔弗丽达到底干了些什么？」

===Original===
“Oh, bother!” said Elfrida. “I want to know what Richard meant about our heads not being important.”
“Your heads will be most important if you wait here much longer!” said the witch sharply. “Come, shall I call the Mouldiwarp, or will you?”
“You do,” said Elfrida. “I say, Dicky, what did you mean? Do tell us﻿—there’s a dear.”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，讨厌！」埃尔弗丽达说。「我倒想知道，理查德说咱们的脑袋不要紧，到底是什么意思。」

「你们再在这儿磨蹭下去，你们的脑袋可就要紧得很啦！」巫婆厉声说。「来，是我来唤穆迪沃普，还是你们自己唤？」

「你来唤吧。」埃尔弗丽达说。「哎，迪基，你那话到底什么意思？快告诉我们吧——做个好哥哥。」

===Original===
Betty Lovell was tearing up the short turf in patches, and pulling the lumps of chalk from under it.
“Help me,” she cried, “or I shan’t be in time!” So they all helped.
“Couldn’t Dick go with us﻿—if we *have* to go?” said Elfrida suddenly.
“No,” said Richard, “I’m not going to﻿—so there!”

===Chinese===
贝蒂·洛弗尔正在把短短的草皮一块块揭起来，从底下把白垩土疙瘩抠出来。

「帮帮我，」她喊道，「不然我就赶不及啦！」于是他们都动手帮忙。

「迪克不能跟咱们一起走吗——要是咱们*非*走不可的话？」埃尔弗丽达忽然说。

「不行，」理查德说，「我不去——就这样！」

===Original===
“Why?” Elfrida gasped, tugging at a great piece of chalk.
“Because I shan’t.”
“Then tell us what you meant before the Mouldiwarp comes.”
“You can’t,” said a little voice, “because it’s come now.”
Everyone sat back on its heels, and watched where out of the earth the white Mouldiwarp was squeezing itself between two blocks of chalk, into the sunlight.

===Chinese===
「为什么？」埃尔弗丽达喘着气问，正使劲拽着一大块白垩土。

「因为我不去。」

「那趁穆迪沃普还没来，把你先前那话的意思说明白呀。」

「说不成了，」一个小小的嗓音说，「因为它现在已经来了。」

人人都往后坐在自己的脚跟上，望着白穆迪沃普从地里、从两块白垩土之间，把自己一点一点挤出来，挤进阳光里。

===Original===
“Why, I hadn’t said any poetry,” said Elfrida.
“I hadn’t made the triangle and the arch,” said old Betty Lovell. “Well, if ever I did!”
“I’ve been here,” said the mole, looking round with something astonishingly like a smile of triumph, “all the time. Why shouldn’t I go where I do please, nows and again? Why should I allus wait on your bidding﻿—eh?” it asked a little pettishly.
“No reason at all,” said Elfrida kindly; “and now, dear, dear Mouldiwarp, please take us away.”

===Chinese===
「咦，我还没念诗呢。」埃尔弗丽达说。

「我还没搭三角形和拱门呢。」老贝蒂·洛弗尔说。「嗬，稀罕事儿，真是稀罕事儿！」

「俺一直在这儿来着，」鼹鼠说，四下张望的神气出奇地像一副得胜的笑，「从头到尾都在。偶尔俺自个儿乐意上哪儿就上哪儿，有啥不行的？凭啥俺老是得听你们调遣——嗯？」它问，带着点儿使性子的味道。

「一点不行也没有。」埃尔弗丽达和和气气地说；「那么现在，亲爱的、亲爱的穆迪沃普，求你带我们走吧。」

===Original===
A confused sound of shouting mixed with the barking of dogs hurried her words a little.
“The hunt is up,” said the old witch-nurse.
“I don’t hold with hunting,” said the Mouldiwarp hastily, “nor yet with dogs. I never could abide dogs, drat the nasty, noisy, toothy things! Here, come inside.”

===Chinese===
一阵乱哄哄的喊嚷混着狗吠，把她的话催得急了些。

「出猎啦。」老巫婆保姆说。

「俺可不赞成打猎，」穆迪沃普急忙说，「也不赞成狗。俺从来就受不了狗，这些又脏又吵又长牙的玩意儿，遭瘟的！来，到里边来。」

===Original===
“Inside where?” said Edred.
“Inside my house,” said the mole.
And then, whether they all got smaller or whether the crack in the chalk got bigger they never quite knew, but they found themselves walking that crack one by one. Only Elfrida got hold of Richard’s hand and held it fast, though he wriggled and twisted to get it free.
“I’m not going back to your own times with you,” he said. “I’ll go my own way.”

===Chinese===
「里边是哪儿？」埃德雷德问。

「俺的家里边。」鼹鼠说。

后来，究竟是他们都变小了，还是白垩上的裂缝变大了，他们始终没能弄清；总之他们发觉自己正一个跟着一个，往那条裂缝里走。唯独埃尔弗丽达抓住了理查德的手，紧紧不放，尽管他又扭又拧，想把手指头抽回去。

「我不跟你们回你们的时代去。」他说。「我要走我自己的路。」

===Original===
“Where to?” said Elfrida.
“To wherever I choose,” said Richard savagely, and regained possession of his own hand. It was too late﻿—the chalk had closed over them all.
As the chalk had closed so thoroughly that not a gleam of daylight could be seen, you might have expected the air they had to breathe to be close and stuffy. Not a bit of it! Coming into the Mouldiwarp’s house out of the May sunshine was like coming out of a human house into the freshness of a May night. But it was darker than any night that ever was. Elfrida got hold of Edred’s hand and then of Richard’s. She always tried to remember what she was told, and the Mouldiwarp had said, “Always hold hands when there’s magic about.”
Richard let his hand be taken, but he said, quite sternly, “You understand I mean what I say: I won’t go back to their times with them.”
“You were much nicer in James the First’s time,” said Elfrida.

===Chinese===
「去哪儿？」埃尔弗丽达说。

「我爱去哪儿就去哪儿。」理查德恶狠狠地说，终于把手夺了回去。可是已经迟了——白垩土已经在他们大家头顶上合拢了。

白垩土合拢得这样严实，连一线天光也看不见，你也许会以为他们呼吸的空气准又闷又浊。才不呢！从五月的阳光里走进穆迪沃普的家，就像从人的屋子里走出去，走进五月夜晚的清凉里。只是这里比任何一个夜晚都黑。埃尔弗丽达先抓住埃德雷德的手，又抓住理查德的手。人家嘱咐她的话，她总尽力记住，而穆迪沃普说过：「有魔法的时候，一定要手拉手。」

理查德由着她的手握着，嘴上却说得相当严厉：「你们明白，我说到做到：我不跟他们回他们那个时代去。」

「你在詹姆斯一世那会儿，可招人喜欢多啦。」埃尔弗丽达说。

===Original===
Then a sound like thunder shook the earth overhead, an almost deafening noise that made them thrill and hold each other very tight.
“It’s only the King’s horses and the King’s men hunting after you,” said the Mouldiwarp cheerfully. “Now I’ll go and make a white clock for you to go home on. You set where you be, and don’t touch nothing till I be come back again.”
Left alone in the fresh, deep darkness, Elfrida persisted in her questions.
“Why don’t you want to come with us to our times?”
“I hate your times. They’re ugly, they’re cruel,” said Richard.
“They don’t cut your head off for nothing anyhow in our times,” said Edred, “and shut you up in the Tower.”

===Chinese===
接着，雷鸣般的声响震得头顶的大地直颤，几乎震聋人的耳朵，吓得他们浑身发麻，彼此紧紧搂住。

「不过是国王的马队和国王的人马来追你们罢了。」穆迪沃普乐呵呵地说。「俺这就去给你们造一口白钟，好坐着它回家。你们给俺在原地坐好，俺回来以前啥也别碰。」

剩下他们独自待在清新而深沉的黑暗里，埃尔弗丽达还只管问她的。

「你为什么不愿意跟咱们回咱们的时代去？」

「我恨你们的时代。它们丑，它们残忍。」理查德说。

「不管怎么说，我们的时代不会无缘无故砍你的头，」埃德雷德说，「也不会把你关进伦敦塔。」

===Original===
“They do worse things,” Richard said. “*I* know. They make people work fourteen hours a day for nine shillings a week, so that they never have enough to eat or wear, and no time to sleep or to be happy in. They won’t give people food or clothes, or let them work to get them; and then they put the people in prison if they take enough to keep them alive. They let people get horrid diseases, till their jaws drop off, so as to have a particular kind of china. Women have to go out to work instead of looking after their babies, and the little girl that’s left in charge drops the baby and it’s crippled for life. Oh! I know. I won’t go back with you. You might keep me there forever.” He shuddered.
“I wouldn’t. And I can’t help about people working, and not enough money and that,” said Edred.
“If *I* were Lord Arden,” said Richard, through the darkness, “I’d make a vow, and I’d keep it too, never to have a day’s holiday or do a single thing I liked till all those things were stopped. But in *your* time nobody cares.”
“It’s not true,” said Elfrida; “we do care﻿—when we know about it. Only we can’t do anything.”
“I *am* Lord Arden,” said Edred, “and when I grow up I’ll do what you say. I shall be in the House of Lords, I think, and of course the House of Lords would have to pay attention to me when I said things. I’ll remember everything you say, and tell them about it.”

===Chinese===
「他们干的坏事更厉害。」理查德说。「*我*知道。他们叫人一天干十四个钟头的活，一星期只给九个先令，叫人永远吃不饱、穿不暖，没有睡觉的工夫，也没有快活的工夫。他们不肯给人饭吃、给人衣穿，也不让人干活去挣；人要是拿了活命的那一点点，他们就把人关进监狱。他们由着人得上可怕的病，烂到下巴都掉下来，就为的要产出一种特别的瓷器。女人不得不撇下自己的吃奶孩子出去做工，看孩子的小姑娘失手摔了娃娃，孩子就落下一辈子的残疾。哦！我知道。我不跟你们回去。你们说不定会把我永远留在那儿。」他打了个寒噤。

「我不会的。再说人们做工啦、钱不够花啦那一类事，我也帮不上忙。」埃德雷德说。

「要是*我*是阿登勋爵，」理查德隔着黑暗说，「我就发一个誓，发了还守着它：那些事不统统禁绝，我就一天假也不休，一件自己喜欢的事也不做。可在*你们的*时代，没有一个人在乎。」

「不是这样的。」埃尔弗丽达说；「我们在乎的——在我们知道的时候。只是我们无能为力。」

「我*就是*阿登勋爵。」埃德雷德说，「等我长大了，就照你说的做。我想我会进上议院，当然，我一开口，上议院就不能不听我说。你说的话我一句句都记住，再去讲给他们听。」

===Original===
“You’re not grown up yet,” said Richard, “and your father’s Lord Arden, not you.”
“Father’s dead, you know,” said Elfrida, in a hushed voice.
“How do you know?” asked Richard.
“There was a letter﻿—”

===Chinese===
「你还没长大呢。」理查德说，「阿登勋爵是你父亲，不是你。」

「父亲已经死了，你知道的。」埃尔弗丽达压低嗓音说。

「你怎么知道的？」理查德问。

「来过一封信——」

===Original===
“Do you think *I’d* trust a letter?” Richard asked indignantly. “If I hadn’t seen my daddy lying dead, do you think I’d believe it? Not till I’d gone back and seen how he died, and where, and had vengeance on the man who’d killed him.”
“But he wasn’t killed.”
“How do you know? You’ve been hunting for the beastly treasure, and never even tried to go back to the time when he was alive﻿—such a little time ago﻿—and find out what really did happen to him.”
“I didn’t know we *could*,” said Elfrida, choking. “And even if we could, it wouldn’t be right, would it? Aunt Edith said he was in heaven. We couldn’t go there, you know. It isn’t like history﻿—it’s all different.”
“Well, then,” said Richard, “I shall have to tell you. You know, I rather took a fancy to you two kids that Gunpowder Plot time; and after you’d gone back to your own times asked Betty Lovell who you were, and she said you were Lord Arden. So the next time I wanted to get away from﻿—from where I was﻿—I gave orders to be taken to Lord Arden. And it﻿—”

===Chinese===
「你以为*我*会信一封信吗？」理查德愤愤地问。「要不是我亲眼看见我爹直挺挺地躺在那儿死了，你以为我会信？非得让我回到那会儿，亲眼看看他是怎么死的、死在哪儿，再向害死他的那个人报了仇，我才能信。」

「可他不是被人害死的呀。」

「你怎么知道？你们一直忙着找那讨厌的宝藏，却从来没想过要回到他还活着的时候去——不过是没多久以前的事——查一查他到底出了什么事。」

「我不知道咱们*能*回去呀。」埃尔弗丽达哽着嗓子说。「再说就算能，那样做也不对，是不是？伊迪丝姑姑说他在天堂。咱们去不了那儿的，你知道。那跟历史不一样——完全是另一回事。」

「那么好吧，」理查德说，「我只好告诉你们了。你们是知道的，火药阴谋那一回，我就挺喜欢你们这两个小家伙；等你们回你们的时代去了，我问贝蒂·洛弗尔你们是谁，她说你们是阿登勋爵。于是下一回我想离开——离开我待的地方——我就吩咐人把我带到阿登勋爵那儿去。结果——」

===Original===
“Come along, do, dear,” said the sudden voice of the Mouldiwarp. “The clock’s all ready.”
A soft light was pressing against their eyes﻿—growing, growing. They saw now that they were in a great chalk cave﻿—the smugglers’ cave, Edred had hardly a doubt. And in the middle of its floor of smooth sand was a great clock-face﻿—figures and hands and all﻿—made of softly gleaming pearls set in ivory. Light seemed to flow from this, and to be reflected back on it by the white chalk walls. It was the most beautiful piece of jeweller’s work that the children﻿—or, I imagine, anyone else﻿—had ever seen.
“Sit on the minute hand,” said the Mouldiwarp, “and home you go.”
“But I can’t go,” said Edred grimly, “till I’ve heard what Richard was saying.”

===Chinese===
「快来吧，亲爱的们，快来。」穆迪沃普的声音突然响起来。「钟已经全备好啦。」

一团柔和的光向他们的眼睛压过来——越来越亮，越来越亮。他们这才看清，他们是在一个巨大的白垩岩洞里——那个走私贩子的山洞，埃德雷德几乎毫不怀疑。洞底铺着平滑的细沙，沙面正中是一面巨大的钟面——数字、指针，一应俱全——由温润生辉的珍珠嵌在象牙里做成。光仿佛从钟面里流泻出来，又被白垩的岩壁反射回去。这是孩子们——我想，换成任何别的人也一样——见所未见的最美的一件珠宝活计。

「坐到分针上去。」穆迪沃普说，「一坐就到家。」

「可我不能走，」埃德雷德倔强地说，「不把理查德的话听完我不走。」

===Original===
“You’ll be caught, then, by the King and his soldiers,” said the witch.
“I must risk that,” said Edred quite quietly. “I will not go near the white clock till Richard has told me what he means.”
“I’ll give him one minute,” said the Mouldiwarp crossly, “not no more than that. I’m sick to death of it, so I am.”

===Chinese===
「那你就等着叫国王和他的兵抓走吧。」巫婆说。

「这个险我非冒不可。」埃德雷德说得非常平静。「理查德不把他的意思告诉我，我就不走近那口白钟。」

「俺给他一分钟，」穆迪沃普气呼呼地说，「一分不多。俺烦都烦死了，真的烦死了。」

===Original===
“Oh, *don’t* be cross,” said Elfrida.
“I bain’t,” said the Mouldiwarp, “not under my fur. It’s this Chop-and-change, and I-will-and-I-won’t as makes me so worritable.”
“Tell me, what did you mean﻿—about my father?” Edred said again.
“I tried to find you﻿—I asked for Lord Arden. What I found wasn’t you﻿—it was your father. And the time was *your* time, July, 1908.”

===Chinese===
「哦，*别*生气嘛。」埃尔弗丽达说。

「俺没气，」穆迪沃普说，「俺这身皮毛底下没气。就是这会儿这么变、那会儿那么变，一会儿要去、一会儿又不要去的，闹得俺烦躁不堪。」

「告诉我，你那话到底是什么意思——关于我父亲？」埃德雷德又问了一遍。

「我想找的是你们——我要找的是阿登勋爵。我找到的却不是你——是你父亲。而那时候，正是*你们的*时代——1908 年 7 月。」

===Original===
“**What!**” cried Edred and Elfrida together.
“Your father﻿—he’s alive﻿—don’t you understand? And you’ve been bothering about finding treasure instead of about finding him.”
“Daddy﻿—alive!” Elfrida clung to her brother. “Oh, it’s not right, mixing *him* up with magic and things. Oh, you’re cruel﻿—I hate you! I know well enough I shall never see my daddy again.”
“You will if you aren’t little cowards as well as little duffers,” said Richard scornfully. “You go and find him, that’s what you’ve got to do. So long!”

===Chinese===
「**什么！**」埃德雷德和埃尔弗丽达一齐喊出声来。

「你们的父亲——他还活着——你们不明白吗？你们一直为找宝藏操心，却不为找他操心。」

「爸爸——还活着！」埃尔弗丽达紧紧抱住哥哥。「哦，这不对，把*他*同魔法这些事搅和到一块儿。哦，你们好狠心——我恨你们！我心里明白得很，我再也见不到我的爸爸了。」

「只要你们不光是小傻瓜，还是小胆小鬼，就见得着的。」理查德轻蔑地说。「你们这就去找他，这才是你们该做的事。回见！」

===Original===
And with that, before the Mouldiwarp or the nurse could interfere, he had leapt on to the long pearl and ivory minute hand of the clock and said, “Home!” just as duchesses (and other people) do to their coachmen (or footmen).
And before anything could be done the hands of the clock began to go round, slowly at first, then faster and faster, till at last they went so fast that they became quite invisible. The ivory and pearl figures of the clock could still be seen on the sand of the cave.
Edred and Elfrida, still clinging together, turned appealing eyes to the Mouldiwarp. They expected it to be very angry indeed, instead of which it seemed to be smiling. (Did you ever see a white mole smile? No? But then, perhaps you have never seen a white mole, and you cannot see a smile without seeing the smiler, except of course in the case of Cheshire cats.)
“He’s a bold boy, a brave boy,” said the witch.
“Ah!” said the Mouldiwarp, “he be summat like an Arden, he be.”

===Chinese===
话音未落，不等穆迪沃普或那保姆来得及拦阻，他已经跳上钟上那根长长的珍珠和象牙做的分针，说了声「回家！」——正像公爵夫人（以及其他的人）对她们的车夫（或者跟班）说话那样。

不等谁来得及动手，钟上的指针便转了起来，起初慢，后来越转越快，到末了快得全然看不见了。钟上象牙和珍珠做的数字却还看得见，留在洞中的沙地上。

埃德雷德和埃尔弗丽达还紧紧偎在一处，把恳求的目光转向穆迪沃普。他们以为它准会大发脾气，谁知它看上去竟像是在微笑。（你见过白鼹鼠微笑吗？没见过？不过嘛，兴许你压根儿就没见过白鼹鼠，而看不见微笑的那张脸，就看不见微笑——当然，柴郡猫不在此例。）

「他是个有胆量的孩子，勇敢的孩子。」巫婆说。

「啊！」穆迪沃普说，「他倒有几分阿登家的样子，有的。」

===Original===
Edred detached himself from Elfrida and stiffened with a resolve to show the Mouldiwarp that he too was not so unlike an Arden as it had too hastily supposed.
“Can’t we get home?” Elfrida asked timidly. “Can’t you make us another white clock, or something?”
“Waste not, want not,” said the mole. “Always wear out your old clocks afore you buys new ’uns. Soon’s he gets off the hand the clock’ll stop; then you can get on it and go safe home.”
“But suppose the King finds us?” said Elfrida.
“He shan’t,” said Betty Lovell. “You open the chalky door, Mouldy, my love, and I’ll keep the King quiet till the young people’s gone home.”
“They’ll duck you for a witch,” said the Mouldiwarp, and it did not seem to mind the familiar way in which Betty spoke to it.

===Chinese===
埃德雷德从埃尔弗丽达身边脱出身来，挺直了身子，下定决心要向穆迪沃普显一显：它未免太性急，把他也看成同阿登家不怎么相像了。

「咱们回不了家了吗？」埃尔弗丽达怯生生地问。「你不能再给咱们造一口白钟，或者别的什么吗？」

「不浪费，不愁缺。」鼹鼠说。「买新钟之前，总得把旧钟使个够本。他一下了那根针，钟就会停；那时你们再上去，稳稳当当回家。」

「可要是国王找到咱们呢？」埃尔弗丽达说。

「他找不着。」贝蒂·洛弗尔说。「你把白垩门打开，穆迪小乖乖，我来稳住国王，等年轻人们回了家。」

「他们要拿你浸池塘，治你行巫的罪。」穆迪沃普说。贝蒂同它说话这样不见外，它似乎也并不介意。

===Original===
