他们发现一条条狭长的走廊，歪歪斜斜挂着黑框旧版画，版画上垂着蛛网，像披了灰纱。他们发现一间间屋子，铺着灰色、凹凸不平的橡木地板，壁炉的炉格里积着老烟灰，还有早已破碎的椋鸟旧巢——不知多少年前在那里孵出过雏鸟。

埃德雷德那块手帕——一块万能抹布——在其中一扇窗上擦出一小片干净，他们凭窗眺望起伏的丘原。房子的这一部分不是建在城堡里面的，这一点很清楚。

他们推开每一扇门，看遍了每一屋子残败的堂皇，然后走出去绕到外面。这才看清，这是从城堡伸出来建的一个侧翼——一个装着近方形窗户的侧翼，窗上有雕花的滴水石。所有的窗玻璃都黄得像羊皮纸，蒙着岁月和蜘蛛给它罩上的内纱。常春藤绕着窗子密密地长，几乎把有些窗户整个遮没了。

===Original===
“Oh!” cried Elfrida, throwing herself down on the turf, “it’s too good to be true. I can’t believe it.”

“What *I* can’t believe,” said Edred, doing likewise, “is that precious mole.”

“But we saw it,” said Elfrida; “you can’t help believing things when you’ve seen them.”

“I can,” said Edred, superior. “You remember the scarlet toadstools in *Hereward*. Suppose those peppermint creams were enchanted—to make us dream things.”

“They were good,” said Elfrida. “I say!”

===Chinese===
「噢！」埃尔弗丽达喊着一头扑倒在草皮上，「这好得都不像真的了。我不敢相信。」

「*我*不敢相信的，」埃德雷德说着也扑倒下去，「是那只宝贝鼹鼠。」

「可咱们亲眼见着它了呀。」埃尔弗丽达说；「亲眼见过的事，由不得你不信。」

「我可由得。」埃德雷德说，一副高人一等的口气。「你记得《赫里沃德》（*Hereward*）里那些猩红毒菇吧。说不定那些薄荷夹心糖着了魔——好叫咱们做梦来着。」

「那糖好吃。」埃尔弗丽达说。「我说！」

===Original===
“Well?”

“Have you made up any poetry to call the mole with?”

“Have you?”

“No; I’ve tried, though.”

“*I’ve* tried. And I’ve done it.”

===Chinese===
「怎么？」

「你编出什么诗能召唤那只鼹鼠了吗？」

「你呢？」

「没有；不过我试过了。」

「*我*可试过了。而且编成了。」

===Original===
“Oh, Edred, you *are* clever. Do say it.”

“If I do, do you think the mole will come?”

“Of course it will.”

“Well,” said Edred slowly, “of course I want to find the treasure and all that. But I *don’t* believe in it. It isn’t *likely*—that’s what I think. Now *is* it likely?”

===Chinese===
「噢，埃德雷德，你*真*聪明。快念念吧。」

「我要是念了，你觉得那鼹鼠会来吗？」

「当然会来。」

「那么，」埃德雷德慢吞吞地说，「我当然想找到宝藏，还有那些种种。可我*不*信这一套。这事*不像*真会有的——我就是这么想。你说*像*真会有的吗？」

===Original===
“Unlikelier things happened in ‘The Amulet,’ ” said Elfrida.

“Ah,” said Edred, “that’s a story.”

“The mole said *we* were in a story. I say, Edred, do say your poetry.”

Edred slowly said it—

> “ ‘Mole, mole,
> Come out of your hole;
> I know you’re blind,
> But *I* don’t mind.’ ”

===Chinese===
「《护身符》里比这更不像真的事都发生过呢。」埃尔弗丽达说。

「啊，」埃德雷德说，「那是故事。」

「那鼹鼠说*咱们*就在故事里呢。我说埃德雷德，快念你的诗吧。」

埃德雷德慢慢念了起来——

> 「『鼹鼠，鼹鼠，
> 快出你的洞府；
> 我知道你看不见，
> 可*我*一点儿也不嫌。』」

===Original===
Elfrida looked eagerly round her. There was the short turf; the castle walls, ivied and grey, rose high above her; pigeons circled overhead, and in the arches of the windows and on the roof of the house they perched, preening their bright feathers or telling each other, “Coo, coo; cooroo, cooroo,” whatever that may mean. But there was no mole—not a hint or a dream or idea of a mole.

“Edred,” said his sister.

“Well?”

“Did you *really* make that up? Don’t be cross, but I do think I’ve heard something like it before.”

“I—I adopted it,” said Edred.

===Chinese===
埃尔弗丽达急切地四下张望。矮草皮还是那片矮草皮；爬满常春藤的灰色城堡高墙耸立在她头顶；鸽子在空中盘旋，落在窗洞的拱券上和屋顶上，梳理着亮泽的羽毛，或者互相絮叨「咕咕，咕咕；咕噜咕，咕噜咕」，不知那是些什么意思。可是没有鼹鼠——连鼹鼠的一丝影儿、一个梦、一点念头都没有。

「埃德雷德。」他姐姐说。

「怎么？」

「那首诗真是*你自个儿*编的吗？你别恼，我总觉得从前听过差不多的。」

「我——我是采用的。」埃德雷德说。

===Original===
“?” said Elfrida.

“Haven’t you seen it in books, ‘Adopted from the French’? I altered it.”

“I don’t believe that’ll do. How much did you alter? What’s the real poetry like?”

> “ ‘The mole, the mole,
> He lives in a hole.
> The mole is blind;
> *I* don’t mind,’ ”

said Edred sulkily. “Auntie told me it the day you went to her with Mrs. Harrison.”

===Chinese===
「？」埃尔弗丽达说。

「你在书里没见过『译自法语』这样的字样吗？我改动过了。」

「我看那恐怕不行。你改动了多少？原诗到底是什么样的？」

> 「『那鼹鼠，那鼹鼠，
> 它住在洞府；
> 那鼹鼠看不见，
> *我*也不嫌。』」

埃德雷德绷着脸说。「你跟哈里森太太上她那儿去的那天，姑姑念给我听的。」

===Original===
“I’m sure you ought to make it up all yourself. You see, the mole doesn’t come.”

“There isn’t any mole,” said Edred.

“Let’s both think hard. I’m sure I could make poetry—if I knew how to begin.”

“If anyone’s got to make it, it’s me,” said Edred. “You’re not Lord Arden.”

“You’re very unkind,” said Elfrida, and Edred knew she was right.

“I don’t mind trying,” he said, condescendingly; “you make the poetry and I’ll say it.”

===Chinese===
「我看你该完完全全自个儿编一首。你瞧，鼹鼠不来了吧。」

「根本就没有什么鼹鼠。」埃德雷德说。

「咱们俩都使劲想吧。我保证我能编诗——只要知道怎么起头。」

「要说编诗，也得*我*来编。」埃德雷德说。「你又不是阿登勋爵。」

「你太刻薄了。」埃尔弗丽达说。埃德雷德自知她说得不错。

「我不妨试一试。」他说，摆出屈尊的口气；「你编诗，我来念。」

===Original===
Elfrida buried her head in her hands and thought till her forehead felt as large as a mangel-wurzel, and her blood throbbed in it like a church clock ticking.

“Got it yet?” he asked, just as she thought she really had got it.

“*Don’t!*” said the poet, in agony.

Then there was silence, except for the pigeons and the skylarks, and the mooing of a cow at a distant red-roofed farm.

“Will this do?” she said at last, lifting her head from her hands and her elbows from the grass; there were deep dents and lines on her elbows made by the grass-stalks she had leaned on so long.

“Spit it out,” said Edred.

===Chinese===
埃尔弗丽达把头埋进双手里，冥思苦想，直想到脑门胀得像一颗饲用甜菜（mangel-wurzel）那么大，血在里面突突地跳，像教堂的大钟滴答作响。

「想出来了吗？」他偏在这时候问——她恰恰刚觉得真想出来了。

「*别吵！*」这位诗人苦痛地说。

接下来是一片寂静，只有鸽子、云雀，和远处一座红顶农庄里奶牛的哞哞声。

「这个行吗？」她终于开口，把头从手里、胳膊肘从草地上抬起来；胳膊肘上压出了深深的印子和道道，是那些草茎硌的——她伏得太久了。

「快吐出来吧。」埃德雷德说。

===Original===
Thus encouraged, Elfrida said, very slowly and carefully, “ ‘Oh, Mouldiwarp’—I think it would rather be called that than mole, don’t you?—‘Oh, Mouldiwarp, do please come out and show us how to set about it’—that means the treasure. I hope it’ll understand.”

“That’s not poetry,” said Edred.

“Yes, it is, if you say it right on—

> “ ‘Oh, Mouldiwarp, do please come out
> And show us how to set about
> It.’ ”

===Chinese===
受到这样的鼓励，埃尔弗丽达便又慢又认真地念道：「『噢，穆迪沃普』——我想它宁可人家叫它这个，不愿意叫鼹鼠，你说是不是？——『噢，穆迪沃普，求你快出来，指点我们该怎样着手』——这指的就是宝藏。但愿它听得明白。」

「那不是诗。」埃德雷德说。

「是的，是诗，只要你这样一路念下去——

> 「『噢，穆迪沃普，求你快出来，
> 指点我们该怎么办、从哪儿开
> 始。』」

===Original===
“There ought to be some more,” said Edred—rather impressed, all the same.

“There is,” said Elfrida. “Oh, wait a minute—I shall remember directly. It—what I mean is, how to find the treasure and make Edred brave and wise and kind.”

“I’m kind enough if it comes to that,” said Lord Arden.

“Oh, I *know* you are; but poetry has to rhyme—you know it has. I expect poets often have to say what they don’t mean because of that.”

“Well, say it straight through,” said Edred, and Elfrida said, obediently—

> “ ‘Oh, Mouldiwarp, do please come out
> And show us how to set about
> It. What I mean is how to find
> The treasure, and make Edred brave and wise and kind.’

I’ll write it down if you’ve got a pencil.”

===Chinese===
「底下还该有点儿。」埃德雷德说——尽管如此，心里倒相当佩服了。

「有的。」埃尔弗丽达说。「噢，等一等——我马上就想起来了。它——我的意思是，怎样找到宝藏，把埃德雷德变得勇敢、聪明又善良。」

「说到善良，我已经够善良的了。」阿登勋爵说。

「噢，我*知道*你善良；可诗得押韵——你知道非押不可。我看诗人们常为这个，不得不写些违背本心的话。」

「好吧，从头到尾连着念一遍。」埃德雷德说。埃尔弗丽达便顺从地念道——

> 「『噢，穆迪沃普，求你快出来，
> 指点我们该怎么办、从哪儿开
> 始。我的意思是，怎样去找
> 宝藏，把埃德雷德变得勇敢、聪明、心眼儿好。』」

「你要是有铅笔，我就把它写下来。」

===Original===
Edred produced a piece of pink chalk, but he had no paper, so Elfrida had to stretch out her white petticoat, put a big stone on the hem, and hold it out tightly with both hands, while Edred wrote at her dictation.

Then Edred studiously repeated the lines again and again, as he was accustomed to repeat “The Battle of Ivry,” till at last he was able to stand up and say—

> “ ‘Oh, Mouldiwarp, do please come out
> And show me how to set about
> It. What I mean is how to find
> The treasure, and make me brave and wise—’

If you don’t mind,” he added.

And instantly there was the white mole.

“What do you want now?” it said very crossly indeed. “And call that poetry?”

===Chinese===
埃德雷德掏出一截粉红色粉笔，可他没有纸，埃尔弗丽达只好扯出她的白衬裙，裙摆上压一块大石头，两只手绷紧了举着，让埃德雷德照她的口授写。

随后埃德雷德用起功来，把那几句诗翻来覆去念了一遍又一遍，就像他惯常背《伊夫里之战》（“The Battle of Ivry”）那样，直到最后他能站起身来念——

> 「『噢，穆迪沃普，求你快出来，
> 指点我该怎么办、从哪儿开
> 始。我的意思是，怎样去找
> 宝藏，把我变得勇敢又聪明——』」

「如果你不介意的话。」他又添了一句。

白鼹鼠立刻就出现了。

「这回你们又想要什么？」它火气十足地说。「你们也好意思管那个叫诗？」

===Original===
“It’s the first I ever made,” said Elfrida, of the hot ears. “Perhaps it’ll be better next time.”

“We want you to do what the spell says,” said Edred.

“Make *you* brave and wise? That can’t be done all in a minute. That’s a long job, that is,” said the mole viciously.

“Don’t be so cross, dear,” said Elfrida; “and if it’s going to be so long hadn’t you better begin?”

“I ain’t agoin’ to do no more’n my share,” said the mole, somewhat softened though, perhaps by the “dear.” “You tell me what you want, and p’raps I’ll do it.”

===Chinese===
「那是我头一回作诗。」埃尔弗丽达说，耳朵发起烧来。「也许下一回会作得好些。」

「我们要你照咒语里说的做。」埃德雷德说。

「把*你*变勇敢变聪明？那可不是一分钟能办完的事。那是桩大活儿，没错。」鼹鼠恶狠狠地说。

「别这么凶嘛，亲爱的。」埃尔弗丽达说；「既然要费那么久，不如趁早开始，好不好？」

「俺可不打算多干俺分外的事。」鼹鼠说，不过语气软了些，兴许是那声「亲爱的」的功劳。「你们说说要什么，俺兴许就办。」

===Original===
“I know what I want,” said Edred, “but I don’t know whether you *can* do it.”

“Ha!” laughed the mole contemptuously.

“I got it out of a book Elfrida got on my birthday,” Edred said. “The children in it went into the past. I’d like to go into the past—and find that treasure!”

“Choose your period,” said the mole wearily.

“Choose—?”

“Your period. What time you’d like to go back to. If you don’t choose before I’ve counted ten it’s all off. One, two, three, four—”

===Chinese===
「我知道我要什么。」埃德雷德说，「可我不知道你*办不办*得到。」

「哈！」鼹鼠轻蔑地笑了。

「我是从一本书里看来的，那是埃尔弗丽达在我生日那天得的。」埃德雷德说。「书里的孩子们到过去去了。我也想回到过去——找到那批宝藏！」

「挑你们的年代吧。」鼹鼠倦倦地说。

「挑——？」

「你们的年代。你们想回到什么时候。等俺数到十你们还不挑，这事就算吹了。一，二，三，四——」

===Original===
It counted ten through a blank silence.

“Nine, ten,” it ended. “Oh, very well, den, you’ll have to take your luck, that’s all.”

“Bother!” said Edred. “I couldn’t think of anything except all the dates of all the kings of England all at once.”

“Lucky to know ’em,” said the mole, and so plainly not believing that he did know them that Edred found himself saying under his breath, “William the First, 1066; William the Second, 1087; Henry the First, 1100.”

The mole yawned, which, of course, was very rude of it.

===Chinese===
它在一片空白的寂静中数满了十。

「九，十。」它数完了。「嗷，那也罢了，你们只好凭运气了，就这么着。」

「真气人！」埃德雷德说。「我脑子里一下涌出来的，净是英格兰历代国王的年头，别的什么也想不成。」

「知道这些就算你有福气喽。」鼹鼠说。它那副样子明明白白是不信他真知道，害得埃德雷德不由得压低嗓门背起来：「威廉一世，1066 年；威廉二世，1087 年；亨利一世，1100 年。」

鼹鼠打了个呵欠。这当然是很失礼的。

===Original===
“Don’t be cross, dear,” said Elfrida again; “you help us your own way.”

“Now you’re talking,” said the mole, which, of course, Elfrida knew. “Well, I’ll give you a piece of advice. Don’t you be nasty to each other for a whole day, and then—”

“*You* needn’t talk,” said Edred, still under his breath.

“Very well,” said the mole, whose ears were sharper than his eyes. “I won’t.”

“Oh, don’t!” sighed Elfrida; “*what* is it we are to do when we’ve been nice to each other for a whole day?”

===Chinese===
「别生气嘛，亲爱的。」埃尔弗丽达又说；「你照你自己的法子帮我们。」

「这还像句话。」鼹鼠说——这话埃尔弗丽达当然心里有数。「好吧，俺给你们一句忠告。你们俩整整一天，谁也不许给谁气受，然后——」

「*你*才没资格说别人。」埃德雷德仍旧压着嗓子说。

「很好。」鼹鼠说——它的耳朵可比眼睛尖得多。「俺不说了。」

「噢，别吵啦！」埃尔弗丽达叹口气；「咱们和和气气处满一天以后，*到底*该做什么呀？」

===Original===
“Well, *when* you’ve done that,” said the mole, “look for the door.”

“What door?” asked Elfrida.

“*The* door,” said the mole.

“But where is it?” Edred asked.

“In the house it be, of course,” said the mole. “Where else to gracious should it be?”

And it ran with mouse-like quickness across the grass and vanished down what looked like a rabbit-hole.

===Chinese===
「这个嘛，等你们*当真*做到了，」鼹鼠说，「就去找那扇门。」

「什么门？」埃尔弗丽达问。

「*那*扇门。」鼹鼠说。

「可它在哪儿呢？」埃德雷德问。

「自然是在宅子里。」鼹鼠说。「老天爷在上，还能在哪儿？」

说罢它老鼠似的飞快蹿过草皮，钻进一个看着像兔子洞的洞里，没了影儿。

===Original===
“Now,” said Elfrida triumphantly, “you’ve got to believe in the mole.”

“Yes,” said Edred, “and you’ve got to be nice to me for a whole day, or it’s no use my believing.”

“Aren’t I generally nice?” the girl pleaded, and her lips trembled.

“Yes,” said her brother. “Yes, Lady Arden; and now I’m going to be nice, too. And where shall we look for the door?”

This problem occupied them till teatime. After tea they decided to paint—with the new paintbox and the beautiful new brushes. Elfrida wanted to paint Mr. Millar’s illustrations in *The Amulet*, and Edred wanted to paint them, too. This could not be, as you will see if you have the book. Edred contended that they were his paints. Elfrida reminded him that it was her book. The heated discussion that followed ended quite suddenly and breathlessly.

===Chinese===
「这下，」埃尔弗丽达得意洋洋地说，「你可得信那只鼹鼠了吧。」

「是啊，」埃德雷德说，「你也得整整一天好好待我，不然我信了也白信。」

「我平时待你不好吗？」姑娘辩道，嘴唇都发颤了。

「好。」她弟弟说。「好，阿登夫人；这下轮到我也好好待你了。咱们上哪儿找那扇门去呢？」

这个问题占了他们一直到喝下午茶的工夫。喝过茶，他们决定画画儿——用新颜料盒和漂亮的新画笔。埃尔弗丽达想画《护身符》里米拉尔先生（Mr. Millar）的插图，埃德雷德也想画。这可没法两全——你手头若有那本书，自然明白。埃德雷德一口咬定颜料是他的。埃尔弗丽达提醒他书是她的。随之而来的那场激烈争论，忽然间戛然而止，倒叫人屏住了气。

===Original===
“*I* wouldn’t be a selfish pig,” said Edred.

“No more would I,” said Elfrida. “Oh, Edred, *is* this being nice to each other for twenty-four hours?”

“Oh,” said Edred. “Yes—well—all right. Never mind. We’ll begin again tomorrow.”

But it is much more difficult than you would think to be really nice to your brother or sister for a whole day. Three days passed before the two Ardens could succeed in this seemingly so simple thing. The days were not dull ones at all. There were beautiful things in them that I wish I had time to tell you about—such as climbings and discoveries and books with pictures, and a bureau with a secret drawer. It had nothing in it but a farthing and a bit of red tape—secret drawers never have—but it was a very nice secret drawer for all that.

And at last a day came when each held its temper with a strong bit. They began by being very polite to each other, and presently it grew to seem like a game.

“Let’s call each other Lord and Lady Arden all the time, and pretend that we’re no relation,” said Elfrida. And really that helped tremendously. It is wonderful how much more polite you can be to outsiders than you can to your relations, who are, when all’s said and done, the people you really love.

===Chinese===
「*我*才不愿当自私的小猪。」埃德雷德说。

「我也不愿。」埃尔弗丽达说。「噢，埃德雷德，咱们这样*算不算*二十四个小时里好好相待呀？」

「噢。」埃德雷德说。「算——嗯——好吧。没关系。咱们明天重新开始。」

可是要整整一天真心实意地好好待你的弟弟或姐姐，比你想的难得多。阿登家这两个孩子足足过了三天，才办成这桩看着如此容易的事。这几天一点也不乏味。里头有不少美妙的事，可惜我没有工夫一一讲给你听——什么攀高爬低啦，新发现啦，带插图的书啦，还有一张带暗屉的写字柜。暗屉里只有一枚四分之一便士铜币和一小截红带子——暗屉从来如此——尽管这样，那也仍是顶好顶好的暗屉。

终于有一天来了，这一天里，两人像勒紧马嚼子似的，死死勒住了各自的脾气。他们起初只是彼此客客气气，不多时便觉得像做游戏了。

「咱们整天互相称阿登勋爵和阿登夫人吧，就装作咱们俩毫不沾亲。」埃尔弗丽达说。这一招当真帮了大忙。你对生人能比对亲戚客气多少，真叫人惊奇——说到底，亲戚才是你真正爱的人呐。

===Original===
As the time went on they grew more and more careful. It was like building a house of cards. As hour after hour of blameless politeness was added to the score, they grew almost breathlessly anxious. If, after all this, some natural annoyance should spoil everything!

“I do hope,” said Edred, towards teatime, “that you won’t go and do anything tiresome.”

“Oh, dear, I do hope I shan’t,” said Elfrida.

And this was just like them both.

After tea they decided to read, so as to lessen the chances of failure. They both wanted the same book—*Treasure Island* it was—and for a moment the niceness of both hung in the balance. Then, with one accord, each said, “No—you have it!” and the matter ended in each taking a quite different book that it didn’t particularly want to read.

At bedtime Edred lighted Elfrida’s candle for her, and she picked up the matches for him when he dropped them.

===Chinese===
时间越往后，他们越发小心。这就像搭一座纸牌房子。当一小时又一小时无可指摘的客气累加到账上，他们紧张得几乎不敢喘气。倘若经过这一切之后，哪一阵天然的心烦意乱毁了全局，那可怎么好！

「我真盼着，」快到下午茶的时候埃德雷德说，「你可别偏偏这会儿做出什么招人烦的事来。」

「天哪，我也真盼着自己别。」埃尔弗丽达说。

而这样的话，正是他们两个都惯说的。

喝过茶，他们决定看书，好减少失败的机会。两人想看的是同一本书——正是《金银岛》（*Treasure Island*）——有一瞬间，两人的好脾气都悬在了秤上。随后两人异口同声，各说了一句「不——你看！」事情最终的结局是，一人拿了一本全然不同的、自己并不特别想看的书。

到了睡觉的时候，埃德雷德替埃尔弗丽达点亮蜡烛；他失手掉了火柴，便由她一根根替他拾起来。

===Original===
“Bless their hearts,” said Mrs. Honeysett, in the passage.

They parted with the heartfelt remark, “We’ve done it this time.”

Now, of course, in the three days when they had not succeeded in being nice to each other they had “looked for the door,” but as the mole had not said where it was, nor what kind of a door, their search had not been fruitful. Most of the rooms had several doors, and as there were a good many rooms the doors numbered fifty-seven, counting cupboards. And among these there was none that seemed worthy to rank above all others as *the* door. Many of the doors in the old part of the house looked as though they might be *the* one, but since there *were* many no one could be sure.

“How shall we know?” Edred asked next morning, through his egg and toast.

“I suppose it’s like when people fall in love,” said Elfrida, through hers. “You see the door and you know at once that it is the only princess in the world, for you—I mean door, of course,” she added.

===Chinese===
「上帝保佑这俩小宝贝。」哈尼塞特太太在过道里说。

他们互道晚安，由衷地说：「这回咱们成了。」

话说回来，在那没能好好相待的三天里，他们自然也「找过那扇门」，可鼹鼠既没说它在哪儿，也没说它是哪一种门，他们的搜寻全无收获。多数屋子都有好几扇门，屋子又多，连碗柜的门也算上，门总共有五十七扇。这中间没有一扇，显得够资格高踞众门之上，当得起*那*扇门。老宅那部分有好多扇门，看着都像是*那*一扇，可既然有这么多扇，谁也拿不准。

「咱们怎么认得出呢？」第二天早晨，埃德雷德就着鸡蛋和吐司问。

「我猜，就跟人们谈恋爱一个样。」埃尔弗丽达也就着她的鸡蛋和吐司说。「你一看见那扇门，立刻就知道它是世上独一无二的公主，是为你预备的——我是说门，当然。」她补了一句。

===Original===
And then, when breakfast was over, they stood up and looked at each other.

“Now,” they said together.

“We’ll look at every single door. Perhaps there’ll be magic writing on *the* door come out in the night, like mushrooms,” said the girl.

“More likely that mole was kidding us,” said the boy.

===Chinese===
然后，早饭一吃完，两人站起来，对望了一眼。

「开始吧。」他们齐声说。

「咱们把每一扇门都看上一遍。说不定一夜之间，*那*扇门上会长出魔法字来，跟长蘑菇似的。」姑娘说。

「更可能是那只鼹鼠耍咱们玩呢。」男孩说。

===Original===
“Oh, *no*,” said the girl; “and we must look at them on both sides—every one. Oh, I do wonder what’s inside the door, don’t you?”

“Bluebeard’s wives, I shouldn’t wonder,” said the boy, “with their heads—”

“If you don’t stop,” said the girl, putting her fingers in her ears, “I won’t look for the door at all. No, I don’t mean to be aggravating; but please don’t. You know I hate it.”

“Come on,” said Edred, “and don’t be a duffer, old chap.”

===Chinese===
「噢，*不会*的。」姑娘说；「咱们还得两面都看——每一扇都要。噢，我真想知道门里面是什么，你不想吗？」

「多半是蓝胡子的那些老婆吧。」男孩说，「脑袋都——」

「你要是再不住口，」姑娘说着把手指堵进耳朵，「我就干脆不找门了。不，我不是存心惹你；求你别说了。你知道我受不了那个。」

「走吧。」埃德雷德说，「别犯傻了，老伙计。」

===Original===
The proudest moments of Elfrida’s life were when her brother called her “old chap.”

So they went and looked at all the fifty-seven doors, one after the other, on the inside and on the outside; some were painted and some were grained, some were carved and some were plain, some had panels and others had none, but they were all of them doors—just doors, and nothing more. Each was just a door, and none of them had any claim at all to be spoken of as **the** door. And when they had looked at all the fifty-seven on the inside and on the outside, there was nothing for it but to look again. So they looked again, very carefully, to see if there were any magic writing that they hadn’t happened to notice. And there wasn’t. So then they began to tap the walls to try and discover a door with a secret spring. And that was no good either.

“There isn’t any old door,” said Edred. “I told you that mole was pulling our leg.”

===Chinese===
埃尔弗丽达一生里最得意的时刻，就是她弟弟唤她「老伙计」的时候。

于是他们去把那五十七扇门一扇接一扇看了个遍，里面看，外面也看；有的上过漆，有的是木纹面，有的雕花，有的素面，有的镶板，有的没有；可它们统统都是门——就是门，仅此而已。每一扇都只是普普通通一扇门，谁也没有半点资格被称作**那**扇门。五十七扇门里外都看过之后，没办法，只好再看一遍。于是他们又看了一遍，非常仔细地看，看有没有什么他们碰巧漏看的魔法字。没有。接着他们开始敲墙，想找出哪扇门装着暗簧。那也没有用。

「根本没有什么了不得的门。」埃德雷德说。「我早说过，那鼹鼠是在拿咱们开心。」

===Original===
“I’m *sure* there is,” said Elfrida, sniffing a little from prolonged anxiety. “Look here—let’s play it like the willing game. I’ll be blindfolded, and you hold my hand and will me to find the door.”

“I don’t believe in the willing game,” said Edred disagreeably.

“No more do I,” said Elfrida; “but we must do something, you know. It’s no good sitting down and saying there isn’t any door.”

“There isn’t, all the same,” said Edred. “Well, come on.”

So Elfrida was blindfolded with her best silk scarf—the blue one with the hemstitched ends—and Edred took her hands. And at once—this happened in the library, where they had found the spell—Elfrida began to walk in a steady and purposeful way. She crossed the hall and went through the green baize door into the other house; went along its corridor and up its dusty stairs—up, and up, and up—

“We’ve looked everywhere here,” said Edred, but Elfrida did not stop for that.

===Chinese===
「我*敢肯定*有。」埃尔弗丽达说，焦心焦得太久，鼻子里抽了抽。「这样吧——咱们就照『意念游戏』（willing game）的玩法来玩。我蒙上眼睛，你牵着我的手，用意念引导我找到那扇门。」

「我可不信心念游戏这一套。」埃德雷德没好气地说。

「我也不信。」埃尔弗丽达说；「可咱们总得想点办法呀。一屁股坐下光嚷着没有门，顶什么用。」

「话虽这么说，反正就是没有。」埃德雷德说。「好吧，来吧。」

于是埃尔弗丽达用她最好的丝巾蒙上了眼睛——就是那条蓝色、两端抽了纱骨的——埃德雷德握住她的双手。她立刻——这事发生在书房里，他们找到咒语的那间——迈开步子走了起来，步子又稳又像笃定了目标。她穿过大厅，推开绿呢门走进那另一座房子；沿走廊走过去，上了积灰的楼梯——上啊，上啊，再上啊——

「这地方咱们哪儿都找过了。」埃德雷德说。可埃尔弗丽达并没有因此停下。

===Original===
“I know I’m going straight to it,” she said. “Oh! do try to believe a little, or we shall never find anything,” and went on along the corridor, where the spiders had draped the picture-frames with their grey crape curtains. There were many doors in this corridor, and Elfrida stopped suddenly at one of them—a door just like the others.

“This,” she said, putting her hand out till it rested on the panel, all spread out like a pink starfish—“this is the door.”

She felt for the handle, turned it, and went in, still pulling at Edred’s hand and with the blue scarf still on her eyes. Edred followed.

“I say!” he said, and then she pulled off the scarf.

The door closed itself very softly behind them.

===Chinese===
「我知道我正笔直朝它走去。」她说。「噢！好歹信一点儿吧，不然咱们什么也找不着。」她沿着走廊继续走，走廊里，蜘蛛给一幅幅画框挂上了灰纱帘。这条走廊上有好多扇门，埃尔弗丽达在其中一扇前猛地站住——一扇跟别的毫无二致的门。

「就是这扇。」她说着伸出手去，直到手贴在门板上，五指张开，活像一只粉红的海星——「这就是那扇门。」

她摸到门把，一拧，走了进去，还拽着埃德雷德的手，蓝丝巾仍蒙在眼上。埃德雷德跟了进去。

「我说！」他喊了一声。这时她扯下了丝巾。

门在他们身后轻轻巧巧地自行合上了。

===Original===
They were in a long attic room close under the roof—a room that they had certainly, in all their explorings, never found before. There were no windows—the roof sloped down at the sides almost to the floor. There was no ceiling—old worm-eaten roof-beams showed the tiles between—and old tie-beams crossed it so that as you stared up it looked like a great ladder with the rungs very far apart. Here and there through the chinks of the tiles a golden dusty light filtered in, and outside was the “tick, tick” of moving pigeon feet, the rustling of pigeon feathers, the “cooroocoo” of pigeon voices. The long room was almost bare; only along each side, close under the roof, was a row of chests, and no two chests were alike.

“Oh!” said Edred. “I’m kind and wise now. I feel it inside me. So now we’ve got the treasure. We’ll rebuild the castle.”

He got to the nearest chest and pushed at the lid, but Elfrida had to push too before he could get the heavy thing up. And when it was up, alas! there was no treasure in the chest—only folded clothes.

So then they tried the next chest.

===Chinese===
他们置身于一间紧贴着屋顶的长阁楼里——这样一间屋子，他们此前历次探险，肯定从没有发现过。屋里没有窗——两侧的屋顶几乎斜落到地板。没有天花板——蛀空的老屋梁间露出一块块瓦——横梁一道道架在上面，你仰头呆望，它就像一架巨大的梯子，梯档隔得老远。瓦缝间这里那里漏进一缕缕金色的、浮着尘的光，外面是鸽子脚走动的「嗒，嗒」声，鸽子翅膀的窸窣声，鸽子嗓门的「咕噜咕」声。长长的屋子几乎空无一物；只有紧贴屋顶的两侧，各排着一列箱子，且没有两只箱子是相像的。

「噢！」埃德雷德说。「我现在又善良又聪明了。我心里感觉得到。所以咱们这就得着宝藏了。咱们要把城堡重新盖起来。」

他奔到最近的一只箱子前，去推箱盖，可直等埃尔弗丽达也来推，那沉沉的家伙才给掀起来。掀起来了，唉！箱子里哪有什么宝藏——只有叠好的衣裳。

于是他们去开第二只箱子。

===Original===
And in all the chests there was no treasure at all—only clothes. Clothes, and more clothes again.

“Well, never mind,” said Elfrida, trying to speak comfortably. “They’ll be splendid for dressing up in.”

“That’s all very well,” said Edred, “but I want the treasure.”

“Perhaps,” said Elfrida, with some want of tact—“perhaps you’re not ‘good and wise’ yet. Not *quite*, I mean,” she hastened to add. “Let’s take the things out and look at them. Perhaps the treasure’s in the pockets.”

But it wasn’t—not a bit of it; not even a threepenny-bit.

===Chinese===
所有的箱子里压根就没有宝藏——只有衣裳。衣裳，又是衣裳。

「嗯，别丧气。」埃尔弗丽达说，尽量说得宽心些。「拿它们化装着玩儿，可棒了。」

「那倒是不错，」埃德雷德说，「可我要的是宝藏。」

「也许，」埃尔弗丽达说，话说得有点不知轻重——「也许你还没到『善良又聪明』的地步。还*差一点点*，我的意思。」她赶紧补上。「咱们把这些东西拿出来瞧瞧吧。说不定宝藏就在口袋里。」

可口袋里没有——半点儿也没有；连一枚三便士硬币都没有。

===Original===
The clothes in the first chest were full riding cloaks and long boots, short-waisted dresses and embroidered scarves, tight breeches and coats with bright buttons. There were very interesting waistcoats and odd-shaped hats. One, a little green one, looked as though it would fit Edred. He tried it on. And at the same minute Elfrida lifted out a little straw bonnet trimmed with blue ribbons. “Here’s one for me,” she said, and put it on.

And then it seemed as though the cooing and rustling of the pigeons came right through the roof and crowded round them in a sort of dazzlement and cloud of pigeon noises. The pigeon noises came closer and closer, and garments were drawn out of the chest and put on the children. They did not know how it was done, any more than you do—but it seemed, somehow, that the pigeon noises were like hands that helped, and presently there the two children stood in clothing such as they had never worn. Elfrida had a short-waisted dress of green-sprigged cotton, with a long and skimpy skirt. Her square-toed brown shoes were gone, and her feet wore flimsy sandals. Her arms were bare, and a muslin handkerchief was folded across her chest. Edred wore very white trousers that came right up under his arms, a blue coat with brass buttons, and a sort of frilly tucker round his neck.

“I say!” they both said, when the pigeon noises had taken themselves away, and they were face to face in the long, empty room.

“That was funny,” Edred added; “let’s go down and show Mrs. Honeysett.”

But when they got out of the door they saw that Mrs. Honeysett, or someone else, must have been very busy while they were on the other side of it, for the floor of the gallery was neatly swept and polished; a strip of carpet, worn, but clean, ran along it, and prints hung straight and square on the cleanly, whitewashed walls, and there was not a cobweb to be seen anywhere. The children opened the gallery doors as they went along, and every room was neat and clean—no dust, no tattered curtains, only perfect neatness and a sort of rather bare comfort showed in all the rooms. Mrs. Honeysett was in none of them. There were no workmen about, yet the baize door was gone, and in its stead was a door of old wood, very shaky and crooked.

===Chinese===
第一只箱子里的衣裳，有下摆宽大的骑装斗篷和长筒靴，有高腰的连衣裙和绣花披巾，有紧腿马裤和缀着亮扣子的外套。还有顶顶有意思的马甲和形状古怪的帽子。有一顶小小的绿帽子，看着正合埃德雷德的头。他试戴上了。同一分钟里，埃尔弗丽达拎出一顶缀着蓝缎带的小草帽。「这顶归我。」她说着戴上了。

接着，鸽子的咕咕声和扑棱声仿佛径直透过屋顶涌了下来，团团裹住他们，化作一片令人目眩的鸽子声的云。鸽子声越来越近，一件件衣裳从箱子里被抽出来，穿到了孩子们身上。这是怎么办成的，他们不知道，你也一样不知道——可不知怎么，那些鸽子声就像一双双帮忙的手，不多时，两个孩子便穿上了他们从未穿过的衣裳。埃尔弗丽达穿一条绿色碎枝花纹棉布的高腰裙，裙身又长又窄。她那双方头的棕色鞋子不见了，脚上是一双薄薄的凉鞋。她双臂裸着，胸前斜折着一方细麻纱手帕。埃德雷德穿一条直提到胳肢窝的雪白裤子，一件缀黄铜扣子的蓝外套，脖子上围了一圈带褶边的颈饰巾。

「我说！」等鸽子声散尽，两人在空荡荡的长屋里打了个照面，齐声说。

「刚才真滑稽。」埃德雷德又说；「咱们下楼给哈尼塞特太太瞧瞧去。」

可他们一出门就明白了：他们待在门这边的时候，哈尼塞特太太，或是别的什么人，想必忙坏了一阵——长廊的地板扫得干干净净，擦得亮亮的；一条磨旧却洁净的地毯顺廊铺去，一幅幅版画端端正正挂在粉刷一新的白墙上，哪里也见不到一张蛛网。孩子们一路推开长廊两旁的门，每间屋子都整洁干净——没有尘土，没有烂窗帘，只有十全十美的齐整，和一种略显空简的舒适。哪间屋里都没有哈尼塞特太太。四下也没有工匠，可那扇绿呢门不见了，原处换上了一扇旧木门，摇摇晃晃，歪歪扭扭。

===Original===
The children ran down the passage to the parlour and burst open the door, looking for Mrs. Honeysett.

There sat a very upright old lady and a very upright old gentleman, and their clothes were not the clothes people wear nowadays. They were like the clothes the children themselves had on. The old lady was hemming a fine white frill; the old gentleman was reading what looked like a page from some newspaper.

“Hoity-toity,” said the old lady very severely; “we forget our manners, I think. Make your curtsey, miss.”

Elfrida made one as well as she could.

“To teach you respect for your elders,” said the old gentleman, “you had best get by heart one of Dr. Watts’s *Divine and Moral Songs*. I leave you to see to it, my lady.”

He laid down the sheet and went out, very straight and dignified, and without quite knowing how it happened the children found themselves sitting on two little stools in a room that was, and was not, the parlour in which they had had that hopeful eggy breakfast, each holding a marbled side of Dr. Watts’s Hymns.

===Chinese===
孩子们顺着过道跑向客厅，一头撞开门，去找哈尼塞特太太。

屋里坐着一位腰板笔直的老太太和一位腰板笔直的老先生，他们穿的衣裳不是如今人们穿的样子，倒和孩子们身上的相仿。老太太正给一条精细的白色褶边缝边；老先生在读一页看着像报纸的东西。

「嗬，放肆。」老太太十分严厉地说；「我看呐，咱们把礼数忘啦。小姐，行个礼吧。」

埃尔弗丽达尽力行了个屈膝礼。

「为教你敬重长辈，」老先生说，「你顶好把华兹博士（Dr. Watts）的《圣诗与道德歌集》（*Divine and Moral Songs*）里的一首背熟。这事就留给您费心了，夫人。」

他放下那页纸，走了出去，腰背笔挺，气度俨然。孩子们还没弄清是怎么回事，就发现自己坐在两枚小凳上，坐在一间既是、又不是他们吃那顿满怀希望的鸡蛋早餐的客厅里，两人一人托着华兹博士那本赞美诗集大理石纹封面的一半。

===Original===
“You will commit to memory the whole of the one commencing—

> “ ‘Happy the child whose youngest years
> Receive instruction well,’

And you will be deprived of pudding with your dinners,” remarked the old lady.

===Chinese===
「你们要把开头是下面这几句的那一首整个背熟——

> 「『那孩子有福，他小小年纪，
> 就把教诲好好领受，』

「而且你们吃饭时再也没有布丁吃了。」老太太说。

===Original===
“I say!” murmured Edred.

“Oh, *hush*!” said Elfrida, as the old lady carried her cambric frills to the window-seat.

“But I won’t stand it,” whispered Edred. “I’ll tell Aunt Edith—and who’s *she* anyhow?” He glowered at the old lady across the speckless carpet.

“Oh, don’t you *understand*?” Elfrida whispered back. “We’ve got turned into somebody else, and she’s our grandmamma.”

I don’t know how it was that Elfrida saw this and Edred didn’t. Perhaps because she was a girl, perhaps because she was two years older than he. They looked hopelessly at the bright sunlight outside, and then at the dull, small print of the marble-backed book.

===Chinese===
「我说！」埃德雷德喃喃道。

「噢，*别作声*！」埃尔弗丽达说。这时老太太正把她那麻纱褶边的活计挪到窗座那边去。

「可我受不了这个。」埃德雷德耳语道。「我要告诉伊迪丝姑姑——再说，*她*到底是谁呀？」他隔着那张一尘不染的地毯，朝老太太怒目而视。

「噢，你*还不明白*吗？」埃尔弗丽达耳语回去。「咱们变成别人啦，她是咱们的祖母。」

埃尔弗丽达怎么看出来的，埃德雷德怎么就没看出来，我不知道。兴许因为她是女孩子，兴许因为她比他大两岁。他们先绝望地望了望外面明晃晃的太阳，又望了望那本大理石纹封底的书上灰扑扑的小字。

===Original===
“Edred,” said the old lady, “hand me the paper.” She pointed at the sheet on the brightly polished table. He got up and carried it across to her, and as he did so he glanced at it and saw:—

> **The Times**
> June 16, 1807

And then he knew, as well as Elfrida did, exactly where he was, and *when*.

===Chinese===
「埃德雷德。」老太太说，「把报纸递给我。」她指了指那张亮闪闪的桌上的一页纸。他起身把纸拿过去，递的时候朝上面瞥了一眼，看见——

> **《泰晤士报》**
> 1807 年 6 月 16 日

这一来，他和埃尔弗丽达一样，确切地知道自己身在何处，身在*何时*了。

## IX The Prisoners in the Tower / 塔中的囚徒

===Original===
Imprisoned in the Tower of London, accused of high treason, and having confessed to a too intimate knowledge of the Gunpowder Plot, Elfrida could not help feeling that it would be nice to be back again in her own time, and at Arden, where, if you left events alone, and didn't interfere with them by any sort of magic mouldiwarpiness, nothing dangerous, romantic or thrilling would ever happen. And yet, when she *was* there, as you know, she never could let events alone. She and Edred could not be content with that castle and that house which, even as they stood, would have made you and me so perfectly happy. They wanted the treasure, and they—Elfrida especially—wanted adventures. Well, now they had got an adventure, both of them. There was no knowing how it would turn out either, and that, after all, is the essence of adventures. Edred was lodged with Lord Arden and several other gentlemen in the White Tower, and Elfrida and Lady Arden were in quite a different part of the building. And the children were not allowed to meet. This, of course, made it impossible for either of them to try to get back to their own times. For though they sometimes quarrelled, as you know, they were really fond of each other, and most of us would hesitate to leave even a person we were *not* very fond of alone a prisoner in the Tower in the time of James I and the Gunpowder Plot.

Elfrida had to wait on her mother and to sew at the sampler, which had been thoughtfully brought by the old nurse with her lady's clothes, and the clothes Elfrida wore. But there were no games, and the only out-of-doors Elfrida could get was on a very narrow terrace where dead flower-stalks stuck up out of a still narrower border, beside a flagged pathway where there was just room for *one* to walk, and not for two. From this terrace you could see the fat, queer-looking ships in the river, and the spire of St. Paul's.

Edred was more fortunate. He was allowed to play in the garden of the Lieutenant of the Tower. But he did not feel much like playing. He wanted to find Elfrida and get back to Arden. Everyone was very kind to him, but he had to be very much quieter than he was used to being, and to say Sir and Madam, and not to speak till he was spoken to. You have no idea how tiresome it is not to speak till you are spoken to, with the world full, as it is, of a thousand interesting things that you want to ask questions about.

One day—for they were there quite a number of days—Edred met someone who seemed to like answering questions, and this made more difference than perhaps you would think.

===Chinese===
身陷伦敦塔，顶着叛国大罪的指控，又招认了自己对火药阴谋知之过详——埃尔弗丽达不由得觉得，要是能回到自己的时代、回到阿登去，该有多好。在阿登，只要你不去招惹世事，不用任何穆迪沃普式的魔法横加干涉，就永远不会有什么危险的事、浪漫的事、惊心动魄的事发生。可她当初*在那儿*的时候——这你是知道的——偏偏从来不肯放过世事。她和埃德雷德守着那座城堡、那座宅子，本该心满意足——照它们本来的样子，早够让你我快活似神仙了。可他们要的是宝藏，他们——尤其是埃尔弗丽达——要的是奇遇。这下好了，奇遇他们俩都摊上了。而且谁也说不好会怎么收场——说到底，这正是奇遇的精髓。埃德雷德同阿登勋爵和另外几位绅士一道，被安置在白塔里；埃尔弗丽达和阿登夫人则住在楼里截然不同的另一处。两个孩子不许见面。这样一来，他们俩谁也没法设法回到自己的时代去了。因为，虽说他们有时也拌嘴——这你也知道——他们心里实在是相亲相爱的；何况我们大多数人，就算对*并不*怎么亲爱的人，也不忍心把人家独自一个撇在詹姆斯一世和火药阴谋年月的伦敦塔里当囚徒。

埃尔弗丽达得伺候母亲，还得在一幅刺绣样本上做针线——亏得老奶妈想得周到，把它连同夫人的衣裳、埃尔弗丽达身上穿的一并带来了。可这里没有一样玩儿的，埃尔弗丽达能沾到的「户外」，只有一条极窄的台地：枯死的花梗戳在一条更窄的花畦里，旁边一条石板小径，只容*一个*人走，容不下两个。从这条台地上望得见河里那些肥肥胖胖、模样古怪的船，还望得见圣保罗教堂的尖顶。

埃德雷德运气好些。他获准在伦敦塔典狱长（the Lieutenant of the Tower）的花园里玩。可他一点也提不起玩兴。他一心想找到埃尔弗丽达，一起回阿登去。人人待他都极和气，可他必须比平日安静得多，得口口声声「老爷」「夫人」，人家不先开口，他就不能说话。这世上有趣的东西成千上万，你样样都想问个明白，偏又只能等人先开口——那份憋闷，你根本想象不到。

有一天——他们在塔里待的日子着实不少——埃德雷德遇上了一位看样子乐意回答问题的人物，而这一来局面改变之大，也许远超你的想象。

===Original===
Edred was walking one bright winter morning in the private garden of the Lieutenant of the Tower, and he saw coming towards him a very handsome old gentleman dressed in very handsome clothes, and, what is more, the clothes blazed with jewels. Now, most of the gentlemen who were prisoners in the Tower at that time thought that their very oldest clothes were good enough to be in prison in, so this splendour that was coming across the garden was very unusual as well as very dazzling, and before Edred could remember the rules about not speaking till you're spoken to, he found that he had suddenly bowed and said—

"Your servant, sir;" adding, "you do look ripping!"

"I do not take your meaning," said the gentleman, but he smiled kindly.

"I mean, how splendid you look!"

The old gentleman looked pleased.

"I am happy to command your admiration," he said.

===Chinese===
一个晴朗的冬日早晨，埃德雷德正在典狱长的私人花园里散步，只见迎面走来一位十分体面的老绅士，穿着一身十分体面的衣裳；更了不得的是，那衣裳上珠光宝气，直晃人眼。当时被关在塔里的绅士们，多半觉得把身上最旧的衣裳穿来坐牢就尽够了，所以这一身正穿过花园来的富丽行头，不但晃得人眼晕，也是难得一见的奇观。埃德雷德还没来得及想起「人家不先开口你不能说话」的规矩，就发现自己已经蓦地鞠了一躬，脱口说道——

「给您请安，老爷；」接着又添上一句，「您瞧着可真神气！」

「我不明白你的意思。」绅士说，却和气地笑了。

「我是说，您瞧上去多气派呀！」

老绅士面有得色。

「能受你赞赏，实为幸事。」他说。

===Original===
"I mean your clothes;" said Edred, and then feeling with a shock that this was not the way to behave, he added, "Your face is splendid too—only I've been taught manners, and I know you mustn't tell people they're handsome in their faces. 'Praise to the face is open disgrace,'—Mrs. Honeysett says so."

"Praise to *my* face isn't open disgrace," said the gentleman, "it is a pleasant novelty in these walls."

"Is it your birthday or anything?" Edred asked.

"It is not my birthday," said the gentleman smiling. "But why the question?"

===Chinese===
「我是说您的衣裳；」埃德雷德说，随即猛然醒悟这样说话不像话，赶紧找补道，「您的脸也叫气派——不过我学过规矩，知道不能当面夸人家的脸长得俊。『当面夸脸，便是丢脸』——哈尼塞特太太说的。」

「当面夸*我*的脸可算不得丢脸，」绅士说，「在这几堵墙里头，这还是桩叫人愉快的新鲜事儿呢。」

「今儿是您的生日还是怎么？」埃德雷德问。

「今天不是我的生日。」绅士含笑说。「不过为什么这么问？」

===Original===
"Because you're so grand," said Edred. "I suppose you're a prince then?"

"No, not a prince—a prisoner."

"Oh, I see," said Edred, as people so often do when they don't; "and you're going to be let out today, and you've put on your best things to go home in. I *am* so glad. At least, I'm sorry you're going, but I'm glad on your account."

"Thou'rt a fine, bold boy," said the gentleman. "But no. I am a prisoner, and like to remain so. And for these gauds," he swelled out his chest so that his diamond buttons and ruby earrings and gem-set collar flashed in the winter sun—"for these gauds, never shall it be said that Walter Raleigh let the shadow of his prison tarnish his pride in the proper arraying of a body that has been honoured to kneel before the Virgin Queen." He took off his hat at the last words and swept it, with a flourish, nearly to the ground.

===Chinese===
「瞧您这么气派呀。」埃德雷德说。「那您是位亲王喽？」

「不，不是亲王——是个囚徒。」

「噢，我明白了。」埃德雷德说——人们不明白的时候，多半都这么答——「您今儿要放出去了，所以穿上最好的衣裳好回家去。我*真*替您高兴。至少，您要走我舍不得，不过为您着想，我是高兴的。」

「你是个又俊又胆大的孩子。」绅士说。「不过不然。我是囚徒，而且多半要一直当下去。至于这身华彩行头，」他挺起胸膛，好教钻石纽扣、红宝石耳环和嵌宝的衣领在冬日的太阳底下熠熠生辉——「至于这身行头，千万不可让人说，沃尔特·雷利（Walter Raleigh）让监狱的阴影玷污了他的一身骄傲——这具身子，既曾有幸跪在童贞女王面前，就该穿扮得配得上它。」说到末几个字，他摘下帽子，潇洒地一挥，几乎挥到地上。

===Original===
"*Oh!*" cried Edred, "are you really Sir Walter Raleigh? Oh, how splendid! And now you'll tell me all about the golden South Americas, and sea-fights, and the Armada and the Spaniards, and what you used to play at when you were a little boy."

"Ay," said Sir Walter, "I'll tell thee tales enow. They'll not let me from speaking with thee, I warrant. I would," he said, looking round impatiently, "that I could see the river again. From my late chamber I saw it, and the goodly ships coming in and out—the ships that go down into the great waters." He sighed, was silent a moment, then spoke. "And so thou didst not know thine old friend Raleigh? He was all forgot, all forgot! And yet thou hast rid astride my sword ere now, and I have played with thee in the courtyard at Arden. When England forgets so soon, who can expect more from a child?"

"I'm sorry," said Edred humbly.

"Nay," said Sir Walter, pinching his ear gently, "'tis two years agone, and short years have short memories. Thou shall come with me to my chamber and I will show thee a chart and a map of Windargocoa, that Her Dear Glorious Majesty permitted me to rename Virginia, after her great and gracious self."

===Chinese===
「*噢！*」埃德雷德喊道，「您当真是沃尔特·雷利爵士？噢，太了不起了！那您这就会给我讲黄金的南美洲，讲海战，讲无敌舰队和西班牙人，再讲讲您小时候都玩些什么了吧。」

「是啊。」沃尔特爵士说，「我给你讲的故事管够。他们总不至于不许我同你说话，这我敢保。但愿，」他不耐烦地四下望了望，「我还能再看见那条河。从先前那间屋子里，我望得见它，望得见那些进进出出的漂亮大船——那些驶向大洋的船。」他叹了口气，沉默片刻，才又开口。「这么说，你不认得你的老朋友雷利了？全忘了，全忘了！可你从前还把我这口剑当马骑过呢，我还在阿登的院子里陪你耍过。连英格兰都忘得这么快，又怎能指望一个孩子记性好呢？」

「对不起。」埃德雷德老老实实地说。

「哪儿的话。」沃尔特爵士说着，轻轻捏了捏他的耳朵。「那是两年前的事了，短短的年月，短短的记性。你随我到我屋里来，我给你看一张航海图，还有温达戈科阿（Windargocoa）的地图——那位亲爱光荣的陛下恩准我把它改名为弗吉尼亚，就照着她老人家那伟大而仁慈的圣躬。」

===Original===
So Edred, very glad and proud, went hand in hand with Sir Walter Raleigh to his apartments, and saw many strange things from overseas—dresses of feathers from Mexico, and strange images in gold from strange islands, and the tip of a narwhal's horn from Greenland, and many other things. And Sir Walter told him of his voyages and his fights, and of how he and Humphrey Gilbert, and Adrian Gilbert, and little Jack Davis used to sail their toy boats in the Long Stream, and how they used to row in and out among the big ships down at the Port, and look at the great figureheads, standing out high above the water, and wonder about them and about the strange lands they came from.

"And often," said Sir Walter, "we found a sea-captain that would tell us lads travellers' tales like these I have told thee. And we sailed our little ships, and then we sailed our big ships—and here I lie in dock, and shall never sail again. But it's oh! to see the Devon moors, and the clear reaches of the Long Stream again! And that I never shall." And with that he leaned his arm on the windowsill, and if he had not been the great Sir Walter Raleigh, who is in all the history books, Edred would have thought he was crying.

"Oh, do cheer up—do!" said Edred awkwardly. "I don't know whether they'll let you go to Devonshire—but I know they'll let you go back to America some day. With twelve ships. I read about it only yesterday; and your ship will be called the *Destiny*, and you'll sail from the Thames, and Lord Arden will see you off and kiss you for farewell, and give you a medal for a keepsake. Your son will go with you. I *know* it's true. It's all in the book."

===Chinese===
于是埃德雷德高高兴兴、满面光彩，手拉手跟着沃尔特·雷利爵士去了他的住处，见识了许多海外的奇物——墨西哥来的羽毛衣裳，奇异岛屿来的奇异金像，格陵兰独角鲸的角尖，还有许许多多别的。沃尔特爵士给他讲自己的航行和战斗，讲他和汉弗莱·吉尔伯特、阿德里安·吉尔伯特，还有小杰克·戴维斯，从前怎样在长溪（the Long Stream）里放他们的玩具小船；讲他们怎样在港口划着小船，在大船中间钻进钻出，仰望那些高高突出水面的巨大船首像，对着它们、对着它们来自的奇异国土出神猜想。

「还常有呢，」沃尔特爵士说，「我们能碰上一位肯给我们这些小子讲海外奇谈的船长，就像我方才讲给你听的这些。我们先放小船，后来就驶大船——如今我泊在这儿进了船坞，再也航不成海了。唉，真想再看一眼德文郡的荒原，再看一眼长溪清亮的河段啊！可我永远看不成了。」说着，他把胳膊支在窗台上；倘若他不是那位写进所有史书里的了不起的沃尔特·雷利爵士，埃德雷德准会以为他在哭。

「噢，您千万想开些——千万！」埃德雷德局促地说。「我不知道他们让不让您回德文郡——可我知道，他们早晚会放您回美洲去。带着十二艘船。我昨天才读到的；您的船会叫『命运号』，您要从泰晤士河启航，阿登勋爵会来给您送行，亲吻您道别，还赠您一枚勋章做纪念。您的儿子也同您一道去。我*知道*这是真的。书里全写着呢。」

===Original===
"The book?" Sir Walter asked. "A prophecy, belike?"

"You can call it that if you want to," said Edred cautiously; "but, anyhow, it's true."

He had read it all in the *History of Arden*.

"If it should be true," said Sir Walter, and the smile came back to his merry eyes, "and if I ever sail to the Golden West again, shrew me but I will sack a Spanish town, and bring thee a collar of gold and pieces of eight—a big bag-full."

"Thank you, very much," said Edred, "it is very kind of you: but I shall not be there."

And all Sir Walter's questions did not make him say how he knew this, or what he meant by it.

===Chinese===
「书？」沃尔特爵士问。「多半是部先知书吧？」

「您愿意这么叫，就这么叫好了。」埃德雷德小心地说，「不过反正，是真的。」

这些他都是从《阿登家史》里读来的。

「倘若这是真的，」沃尔特爵士说，笑意回到他那双快活的眼睛里，「倘若我当真能再航向金色的西方，嘿，那我非去劫一座西班牙城镇不可，给你带回一条金项圈，外加八雷亚尔银币——满满一大袋。」

「谢谢您，多谢多谢，」埃德雷德说，「您真是太好心了：可惜到那时候，我不在了。」

沃尔特爵士左问右问，也没能问出他是怎么知道的，或是这话是什么意思。

===Original===
After this he met Sir Walter every day in the lieutenant's garden, and the two prisoners comforted each other. At least Edred was comforted, and Sir Walter *seemed* to be. But no one could be sure if it was more than seeming. This was one of the questions that always puzzled the children—and they used to talk it over together till their heads seemed to be spinning round. The question of course was: Did their being in past times make any difference to the other people in past times? In other words, when you were taking part in historical scenes, did it matter what you said or did? Of course, it seemed to matter extremely—at the time. But then if this going into the past was only a sort of dream, then, of course, the people in the past would know nothing about it, unless they had dreamed the same sort of dream—which, as Elfrida often pointed out, was quite likely, especially if time didn't count, or could be cheated by white clocks. On the other hand, if they *really* went into the *real* past—well, then, of course, what they did must count for real too, as Edred so often said. And yet how could it, since they took with them into the past all that they learned here? And with that knowledge they could have revealed plots, shown the issue of wars and the fate of kings, and, as Elfrida put it, "made history turn out quite different." You see the difficulties, don't you? And Betty Lovell's having said that they could leave no trace on times past did not seem to make much difference somehow, one way or the other.

However, just now Elfrida and Edred were in the Tower, and not able to see each other, so they could not discuss that or any other question. And they always hoped that they would meet, but they never did.

But by and by the Queen thought of Lady Arden, and decided that she and her son Edred ought to be let out of the Tower, and she told the King so, and he told Lord Somebody or other, who told the Lieutenant of the Tower, and behold Lady Arden and Edred were abruptly sent home in their own coach, which had been suddenly sent for from Arden House; but Elfrida was left in charge of the wife of the Lieutenant of the Tower, who was a very kind lady. So now Elfrida was in the Tower, and Edred was at Arden House in Soho, and they had not been able to speak to each other or arrange any plan for getting back to 1908 and Arden Castle by the sea.

===Chinese===
打这以后，埃德雷德天天在典狱长的花园里同沃尔特爵士见面，两个囚徒互相宽慰。至少埃德雷德是得了安慰的，沃尔特爵士*看上去*也得着了。可这不止是看上去像不像，谁也不敢断言。这正是老叫孩子们犯嘀咕的问题之一——他们常凑在一块儿翻来覆去地讨论，直讨论得脑袋嗡嗡打转。问题自然是：他们到了往昔，对往昔里的旁人到底有没有影响？换句话说，当你亲身参与历史场面的时候，你说什么、做什么，到底作数不作数？当然，在当时当地，似乎作数得很。可要是这趟回到过去只是一场梦，那么过去的人自然对此一无所知，除非他们恰好做了同样的梦——埃尔弗丽达就常指出，这很可能，尤其是在时间不算数、或者能叫白钟蒙混过去的时候。可话说回来，倘若他们*当真*去了*真实的*过去——那么，正如埃德雷德常说的，他们所做的一切自然也得真真实实地作数。可这又怎么可能呢？要知道，他们把在这里学到的一切都带回了过去啊！凭着这些学问，他们本可以揭破阴谋，预言战争的胜负和国王的命运，照埃尔弗丽达的说法，「把历史改出个全然不同的结局来」。你瞧瞧这里头的难处，是不是？至于贝蒂·洛弗尔说过的、他们不会在往昔留下任何痕迹那句话，不知怎么的，似乎也没把事情朝哪边推明白多少。

不过眼下，埃尔弗丽达和埃德雷德都关在塔里，见不着面，所以这问题也好，别的也好，都无从谈起。他们总盼着能见上一面，可始终没能。

可后来王后想起了阿登夫人，认定她和她儿子埃德雷德该放出塔去，便告诉了国王；国王告诉了某位勋爵大人，那位勋爵又告诉了伦敦塔典狱长；于是瞧啊，阿登夫人和埃德雷德猝然被自家马车接回家去——那辆车是从阿登宅邸急急召来的；埃尔弗丽达则托付给了典狱长夫人，一位极和善的夫人。就这样，埃尔弗丽达留在了伦敦塔，埃德雷德住进了索霍的阿登宅邸，两人始终没能说上一句话，也没能商量出任何回 1908 年、回海边阿登城堡的法子。

===Original===
Of course Elfrida was kept in the Tower because she had sung the rhyme about—

> "Please to remember
> The fifth of November—
> The Gunpowder Treason and Plot,"

and this made people think—or seem to think—that she knew all about the Gunpowder Plot. And so of course she did, though it would have been very difficult for her to show anyone at that time *how* she knew it, without being a traitor.

She was now allowed to see Lord Arden every day, and she grew very fond of him. He was curiously like her own daddy, who had gone away to South America with Uncle Jim, and had never come back to his little girl. Lord Arden also seemed to grow fonder of her every day. "Thou'rt a bold piece," he'd tell her, "and thou growest bolder with each day. Hast thou no fear that thy daddy will have thee whipped for answering him so pert?"

"No!" Elfrida would say, hugging him as well as she could for his ruff. "*I* know you wouldn't beat your girl, don't I, daddy?" And as she hugged him it felt *almost* like hugging her own daddy, who would never come home from America.

===Chinese===
当然，埃尔弗丽达被扣在塔里，是因为她哼过那支歌谣——

> 「请记住呀，
> 十一月五日——
> 火药叛国的阴谋。」

这一哼，叫人觉得——或者装作觉得——她对火药阴谋了如指掌。她也确实了如指掌，只是要在那时候向任何人证明自己*怎么*知道的，除非去当叛徒，否则实在难办。

如今她获准每天见阿登勋爵，她也一天比一天黏他。他竟出奇地像她自己的爸爸——那个跟吉姆叔叔去了南美、再没回到小女儿身边的爸爸。阿登勋爵似乎也一天比一天更疼她。「你是个天不怕地不怕的小丫头，」他总对她说，「而且一天胆大过一天。你就不怕你爸爸恼你回嘴回得这样尖，叫人拿鞭子抽你？」

「不怕！」埃尔弗丽达总这么说，隔着他的大皱领尽力搂他。「*我*知道您舍不得打您的丫头，对不对，爸爸？」搂着他的时候，那感觉*几乎*就像搂着她自己的爸爸——那个再也不会从美洲回家的爸爸。

===Original===
So she was almost contented. She knew that Lord Arden was not one of those to suffer for the Gunpowder Plot. She knew from the History of Arden that he would just be banished from the Court, and end his days happily at Arden, and she was almost tempted just to go on and let what would happen, and stay with this new daddy who had lived three hundred years before, and pet him and be petted by him. Only she felt that she must do something because of Edred. The worst of it was that she could not think of anything to do. She did not know at all what was happening to Edred—whether he was being happy or unhappy.

As it happened he was being, if not unhappy, at least uncomfortable. Mr. Parados, the tutor, who was as nasty a man as you will find in any seaside academy for young gentlemen, still remained at Arden House, and taught the boys—Edred and his cousin Richard. Mr. Parados was in high favour with the King, because he had listened to what wasn't meant for him, reported it where it would do most mischief—a thing always very pleasing to King James the First—and Lady Arden dared not dismiss him. Besides, she was ill with trouble and anxiety, which Edred could not at all soothe by saying again and again, "Father *won't* be found guilty of treason—he *won't* be executed. He'll just be sent to Arden, and live there quietly with you. I saw it all in a book."

But Lady Arden only cried and cried.

Mr. Parados was very severe, and rapped Edred's knuckles almost continuously during lesson-time, and out of it; said Cousin Richard, "He is forever bent on spying and browbeating of us."

===Chinese===
这样，她差不多心满意足了。她知道，阿登勋爵不在为火药阴谋受难的那批人里。她从《阿登家史》里知道，他不过被逐出宫廷，日后在阿登安享天年。她几乎当真动了念头：索性由它去，听凭事情自然发生，就留在这位在三百年前活过的新爸爸身边，疼他，也受他疼。只是为了埃德雷德，她觉得总得做点什么。糟糕的是，她想不出能做什么。埃德雷德那边怎么样了，她一概不知——他是快活呢，还是不快活。

说来也巧，他即便不算不快活，至少也不痛快。家庭教师帕拉多斯先生（Mr. Parados）——就是那种你在随便哪所培养少年绅士的海滨学堂里都挑得出的坏东西——仍旧待在阿登宅邸，教两个男孩——埃德雷德和他的表哥理查德。帕拉多斯先生正得国王的宠，因为他偷听了不该他听的话，又把话递到为害最大的地方去——这种事向来最讨詹姆斯一世国王的喜欢——阿登夫人不敢辞退他。加上她忧愁焦虑，病倒了。埃德雷德一遍又一遍地对她说，「父亲*不会*被判叛国罪的——他*不会*被处死的。他只会被送往阿登，同你安安静静住在那儿。我在一本书里全看到了。」可这话一点也宽慰不了她。

阿登夫人只是哭了又哭。

帕拉多斯先生极严厉，上课时几乎不停地敲埃德雷德的指节，下了课也照敲不误。理查德表哥说：「他一天到晚就惦记着刺探咱们、威吓咱们。」

===Original===
"He's always messing about—nasty sneak," said Edred. "I should like to be even with him before I go. And I will too."

"Before you go? Go whither?" Cousin Richard asked.

"Elfrida and I are going away," Edred began, and then felt how useless it was to go on, since even when the 1908 Edred—who he was—had gone, the 1605 Elfrida and Edred would of course still be there—that is if … He checked the old questions, which he had now no time to consider, and said, in a firm tone which was new to him, and which Elfrida would have been astonished and delighted to hear—

"Yes, I've got two things to do: to be even with old Parrot-nose—to be revenged on him, I mean—and to get Elfrida out of the Tower. And I'll do that first, because she'll like to help with the other."

===Chinese===
「他老在眼前晃来晃去——卑鄙的告密鬼。」埃德雷德说。「我走以前，非同他算清这笔账不可。我也一定算得清。」

「走以前？」理查德表哥问。「上哪儿去？」

「埃尔弗丽达和我打算走。」埃德雷德开了个头，随即觉出再说下去也是白说：因为即便 1908 年的埃德雷德——也就是他自己——走了，1605 年的埃尔弗丽达和埃德雷德自然还会在原地——也就是说，倘若……他把那些老问题咽了回去——眼下没工夫细想——然后用一种他自己也陌生的坚定口吻开了腔，那口吻埃尔弗丽达听了准会又惊又喜——

「对，我有两件事要办：一件是同老鹦鹉鼻子算账——我是说，报他的仇——一件是把埃尔弗丽达从塔里救出去。我先办救她这件，因为另一件，她准乐意搭把手。」

===Original===
The boys were on the leads, their backs to a chimney and their faces towards the trapdoor, which was the only way of getting on to the roof. It was very cold, and the north wind was blowing, but they had come there because it was one of the few places where Mr. Parrot-nose could not possibly come creeping up behind them to listen to what they were saying.

"Get her out of the Tower?" Dick laughed and then was sad. "I would we could!" he said.

"We *can*," said Edred earnestly. "I've been thinking about it all the time, ever since we came out of the Tower, and I know the way. I shall want you to help me, Dick. You and one grownup." He spoke in the same grim, self-reliant tone that was so new to him.

"One grownup?" Dick asked.

"Yes. *I* think Nurse would do it. And I'm going to find out if we can trust her."

===Chinese===
两个男孩正在屋顶的铅板平台上，背靠一根烟囱，脸朝着活动天窗——那是登上屋顶的唯一通路。天寒地冻，北风呼呼地刮，可他们偏挑了这儿，因为这是寥寥几处老鹦鹉鼻子决计没法蹑手蹑脚绕到背后偷听他们说话的地方之一。

「把她从塔里救出来？」迪克笑了，随即又黯然。「咱们要真能办到才好呢！」他说。

「咱们*办得到*。」埃德雷德恳切地说。「自打从塔里出来，我就一直在琢磨这事，路子我有了。我要你帮我，迪克。你，外加一个大人。」他用的还是那种全然陌生于他的、狠巴巴又自拿主意的口吻。

「一个大人？」迪克问。

「对。*我*看奶妈就肯干。我这就去探探，看她信不信得过。」

===Original===
"Trust her?" said Dick. "Why, she'd die for any of us Ardens. Ay, and die on the rack before she would betray the lightest word of any of us."

"Then *that's* all right," said Edred.

"What is thy plot?" Dick asked; and he did not laugh, though he might well have wanted to. You see, Edred looked so very small and weak and the Tower was so very big and strong.

"I'm going to get Elfrida out," said Edred, "and I'm going to do it like Lady Nithsdale got her husband out. It will be quite easy. It all depends on knowing when the guard is changed, and I *do* know that."

===Chinese===
「信不信得过？」迪克说。「嗐，为了咱们阿登家随便哪一个，她连性命都肯豁出去。是啊，把她架上拉肢刑架，她也决不肯吐露咱们任何人最不要紧的一句话。」

「那就*成了*。」埃德雷德说。

「你的计策是什么？」迪克问。他没有笑，虽然他满可以想笑。你想啊，埃德雷德看上去那么瘦小孱弱，伦敦塔又是那么庞大坚固。

「我打算把埃尔弗丽达救出来，」埃德雷德说，「办法就照尼斯代尔夫人（Lady Nithsdale）救她丈夫的那样。容易得很。全靠知道守卫什么时候换岗，而这我*恰恰*知道。」

===Original===
"But how did my Lady Nithsdale get my Lord Nithsdale out—and from what?" Dick asked.

"Why, out of the Tower, you know," Edred was beginning, when he remembered that Dick did *not* know and couldn't know, because Lord Nithsdale hadn't yet been taken out of the Tower, hadn't even been put in—perhaps, for anything Edred knew, wasn't even born yet. So he said—

"Never mind. I'll tell you all about Lady Nithsdale," and proceeded to tell Dick, vaguely yet inspiringly, the story of that wise and brave lady. *I* haven't time to tell *you* the story, but any grownup who knows history will be only too pleased to tell it.

Dick listened with most flattering interest, though it was getting dusk and colder than ever. The lights were lighted in the house and the trapdoor had become a yellow square. A shadow in this yellow square warned Dick, and he pinched Edred's arm.

===Chinese===
「可是那位尼斯代尔夫人是怎么把尼斯代尔老爷救出来的——又是从哪儿救出来的？」迪克问。

「嘿，就是从这伦敦塔里呀，你知道的。」埃德雷德刚要这么说，猛地想起迪克*并*不知道，也不可能知道——因为尼斯代尔勋爵还没被人从伦敦塔里救出去，甚至还没被关进去——就埃德雷德所知，说不定人还没出生呢。于是他说——

「不相干。我把尼斯代尔夫人的事，原原本本讲给你听。」接着他就把那位又聪明又勇敢的夫人的故事，讲得隐隐约约又鼓荡人心，说给迪克听。*我*可没工夫讲给*你*听，不过随便哪位懂历史的大人，都会巴不得讲给你听的。

迪克听得津津有味、受宠若惊，尽管天色渐晚，也比先前更冷了。屋里点起了灯，那扇活动天窗成了一方黄澄澄的亮块。亮块里一道影子给迪克报了信，他拧了一把埃德雷德的胳膊。

===Original===
"Come," he said, "and let us apply ourselves to our books. Virtuous youths always act in their preceptors' absence as they would if their preceptors were present. I feel as though mine *were* present. Therefore, I take it, I am a virtuous youth."

On which the shadow disappeared very suddenly, and the two boys, laughing in a choking inside sort of way, went down to learn their lessons by the light of two guttering tallow candles in solid silver candlesticks.

The next day Edred got the old nurse to take him to the Court, and because the Queen was very fond of Lady Arden he actually managed to see her Majesty and, what is more, to get permission to visit his father and sister in the Tower. The permission was written by the Queen's own hand and bade the Lieutenant of the Tower to admit Master Edred Arden and Master Richard Arden and an attendant. Then the nurse became very busy with sewing, and two days went by, and Mr. Parados rapped the boys' fingers and scolded them and scowled at them and wondered why they bore it all so patiently. Then came The Day, and it was bitterly cold, and as the afternoon got older snow began to fall.

"So much the better," said the old nurse, "so much the better."

===Chinese===
「来，」他说，「咱们用功读书去吧。有德的少年，在先生不在跟前时的一举一动，总同先生在跟前时一个样。我觉得我的先生*就*在跟前。所以呢，依我看，我是个有德的少年。」

话音未落，那道影子倏地不见了。两个男孩憋着嗓子、闷在肚里直笑，下楼就着两支烛泪涔涔的牛油烛——插在实心银烛台上——念书去了。

第二天，埃德雷德求老奶妈带他进宫。因为王后极喜欢阿登夫人，他居然当真见到了王后陛下，而且不但如此，还讨得一道恩准，准他到塔里探视父亲和姐姐。恩准出自王后亲笔，命伦敦塔典狱长放埃德雷德·阿登少爷、理查德·阿登少爷及一名仆从入内。随后奶妈便忙着做针线，两天过去了。帕拉多斯先生敲两个男孩的手指，骂他们，瞪他们，纳闷他们怎么忍得这样纹丝不动。然后，那个日子到了。天冷得砭骨，午后渐深，雪下起来了。

「那就更好了。」老奶妈说，「那就更好了。」

===Original===
It was at dusk that the guard was changed at the Tower Gate, and a quarter of an hour before dusk Lord Arden's carriage stopped at the Tower Gate and an old nurse in ruff and cap and red cloak got out of it and lifted out two little gentlemen, one in black with a cloak trimmed with squirrel fur, which was Edred, and another, which was Richard, in grey velvet and marten's fur. And the lieutenant was called, and he read the Queen's order and nodded kindly to Edred, and they all went in. And as they went across the yard to the White Tower, where Lord Arden's lodging was, the snow fell thick on their cloaks and furs and froze to the stuff, for it was bitter cold.

And again, "So much the better," the nurse said, "so much the better."

Elfrida was with Lord Arden, sitting on his knee, when the visitors came in. She jumped up and greeted Edred with a glad cry and a very close hug.

"Go with Nurse," he whispered through the hug. "Do exactly what she tells you."

===Chinese===
塔门换岗在暮色四合时进行。暮色前一刻钟，阿登勋爵的马车停在了塔门前，一位头上戴帽、颈间皱领、身披红斗篷的老奶妈下了车，又抱下两位小少爷：一位一身黑，斗篷镶着松鼠皮——是埃德雷德；另一位——是理查德——穿灰天鹅绒，镶貂皮。典狱长被请了来，他读了王后的手谕，朝埃德雷德和气地点点头，一行人便进去了。穿过院子往白塔——阿登勋爵的住处——去的一路上，雪浓密地落在他们的斗篷和皮毛上，冻在了衣料上，因为天冷得砭骨。

奶妈又说了一遍：「那就更好了，那就更好了。」

探视的人进来时，埃尔弗丽达正挨着阿登勋爵，坐在他膝上。她跳起身，欢叫一声迎接埃德雷德，紧紧搂住他。

「跟奶妈走。」他隔着拥抱悄声说。「她吩咐什么，你就一丝不差地做什么。」

===Original===
"But I've made a piece of poetry," Elfrida whispered, "and now you're here."

"*Do what you're told*," whispered Edred in a tone she had never heard from him before and so fiercely that she said no more about poetry. "We must get you out of this," Edred went on. "Don't be a duffer—think of Lady Nithsdale."

Then Elfrida understood. Her arms fell from round Edred's neck and she ran back to Lord Arden and put her arms round *his* neck and kissed him over and over again.

"There, there, my maid, there, there!" he said, patting her shoulder softly, for she was crying.

===Chinese===
「可我已经作好一首诗了，」埃尔弗丽达悄声说，「这不，你也来了。」

「*叫你怎么做你就怎么做*。」埃德雷德悄声说，那口气她从没听他用过，凶得很，吓得她再没提诗的事。「我们必须把你救出去，」埃德雷德接着说，「别犯傻——想想尼斯代尔夫人。」

埃尔弗丽达顿时明白了。她的胳膊从埃德雷德脖子上松开，跑回阿登勋爵跟前，搂住*他*的脖子，一遍又一遍地亲他。

「好了，好了，我的小丫头，好了，好了！」他说，轻轻拍她的肩膀，因为她哭了。

===Original===
"Come with me to thy chamber," said the nurse. "I would take thy measure for a new gown and petticoat."

But Elfrida clung closer. "She does not want to leave her dad," said Lord Arden—"dost thou, my maid?"

"No, no," said Elfrida quite wildly, "I don't want to leave my daddy!"

"Come," said Lord Arden, "'tis but for a measuring time. Thou'lt come back, sweet lamb as thou art. Go now to return the more quickly."

"Goodbye, dear, dear, *dear* daddy!" said Elfrida, suddenly standing up. "Oh, my dear daddy, goodbye!"

===Chinese===
「跟我到你屋里去。」奶妈说。「我要给你量尺寸，做件新袍子、新衬裙。」

埃尔弗丽达却搂得更紧了。「她舍不得离开她爸爸。」阿登勋爵说——「是不是，我的小丫头？」

「不嘛，不嘛，」埃尔弗丽达几乎发狂地喊，「我不要离开我的爸爸！」

「来吧。」阿登勋爵说，「不过量个尺寸的工夫。你会回来的，我的小乖羊。现在去，才回得快。」

「再见，亲爱的、亲爱的、*亲爱*的爸爸！」埃尔弗丽达忽然站起身来。「噢，我亲爱的爸爸，再见！」

===Original===
"Why, what a piece of work about a new frock!" said the nurse crossly. "I've no patience with the child," and she caught Elfrida's hand and dragged her into the next room.

"Now," she whispered, already on her knees undoing Elfrida's gown, "not a moment to lose. Hold thy handkerchief to thy face and seem to weep as we go out. Why, thou'rt weeping already! So much the better!"

From under her wide hoop and petticoat the nurse drew out the clothes that were hidden there, a little suit of black exactly like Edred's—cap, cloak, stockings, shoes—all like Edred's to a hair.

And Elfrida before she had finished crying stood up the exact image of her brother—except her face—and that would be hidden by the handkerchief. Then very quickly the nurse went to the door of the apartment and spoke to the guard there.

===Chinese===
「嗐，为件新衣裳，闹成这样！」奶妈不耐烦地说。「这孩子真叫人耐不得性子。」说着抓住埃尔弗丽达的手，把她拽进了隔壁屋子。

「好了。」她悄声说，人已跪在地上解埃尔弗丽达的袍子，「一刻也不能耽误。出去时拿手绢捂着脸，做出哭的样子。哟，你已经哭了！那就更好了！」

奶妈从宽大的裙撑和衬裙底下，掏出藏在那儿的衣裳：一套小小的黑色衣裤，同埃德雷德那套一模一样——帽子、斗篷、长袜、鞋子——同埃德雷德的分毫不差。

埃尔弗丽达还没哭完，就站起身来，变成了她弟弟的翻版——只除了一张脸——而那张脸会叫手绢遮住。接着奶妈快步走到套间门口，同守在那儿的卫兵搭话。

===Original===
"Good luck, good gentleman," she said, "my little master is ill—he is too frail to bear these sad meetings and sadder partings. Convey us, I pray you, to the outer gate, that I may find our coach and take him home, and afterwards I will return for my other charge, his noble cousin."

"Is it so?" said the guard kindly. "Poor child! Well, such is life, mistress, and we all have tears to weep."

But he could not leave his post at Lord Arden's door to conduct them to the gates. But he told them the way, and they crossed the courtyard alone, and as they went the snow fell on their cloaks and froze there.

So that the guard at the gate, who had seen an old nurse and two little boys go in through the snow, now saw an old nurse and one little boy go out, all snow-covered, and the little boy appeared to be crying bitterly, and no wonder, the nurse explained, seeing his dear father and sister thus.

===Chinese===
「行行好，好心的先生。」她说，「我的小少爷病了——他身子太弱，受不住这些伤心的相见和更伤心的相别。求您行个好，领我们到外门去，好让我找着咱们的马车，送他回家，回头我再来接我照看的另一位，他那位贵族表哥。」

「是吗？」卫兵和气地说。「可怜的孩子！唉，人生就是这样，太太，谁都有掉泪的时候。」

可他不能离开阿登勋爵门口的岗位送他们去大门，只给他们指了路。娘儿俩便独自穿过院子，一路上雪落在斗篷上，冻在了上面。

就这样，大门的卫兵——先前眼见一位老奶妈带着两个小少爷冒雪进去的那位——如今看见一位老奶妈带着一个小少爷出来，浑身覆满了雪；那小少爷看样子哭得好不伤心，也难怪，奶妈解释说，瞧见亲爱的父亲和姐姐成了这个样子。

===Original===
"I will convey him to our coach, good masters," she said to the guard, "and return for my other charge, young Master Richard Arden."

And on that she got Elfrida in her boy's clothes out at the gate and into the waiting carriage. The coachman, by previous arrangement with the old nurse, was asleep on the box, and the footman, also by previous arrangement, was refreshing himself at a tavern near by.

"Under the seat," said the old nurse, and thrusting Elfrida in, shut the coach door and left her. And there was Elfrida, dressed like a boy, huddled up among the straw at the bottom of the coach.

So far, so good. But the most dangerous part of the adventure still remained. The nurse got in again easily enough; she was let in by the guard who had seen her come out. And as she went slowly across the snowy courtyard she heard ring under the gateway the stamping feet of the men who had come to relieve guard, and to be themselves the new guard. So far, again, so good. The danger lay with the guard at the door of Lord Arden's rooms, and in the chance that some of the old guard might be lingering about the gateway when she came out, not with *one* little boy as they would expect, but with *two*. But this had to be risked. The nurse waited as long as she dared so as to lessen the chance of meeting any of the old guard as she went out with her charges. She waited quietly in a corner while Lord Arden talked with the boys. And when at last she said, "The time is done, my Lord," she already knew that the guard at the room door had been changed.

===Chinese===
「我这就送他上咱们的马车，好心的长官。」她对卫兵说，「回头再来接我照看的另一位，理查德·阿登小少爷。」

就这样，她带着一身男孩打扮的埃尔弗丽达出了大门，上了等候在那里的马车。车夫——同老奶妈事先讲定了的——在车座上自顾睡着，跟班——也是事先讲定了的——正在近旁一家酒馆里歇脚提神。

「钻到座位底下去。」老奶妈说着，把埃尔弗丽达塞了进去，关上车门，转身走了。埃尔弗丽达就这样穿着男孩的衣裳，蜷在车厢底部的干草堆里。

到这里为止，一切顺利。可这趟奇遇最凶险的部分还在后头。奶妈再进去倒容易——眼见她出去的那个卫兵放她进去了。她慢慢穿过落雪的院子时，听见门洞底下回响着换岗士兵跺脚的声音——他们来接班，自己就要当新岗了。到这里为止，又一次，一切顺利。危险在阿登勋爵房门口那个卫兵，还在于她出来时撞上旧岗在门洞一带流连的可能——他们料想她带的是*一个*小少爷，她带的却是*两个*。可这风险非冒不可。奶妈尽量多捱了一阵，好教她带着孩子们出去时，少撞见旧岗的人。阿登勋爵同两个男孩说话的当儿，她安安静静待在角落里。等到她终于开口——「时候到了，老爷」——房门口的岗，她早已知道换过了。

===Original===
"So now for it," said Edred, as he and Richard followed the nurse down the narrow steps and across the snowy courtyard.

The new guard saw the woman and two boys, and the captain of the guard read the Queen's paper, which the old nurse had taken care to get back from the lieutenant. And as plainly Master Edred Arden and Master Richard Arden, with their attendant, had passed in, so now they were permitted to pass out, and two minutes later a great coach was lumbering along the snowy streets, and inside it four people were embracing in rapture at the success of their stratagem.

"But it was Edred thought of it," said Richard, as in honour bound, "and he arranged everything and carried it out."

"How splendid of him!" said Elfrida warmly; and I think it was rather splendid of *her* not to spoil his pride and pleasure in this, the first adventure he had ever planned and executed entirely on his own account. She could very easily have spoiled it, you know, by pointing out to him that the whole thing was quite unnecessary, and that they could have got away much more easily by going into a corner in the Tower and saying poetry to the Mouldiwarp.

===Chinese===
「这就干起来。」埃德雷德说。他和理查德跟着奶妈走下窄窄的台阶，穿过落雪的院子。

新岗看见一个女人和两个男孩，卫兵队长验读了王后的手谕——老奶妈早留了心，从典狱长手里讨了回来。埃德雷德·阿登少爷和理查德·阿登少爷带着仆从，先前如何明明白白进了来，此刻也就如何被放了出去。两分钟后，一辆大马车正沿着雪街辚辚而行，车厢里四个人喜不自胜，互相拥抱着，庆贺计策得成。

「不过，这是埃德雷德想出来的。」理查德说——道义所在，不能不提——「筹划是他，办成也是他。」

「他可真了不起！」埃尔弗丽达热忱地说。而我觉得*她*也相当了不起——没有扫他的得意和高兴：这可是他头一回全凭自己谋划、全凭自己办成的奇遇。你要知道，她本来很容易就能泼他一瓢冷水——只消指出这整件事大可不必，只消指出他们本可以在塔里找个角落，对穆迪沃普念念诗，脱身要轻省得多。

===Original===
So they came to Arden House.

The coachman was apparently asleep again, and the footman went round and did something to the harness after he had got the front door opened, and it was quite easy for the nurse to send the footman who opened the door to order a meal to be served at once for Mr. Arden and Mr. Richard. So that no one saw that instead of the two little boys who had left Arden House in the afternoon three came back to it in the evening.

Then the nurse took them into the parlour and shut the door.

"Now," she said, "Master Richard will go take off his fine suit, and Miss Arden will go into the little room and change her raiment. And for you, Master Edred, you wait here with me."

When the others had obediently gone, the nurse stood looking at Edred with eyes that grew larger and different, and he stood looking at her with eyes that grew rounder and rounder.

===Chinese===
就这样，他们回到了阿登宅邸。

车夫看样子又睡着了，跟班开了前门，便绕到车后摆弄马具去了。奶妈顺顺当当打发开门的跟班去传话，给阿登少爷和理查德少爷立刻开饭。就这样，下午离开阿登宅邸的是两个小男孩，晚上回来的却是三个——谁也没有瞧出来。

随后奶妈把他们领进客厅，关上了门。

「现在，」她说，「理查德少爷去脱下他这身漂亮衣裳，阿登小姐到小屋里去换她的衣裙。至于你，埃德雷德少爷，你陪我在这儿等着。」

两个乖乖走了之后，奶妈站在那儿望着埃德雷德，眼睛越睁越大，越变越异样；他站在那儿望着她，眼睛越瞪越圆。

===Original===
"Why," he said at last, "you're the witch—the witch we took the tea and things to."

"And if I am?" said she. "Do you think you're the only people who can come back into other times? You're not all the world yet, Master Arden of Arden. But you've got the makings of a fine boy and a fine man, and I think you've learned something in these old ancient times."

He had, there is no doubt of it. Whether it was being thought important enough to be imprisoned in the Tower, or whether it was the long talks he had with Sir Walter Raleigh, that fine genius and great gentleman, or whether it was Mr. Parados's knuckle-rappings and scowlings, I do not know. But it is certain that this adventure was the beginning of the change in Edred which ended in his being "brave and kind and wise" as the old rhyme had told him to be.

"And now," said the nurse, as Elfrida appeared in her girl's clothes, "there is not a moment to lose. Already at the Tower they have found out our trick. You must go back to your own times."

===Chinese===
「哎呀，」他终于开口，「你是那个女巫——咱们送茶和点心去的那个女巫。」

「是又怎么样？」她说。「你们当真以为，天底下只有你们几个能回到别的时代里去？阿登家的阿登少爷，你们还没那么大的脸面呢。不过你这块料，好男孩、好汉子的坯子都在了；我看，在这些老老古早的年头里，你也学到些东西了。」

学到了，这一点毫无疑问。是被人看得重要到要关进伦敦塔的缘故呢，还是同沃尔特·雷利爵士——那位杰出的天才、真正的大绅士——长谈的缘故呢，还是帕拉多斯先生敲指节、皱眉瞪眼的功劳呢，我说不上。但确定无疑的是，这场奇遇正是埃德雷德转变的开端——那转变一路走到头，正应了古老歌谣里嘱咐他的话，叫他成了「勇敢、善良又明智」的人。

「现在，」埃尔弗丽达换回女孩装束出来时，奶妈说，「一刻也耽搁不得了。塔里那头，已经识破咱们的把戏了。你们必须回自己的时代去。」

===Original===
"She's the witch," Edred briefly answered the open amazement in Elfrida's eyes.

"There is no time to lose," the nurse repeated.

"I *must* be even with old Parados first," said Edred; and so he was, and it took exactly twenty minutes, and I will tell you all about it afterwards.

When he *was* even with old Parados the old nurse sent Richard to bed; and then Elfrida made haste to say, "I did make some poetry to call the Mouldiwarp, but it's all about the Tower, and we're not there now. It's no use saying—

> "'Oh, Mouldiwarp, you have the power
> To get us out of this beastly Tower,'

when we're not *in* the Tower, and I can't think of anything else, and.…"

===Chinese===
「她就是那个女巫。」埃德雷德简短地答道，回答埃尔弗丽达眼里满脸的惊愕。

「没工夫耽搁了。」奶妈又说了一遍。

「我*非得*先同老鹦鹉鼻子算清账不可。」埃德雷德说。他也当真算了——不多不少，恰好用了二十分钟。这里头的事，我回头再细细讲给你听。

账算清之后，老奶妈打发理查德上床睡觉；埃尔弗丽达赶忙说：「我倒是作了几句诗，好唤穆迪沃普的，可那诗全是说塔的，如今咱们不在塔里了。念这个没用——

> 『哦，穆迪沃普，你本领大，
> 快救咱们逃出这混账塔。』

咱们既然*不*在塔里，念它做什么。别的我又想不出来，还有……」

===Original===
But the nurse interrupted her.

"Never mind about poetry," she said; "poetry's all very well for children, but I know a trick worth two of that."

She led them into the dining-room, where the sideboard stood covered with silver, set down the candle, lifted down the great salver with the arms of Arden engraved upon it, and put it on the table.

She breathed on the salver and traced triangles and a circle on the drilled surface; and as the mistiness of her breath faded and the silver shone out again undimmed, there, suddenly, in the middle of the salver, was the live white Mouldiwarp of Arden, looking extremely cross!

"You've no manners," it said to the nurse, "bringing me here in that offhand, rude way, without 'With your leave,' or 'By your leave'! Elfrida could easily have made some poetry. You know well enough," it added angrily, "that it's positively painful to me to be summoned by your triangles and things. Poetry's so easy and simple."

===Chinese===
奶妈打断了她。

「诗就不用管了。」她说。「诗给小孩子念念也好，可我知道一招，比那高明一倍。」

她领他们进了餐室，那儿餐具柜上摆满银器。她放下蜡烛，取下那只刻着阿登纹章的大银盘，放到桌上。

她朝银盘呵了口气，在那钻了细孔的盘面上勾画三角形和圆圈；呵出的雾气散去、银盘重又灿然生光时——瞧，盘心突然现出了阿登那只活生生的白鼹穆迪沃普，一脸的怒气！

「你太没规矩。」它对奶妈说，「就这么随随便便、粗粗鲁鲁地把俺弄来，连句『可否劳驾』『可否借光』都没有！埃尔弗丽达随便就作得出几句诗。你心里也清楚得很，」它气冲冲地补上一句，「拿你那三角形什么的东西来召俺，俺浑身不得劲。诗多省事、多简便呐。」

===Original===
"Poetry's too slow for this night's work," said the nurse shortly. "Come, take the children away, I have done with it."

"You make everything so difficult," said the Mouldiwarp, more crossly than ever. "That's the worst of people who think they know a lot and really only know a little, and pretend they know everything. If I'd come the easy poetry way, I could have taken them back as easily. But now—Well, it can't be helped. I'll take them back, of course, but it'll be a way they won't like. They'll have to go on to the top of the roof and jump off."

"I don't believe that is necessary," said the witch nurse.

"All right," said the Mouldiwarp, "get them away yourself then," and it actually began to disappear.

"No, no!" said Elfrida, "we'll do anything you say."

===Chinese===
「今儿夜里的事，可等不得诗。」奶妈简短地说。「来，把孩子们带走，我这边的事了啦。」

「你把什么都弄得这么费劲。」穆迪沃普说，比先前更气了。「自以为懂得多、其实只懂得一星半点儿、还偏装作什么都懂的人，最糟糕就糟糕在这儿。俺要是走作诗那条省事的路来的，回去也能照样省事。可如今——唉，没法子。带他们回去，俺当然带，可那法子他们不会喜欢。他们得上屋顶最高处，往下跳。」

「我不信非要那样不可。」女巫奶妈说。

「好啊。」穆迪沃普说，「那你自己送他们走。」说着它竟当真开始隐没了。

「别，别！」埃尔弗丽达说，「你怎么说我们怎么做。」

===Original===
"There's a foot of snow on the roof," said the witch nurse.

"So much the better," said the Mouldiwarp, "so much the better. You ought to know that."

"You think yourself very clever," said the nurse.

"Not half so clever as I *am*," said the Mouldiwarp, rather unreasonably Elfrida thought. "There!" it added sharply as a great hammering at the front door shattered the quiet of the night. "There, to the roof for your lives! And I'm not at all sure that it's not too late."

The knocking was growing louder and louder.

===Chinese===
「屋顶上还有一尺深的雪呢。」女巫奶妈说。

「那就更好了。」穆迪沃普说，「那就更好了。这个理你该懂。」

「你自以为聪明得很。」奶妈说。

「你那点估量，连俺*实打实*的聪明的一半都够不着。」穆迪沃普说——埃尔弗丽达觉得，这话实在不讲道理。「听！」它猛地加了一句——前门上一阵大力的捶打，砸碎了夜的寂静。「听！逃命上房顶去！来不来得及，俺可是半点没数。」

捶门声越来越响，越来越响。

## VI The Secret Panel / 暗板

===Original===

“Where shall we hide him?” Elfrida asked impatiently.

Cousin Bet, fired by Elfrida’s enthusiasm, jumped up and began to finger the carved flowers above the chimneypiece.

“The secret room,” she said; “but slip the bolt to and turn the key in the lock.”

Elfrida locked the room door, and turned to see the carved mantelpiece open like a cupboard.

Then Elfrida flew to the window and set back the casement very wide, and in climbed the beautiful gentleman and stood there, very handsome and tall, bowing to Miss Betty, who sank on her knees and kissed the white, jewelled hand he held out.

===Chinese===

「咱们把他藏在哪儿？」埃尔弗丽达不耐烦地问。

贝特表姐（Cousin Bet）受了埃尔弗丽达这股热乎劲儿的感染，跳起身来，伸手去摸壁炉台上方的雕花。

「密室。」她说；「不过得把门闩插上，钥匙也在锁眼里转上。」

埃尔弗丽达锁上房门，回过头来，正看见雕花的壁炉台像橱柜一样开了。

接着埃尔弗丽达飞奔到窗前，把窗扉整个推开，那位漂亮的绅士翻身爬了进来，站在那儿，又俊美又高大，向贝蒂（Betty）小姐躬身行礼；贝蒂双膝跪倒，吻了他伸出来的那只戴满珠宝的白手。

===Original===

“Quick!” said Elfrida. “Get into the hole.”

“There are stairs,” said Betty, snatching a candle in its silver candlestick and holding it high.

The Chevalier St. George sprang to a chair, got his knee on the mantelpiece, and went into the hole, just as Alice goes through the looking-glass in Mr. Tenniel’s picture. Betty handed him the candle, which his white hand reached down to take. Then Elfrida jumped on the chair and shut the panel, leaped down, and opened the room door just as the maid reached its other side with the supper-tray.

When the cousins were alone Bet threw her arms round Elfrida.

===Chinese===

「快！」埃尔弗丽达说。「进洞里去。」

「里面有楼梯。」贝蒂说着，抓起银烛台上的一支蜡烛，高高举着。

圣乔治骑士（the Chevalier St. George）一纵身跳上椅子，膝盖抵住壁炉台，钻进了洞里，活像坦尼尔先生（Mr. Tenniel）画里爱丽丝穿镜而过。贝蒂把蜡烛递上去，他那只白手探下来接住。随后埃尔弗丽达跳上椅子，合上暗板，再跳下来，恰好在女仆端着晚餐托盘走到门外那一刻，打开了房门。

表姐妹俩单独待在屋里时，贝特张开双臂搂住了埃尔弗丽达。

===Original===

“Don’t be afraid, little cousin,” she whispered, “your Cousin Bet will see that no harm comes to you from this adventure.”

“Well, I do think!” said Elfrida getting out of the embrace most promptly, “when it was me let him in, and you’d have screamed the house down, if I hadn’t stopped you﻿—”

“Stop chattering, child,” said Bet, drawing a distracted hand over her pretty forehead, “and let me set my wits to work how I may serve my King.”

“*I*,” said Elfrida scornfully, “should give him something to eat and see that his bed’s aired; but I suppose that would be too vulgar and common for you.”

The two looked at each other across the untasted supper.

===Chinese===

「别怕，小表妹，」她悄声说，「你的贝特表姐保管这场冒险伤不着你一根毫毛。」

「嗬，亏你说得出口！」埃尔弗丽达说着一秒也不耽搁地从拥抱里挣出来，「明明是我放他进来的，要不是我拦着，你早把整座宅子叫塌了——」

「别絮叨了，孩子。」贝特说，心烦意乱地抬手抹过她那漂亮的额头，「让我动动脑筋，想想怎么效劳我的王上。」

「要是*我*呀，」埃尔弗丽达不屑地说，「就会给他弄点吃的，再瞧瞧他的床铺焐干没有；不过我料想，这在你眼里未免太俗气、太不体面了。」

两人隔着那顿没动过的晚饭，你瞪我，我瞪你。

===Original===

“Impertinent chit!” said Bet.

“Chit yourself,” said Elfrida.

Then she laughed.

“Come, Cousin Bet,” she said, “your uncle’s away and you’re grown up. I’ll tell you what to do. You just be wise and splendid, so that your portrait’ll be in the illustrated Christmas numbers in white satin and an anxious expression. ‘The saviour of her King’﻿—that’s what it’ll say.”

“Don’t wander in your speech, child,” said Cousin Bet, pressing her hand to her brow, “I’ve enough to distract me without that. And if you desire to ask my pardon, do so.”

===Chinese===

「没规矩的小丫头！」贝特说。

「你才没规矩。」埃尔弗丽达说。

接着她笑了起来。

「来吧，贝特表姐，」她说，「你叔叔不在家，你又长大成人了。我告诉你该怎么办。你只管又贤明又威风，好让你的画像登上带插图的圣诞专号：一身白缎子裙，一脸焦虑深情。『救王于危难的巾帼』——底下准会这么写。」

「说话别东拉西扯，孩子。」贝特表姐说着，一手抵住额头，「我这颗心已经够乱的了，你就别再添乱。你要是真想求我原谅，那就求吧。」

===Original===

“Oh, well, I beg your pardon﻿—there!” said Elfrida, with extreme irritation. “*Now* perhaps you’ll give your King something to eat.”

“Climb into that hole﻿—with a tray? And the servants, perhaps, coming in any minute? What would you say to them if they did?”

“All right, then, *I’ll* go,” said Elfrida, only too glad of the chance.

Bet touched the secret spring, and when Elfrida had climbed into the dark hole﻿—which she did quite easily﻿—handed her the supper-tray.

“Oh, bother,” said Elfrida, setting it down at her feet with great promptness. “It’s too heavy. He’ll have to come down and fetch it. Give me a candle and shut the panel, and tell me which way to go.”

“To the right and up the steps. Be sure you kneel and kiss his hand before you say a word.”

===Chinese===

「好好好，我求你原谅——行了吧！」埃尔弗丽达说，气得不行。「*这会儿*你总可以给你王上弄点吃的了吧。」

「爬进那个洞——还端着托盘？仆人们保不齐一会儿就进来？真进来了，你拿什么话对他们说？」

「那好，*我*去。」埃尔弗丽达说，巴不得有这个机会。

贝特表姐碰了碰那处暗簧，等埃尔弗丽达钻进那个黑洞——她钻得毫不费事——便把晚餐托盘递给了她。

「哎呀，真麻烦。」埃尔弗丽达说着，手脚麻利地把托盘搁在脚边。「太沉了。他只好自己下来拿。给我一支蜡烛，关上暗板，再告诉我往哪边走。」

「往右，上台阶。记着，先跪下吻他的手，再开口说一个字。」

===Original===

Elfrida reached down for the candle in its silver candlestick, the panel clicked into place, and she stood there among the cobwebby shadows of the secret passage, the light in her hand and the tray at her feet.

“It’s only a Mouldiwarp magic adventure,” she said, to hearten herself, turned to the right, and went up the stairs. They were steep and narrow. At the top she saw the long, light-line of a slightly opened door. To knock seemed unwise. Instead she spoke softly, her lips against the line of light.

“It’s me,” she said, and instantly the door opened, and the beautiful gentleman stood before her.

The secret room had a little furniture﻿—a couch, a table, chairs﻿—all old-fashioned, and their shapes showed beautiful, even in the dim light of the two candles.

===Chinese===

埃尔弗丽达探身接过银烛台上的蜡烛，暗板咔哒一声合严，她便站在了秘密过道里那蛛网般的暗影中间，一手举着烛光，一脚边搁着托盘。

「不过是场白鼹穆迪沃普（Mouldiwarp）式的魔法冒险罢了。」她给自己壮着胆说，随即向右转，上了楼梯。楼梯又陡又窄。到得顶上，她看见一道长长的光线，从一扇虚掩的门缝里漏出来。敲门似乎不妥。她便把嘴唇凑在那道光线上，柔声开了口。

「是我。」她说。门应声而开，那位漂亮的绅士站在了她面前。

密室里有几件家具——一张睡榻、一张桌子、几把椅子——都是老式样，即便在两支蜡烛的昏光里，那形制也透着美。

===Original===

“Your supper,” said Elfrida, “is at the bottom of the stairs. The tray was too heavy for me. Do you mind fetching it up?”

“If you’ll show me a light,” he said, and went.

“You’ll stay and eat with me?” said he, when she had lighted him back to the secret room, and he had set the tray on the table.

“I mustn’t,” said Elfrida. “Cousin Bet’s such a muff; she wouldn’t know where to say I was if the servants came in. Oh, I say! I’m so sorry I forgot. She told me to kneel and kiss your hand before I said anything about supper. I’ll do it now.”

“Nay,” said he, “I’ll kiss thy cheek, little lady, and drink a health to him who shall have thy lips when thou’rt seventeen and I am﻿—what was it﻿—five hundred?”

“Two hundred and thirty,” said Elfrida, returning his kiss cordially. “You are nice, you know. I wish you were real. I’d better go back to Bet now.”

===Chinese===

「您的晚饭，」埃尔弗丽达说，「在楼梯脚底下。托盘太沉，我端不动。您介不介意自己下去取上来？」

「你给我照个亮儿。」他说着，人就去了。

「你留下来陪我一道吃，好不好？」等她照着他回到密室、他把托盘在桌上放稳之后，他这么说。

「那可不行。」埃尔弗丽达说。「贝特表姐是个大脓包；仆人们要是进来，她都不知道该说我人在哪儿。哎呀，对了！真对不起，我忘了。她吩咐过，谈晚饭之前要先跪下吻您的手。我现在就补上。」

「不必，」他说，「我来亲亲你的脸颊，小姑娘，再为那个人祝一盏酒——你满十七岁那年，要得到你香吻的那个人。那时我多少岁来着——五百岁？」

「两百三十岁。」埃尔弗丽达说着，亲亲热热地回了他一吻。「你知道吗，你真好。你要是真的就好了。我最好这就回贝特那儿去。」

===Original===

“Real?” he said.

“Oh, I’m talking nonsense, I know,” said Elfrida. “I’ll go now.”

“The absent tray will betray you,” said he, taking food and wine from it and setting them on the table. “Now I will carry this down again. You have all the courage, but not quite the cunning of a conspirator.”

“How long are you going to stay here?” Elfrida asked. “I suppose you’re escaping from someone or something, like in history?”

“I shall not stay long,” he said. “If anyone should ask you if you have seen the King, what would you say?”

===Chinese===

「真的？」他说。

「哦，我在说傻话，我知道。」埃尔弗丽达说。「我这就走。」

「托盘一失踪，就出卖你了。」他说着，从托盘里取出酒食摆上桌。「现在我把它送下去。你有的是胆量，只是还欠着点儿阴谋家的心机。」

「您打算在这儿待多久？」埃尔弗丽达问。「我猜您是在躲什么人或者什么事，就像历史书里那样？」

「我待不长。」他说。「要是有人问你见没见过王上，你会怎么说？」

===Original===

“I should say ‘no,’ ” said Elfrida boldly. “You see, I can’t possibly know that you’re the King. You just say so, that’s all. Perhaps really you aren’t.”

“Exquisite!” said he. “So you don’t believe me?”

“Oh, yes, I do!” said Elfrida; “but I needn’t, you know.”

“S’life!” he said. “But I wish I were. There’d be a coronet for somebody.”

“You wish you were﻿—”

“Safely away, my little lady. And as for coronets, the jewels are safe. See, I have set them in the cupboard in the corner.”

===Chinese===

「我就说『没见过』。」埃尔弗丽达大着胆子答。「您瞧，我根本没法知道您是王上。您不过是自己这么一说罢了。说不定您真的不是。」

「妙极了！」他说。「这么说，你不信我？」

「哦，不，我信！」埃尔弗丽达说；「不过嘛，您知道，我本不必信的。」

「老天作证！」他说。「可惜我倒真愿意我是。那样，就有人要戴上小宝冠啦。」

「您倒愿意您是——」

「平安脱身，我的小姑娘。至于宝冠嘛，珠宝都安然无恙。瞧，我把它们收进墙角的柜子里了。」

===Original===

And he had.

Then he carried down the tray, and Elfrida, who was very hungry, tried to persuade Bet that she must eat, if only to keep up her strength for the deeds of daring that might want doing at any moment.

But Bet declared that she could not eat; the least morsel would choke her. And as for going to bed, she was assuring her cousin that she knew her duty to her King better than that, and that she would defend her Sovereign with her life, if need were, when her loyal ecstasies were suddenly interrupted.

For the quiet of the night was broken by a great knocking at the castle door and the heavy voice of a man crying﻿—

“Open, in the Queen’s name!”

===Chinese===

他确实收好了。

然后他把托盘送了下去。埃尔弗丽达肚子早饿扁了，便劝贝特非吃点东西不可——哪怕只为攒足力气，好应付随时可能派上用场的那些胆大包天的勾当。

可贝特咬定自己吃不下，说一小口也会把她噎住。至于上床睡觉——她正向表妹担保，说自己对王上的本分决不至于糊涂到那个地步，必要时她愿拿性命保卫她的君王——一腔忠烈的豪情正说到这儿，突然被人打断了。

因为夜静被人敲碎了：城堡大门响起咚咚的擂门声，还夹着一个男人粗重的嗓音，喊的是——

「开门，奉女王之名！」

===Original===

“They’ve come for him! All is lost! We are betrayed! What shall we do?”

“*Eat*,” said Elfrida﻿—“eat for your life.”

She pushed Bet into a chair and thrust a plate before her, put a chunk of meat-pie on her plate and another on her own.

“Get your mouth full,” she whispered, filling her own as she spoke﻿—“so full you can’t speak﻿—it’ll give you time to think.”

And then the door opened, and in a moment the room was full of gentlemen in riding dress, with very stern faces. And they all had swords.

===Chinese===

「他们是冲他来的！全完了！咱们被人出卖了！可怎么办哪？」

「*吃*。」埃尔弗丽达说——「拼命吃，保命要紧。」

她把贝特按进椅子，塞给她一只盘子，往她盘里搁了一大块肉馅饼，又往自己盘里搁了一大块。

「把嘴塞满。」她一面往自己嘴里塞，一面悄声说——「塞得你说不出话来——也好有工夫动动脑子。」

接着门开了，转眼间满屋子都是骑马装束的绅士，个个面色严峻。而且人人佩着剑。

===Original===

Betty, with her mouth quite full, was trying not to look towards the panel.

Elfrida, whose mouth was equally full, looked at the gentleman who seemed to be leading the others, and remarked﻿—

“This is a nice time of night to come knocking people up!”

“All hours are alike to a loyal subject,” said a round, fat, blue-eyed gentleman in a green suit. “Have you any strangers under your roof tonight?”

“Oh!” cried Bet, “all is lost!”

===Chinese===

贝蒂嘴里塞得满满的，拼命忍着不朝暗板那边看。

埃尔弗丽达嘴里同样塞得满满的，她望着那位像是领头的绅士，开口道——

「这个钟点跑来把人从被窝里敲起来，可真有你们的！」

「忠心的臣仆，不分什么钟点。」一位圆脸盘、胖乎乎、蓝眼睛的绿衣绅士说。「今晚贵府可宿着陌生人？」

「哎呀！」贝特叫道，「全完了！」

===Original===
