The gentlemen exchanged glances and crowded round her. Elfrida shrugged the shoulders of her mind﻿—if a mind has shoulders﻿—and told herself that it didn’t matter. History knew best, no doubt, and whatever seemed to be happening now was only history.

“You *have* a stranger here?” they asked; and, “Where is he? You cannot refuse to give him up.”

“My heart told me so,” cried Bet. “I knew it was he you were seeking,” and with that she fainted elegantly into the arms of the nearest gentleman, who was dressed in plum-colour, and seemed to be struggling with some emotion which made him look as if he were laughing.

“Ask the child﻿—children and fools speak the truth,” said the fat, blue-eyed gentleman.

===Chinese===

绅士们交换了一下眼色，把她围了起来。埃尔弗丽达耸了耸她精神上的肩膀——倘若精神也长肩膀的话——心想这都不要紧。毫无疑问，历史最清楚轻重；此刻看着像要发生的一切，不过是历史罢了。

「你们这儿*当真*有位陌生人？」他们问；又问：「他在哪儿？你们不能拒不交人。」

「我的心早就这么告诉我了。」贝特叫道，「我就知道你们要找的是他。」话音未落，她优雅地晕进了离她最近的那位绅士怀里。那位绅士穿一身梅紫色，像是正跟某种情绪搏斗，憋得那模样倒像在发笑。

「问问这孩子——孩子和傻子嘴里出实话。」圆胖的蓝眼睛绅士说。

===Original===

Elfrida found herself suddenly lifted on to the table, from which she could see over the heads of the gentlemen who stood all round her. She could see Bet reclining on the sofa, and the open door with servants crowding in it, all eyes and ears.

“Now,” said a dozen voices, “the truth, little miss.”

“What do you want to know?” she asked; and, in a much lower tone, “I shan’t tell you anything unless you send the servants away.”

The door was closed and the truth was asked for again.

“If you’ll only tell me what you want to know,” she said again.

===Chinese===

埃尔弗丽达发觉自己突然被举到了桌上，从那儿能越过围在四面的绅士们的头顶望出去。她看见贝特表姐斜倚在沙发上，还看见敞开的门口挤满了仆人，一个个伸着眼睛、竖着耳朵。

「好了，」十几个声音一齐说，「说实话吧，小姑娘。」

「你们想知道什么？」她问；又压低了好些的嗓音补一句：「你们不把仆人打发走，我一个字也不说。」

门关上了，实话又被追问了一遍。

「只要你们先说清楚想知道什么呀。」她又说了一遍。

===Original===

“Does any stranger lie here tonight?”

“No,” said Elfrida. She knew that the beautiful gentleman in the secret chamber was not lying down, but sitting to his supper.

“But Miss Arden said ‘All is lost,’ and she knew ’twas he whom we sought.”

“Well,” Elfrida carefully explained, “it’s like this. You see, we were robbed by a highwayman today, and I think that upset my cousin. She’s rather easily upset, I’m afraid.”

“Very easily,” several voices agreed, and someone added that it was a harebrained business.

===Chinese===

「今晚可有什么陌生人躺在这儿？」

「没有。」埃尔弗丽达说。她知道密室里那位漂亮的绅士并没有躺着，而是正坐着用他的晚饭呢。

「可阿登小姐方才说『全完了』，她还知道我们要找的就是他。」

「呃，」埃尔弗丽达一字一板地解释，「是这么回事。你瞧，我们今儿白天叫拦路强盗劫了道，我想我表姐就是为这个受了惊。恐怕她是顶容易受惊的。」

「可不是顶容易嘛。」好几个声音随声附和，还有人添了一句，说这档子事本来就是个毛毛躁躁的荒唐局。

===Original===

“The shortest way’s the best,” said the plum-coloured gentleman. “Is Sir Edward Talbot here?”

“No, he isn’t,” said Elfrida downrightly, “and I don’t believe you’ve got any business coming into people’s houses and frightening other people into fits, and I shall tell Lord Arden when he comes home. So now you know.”

“Zooks!” someone cried, “the child’s got a spirit; and she’s right, too, strike me if she isn’t.”

“But, snails!” exclaimed another, “we do but protect Lord Arden’s house in his absence.”

“If,” said Elfrida, “you think your Talbot’s playing hide-and-seek here, and if he’s done anything wrong, you can look for him if you like. But I don’t believe Lord Arden will be pleased. That’s all. I should like to get down on to the floor, if you please!”

===Chinese===

「长话短说吧。」梅紫衣绅士说。「爱德华·塔尔博特爵士（Sir Edward Talbot）在这儿吗？」

「不，他不在。」埃尔弗丽达说得直截了当，「我也不信你们有什么道理闯进人家宅子里，把人吓得半死。等阿登勋爵回家，我要一五一十告诉他的。这下你们知道了。」

「好家伙！」有人喊道，「这孩子有股子气性；她说的还在理——不在理的话，叫我天打雷劈。」

「可是，老天爷！」另一位嚷道，「我们不过是替阿登勋爵看守他不在家时的宅子呀。」

「要是，」埃尔弗丽达说，「你们以为你们那位塔尔博特在这儿捉迷藏，要是他干了什么坏事，你们只管找去。可我不信阿登勋爵会高兴。要说的就这些。劳驾，我想下到地板上去！」

===Original===

I don’t know whether Elfrida would have had the courage to say all this if she had not remembered that this was history-times, and not now-times. But the gentlemen seemed delighted with her bravery.

They lifted her gently down, and with many apologies for having discommoded the ladies, they went out of the room and out of the castle. Through the window Elfrida heard the laughing voices and clatter and stamp of their horses’ hoofs as they mounted and rode off. They all seemed to be laughing. And she felt that she moved in the midst of mysteries.

She could not bear to go back into her own time without seeing the end of the adventure. So she went to bed in a large four-poster, with Cousin Bet for company. The fainting fit lasted exactly as long as the strange gentlemen were in the house and no longer, which was very convenient.

Elfrida got up extremely early in the morning and went down into the parlour. She had meant to go and see how the King was, and whether he wanted his shaving-water first thing, as her daddy used to do. But it was so very, very early that she decided that it would be better to wait a little. The King might be sleepy, and sleepy people were not always grateful, she knew, for early shaving-water.

So she went out into the fields where the dew was grey on the grass, and up on to Arden Knoll. And she stood there and heard the skylarks, and looked at the castle and thought how new the mortar looked in the parts about the living house. And presently she saw two figures coming across the fields from where the spire of Arden Church rose out of the tops of trees as round and green as the best double-curled parsley. And one of the gentlemen wore a green coat and the other a purple coat, and she thought to herself how convenient it was to recognise people half a mile away by the colour of their clothes.

===Chinese===

我不知道，埃尔弗丽达要不是记着这是历史时代、不是现在时代，还敢不敢把这一整套话都说出来。不过绅士们倒似乎很欣赏她的胆气。

他们把她轻轻抱下桌来，为唐突了女士们赔了数不清的不是，然后走出房间，走出城堡。埃尔弗丽达隔着窗子听见他们上马驰去时的笑语声，还有马蹄的嘚嘚与踏响。他们像是个个都在笑。她觉得自己正行走在一重重秘密中间。

不见识这场冒险的结局，她不甘心就这么回自己的时代去。于是她上了那张四柱大床睡下，由贝特表姐作伴。那一场昏厥不多不少，恰好在陌生绅士们待在宅子里的整段时间里持续，人一走便醒，真是方便极了。

第二天清早，埃尔弗丽达起了个大早，下楼来到客厅。她本想去看望王上安好，看要不要一起来先送上刮脸水——她爸爸从前就有这个习惯。可天实在太早太早了，她决定还是稍等一等为好。王上兴许还困着呢；她知道，困着的人对一大早的刮脸水，未必回回领情。

于是她走进田野，草上的露水灰蒙蒙的，她又登上阿登丘。她站在那儿听云雀啼啭，望着城堡，心想住宅那一带的灰浆显得多新啊。不多时，她望见两个人影穿过田野走来，来的那头正是阿登教堂的尖塔从树梢里探出来的地方——树冠圆滚滚、绿油油，活像顶好的重瓣皱叶欧芹。两位绅士一位穿绿外套，一位穿紫外套，她心里暗想：隔着半里地就能凭衣裳颜色认出人来，多省事。

===Original===

Quite plainly they were going to the castle﻿—so she went down, too, and met them at the gate with a civil “Good morning.”

“You are no lie-abed at least,” said the green gentleman. “And so no stranger lay at Arden last night, eh?”

Elfrida found this difficult to answer. No doubt the King had lain﻿—was probably still lying﻿—in the secret chamber. But was he a stranger? No, of course he wasn’t. So﻿—

“No,” she said.

And then through the open window of the parlour came, very unexpectedly and suddenly, a leg in a riding-boot, then another leg, and the whole of the beautiful gentleman stood in front of them.

“So-ho!” he said. “Speak softly, for the servants are not yet about.”

===Chinese===

明明白白，他们是往城堡去的——于是她也下了丘，在大门口迎上他们，客客气气道一声「早安」。

「至少你不是个恋被窝的。」绿衣绅士说。「这么说，昨晚没有陌生人宿在阿登，呃？」

埃尔弗丽达觉得这话不好答。毫无疑问，王上昨夜在密室里待过——多半这会儿还待着。可他算陌生人吗？不算，当然不算。所以——

「没有。」她说。

接着，谁也没料到，客厅敞开的窗子里冷不丁先伸进来一条穿马靴的腿，跟着又一条腿，然后那位漂亮的绅士整个人立在了他们面前。

「嘘——嗬！」他说。「小点声，仆人们还没起来走动呢。」

===Original===

“They *are*,” said Elfrida, “only they’re at the back. Creep along under the wall; you will get away without their seeing you then.”

“Always a wonderful counsellor,” said the beautiful gentleman, bowing gracefully. “Come with us, little maid. I have no secrets from thee.”

So they all crept along close to the castle wall to that corner from which, between two shoulders of down, you can see the sea. There they stopped.

“And the wager’s mine,” said the beautiful gentleman, “for all you tried to spoil it. That was not in the bond, Fitzgerald, entering Arden at night at nine of the clock, to ferret me out like a pack of hounds after Reynard.”

“There was nothing barred,” said the green gentleman. “We tried waylaying you on the road, but you were an hour early.”

“Ah,” said the beautiful gentleman, “putting back clocks is easy work. And the ostler at the ‘Bull’ loves a handsome wager nigh as well as he loves a guinea.”

===Chinese===

「*起来了*，」埃尔弗丽达说，「只是在后头呢。你贴着墙根溜过去，他们瞧不见你，你就脱身了。」

「永远是个了不起的小军师。」漂亮的绅士说着，风度翩翩地一躬身。「跟我们来吧，小姑娘。我对你没有秘密。」

于是他们都贴着城堡墙根，悄悄溜到那个拐角——从那儿，隔着两道白垩丘的坡肩，望得见大海。他们在那儿停下。

「这场赌是我赢了，」漂亮的绅士说，「尽管你们百般想搅黄它。菲茨杰拉德（Fitzgerald），深更半夜九点进阿登，像一群猎犬围捕列那狐（Reynard）似的把我搜出来——这可不在契约里。」

「契约里可什么也没禁止。」绿衣绅士说。「我们试过在半路上堵你，可你早到了一个钟头。」

「啊，」漂亮的绅士说，「把钟拨慢是顶容易的营生。再说『公牛』客栈的马夫爱一场体面的好赌，差不多同爱几尼金币一样。」

===Original===

“I do *wish* you’d explain,” said Elfrida, almost stamping with curiosity and impatience.

“And so I will, my pretty,” said he, laughing.

“Aren’t you the King? You said you were.”

“Nay, nay﻿—not so fast. I asked thee what thou wouldst say if I told you I was King James.”

“Then who *are* you?” she asked.

“Plain Edward Talbot, Baronet, at your ladyship’s service,” he said, with another of his fine bows.

===Chinese===

「我*真*希望你们解释解释。」埃尔弗丽达说，好奇和心急差点让她跺起脚来。

「我这就解释，小美人儿。」他笑着说。

「您不是王上吗？您自己说过的。」

「不，不——别急。我问的是你：要是我对你说我是詹姆斯王，你会怎么说。」

「那您*到底*是谁？」她问。

「在下爱德华·塔尔博特，从男爵，听候小姐差遣。」他说着，又行了一个漂亮的躬身礼。

===Original===

“But I don’t *understand*,” she said, “*do* tell me all about it from the beginning.” So he told her, and the other gentlemen stood by, laughing.

“The other night I was dining with Mr. Fitzgerald here, and the talk turned on highway robbery, and on Arden Castle here, with other matters. And these gentlemen, with others of the party, laid me a wager﻿—five hundred guineas it was﻿—that I would not rob a coach. I took the wager. And I wagered beside that I would rob a coach of the Arden jewels, and that I would lie a night at Arden beside, and no one should know my name there. And I have done all three and won my wager. I am but newly come home from foreign parts, so your cousin could not know my face. But zounds, child! had it not been for thee I had lost my wager. I counted on Miss Arden’s help﻿—and a pale-faced, fainting, useless fine lady I should have found her. But thou﻿—thou’rt a girl in a thousand. And I’ll buy thee the finest fairing I can find next time I go to London. We are all friends. Tell pretty miss to hold that tongue of hers, and none shall hear the tale from us.”

“But all these gentlemen coming last night. All the servants know.”

“The gentlemen came, no doubt, to protect Miss Arden, in case the villainous highwayman should have hidden behind the window curtain. Oh, but the wise child it is﻿—has a care for every weak point in our armour!”

===Chinese===

「可我*不明白*呀，」她说，「*千万*从头到尾给我讲讲。」于是他讲了，其余绅士站在一旁听着直笑。

「前几天晚上，我在这儿同菲茨杰拉德先生吃饭，话头转到拦路抢劫，又转到这座阿登城堡，还捎带些别的。这几位先生，连同席间另外几位，跟我打了个赌——赌注五百几尼——赌我不敢去劫一辆马车。我接了赌。我还加赌一层：我要劫的正是运阿登家珠宝的那辆车，而且事后还要在阿登宿上一夜，不能有一个人知道我的姓名。三件我都办到了，赌也赢了。我刚从海外回来不久，所以你表姐不认得我的脸。可是天哪，孩子！要不是有你，我这场赌就输定了。我原指望阿登小姐搭把手——到头来准会发现她是位脸色苍白、动不动就晕、中看不中用的娇小姐。可你——你是千里挑一的姑娘。下回我去伦敦，给你买一件能寻到的最漂亮的小礼物。咱们都是朋友。叫漂亮小姐管住她那条小舌头，这桩事咱们谁也不会往外说。」

「可是昨晚这些绅士们闯了来。仆人们全都知道了。」

「诸位先生昨晚来，不用说，是为了保护阿登小姐，怕那个恶棍拦路强盗藏在窗帘后头。哦，好个伶俐的孩子——咱们这身盔甲上有哪条缝儿，她都替咱们照看着呢！」

===Original===

Then he told his friends the whole of the adventure, and they laughed very merrily, for all they had lost their wager, and went home to breakfast across the dewy fields.

“It’s nice of him to think me brave and all that,” Elfrida told herself, “but I *do* wish he’d *really* been the King.”

When she had told Betty what had happened everything seemed suddenly to be not worth while; she did not feel as though she cared to stay any longer in that part of the past﻿—so she ran upstairs, through the attic and the pigeon noises, back into her own times, and went down and found Edred sitting on the second hand of the daisy-clock; and he did not believe that she had been away at all. For all the time she had been away seemed no time to him, because he had been sitting on that second hand.

So when the Mouldiwarp told them to go along in, they went; and the way they went was not in, but out, and round under the castle wall to the corner from which you could see the sea. And there they lay on the warm grass, and Elfrida told Edred the whole story, and at first he did not believe a word of it.

“But it’s true, I tell you,” said she. “You don’t suppose I should make up a whole tale like that, do you?”

===Chinese===

接着他把这番冒险从头到尾讲给朋友们听，他们虽然输了赌，却笑得十分快活，然后踏着露水未干的田野，回家吃早饭去了。

「他觉得我勇敢什么的，倒难为他有心，」埃尔弗丽达对自己说，「可我*真*希望他*当真*是王上啊。」

等她把事情原原本本讲给贝蒂听了，一切忽然显得没意思起来；她不想在那一段过去里再多待了——于是她跑上楼，穿过阁楼和鸽子的咕咕声，回到自己的时代，下楼来，看见埃德雷德正坐在雏菊钟的秒针上；他压根儿不信她离开过。因为她离开的那整段时间，在他看来根本算不得时间——他一直坐在那根秒针上呢。

后来穆迪沃普叫他们进去，他们就去了；可他们走的不是进去的路，倒是出来，绕到城堡墙根下那个望得见大海的拐角。他们在暖烘烘的草地上躺下，埃尔弗丽达把整个故事讲给埃德雷德听，起初他一个字也不信。

「可这是真的，我跟你说。」她说。「你总不至于以为，我会编出这么一整套故事来吧？」

===Original===

“No,” said Edred. “Of course, you’re not clever enough. But you might have read it in a book.”

“Well, I didn’t,” said Elfrida﻿—“so there!”

“If it was really true, you might have come back for me. You know how I’ve always wanted to meet a highwayman﻿—you know you do.”

“How could I come back? How was I to get off the horse and run home and get in among the chests and the pigeon noises and come out here and take you back? The highwayman﻿—Talbot, I mean﻿—would have been gone long before we got back.”

“No, he wouldn’t,” said Edred obstinately. “You forget I was sitting on the clock and stopping it. There wasn’t any time while you were gone﻿—if you *were* gone.”

===Chinese===

「不至于。」埃德雷德说。「当然啦，你没那份聪明。可你也可能是从哪本书里看来的。」

「反正我没看。」埃尔弗丽达说——「哼，就是这样！」

「要是这是真的，你就该回来接我嘛。你明知道我一向想见见拦路强盗——你是知道的，你知道的。」

「我怎么回来？我怎么下马、跑回家、钻进那些箱子和鸽子咕咕声里头，再出来到这儿接你回去？那强盗——我是说塔尔博特——等我们回去，早走了不知多久了。」

「不，他不会走。」埃德雷德犟起来。「你忘了，我就坐在钟上，把钟停住了。你不在的时候压根儿没有时间——*如果*你真是不在的话。」

===Original===

“There was with *me*,” said Elfrida. “Don’t you see﻿—”

“There wouldn’t have been if you’d come back where I was,” Edred interrupted.

“How can you be so aggravating?” Elfrida found suddenly that she was losing her temper. “You *can’t* be as stupid as that, really.”

“Oh, can’t I?” said Edred. “I can though, if I like. And stupider﻿—*much* stupider,” he added darkly. “You wait.”

“Edred,” said his sister slowly and fervently, “sometimes I feel as if I *must* shake you.”

“You daren’t!” said Edred.

===Chinese===

「*我这边*可是有时间的。」埃尔弗丽达说。「你还不明白——」

「你要是回到我这边来，就没有了。」埃德雷德插嘴道。

「你怎么能这么气人？」埃尔弗丽达忽然发觉自己要动气了。「你不可能蠢成那样，真的。」

「哦，不能吗？」埃德雷德说。「只要我乐意，就能。还能更蠢——*蠢得多*呢。」他阴沉沉地添了一句。「你等着瞧。」

「埃德雷德，」他姐姐一字一顿、热切地说，「有时候我觉得自己*非*摇你一顿不可。」

「你不敢！」埃德雷德说。

===Original===

“Do you dare me to?”

“*Yes*,” said Edred fiercely.

Of course, you are aware that after that, by all family laws, Elfrida was obliged to shake him. She did, and burst into tears. He looked at her for a moment and﻿—but no﻿—tears are unmanly. I would not betray the weakness of my hero. Let us draw a veil, or take a turn round the castle and come back to them presently.

===Chinese===

「你敢说我不敢？」

「*敢*。」埃德雷德凶巴巴地说。

当然，你们心里有数：事已至此，按家法的铁律，埃尔弗丽达就非摇他不可了。她也真摇了，跟着放声大哭。他望了她片刻，然后——不，不行——眼泪可不是男子汉的东西。我决不出卖我主人公的弱点。咱们还是垂一层纱幕吧，要不就绕着城堡兜一圈，过一会儿再回到他们这儿来。

---

===Original===

It is just as well that we went away when we did, for we really turned our backs on a most unpleasant scene. And now that we come back to them, though crying is still going on, Elfrida is saying that she is very sorry, and is trying to find her handkerchief to lend to Edred, whose own is unexpectedly mislaid.

“Oh, all right,” he says, “I’m sorry too. There! But us saying we’re sorry won’t make us unquarrel. That’s the worst of it. We shan’t be able to find The Door for three days now. I do wish we hadn’t. It *is* sickening.”

“Never mind,” said Elfrida; “we didn’t have a real I’ll-never-speak-to-you-again-you-see-if-I-do quarrel, did we?”

“I don’t suppose it matters what sort of quarrel you had,” said the boy in gloom. “Look here﻿—I’ll tell you what﻿—you tell me all about it over again and I’ll try to believe you. I really will, on the honour of an Arden.”

So she told him all over again.

===Chinese===

幸亏我们走得及时，因为那实在是一幕很不体面的光景，我们正好眼不见为净。如今我们回到他们跟前，哭虽然还在哭，埃尔弗丽达却正说着十分对不起，还想找出自己的手帕借给埃德雷德——他自己的那条，不知怎么竟不知去向了。

「哦，好吧，」他说，「我也对不起。行了！可咱们光互相说对不起，也没法把吵过的架吵回去呀。最糟就糟在这儿。这一来，咱们要等上三天才找得着那扇『门』了。真不如不吵。*真气死人*。」

「不要紧，」埃尔弗丽达说；「咱们又没有真的吵那种『我再也不理你了——不信你瞧』的架，对吧？」

「我看吵的是哪种架，都一样糟糕。」男孩阴郁地说。「这么着——我告诉你——你把前前后后再给我讲一遍，我试着信你。我真的会信，凭一个阿登家人的名誉起誓。」

于是她又从头到尾讲了一遍。

===Original===

“And where,” said Edred, when she had quite finished﻿—“where did you put the jewels?”

“I﻿—they﻿—he put them in the corner cupboard in the secret room,” said Elfrida.

“If you’d taken me and not been in such a hurry﻿—no, I’m not quarrelling, I’m only reasoning with you like Aunt Edith﻿—if *I’d* been there I should have buried those jewels somewhere and then come back for me, and we’d have dug them up, and been rich beyond the dreams of﻿—what do they call it?”

“But I never told Betty where they were. Perhaps they’re there now. Let’s go and look.”

“If they are,” said he, “I’ll believe everything you’ve been telling me without trying at all.”

===Chinese===

「那么，」等她讲完，埃德雷德问——「你们把珠宝搁哪儿了？」

「我——它们——他把它们搁进密室的墙角柜里了。」埃尔弗丽达说。

「你要是带上我，别那么急吼吼的——不，我不是在吵架，我只是学伊迪丝姑姑那样跟你讲道理——要是*我*当时在场，我就会先把那些珠宝找地方埋起来，然后回来接我，咱们再把它们挖出来，富得超出——他们管那叫什么来着？——的梦想了。」

「可我一直没告诉贝蒂它们在哪儿呀。说不定它们这会儿还在那儿呢。咱们去找找。」

「要真在，」他说，「我就不费一点劲儿，全信你方才讲的那一套。」

===Original===

“You’ll have to do that﻿—if there’s a secret room, won’t you?”

“P’r’aps,” said Edred; “let’s go and see. I expect I shall have got a headache presently. You didn’t ought to have shaken me. Mrs. Honeysett says it’s very bad for people to be shaken﻿—it mixes up their brains inside their heads so that they ache, and you’re stupid. I expect that’s what made you say I was stupid.”

“Oh, dear,” said Elfrida despairingly. “You know that was before I shook you, and I did say I was sorry.”

“I know it was, but it comes to the same thing. Come on﻿—let’s have a squint at your old secret room.”

But, unfortunately it was now dinnertime. If you do happen to know the secret of a carved panel with a staircase hidden away behind it, you don’t want to tell that secret lightly﻿—as though it were the day of the week, or the date of the Battle of Waterloo, or what nine times seven is﻿—not even to a grownup so justly liked as Mrs. Honeysett. And, besides, a hot beefsteak pudding and greens do not seem to go well with the romances of old days. To have looked for the spring of that panel while that dinner smoked on the board would have been as unseemly as to try on a new gold crown over curl-papers. Elfrida felt this. And Edred did not more than half believe in the secret, anyway. And besides he was very hungry.

===Chinese===

「反正你也只能信——要是真有间密室的话，对吧？」

「兴许吧。」埃德雷德说；「走，瞧瞧去。我看我过一会儿就该头疼了。你不该摇我。哈尼塞特太太（Mrs. Honeysett）说，摇人对人顶不好——会把人脑袋里的脑浆子摇乱，闹得头疼，人就变笨。我瞧你说我笨，八成就是这么来的。」

「哎呀，天哪。」埃尔弗丽达绝望地说。「你知道那是在我摇你之前说的，而且我也道过歉了呀。」

「我知道是之前，可还不是一回事。走吧——去瞅一眼你那老密室。」

可惜，这时偏偏到了开饭的钟点。倘若你恰好知道一扇雕花板背后藏着楼梯的秘密，你总不肯把它轻飘飘地说出去——像说今天星期几、滑铁卢战役是哪天打的、九乘七得多少那样随口——哪怕对方是哈尼塞特太太这样招人喜欢的大人。况且，热腾腾的牛排布丁配青菜，跟往昔年月的浪漫传奇也实在不搭调。饭菜热气腾腾摆上桌的时候去摸那暗板的机关，就跟头上还卷着卷发纸便去试戴一顶新金冠一样不成体统。这一点埃尔弗丽达是感觉得到的。而埃德雷德呢，反正对那秘密连一半也没信。再说，他也实在饿了。

===Original===

“Wait till afterwards,” was what they said to each other in whispers, while Mrs. Honeysett was changing the plates.

“You do do beautiful cooking,” Edred remarked, as the gooseberry-pie was cut open and revealed its chrysoprase-coloured contents.

“You do the beautiful eating then,” said Mrs. Honeysett, “and you be quick about it. You ain’t got into no mischief this morning, have you? You look as though butter wouldn’t melt in either of your mouths, and that’s always a sign of something being up with most children.”

“No, *indeed* we haven’t,” said Elfrida earnestly, “and we don’t mean to either. And our looking like that’s only because we brushed our hairs with wet brushes, most likely. It does make you look good, somehow; I’ve often noticed it.”

“I’ve been flying round this morning,” Mrs. Honeysett went on, “so as to get down to my sister’s for a bit this afternoon. She’s not so well again, poor old dear, and I might be kept late. But my niece Emily’s coming up to take charge. She’s a nice lively young girl; she’ll get you your teas, and look after you as nice as nice. Now don’t you go doing anything what you wouldn’t if I was behind of you, will you? That’s dears.”

===Chinese===

「等吃完了再说。」哈尼塞特太太换盘子的当口，他俩咬着耳朵互相叮嘱。

「您做饭真是做得漂亮。」埃德雷德说，这时醋栗馅饼切开，露出了绿玉髓色的馅心。

「那你就漂亮地吃吧。」哈尼塞特太太说，「快着点儿。你们今儿早上没闯祸吧？瞧你们俩那副乖样儿，活像嘴里含着黄油也化不开——大多数孩子一摆出这副模样，八成就是憋着事儿呢。」

「没有，*真的*没有。」埃尔弗丽达一脸诚恳，「我们也不打算闯。我们瞧着像那样，多半只是因为用湿刷子刷过头了。不知怎么的，那样确实显得乖；我常注意到。」

「今儿早上我忙得脚不沾地，」哈尼塞特太太接着说，「好腾出下午到我妹子家去待一小会儿。她身子又不大好了，可怜的人，我兴许会耽搁到天晚。好在我侄女埃米莉（Emily）要来当家。她是个活泼的好姑娘；她会给你们弄茶点，把你们照看得妥妥帖帖。你们可别趁我不在，干出什么我在跟前时你们不敢干的事来，好不好？乖孩子。」

===Original===

Nothing could have happened better. Both children felt that Emily, being a young girl, would be more easy to manage than Mrs. Honeysett. As soon as they were alone they talked it over comfortably, and decided that the best thing would be to ask Emily if she would go down to the station and see if there was a parcel there for Master Arden or Miss Arden.

“And if there isn’t,” Elfrida giggled, “we’ll say she’d better wait till it comes. We’ll run down and fetch her as soon as we’ve explored the secret chamber.”

“I say,” Edred remarked thoughtfully, “we haven’t bothered much about finding the treasure, have we? I thought that was what we were going into history for.”

“Now, Edred,” said his sister, “you know very well we didn’t go into history on purpose.”

“No; but,” said Edred, “we ought to have. Suppose the treasure is really those jewels. We’d sell them and rebuild Arden Castle like it used to be, wouldn’t we?”

===Chinese===

再没有比这更顺当的了。姐弟俩都觉得，埃米莉既然是个年轻姑娘，总比哈尼塞特太太好打发。两人一单独待着，便从从容容商量了一回，决定最好的法子，就是问埃米莉肯不肯跑一趟车站，看看有没有寄给阿登少爷或阿登小姐的包裹。

「要是没有呢，」埃尔弗丽达咯咯笑着说，「我们就说包裹没到，她最好等它到了再回来。等咱们探完密室，马上跑下去接她。」

「喂，」埃德雷德若有所思地说，「咱们对寻宝这事儿也没怎么上心，是不是？我还以为咱们进历史，就是为了这个呢。」

「行了，埃德雷德，」他姐姐说，「你明知道咱们不是存心进的历史。」

「是没存心；可是，」埃德雷德说，「咱们本该存心的。万一宝藏真就是那些珠宝呢。咱们把它们卖了，把阿登城堡照老样子重建起来，好不好？」

===Original===

“We’d give Auntie Edith a few jewels, I think, wouldn’t we? She is such a dear, you know.”

“Yes; she should have first choice. I do believe we’re on the brink, and I feel just exactly like as if something real was going to happen﻿—not in history, but here at Arden﻿—Now-Arden.”

“I *do* hope we find the jewels,” said Elfrida. “Oh, I do! And I do hope we manage the lively young girl all right.”

Mrs. Honeysett’s best dress was a nice bright red﻿—the kind of colour you can see a long way off. They watched it till it disappeared round a shoulder of the downs, and then set about the task of managing Emily.

===Chinese===

「我想咱们还该分给伊迪丝姑姑几件珠宝，对吧？你知道，她多可爱呀。」

「对；她该挑头一份。我真的相信，咱们就站在边儿上了。我觉得分明有什么真事要发生——不在历史里，就在这儿，在阿登——此时此地的阿登。」

「我*真*希望咱们找得着那些珠宝。」埃尔弗丽达说。「哦，真希望！也真希望咱们能把那位活泼姑娘顺顺当当地打发过去。」

哈尼塞特太太的好衣裳是一件鲜亮的红——就是老远也望得见的那种颜色。他们望着它，直到它拐过白垩丘的坡肩没了影儿，这才着手办「打发埃米莉」这件大事。

===Original===

The lively young girl proved quite easy to manage. The idea of “popping on her hat” and running down to the station was naturally much pleasanter to her than the idea of washing the plates that had been used for beefsteak pudding and gooseberry-pie, and then giving the kitchen a thorough scrub out﻿—which was the way Mrs. Honeysett had meant her to spend the afternoon.

*Her* best dress﻿—she had slipped the skirt over her print gown so as to look smart as she came up through the village﻿—was a vivid violet, another good distance colour. It also was watched till it dipped into the lane.

“And now,” cried Elfrida, “we’re all alone, and we can explore the great secret!”

“But suppose somebody comes,” said Edred, “and interrupts, and finds it out, and grabs the jewels, and all is lost. There’s tramps, you know, and gipsy-women with baskets.”

===Chinese===

那位活泼姑娘果然顶好打发。「戴上帽子就跑一趟车站」这个主意，对她自然比另一个主意痛快得多——那另一个，是洗掉牛排布丁和醋栗馅饼用过的盘子，再把厨房彻底刷洗一遍；哈尼塞特太太原是想让她这么打发一下午的。

*她*的好衣裳——她在印花布裙外头罩了条裙子，好走过村子时显得体面——是鲜艳的紫罗兰色，又是一种老远就认得出的颜色。这件衣裳也一直被望着，直到它没入小径不见了。

「这下，」埃尔弗丽达喊道，「就剩咱们自己啦，可以去探那个天大的秘密啦！」

「可万一有人来呢，」埃德雷德说，「打断了咱们，发现了秘密，把珠宝抢走，那就全完了。你知道，外头有流浪汉，还有提篮子的吉卜赛女人。」

===Original===

“Yes﻿—or drink of water, or to ask the time. I’ll tell you what﻿—we’ll lock up the doors, back and front.”

They did. But even this did not satisfy the suddenly cautious Edred.

“The parlour door, too,” he said.

So they locked the parlour door, and Elfrida put the key in a safe place, “for fear of accidents,” she said. I do not at all know what she meant, and when she came to think it over she did not know either. But it seemed all right at the time.

They had provided themselves with a box of matches and a candle﻿—and now the decisive moment had come, as they say about battles.

===Chinese===

「是呀——再不就是讨口水喝，或者问问钟点。我告诉你怎么办——咱们把前后门都锁上。」

他们锁了。可这样也没能让忽然谨慎起来的埃德雷德安下心。

「客厅门也锁上。」他说。

于是他们锁上客厅门，埃尔弗丽达把钥匙收进一个妥当地方。「怕出意外嘛。」她说。我全然不明白她指什么，她自己后来细想，也不知道。可当时听着，倒是挺有道理。

他们备好了一盒火柴和一支蜡烛——如今，决战的时刻到了，就像人们讲到战役时说的那样。

===Original===

Elfrida fumbled for the secret spring.

“How does it open?” asked the boy.

“I’ll show you presently,” said the girl. She could not show him then, because, in point of fact, she did not know. She only knew there *was* a secret spring, and she was feeling for it with both hands among the carved wreaths of the panels, as she stood with one foot on each of the arms of a very high chair﻿—the only chair in the room high enough for her to be able to reach all round the panel. Suddenly something clicked and the secret door flew open﻿—she just had time to jump to the floor, or it would have knocked her down.

Then she climbed up again and got into the hole, and Edred handed her the candle.

“Where’s the matches?” she asked.

===Chinese===

埃尔弗丽达摸索着找那处暗簧。

「它怎么开？」男孩问。

「我一会儿做给你看。」姑娘说。她当时做不出来，因为说实话，她自己也不知道。她只知道那儿*确实*有处暗簧，便两只手在雕花板的花环浮雕里摸来摸去。她站在一把很高的椅子上——两脚各踩着一边扶手——那是屋里唯一够高的椅子，让她能把暗板四周都够到。忽然咔哒一响，暗门猛地弹开——她刚来得及跳到地上，不然准被它撞个正着。

接着她重新爬上去，钻进洞里，埃德雷德把蜡烛递给她。

「火柴呢？」她问。

===Original===

“In my pocket,” said he firmly. “I’m not going to have you starting off without me﻿—*again*.”

“Well, come on, then,” said Elfrida, ignoring the injustice of this speech.

“All right,” said Edred, climbing on the chair. “How does it open?”

He had half closed the door, and was feeling among the carved leaves, as he had seen her do.

“Oh, come on,” said Elfrida, “oh, look out!”

Well might she request her careless brother to look out. As he reached up to touch the carving, the chair tilted, he was jerked forward, caught at the carving to save himself, missed it, and fell forward with all his weight against the half-open door. It shut with a loud bang. Then a resounding crash echoed through the quiet house as Edred and the big chair fell to the floor in, so to speak, each other’s arms.

===Chinese===

「在我口袋里。」他说得斩钉截铁。「我可不让你再丢下我一个人跑了——*又*来一回。」

「那好，快来呀。」埃尔弗丽达说，只当没听见这话里的冤枉。

「来了。」埃德雷德说着爬上椅子。「它怎么开？」

他把那扇门半掩上，学着她方才的样子，在雕花叶子里摸。

「哦，快来呀，」埃尔弗丽达说，「哦，当心！」

她这位冒失的弟弟，实在当得起她这一声「当心」。他伸手去够雕花时，椅子一歪，他整个人朝前一窜，慌忙去抓雕花自救，没抓着，全身的分量便撞在半开的门上。门砰的一声巨响关上了。接着一声轰然巨响回荡在寂静的宅子里——埃德雷德和那把大椅子，可以说，是互相搂抱着摔到地板上的。

===Original===

There was a stricken pause. Then Elfrida from the other side of the panel beat upon it with her fists and shouted﻿—

“Open the door! You aren’t hurt, are you?”

“Yes, I am﻿—very much,” said Edred, from the outside of the secret door, and also from the hearthrug. “I’ve twisted my leg in the knickerbocker part, and I’ve got a great bump on my head, and I think I’m going to be very poorly.”

“Well, open the panel first,” said Elfrida rather unfeelingly. But then she was alone in the dark on the other side of the panel.

===Chinese===

一阵吓呆了的静默。随后埃尔弗丽达在暗板那头抡起拳头捶门，大喊——

「开门呀！你没摔伤吧？」

「伤了——伤得厉害。」埃德雷德在暗门外头——也是在炉前地毯上——说。「我把腿扭了，就在灯笼裤那一截，头上还磕了个大包，我看我要病得不轻。」

「那你也先把暗板打开呀。」埃尔弗丽达说得颇为无情。可别忘了，她正独自一人待在暗板另一侧的黑地里呢。

===Original===

“I don’t know how to,” said Edred, and Elfrida heard the sound of someone picking himself up from among disordered furniture.

“Feel among the leaves, like I did,” she said. “It’s quite easy. You’ll soon find it.”

Silence.

===Chinese===

「我不知道怎么开呀。」埃德雷德说。埃尔弗丽达听见有人从乱作一团的家具中间爬起来的声音。

「像我那样在叶子里摸，」她说，「容易得很。你一会儿就找着了。」

一片寂静。

## X White Wings and a Brownie / 白翼与布朗尼

===Original===
Perhaps I had better begin this chapter by telling you exactly how Edred “got even with old Parrot-nose,” as he put it. You will remember that Master Parados was the Ardens’ tutor in the time of King James I, and that it was through his eavesdropping and talebearing that Edred and Elfrida were imprisoned in the Tower of London. There was very little time in which to get even with anyone, and, of course, getting even with people is not really at all a proper thing to do. Yet Edred did it.

Edred had got Elfrida out of the Tower just as Lady Nithsdale got her lord out, and now he and she and Cousin Richard were at Arden House, in Soho, and the old nurse, who was also, astonishingly, the old witch, had said that there was no time to be lost.

===Chinese===
这一章，也许我最好先原原本本告诉你们，埃德雷德是怎么——用他自己的话说——「跟老鹦鹉鼻子算账」的。你们想必还记得，帕拉多斯先生是詹姆斯一世时代阿登家的家庭教师，正是因为他偷听告密，埃德雷德和埃尔弗丽达才被关进了伦敦塔。要跟谁算账，时间本来少得很，何况跟人算账实在也算不得什么体面事。可埃德雷德还是干了。

埃德雷德把埃尔弗丽达救出了伦敦塔，用的正是当年尼斯代尔夫人（Lady Nithsdale）救她丈夫的法子；如今他和她、还有理查德表哥都回到了索霍的阿登宅邸，而那位老奶妈——说来叫人吃惊，也正是那位老巫婆——说事不宜迟，一刻也耽搁不得。

===Original===
“But I *must* be even with old Parrot-nose,” said Edred. He was feeling awfully brave and splendid inside, because of the way he had planned and carried out the Nithsdale rescue of Elfrida; and also he felt that he could not bear to go back to his own times without somehow marking his feelings about Mr. Parados.

As to how it was to be done. Cousin Richard was not to have anything to do with it, because while they would be whisked away by some white road that the Mouldiwarp would find for them when they called it to their help by spoken poetry, he would be left behind to bear the blame of everything. This Edred and Elfrida decided in a quick-whispered conference, but Cousin Dick wanted to know what they were talking about, and why he wasn’t to help in what he had wanted to do these four years.

===Chinese===
「可我*非*跟老鹦鹉鼻子算这笔账不可。」埃德雷德说。他心里正觉得勇敢极了、威风极了，因为他一手谋划、一手办成了那场尼斯代尔式的埃尔弗丽达营救；再说他也觉得，要是不想个法子表示一下他对帕拉多斯先生的看法，就这样回自己的时代去，实在忍不下这口气。

至于怎么个算法。理查德表哥是不能沾边的，因为等他们念出诗歌、把穆迪沃普请来帮忙，它自会找一条白路把他们一下子带走，而他就要被撇下来，承担全部罪责。这是埃德雷德和埃尔弗丽达在一阵急急的低声合计里定下的，可迪克表哥偏要知道他们在嘀咕什么，为什么这桩他盼了四年想做的事，倒不让他插手。

===Original===
“If we tell you,” said Elfrida, “you won’t believe us.”

“You might at least make the trial,” said Cousin Richard.

So they told him, and though they were as quick as possible, the story took some time to tell. Richard Arden listened intently. When the tale was told he said nothing.

===Chinese===
「要是告诉你，」埃尔弗丽达说，「你准不信。」

「那你也总得试一试呀。」理查德表哥说。

于是他们讲了。尽管已经尽量拣快的说，这故事还是讲了老半天。理查德·阿登听得聚精会神。故事讲完，他一言不发。

===Original===
“You don’t believe it,” said Edred; “I knew you wouldn’t. Well, it doesn’t matter. What can we do to pay out old Parrot-nose?”

“I don’t like it,” said Richard suddenly; “it’s never been like this before. It makes it seem not real. It’s only a dream really, I suppose. And I always believed so that it wasn’t.”

“I don’t understand a word you’re saying,” said Edred, “but what we’ve been saying’s true anyhow. Look here.” He darted to the dark corner of the parlour, where he had hidden the camera behind a curtain. “Look here, I bet you haven’t got anything like this. It comes from *our* times, ever so far on in history—out of the times where *we* come from—the times that haven’t happened yet—at least *now* we’re *here* they haven’t happened yet. You don’t know what it is. It’s a machine for the sun to make pictures with.”

===Chinese===
「你不信。」埃德雷德说，「我早知道你不信。唉，不要紧。咱们能想个什么法子整治老鹦鹉鼻子呢？」

「我不喜欢这样。」理查德忽然说，「从前从没这样过。这样一来，事情就好像不是真的了。我想，这其实只是一场梦。而我从前一直相信它不是梦来着。」

「你说的话我一个字也不明白，」埃德雷德说，「可不管怎样，我们方才说的都是真的。瞧这儿。」他一个箭步蹿到客厅的暗角，把藏在一幅帘子后面的照相机取了出来。「瞧这儿，我敢打赌，你绝没有这样的东西。这是从*我们*的时代来的，在历史上还要往后好远好远——从*我们*来的那个时代——还没发生过的时代——至少*现在*，趁*我们在这儿*，那些时代还没发生呢。你不知道这是件什么家伙。这是一部让太阳用来拍照的机器。」

===Original===
“Oh, stow that,” said Richard wearily. “I know now it’s all a silly dream. But it’s not worth while trying to dream that I don’t know a Kodak when I see it. That’s a Brownie!”

There was a pause, full of speechless amazement.

Then—“If you’ve dreamed about our times,” said Elfrida, “you might believe in us dreaming about yours. Did you dream of anything except Brownies? Did you ever dream of fine carriages, fine boats, and—”

===Chinese===
「噢，收起你那一套吧。」理查德没精打采地说。「我现在明白了，这全是一场无聊的梦。可要梦得连柯达（Kodak）都不认得，那也犯不上。那是一架布朗尼（Brownie）！」

一阵静默，满是无言的惊愕。

随后——「既然你梦见过我们的时代，」埃尔弗丽达说，「那你也许肯相信，我们也会梦见你们的时代。除了布朗尼，你还梦见过别的吗？你梦见过漂亮的马车、漂亮的船，还有——」

===Original===
“Don’t talk as if I were a baby,” Richard interrupted. “I know all about railways and steamboats, and the Hippodrome and the Crystal Palace. I know Kent made 615 against Derbyshire last Thursday. Now, then—”

“But I say. Do tell us—”

“I shan’t tell you anything more. But I’ll help you to get even with Parrot-nose. I don’t care if I am left here after you go,” said Richard. “Let’s shovel all the snow off the roof into his room, and take our chance.”

===Chinese===
「别拿我当吃奶的娃娃。」理查德打断她。「铁路、轮船、希波德罗姆剧场（the Hippodrome）、水晶宫（the Crystal Palace），我全知道。肯特郡上星期四对德比郡拿了 615 分，我也知道。怎么样——」

「哎，我说，快告诉我们——」

「我再也不多告诉你什么了。不过帮你跟鹦鹉鼻子算账，我乐意。你们走后把我一个人撇在这儿，我也不在乎。」理查德说。「咱们把屋顶上的雪统统铲进他屋里去，碰碰运气。」

===Original===
Edred and Elfrida would have liked something more subtle, but there was no time to think of anything.

“I know where there are shovels,” said Richard, “if they’ve not got mixed up in the dream.”

“I say,” said Edred slowly, “I’d like to write that down about Kent, and see if it’s right afterwards.”

===Chinese===
埃德雷德和埃尔弗丽达本想要点更巧妙的手笔，可是已经来不及多想了。

「我知道哪儿有铲子，」理查德说，「只要它们没在梦里搅和乱了。」

「我说，」埃德雷德慢吞吞地说，「我想把肯特那句记下来，回头看看灵不灵。」

===Original===
There was a quill sticking out of the pewter inkstand on the table where they were used to do their lessons. But no paper.

“Here, hurry up,” said Cousin Richard, and pulled a paper out of the front of his doublet. “I’ll write it, shall I?”

He wrote, and gave the thing screwed up to Edred, who put it in the front of *his* doublet.

===Chinese===
他们平日念书的那张桌上，白镴墨水台里插着一支鹅毛笔。可是没有纸。

「喂，快点。」理查德表哥说着，从紧身上衣的前襟里抽出一张纸。「我来写，好不好？」

他写了，把纸团成一团递给埃德雷德；埃德雷德把它塞进了*自己的*紧身衣前襟。

===Original===
Then the three went up on to the roof, groped among the snow till they found the edge of the skylight that was the tutor’s window—for learning was lodged in the attic at Arden House. They broke the thick glass with the edges of their spades, and shovelled in the thick, white snow—shovelled all the harder for the shouts and angry words that presently sounded below them. Then, when Mr. Parados came angrily up on to the roof, shivering and stumbling among the snow, they slipped behind the chimney-stack, and so got back to the trapdoor before he did, and shut it and bolted it, and said “A-ha!” underneath it, and went away—locking his room door as they passed, and leaving him to stand there on the roof and shout for help from the street below, or else to drop through his broken skylight into the heaped snow in his room. He was quite free, and could do whichever he chose.

===Chinese===
随后三个人爬上屋顶，在雪里一路摸索，找到那扇充当教师窗户的天窗——原来在阿登宅邸，学问是安置在阁楼里的。他们用铲刃凿破厚玻璃，把又厚又白的雪一铲一铲灌进去——底下很快传上来喊叫和怒骂，他们铲得愈发卖力。等帕拉多斯先生气冲冲地爬上屋顶，在雪里哆哆嗦嗦、跌跌撞撞的时候，他们早已溜到烟囱背后，抢在他之前回到活板门口，把门关上、闩死，隔着门喊了一声「啊哈！」，扬长而去——路过他房门时顺手锁上，由他站在屋顶上朝着底下的街面喊救命，或者从他砸破的天窗跳进屋里堆成山的雪里。他完全可以自便，爱怎么着就怎么着。

===Original===
They never knew which he *did* choose, and you will never know either.

And then Richard was sent to bed by the old witch-nurse, and went.

And the Mouldiwarp was summoned, and insisted that the only way back to their own times was by jumping off the roof. And, of course, Mr. Parados was on the roof, which made all the difference. And the soldiers of the guard were knocking at the front door with the butts of their pistols.

===Chinese===
他*到底*选了哪一样，他们始终不知道，你们也永远不会知道。

接着，理查德被那巫婆保姆打发上了床，他就去了。

接着穆迪沃普被召了来，一口咬定回他们自己时代的唯一法子就是从屋顶跳下去。可偏偏帕拉多斯先生正在屋顶上，这就大不相同了。而卫队的士兵们正用手枪枪托砸着前门。

===Original===
“But we can’t go on to the roof,” said Edred, and explained about Mr. Parados.

“Humph,” said the Mouldiwarp, “that’s terr’ble unfortunate, that is. Well, the top landing window will have to do, that’s all. Where’s the other child?”

“Gone to bed,” said the witch-nurse shortly.

===Chinese===
「可我们上不了屋顶呀。」埃德雷德说，就把帕拉多斯先生的事讲了一遍。

「哼，」穆迪沃普说，「这可真倒霉透顶，真是的。唉，那就只好用顶层楼梯间的窗户了，没别的法子。另一个孩子呢？」

「睡觉去了。」巫婆保姆没好气地说。

===Original===
“Te-he!” chuckled the Mouldiwarp. “Some people’s too clever by half. Think of you not having found *that* out, and you a witch too. Te-he!”

And all the time the soldiers were hammering away like mad at the front door.

Elfrida caught the Mouldiwarp and the nurse caught Edred’s hand, and the four raced up the stairs to the very top landing, where there was a little window at the very end. The air was keen and cold. The window opened difficultly, and when it was opened the air was much colder than before.

===Chinese===
「嘿儿嘿！」穆迪沃普窃笑。「有些人就是聪明过头喽。瞧瞧你，连*这个*都没瞧出来，亏你还是个巫婆呢。嘿儿嘿！」

而这工夫，士兵们还在前门上疯了一样地砸个不停。

埃尔弗丽达抱起穆迪沃普，奶妈攥住埃德雷德的手，四个一路奔上楼梯，直奔最顶层的楼梯间——尽头有扇小窗。空气凛冽刺骨。窗子好不容易才打开，一开开，空气比先前更冷得多了。

===Original===
“Now, then, out with you—ladies first,” cried the Mouldiwarp.

“You don’t really mean,” said Elfrida—“you *can’t* mean that we’re to jump out into—into nothing?”

“I mean you’re to jump out right enough,” said the Mouldiwarp. “What you’re to jump into’s any pair of shoes—and it’s my lookout, anyway.”

“It’s ours a little too, isn’t it?” said Elfrida timidly, and her teeth were chattering; she always said afterwards that it was with cold.

===Chinese===
「好了，出去吧——女士优先。」穆迪沃普喊道。

「你不是当真的吧，」埃尔弗丽达说——「你*总不能*是叫我们跳出去——跳进虚无里去吧？」

「我就是叫你们往外跳，一点不假。」穆迪沃普说。「至于你们要跳进的是双什么鞋，那可没准儿——反正归我操心。」

「那也总有一点点归我们操心吧？」埃尔弗丽达怯生生地说。她的牙齿直打颤——事后她一口咬定，那是冻的。

===Original===
“Then get along home your way,” said the Mouldiwarp, beginning to vanish.

“Oh, *don’t*! Don’t go!” Elfrida cried, and the pounding on the door downstairs got louder and louder.

“If I don’t then you must,” said the Mouldiwarp testily. But it stopped vanishing.

“Put me down,” it said. “Put me down and jump, for goodness’ sake!”

===Chinese===
「那你们就自己走回家去吧。」穆迪沃普说着，开始隐没。

「噢，*别*！别走呀！」埃尔弗丽达喊起来。楼下砸门的声音越来越响、越来越响。

「我要是不走，那你们就只好跳了。」穆迪沃普不耐烦地说。不过它不再隐没了。

「把我放下来。」它说。「行行好，把我放下，快跳吧！」

===Original===
She put it down.

Suddenly the nurse caught Elfrida in her arms and kissed her many times.

“Farewell, my honey love,” she said. “All partings are not forever, else I could scarce let thee go. Now, climb up; set thy foot here on the beam, now thy knee on the sill. So—jump!”

===Chinese===
她把它放了下来。

奶妈忽然张开双臂搂住埃尔弗丽达，一遍又一遍地吻她。

「别了，我的心肝宝贝。」她说。「天下离别不尽是永诀，不然我怎么也舍不得放你走。来，爬上去；把脚踩在这根横梁上，再把膝盖搁上窗台。就这样——跳！」

===Original===
Elfrida crouched on the window-ledge, where the snow lay thick and crisp. It was very, very cold. Have you ever had to jump out of a top-floor window into the dark when it was snowing heavily? If so, you will remember how much courage it needed. Elfrida set her teeth, looking down into black nothing dotted with snowflakes. Then she looked back into a black passage, lighted only by the rushlight the nurse carried.

===Chinese===
埃尔弗丽达蹲在窗台上，雪在窗台上积得又厚又脆。天好冷好冷。你可曾在下着大雪的夜里，不得不从顶楼的窗户往黑暗里跳？要是跳过，你就记得那需要多大的勇气。埃尔弗丽达咬紧牙关，望着底下那点缀着雪花的漆黑一片。然后她回头望了一眼漆黑的楼梯间，只有奶妈手里那支灯芯草烛照着一点亮。

===Original===
“Edred’ll be all right?” she asked. “You’re sure he’ll jump all right?”

“Of course I shall,” said Edred, in his new voice. “Here, let me go first, to show you I’m not a coward.”

Of course, Elfrida instantly jumped. And next moment Edred jumped too.

===Chinese===
「埃德雷德不会有事吧？」她问。「你担保他跳下去不会有事？」

「我当然不会有事。」埃德雷德用他那副新换的嗓音说。「喂，让我先跳，好叫你看看我不是胆小鬼。」

果然，埃尔弗丽达立刻就跳了。紧接着，埃德雷德也跳了。

===Original===
It was a horrible moment because, however much you trusted the Mouldiwarp, you could not in an instant forget what you had been taught all your life—that if you jumped out of top-floor windows you would certainly be smashed to pieces on the stones below. To remember this and, remembering it, to jump clear, is a very brave deed. And brave deeds, sooner or later, have their reward.

===Chinese===
那一刻真是可怕，因为任凭你多么信得过穆迪沃普，你也不可能一眨眼就忘掉你一辈子受的教导——从顶楼窗户跳下去，准会摔在底下的石头上粉身碎骨。心里记着这一条，又记着这一条而纵身跳出，是件非常勇敢的壮举。而壮举，迟早会有它的报偿。

===Original===
The brave deed of Edred and Elfrida received its reward sooner. As Elfrida jumped she saw the snowflakes gather and thicken into a cloud beneath her. The cloud was not the sort that lets you through, either. It was solid and soft as piled eiderdown feathers; she knew this as it rose up and caught her, or as she fell on it—she never knew which. Next moment Edred was beside her, and the white, downy softness was shaping itself round and under them into the form of a seat—a back, arms, and place for the feet to rest.

===Chinese===
埃德雷德和埃尔弗丽达的壮举，报偿来得很快。埃尔弗丽达跳下去时，只见雪花在身子底下聚拢起来，越积越厚，凝成了一朵云。这云也不是那种一穿就过的云。它又结实又柔软，像堆起来的鸭绒羽毛；是它涌上来接住她的呢，还是她落在它上面的呢——她后来始终没弄明白。转眼间埃德雷德已在她身边，那白色的、绒毛似的柔软东西，在他们四周和身底下渐渐成形，变成一个座位——有靠背，有扶手，还有搁脚的地方。

===Original===
“It’s—what’s that in your hand?” Elfrida asked.

“Reins,” said Edred, with certainty. “White reins. It’s a carriage.”

It was—a carriage made of white snowflakes—the snowflakes that were warm and soft as feathers. There were white, soft carriage-rugs that curled round and tucked themselves in entirely of their own accord. The reins were of snowflakes, joined together by some magic weaving, and warm and soft as white velvet. And the horses!

===Chinese===
「这是——你手里拿的是什么？」埃尔弗丽达问。

「缰绳。」埃德雷德说得斩钉截铁。「白缰绳。这是一辆马车。」

正是——一辆白雪花做的马车——那雪花暖烘烘、软乎乎，像羽毛一样。车里有白色柔软的车毯，自动卷过来裹住他们，还自动把自己掖得严严实实。缰绳也是雪花做的，靠某种魔法编织连在一处，又暖又软，像白天鹅绒。还有那拉车的马！

===Original===
“There aren’t any horses; they’re swans—white swans!” cried Elfrida, and the voice of the Mouldiwarp, behind and above, cried softly, “All white things obey me.”

Edred knew how to drive. And now he could not resist the temptation to drive the six white swans round to the front of the house and to swoop down, passing just over the heads of the soldiers of the guard who were still earnestly pounding at the door of Arden House, and yelled to them, “Ha, ha! Sold again!” Which seemed to startle them very much. Then he wheeled the swans round and drove quickly through the air along the way which he knew quite well, without being told, to be the right way. And as the snow-carriage wheeled, both Edred and Elfrida had a strange, sudden vision of another smaller snow-carriage, drawn by two swans only, that circled above theirs and vanished in the deep dark of the sky, giving them an odd, tantalising glimpse of a face they knew and yet couldn’t remember distinctly enough to give a name to the owner of it.

===Chinese===
「根本没有马；那是天鹅——白天鹅！」埃尔弗丽达喊道。身后上方传来穆迪沃普的声音，轻轻地喊道：「一切白色的东西都听命于我。」

埃德雷德会赶车。此刻他忍不住一阵诱惑，赶着六只白天鹅绕到宅子正面，一个俯冲，贴着那些还在起劲砸阿登宅邸大门的卫兵头顶掠过，冲他们大喊：「哈哈！又上当啦！」这一下似乎把他们吓得不轻。然后他拨转天鹅，在空中疾驰而去，走的正是一条他不用人指点、心里明明白白知道该走的路。雪车盘旋转弯的时候，埃德雷德和埃尔弗丽达都忽然生出一种奇异的幻象：还有一辆小些的雪车，只由两只天鹅拉着，在他们那辆上方绕了一圈，消失在天空的深黑里，让他们飞快又撩人地瞥见一张熟识的脸——却又记不真切，怎么也叫不出那张脸的主人的名字。

===Original===
Then the swans spread their white, mighty wings to the air, and strained with their long, strong necks against their collars, and the snow equipage streamed out of London like a slender white scarf driven along in the wind. And London was left behind, and the snowstorm, and soon the dark blue of the sky was over them, jewelled with the quiet silver of watchful stars, and the deeper dark of the Kentish county lay below, jewelled with the quiet gold from the windows of farms already half-asleep, and the air that rushed past their faces as they went was no longer cold, but soft as June air is, and Elfrida always declared afterwards that she could smell white lilies all the way.

===Chinese===
于是天鹅把白色的大翅膀向空中展开，用长长的、强有力的脖子绷紧了挽具，那套雪车行头便像一条迎风疾驰的细长白纱巾，一气驶出了伦敦。伦敦落在身后，暴风雪也落在身后，不多时，头顶已是深蓝色的天空，缀满守望星辰的静谧银光；脚下是肯特郡更深的黑，缀着半睡半醒的农舍窗户透出的静谧金光；迎面扑来的风，也渐渐不再寒冷，而是柔软如六月的空气。埃尔弗丽达事后一直一口咬定，她一路上闻得见白百合的香气。

===Original===
So across the darkened counties they went, and the ride was more wonderful than any ride they had ever had before or would ever have again.

All too soon the swans hung, poised on long, level wings, outside the window of a tower in Arden Castle—a tower they did not know.

But though they did not know the tower, it was quite plain that they were meant to get in at the window of it.

===Chinese===
他们就这样越过一个个暗下来的郡县。这一趟车，比他们从前坐过的任何一趟都奇妙，也比他们往后将要坐的任何一趟都奇妙。

太快了，快得人意犹未尽，天鹅已张开修长平展的翅膀，悬停在阿登城堡一座塔楼的窗外——一座他们不认识的塔楼。

塔楼虽不认识，可明明白白，人家就是要他们从这扇窗户进去。

===Original===
“Dear swans,” said Elfrida, who had been thinking as she sat clutching her Brownie, “can’t we stay in your carriage till it’s light? We do so want to take a photograph of the castle.”

The swans shook their white, flat, snakelike heads, just as though they understood. And there was the open window, evidently waiting to welcome the children.

So they got out—very much against their wills. And there they were in the dark room of the tower, and it was very cold.

===Chinese===
「亲爱的天鹅，」埃尔弗丽达说——她坐着抱紧她的布朗尼，心里一直在盘算——「我们不能在你们车里待到天亮吗？我们多想给城堡拍张照呀。」

天鹅摇了摇它们那些扁扁的、蛇一样的白脑袋，一副全听懂了的模样。那扇敞开的窗就在那儿，分明正等着迎接孩子们。

于是他们下了车——一百个不情愿。就这样，他们站在了塔楼漆黑的屋子里，屋里冷极了。

===Original===
But before they had time to begin to understand how cold it was, and how uncomfortable they were likely to be for the rest of the night, six swan’s heads appeared at the window and said something.

“Oh,” said Elfrida, “I do wish we’d learned Swanish instead of French at school!”

But it did not matter. The next moment the swans’ heads ducked and reappeared, holding in their beaks the soft, fluffy, white rugs that had kept the children so warm in the snow-carriage. The swans pushed the rugs through the window with their strong, white wings, and made some more remarks in swan language.

“Oh, thank you!” said the children. “Goodbye, goodbye.”

===Chinese===
可不等他们来得及明白这有多冷、下半夜又该多难受，六只天鹅的脑袋已经出现在窗口，说了句什么。

「唉，」埃尔弗丽达说，「真希望我们上学学的是天鹅语（Swanish），不是法语！」

不过那也没关系。转眼间天鹅们把头一低再一抬，喙里衔着那几条曾在雪车里给孩子们保暖的软绒绒的白毯子。天鹅用强健的白翅膀把毯子从窗口推进来，又用天鹅语说了几句话。

「噢，谢谢你们！」孩子们说。「再见，再见。」

===Original===
Then there was the rush of wide-going wings, and the children, tired out, cuddled on the floor, wrapped in the soft rugs.

The happiest kind of dreams were tucked up in that coverlet, and it seemed hardly any time at all before the children woke to find the winter sunshine looking in at them through the narrow windows of the tower.

Elfrida jumped up and threw off the silver-white, downy-soft coverlet. It instantly tore itself into five pieces of different shapes and sizes, and these screwed themselves up, and drew themselves in, and blew themselves out, and turned before her very eyes into a silver basin of warm water, a piece of lily-scented soap, a towel, a silver comb, and an ivory toothbrush.

===Chinese===
接着是一阵张翅远去的疾风，孩子们累坏了，裹着软毯子，蜷在地板上依偎在一起。

最最快乐的种种梦，都掖在了那床毯子里。仿佛只过了不大工夫，孩子们就醒了，发现冬日的阳光正透过塔楼窄窄的窗子望着他们。

埃尔弗丽达跳起来，一把掀开那银白色、软绒绒的盖毯。毯子立刻自己撕成五块，形状大小各不相同；这几块又各自团起来、缩拢来、鼓起来，当着她的面变成了一银盆温水、一块百合香型的肥皂、一条毛巾、一把银梳子，还有一把象牙牙刷。

===Original===
“Well!” said Elfrida. When she had finished her simple toilet, the basin, soap, towel, toothbrush, and comb ran together like globules of quicksilver, made a curious tousled lump of themselves, and straightened out into the fluffy coverlet again.

“Well!” said Elfrida, again. Then she woke Edred, and his coverlet played the same clever and pretty trick for him.

And when the children started to go down with the Brownie and take the photographs of the castle, the shining coverlets jumped up into two white furry coats, such as the very affluent might wear when they went a-motoring—if the very affluent ever thought of anything so pretty. And one of the coats came politely to the side of each child, holding out its arms as if it were saying—

===Chinese===
「嚯！」埃尔弗丽达说。她草草梳洗完毕，那脸盆、肥皂、毛巾、牙刷和梳子便像一颗颗水银珠似的汇聚到一起，乱蓬蓬地抱成一团，又舒展开来，变回了那条绒毛毯子。

「嚯！」埃尔弗丽达又说了一声。然后她叫醒埃德雷德，他的盖毯也给他变了一模一样的又聪明又漂亮的戏法。

等孩子们带着布朗尼要下楼去给城堡拍照时，那两条亮闪闪的盖毯一跃而起，变成两件白色的毛皮外套，就是顶阔气的人开车兜风时穿的那一种——倘若顶阔气的人当真想得到这么美的东西的话。一件外套彬彬有礼地走到每个孩子身边，伸出袖子，好像在说——

===Original===
“Do, please, oblige me by putting me on.”

Which, of course, both the children did.

They crept down the corkscrew stairs, and through a heavy door that opened under the arch of the great gateway. The great gate was open, and on the step of the door opposite to the one by which they had come out a soldier sat. He held his helmet between his knees, and was scouring it with sand and whistling as he scoured. He touched his forehead with his sandy hand, but did not get up.

===Chinese===
「劳驾，请赏光把我穿上吧。」

两个孩子自然都照办了。

他们顺着螺旋楼梯悄悄下来，穿过大拱门底下一扇厚重的门。大门敞开着，正对他们出来的那扇门，门前台阶上坐着一个士兵。他把头盔夹在两膝当中，正用沙子擦它，一边擦一边吹口哨。他那只沾满沙子的手朝额头碰了碰，却没有起身。

===Original===
“You’re early afield,” he said, and went on rubbing the sand on the helmet.

“It’s such a pretty day,” said Elfrida. “May we go out?”

“And welcome,” said the man simply; “but go not beyond the twelve acre, for fear of rough folk and Egyptians. And go not far. But breakfast will have a strong voice to call you back.”

===Chinese===
「你们起得可早。」他说，手上继续往头盔上蹭沙子。

「今天天儿真好。」埃尔弗丽达说。「我们可以出去吗？」

「去呗，尽管去。」那人说得干脆，「可别走出十二英亩那块地，怕遇上歹人和吉普赛人。也别走远。早饭自有副大嗓门，会把你们喊回来的。」

===Original===
They went out, and instead of stepping straight on to the turf of the downs, their stout shoes struck echoing notes from the wooden planks of a bridge.

“It’s a drawbridge,” said Edred, in tones of awe; “and there’s a moat, look—and it’s covered with cat-ice at the edges.”

There was, and it was. And at the moat’s far edge, their feet fast in the cat-ice, were reeds and sedge—brown and yellow and dried, that rustled and whispered as a wild duck flew out of them.

===Chinese===
他们走了出去，一脚没有踏上丘原的草皮，结实的鞋底却踩响了一座木桥的桥板，发出回声。

「这是吊桥。」埃德雷德说，语气里满是敬畏，「还有护城河呢，瞧——边上还结着一层薄冰。」

吊桥果然是吊桥，薄冰也果然是薄冰。护城河对岸的边上，芦苇和莞草的脚跟冻在薄冰里——褐的、黄的、枯干的，一只野鸭从里头扑棱棱飞出来，惊得它们沙沙作响、窃窃私语。

===Original===
“How lovely!” said Elfrida. “I do *wish* Arden had a moat now.”

“If we found out where the water comes from,” said Edred practically, “we might get the moat back when we’d found the treasure.”

So when they had crossed the moat, and felt the frozen dew crackle under their feet as they trod the grass, they set out, before photographing the castle, to find out where the moat water came from.

===Chinese===
「多美呀！」埃尔弗丽达说。「我多*希望*阿登现在也有条护城河啊。」

「咱们要是查出水是从哪儿来的，」埃德雷德说得挺实在，「等找到宝藏，说不定就能把护城河弄回来。」

于是过了护城河，踏上草地，脚底下冻凝的露水咔嚓作响，他们便先不忙着拍城堡，动身去查护城河的水是从哪儿来的。

===Original===
The moat, they found, was fed by a stream that came across the field from Arden Knoll and entered the moat at the northeast corner, leaving it at the corner that was in the southwest. They followed the stream, and it was not till they had got quite into the middle of the field, and well away from the castle, that they saw how very beautiful the castle really was. It was quite perfect—no crumbled arches, no broken pillars, no shattered, battered walls.

===Chinese===
他们发现，给护城河供水的是一条小溪，从阿登丘那边穿过田野而来，在东北角流进护城河，又从西南那头的角上流出去。他们沿着溪走，直到走进田野正中、离城堡远远的了，才看清这城堡究竟有多美。它完好无损——没有坍塌的拱门，没有断裂的廊柱，没有残破剥蚀的墙壁。

===Original===
“Oh,” said Edred, “how beautiful it is! How glad I am that we’ve got a castle like this!”

“Our castle isn’t like this,” said Elfrida.

“No; but it shall be, when we’ve found the treasure. You’ve got the two film rolls all right?”

“Yes,” said Elfrida, who had got them in a great unwieldy pocket that was hanging and banging against her legs under the full skirt. “Oh, look! Where’s the river? It stops short!”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，」埃德雷德说，「多美呀！咱们有这样一座城堡，我真高兴！」

「咱们的城堡可不是这样。」埃尔弗丽达说。

「现在不是；可等咱们找到了宝藏，它就会是了。那两卷胶卷你没弄丢吧？」

「没丢。」埃尔弗丽达说。她把胶卷揣在一只肥大累赘的口袋里，那口袋在宽大的裙子底下晃晃荡荡，直磕她的腿。「哎呀，瞧！小河呢？它断掉了！」

===Original===
It certainly seemed to. They were walking beside it, and it ran swiftly—looking like a steel-grey ribbon on the green cloth of the field—and halfway across the field it did stop short; there wasn’t any more of it—as though the ribbon had been snipped off by a giant pair of scissors, and the rest of it rolled up and put by safely somewhere out of the way.

===Chinese===
看样子确实断掉了。他们正沿着溪走，溪水流得湍急——像田野这块绿呢子上一条钢灰色的缎带——走到田野中间，它当真戛然而止；再没有下文了——仿佛那缎带叫一把巨人剪刀齐齐剪断，余下的部分卷了起来，收存在什么不碍事的地方了。

===Original===
“My hat!” said Edred; “it does stop short; and no mistake.” Curiosity pricked him, and he started running. They both ran. They ran to the spot where the giant scissors seemed to have snipped off the stream, and when they got there they found that the stream seemed to have got tired of running aboveground, and without any warning at all, any sloping of its bed, or any deepening of its banks, plunged straight down into the earth through a hole not eight feet across.

They stood fascinated, watching the water as it shot over the edge of the hole, like a steel band on a driving-wheel, smooth and shining, and moving so swiftly that it hardly seemed to move at all. It was Edred who roused himself to say, “I could watch it forever. But we’ll have it back; we’ll have it back. Come along; let’s go and see where it comes from.”

===Chinese===
「我的老天！」埃德雷德说，「真是一刀两断，一点不含糊。」好奇心刺得他痒痒的，撒腿就跑。两个人都跑了起来。他们跑到巨人剪刀剪断小溪的地方，到了才发现，这条小溪似乎是跑地面跑腻了，一点预兆也没有，河床不斜一下，堤岸不深一分，就从一个不到八英尺宽的洞里，一头扎进了地底。

他们看迷了，站在那里望那股水从洞沿上射下去，像动力轮上一条钢皮带，平滑，锃亮，流得那么快，快得几乎看不出在动。还是埃德雷德先回过神来：「我能在这儿看上一辈子。可咱们要把它弄回来的；一定要弄回来。走吧，去瞧瞧它是从哪儿来的。」

===Original===
“Let’s photograph this place first,” said Elfrida, “so as to know, you know.” And the Brownie clicked twice.

Then they retraced their steps beside the stream and round two sides of the moat and across the field to Arden Knoll, and there—oh, wonderful to see!—the stream came straight out of the Knoll at the part where it joined on to the rest of the world—came out under a rough, low arch of stone that lay close against the very lip of the water.

“So that’s where it came from and that’s where it goes to,” said Elfrida. “I wonder what became of it, and why it isn’t at Arden now?”

===Chinese===
「先给这地方拍张照吧，」埃尔弗丽达说，「好心里有个数，你懂的。」于是布朗尼相机咔嗒咔嗒响了两声。

随后他们原路折回：沿着小溪，绕过护城河的两边，穿过田野，回到阿登丘。就在那儿——啊，看上去多神奇！——小溪从阿登丘连接外面世界的那一面直直地涌出来——从一道粗石砌的低矮拱门底下涌出来，拱门紧贴着水唇。

「这么说，它是打这儿来，又往那儿去。」埃尔弗丽达说。「不知道它后来怎么了，怎么如今阿登就没有它了？」

===Original===
“We’ll bring it back,” said Edred firmly—“when we find the treasure.”

And again the Brownie clicked.

“And we’ll make the castle like it is now,” said Elfrida. “Come on; let’s photograph it.”

So they went back, and they photographed the castle. They photographed it from the north and the south and the east and the west, and the northeast and the southeast, and the north-north-west—and all the rest of the points of the compass that I could easily tell you if I liked; but why be wearisome and instructive?

===Chinese===
「咱们会把它弄回来的。」埃德雷德说得斩钉截铁——「等咱们找到宝藏。」

布朗尼相机又咔嗒响了一声。

「咱们还要把城堡修得跟现在这个样儿。」埃尔弗丽达说。「来，给它拍一张。」

于是他们回去，给城堡拍了照。从北面拍、南面拍、东面拍、西面拍，又从东北面、东南面拍，从北北西面拍——还有罗盘上其余所有方位，我高兴的话本可以一一说给你们听；可何必惹人嫌、摆出一副说教的架子呢？

===Original===
And they went back across the hollow-echoing drawbridge, and past the soldier, who had now polished his helmet to his complete satisfaction and was wearing it.

There was a brief and ardent conference on the drawbridge; the subject of it, breakfast. Edred wanted to stay; he was curious to see what sort of breakfast people had in the country in James the First’s time, Elfrida wanted to get back to 1908, and the certainty of eggs and bacon.

“If we stay here we shall only be dragged into some new adventure,” she urged, “I know we shall. I never in my life knew such a place as history for adventures to happen in. And I’m tired, besides. Oh, Edred, do come along!”

===Chinese===
他们又从那空洞作响的吊桥上走回去，路过那士兵身边——他这时已把头盔擦得称心如意，正戴在头上。

吊桥上展开了一场简短而热烈的商谈，议题是早饭。埃德雷德想留下，他好奇詹姆斯一世时代乡下人吃什么样的早饭；埃尔弗丽达却想回 1908 年，回到稳稳当当的鸡蛋和咸肉跟前。

「咱们要是待在这儿，保准又给拽进什么新的冒险里去。」她劝道，「我知道准会。我这辈子就没见过历史这种地方，冒险事儿一桩接着一桩。再说我也累了。噢，埃德雷德，快走吧！」

===Original===
“I believe it’s ducks,” said Edred, and he sniffed questioningly; “it smells like onion stuffing.”

“Stuff and nonsense,” said Elfrida; “that’s for dinner, most likely. I expect breakfast for *us* would be bread and water. You’d find we’d done something wrong, as likely as not. Oh, come along, do, before we get punished for it. Besides, don’t you want to know whether what Cousin Richard said about the cricket was right?”

“Well, yes,” said Edred, “and we can always come back here, can’t we?”

“Of course we can,” Elfrida said eagerly. “Oh, come on.”

===Chinese===
「我猜是鸭子。」埃德雷德说，将信将疑地抽了抽鼻子，「闻着像洋葱填料。」

「胡说八道。」埃尔弗丽达说，「那多半是晚饭的菜。我看轮到*我们*的早饭，多半是面包白水。弄不好咱们已经犯了什么过错。噢，快走吧，趁还没为这个挨罚。再说，理查德表哥说的板球那件事灵不灵，你不想知道吗？」

「嗯，想。」埃德雷德说，「反正咱们随时还能回来，对不对？」

「当然能。」埃尔弗丽达急切地说。「噢，走吧。」

===Original===
So they climbed up to the twisty-twiny, corkscrew staircase, and found the door of the room where they had slept under the wonderful white coverlets that now were coats. Then they stood still and looked at each other, with a sudden shock.

“How are we to get back?” was the unspoken question that trembled on each lip.

The magic white coats cuddled close round their necks. There was, somehow, comfort and confidence in the soft, friendly touch of that magic fur. When you are wearing that sort of coat, it is quite impossible to feel that everything will not come perfectly right the moment you really, earnestly, and thoroughly wish that it *should* come right.

===Chinese===
于是他们爬上那盘盘绕绕、螺旋似的楼梯，找到了那间屋子的门——他们曾在屋里那些奇妙的白盖毯底下睡觉，如今盖毯已变成了外套。随后他们猛地一怔，站住不动，你看着我，我看着你。

「咱们怎么回去呢？」——这个问题没说出口，却颤动在两个人的唇边。

那两件白色的魔衣亲亲热热地贴上来，拢住他们的脖颈。那魔法毛皮柔软友好的触碰里，不知怎么就带着安慰，带着底气。人一穿上这种外套，就怎么也没法不相信：只要你真心实意、诚心诚意、彻头彻尾地巴望事情*就该*好，事情立刻就会好得十全十美。

===Original===
“Our clothes,” said Elfrida.

“Oh, yes, of course,” said Edred, “I was forgetting.”

“You may as well go on forgetting,” said his sister, “because the clothes aren’t here. They’re the other side of that twisty-twiny, inside-out, upside-down shakiness that turned the attic into the tower. I suppose the tower would turn back into the attic if we could only start that shaky upside-downness going—wrong way before, you know.”

===Chinese===
「咱们的衣裳。」埃尔弗丽达说。

「噢，对，可不是。」埃德雷德说，「我忘了。」

「你索性一直忘下去也行。」他姐姐说，「因为衣裳不在这儿。它们在另一头，隔着那阵把阁楼变成塔楼的、盘盘绕绕、里外翻个、天翻地覆的摇晃劲儿。我想，只要咱们能让那阵天翻地覆的摇晃劲儿重新转起来——倒着转，你懂吗——塔楼准会变回阁楼。」

===Original===
“I suppose it would,” said Edred, stopping-short, with his fingers between the buttons of his doublet. “Hallo! What’s this?”

He pulled out a folded paper.

“It’s the thing about cricket that Cousin Richard gave you. Don’t bother about that now. I want to get back. I suppose we ought to make some poetry.”

===Chinese===
「我料想会吧。」埃德雷德说着，陡然住手，手指正插在紧身衣的扣眼间。「嘿！这是什么？」

他抽出一张折起的纸。

「是理查德表哥给你的那张板球条子。这会儿别管它。我要回去。我看咱们该作点诗了。」

===Original===
But Edred pulled out the paper and unfolded it.

“It might vanish, you know,” he said, “or get stuck here, and when we got home we should find it gone when we came to look for it. Let’s just see what he says Kent *did* make.”

He straightened out the paper, looked at it, looked again, and held it out with a sudden arm’s-length gesture.

===Chinese===
可埃德雷德把纸抽出来，展开了。

「要知道，它没准儿会没了影儿，」他说，「要不就给卡在这儿，等咱们回了家再找，就发现它不见了。咱们就瞧瞧，他说肯特郡*到底*得了多少分吧。」

他把纸抹平，看了看，又看了看，忽然一伸胳膊，把纸递了出去。

===Original===
“Look at that,” he said. “If that’s true, Richard *has* dreamed our times, and no mistake. And, what’s more, he’s brought things back here out of our times.”

Elfrida took the paper and looked at it, and her mouth dropped open. “If it’s true?” said she. “But it *must* be true!” The paper almost fell from her hand, for it was a bill from Gamage’s for three ships’ guns, a compass, and a half-dozen flags—and the bill was made out to Mr. R. D. Arden, 117, Laurie Grove, New Cross, London, S.E. On the other side was the pencilled record of the runs made by Kent the previous Thursday.

===Chinese===
「你瞧这个。」他说。「要是这是真的，理查德就*确实*梦见过我们的时代，错不了。还有呢，他还从我们的时代带了东西回来。」

埃尔弗丽达接过纸来一看，嘴巴张开了。「要是这是真的？」她说。「可它*必定*是真的！」那张纸差点从她手里掉下去，因为那是加米奇商店（Gamage's）开来的一张账单，货是三尊舰炮、一个罗盘、半打卡旗——账单抬头开给 R. D. 阿登先生（Mr. R. D. Arden），伦敦东南区（S.E.）新克罗斯（New Cross）劳里巷（Laurie Grove）117 号。纸的另一面，是铅笔记下的肯特郡上星期四的得分。

===Original===
“I *say*,” said Elfrida, and was going on to say I don’t know what clever and interesting things, when she felt the fur coat creep and wriggle all through its soft length, and along its soft width, and no wriggle that ever was wriggled expressed so completely “Danger! danger! danger! You’d better get off while you can—while you can.” A quite violent ruffling of the fur round the neck of her coat said, as plain as it could speak, “Don’t stop to jaw. Go now—*now—now*!”

===Chinese===
「我*说*——」埃尔弗丽达刚要往下说些我不知道多聪明多有趣的话，忽然觉出那件毛皮外套顺着它软软的全身、沿着它软软的宽度蠕动起来、扭动起来——天底下从没有哪一阵扭动，能把「危险！危险！危险！趁还来得及快走——趁还来得及」表达得这么淋漓尽致。外套领子四周的毛猛地一阵竖起，像开口说话一样明白：「别磨牙了。快走——*马上——马上*！」

===Original===
When you say a lady is a “true daughter of Eve” you mean that she is inquisitive. Elfrida was enough Eve’s daughter to scurry to the window and look out.

A thrill ran right down her backbone and ended in an empty feeling at the ends of her fingers and feet.

“Soldiers!” she cried, “And they’re after us—I know they are.”

The fur coat knew it too, if knowledge can be expressed by wriggling.

===Chinese===
说一位小姐是「夏娃的真女儿」，意思是她好奇心重。埃尔弗丽达身上的夏娃血统，足足够她一阵风抢到窗前，探头往外看。

一阵寒栗顺着她的脊梁骨直溜下去，末了在她的手指尖和脚趾尖上，变成了一种空落落的感觉。

「士兵！」她喊道，「他们是冲咱们来的——我知道准是。」

那件毛皮外套也知道了——倘若扭动也能算作一种「知道」的话。

===Original===
“Oh, and they’re pulling up the drawbridge! What for?” said Edred, who had come to the window too. “And, I say, doesn’t the portcullis look guillotinish when it comes down like that?”

Through the window one looked straight down on to the drawbridge, and as the tower stuck out beyond the gate, its side window gave an excellent view of the slowly descending portcullis.

“I say,” said Elfrida, “my fluffy coat says ‘Go!’ Doesn’t yours?”

“It would if I’d listen to it,” said Edred carelessly.

===Chinese===
「哎呀，他们在拉起吊桥！这是干什么？」也走到窗前的埃德雷德说。「还有，我说，铁闸门这么落下来的时候，瞧着多像断头台呀？」

从这扇窗可以径直俯视吊桥；塔楼探出在大门之外，所以这扇侧窗把缓缓下降的铁闸门看得一清二楚。

「我说，」埃尔弗丽达说，「我的绒毛外套在喊『快走！』你的不喊吗？」

「我要肯听，它自然也会喊。」埃德雷德满不在乎地说。

===Original===
The soldiers were quite near now—so near that Elfrida could see how fierce they looked. And she knew that they were the same soldiers who had hammered so loud and so hard at the door of Arden House, in Soho. They must have ridden all night. So she screwed her mind up to make poetry, just as you screw your muscles up to jump a gate or run a hundred yards. And almost before she knew that she was screwing it up at all the screw had acted and she had screwed out a piece of Mouldiwarp poetry and was saying it aloud—

===Chinese===
这会儿士兵们已经很近了——近得埃尔弗丽达都看清了他们一脸的凶相。她也认出来了，正是那批在索霍的阿登宅邸大门上砸得山响的士兵。他们准是骑了一整夜的马。于是她把心思绞紧了来作诗，就像你把肌肉绷紧了跳栅栏、跑百码一样。她几乎还没觉出自己在绞，那股绞劲儿已经起了作用，一段穆迪沃普的诗绞出来了，正朗诵出声——

===Original===
> “Dear Mouldiwarp, since Cousin Dick

> Buys his beautiful flags from Gamage’s,

> Take us away, and take us quick,

> Before the soldiers do us any damages.”

===Chinese===
> 「亲爱的穆迪沃普，既然迪克表哥

> 在加米奇买了漂亮的旗，

> 带我们走吧，快快带我们走，

> 趁士兵们还没把咱们欺。」

===Original===
And the moment she had said it, the white magic coats grew up and grew down and wrapped the children up as tight and as soft as ever a silkworm wrapped itself when it was tired of being a silkworm and entered into its cocoon, as the first step towards being a person with wings.

Can you imagine what it would be like to have lovely liquid sleep emptied on you by the warm tubful? That is what it felt like inside the white, wonderful cocoons. The children knew that the tower was turning wrong way up and inside out, but it didn’t matter a bit. Sleep was raining down on them in magic showers—no; it was closing on them, closer and closer, nearer and nearer, soft, delicious layers of warm delight. A soft, humming sound was in their ears, like the sound of bees when you push through a bed of Canterbury bells, and the next thing that happened was that they came out of the past into the present with a sort of snap of light and a twist of sound. It was like coming out of a railway tunnel into daylight.

===Chinese===
她话音刚落，那两件白色的魔衣便朝上长、朝下长，把两个孩子紧紧裹住，裹得又严实又柔软——一只蚕当蚕当腻了，要进茧去、迈出长翅膀的第一步时，就是那样把自己裹起来的。

你能想象暖暖的一大桶、满怀满怀地往你身上倾倒可爱的液态睡意，是什么滋味吗？在那些白色的奇妙茧子里，就是那种滋味。孩子们知道塔楼正在倒竖过来、正在里外翻转，可这一点也不要紧。睡意化作魔法的一阵阵细雨落在他们身上——不；是正在合拢过来，越合越近，越拢越紧，一层又软又甜的暖意一层层裹上来。他们耳中是一片轻柔的嗡嗡声，像你拨开一畦风铃草（Canterbury bells）时蜜蜂的动静。接下来发生的事，是他们随着一道光的脆响、一声音的拧转，从过去回到了现在。就像坐火车钻出隧道，一头扎进日光里。

===Original===
The magic coverlet-coat-cocoons had even saved them the trouble of changing into their own clothes, for they found that the stiff, heavy clothes had gone, and they were dressed in the little ordinary things that they had always been used to.

“And now,” said Elfrida, “let’s have another look at that Gamage paper, if it hasn’t disappeared. I expect it has though.”

But it hadn’t.

===Chinese===
那些魔毯——魔衣——魔茧甚至省去了他们换回自己衣裳的麻烦，因为他们发现，那身又硬又沉的衣裳已经不见了，他们穿的正是自己一向穿惯的那些家常小衣裳。

「现在，」埃尔弗丽达说，「咱们再瞧瞧那张加米奇的纸吧——要是它没消失的话。不过我看它是消失了。」

可它没有。

===Original===
“I should like to meet Dick again,” said Edred, as they went downstairs. “He was much the jolliest boy I ever met.”

“Perhaps we shall,” Elfrida said hopefully. “You see he *does* come into our times. I expect that New Cross time he stayed quite a long while, like we did when we went to Gunpowder Plot times. Or we might go back there, a little later, when the Gunpowder Plot has all died away and been forgotten.”

“It isn’t forgotten *yet*,” said Edred, “and it’s three hundred years ago. Now let’s develop our films; I’m not at all sure about those films. You see, we took the films with us, and of course we’ve brought them back, but the *picture* that’s on the films—we didn’t take that with us. I shouldn’t be a bit surprised if the films are all blank.”

===Chinese===
「我倒想再见见迪克。」下楼的时候，埃德雷德说。「他是我见过的孩子里头顶顶痛快的。」

「也许见得着呢。」埃尔弗丽达满有希望地说。「你瞧，他*确实*来过我们的时代。我看新克罗斯那回，他待了挺久，就像我们去火药阴谋年月那样。要不咱们过些日子再回去一趟，等火药阴谋都烟消云散、被人忘干净了。」

「到如今还*没*忘呢。」埃德雷德说，「那可是三百年前的事了。走，咱们去冲洗胶片吧；那几卷胶片我心里一点底也没有。你瞧，胶卷是咱们带去的，当然也带回来了，可胶卷上的*影像*——咱们没带它去呀。这几卷全成了空白，我一点也不会奇怪。」

===Original===
“It’s very, very clever of you to think of it,” said Elfrida respectfully; “but I do hope it’s a perfectly silly idea of yours. Let’s ask Mrs. Honeysett if we may use the old room she said used to be the still-room to develop them in. It’ll be a ripping darkroom when the shutters are up.”

“Course you may,” said Mrs. Honeysett. “Yes; an’ I’ll carry you in a couple of pails of water. The floor’s stone; so it won’t matter if you do slop a bit. You pump, my lord, and I’ll hold the pails.”

“Why was that part of the house let to go all dirty and cobwebby?” asked Elfrida, when the hoarse voice of the pump had ceased to be heard.

“It’s always been so,” said Mrs. Honeysett. “I couldn’t take upon me to clear up without Miss Edith’s orders. Not but what my fingers itch to be at it with a broom and a scrubbing brush.”

===Chinese===
「你能想到这一层，聪明，真聪明。」埃尔弗丽达恭恭敬敬地说，「可我巴不得这是个蠢透了的主意。咱们去问问哈尼塞特太太，能不能借用她说原先当配膳室的那间旧屋来冲洗。百叶窗一关，那就是一间顶呱呱的暗房。」

「怎么不能。」哈尼塞特太太说。「行；我给你们拎两桶水来。地是石头的；你们洒点儿也不碍事。老爷，您压水，我来扶桶。」

等水泵那沙哑的声音听不见了，埃尔弗丽达问：「宅子里这一处，怎么就由着它脏兮兮、结满蜘蛛网呢？」

「一直就是这个样儿。」哈尼塞特太太说。「没有伊迪丝小姐的话，我可不敢自作主张去收拾。不过说真的，我这双手早就痒痒了，恨不得抄起扫帚和板刷大干一场。」

===Original===
“But *why*?” Elfrida persisted.

“Oh, it’s one of them old, ancient tales,” said Mrs. Honeysett. “Old Beale could tell you, if anyone could.”

“We’ll go down to old Beale’s,” said Edred decidedly, “as soon as we’ve developed our pictures of the castle—if there *are* any pictures,” he added.

“You never can tell with them photo-machines, can you?” said Mrs. Honeysett sympathetically. “My husband’s cousin’s wife was took, with all her family, by her own back door, and when they come to wash out the picture it turned out they’d took the next door people’s water-butt by mistake, owing to their billy-goat jogging the young man’s elbow that had got the camera. And it wasn’t a bit like any of them.”

===Chinese===
「可*到底为什么*呢？」埃尔弗丽达追问。

「唉，那是老掉牙的古老故事里的一桩。」哈尼塞特太太说。「要是有人讲得清，老比尔准行。」

「等咱们把城堡的照片冲洗出来，就下老比尔家去。」埃德雷德拿定了主意，「——要是*真有*照片的话。」他补了一句。

「这种照相机器的事儿，谁说得准呢，是不是？」哈尼塞特太太深表同情地说。「我男人表弟的媳妇儿，带着一家老小，在自家后门口照了一张，等到洗出片子来，才发现照的是隔壁家的水缸——都怪他们那头公山羊，撞了拿相机那小子的胳膊肘一下。那张片子跟他们一家子，没有一处像的。」
